# 1761 Pochayiv Menaion — Vol. 10: June (Pochayiv Lavra, 1761) <a id="1761-pochayiv-menaion-vol-10-june-pochayiv-lavra-1761"></a>

*[Typographical decorative border]*

**BOOK OF THE MENAION**  
**MONTH OF JUNE**

To the glory of the Holy, Consubstantial, Life-Creating, and Undivided Trinity: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit; under the reign of the Great
KING AUGUSTUS III,[^1]
By the blessing of His Excellency, the Lord
SYLVESTER LUBIENIECKI-RUDNITSKY,[^2]
Exarch of the Metropolia of Kyiv, Halych, and all Rus', Bishop of Lutsk and Ostroh; by the good pleasure of the Most Reverend Lord Father Hieromonk
HYPATIUS BILIANSKY,[^3]
Protoarchimandrite of the Order of Saint Basil the Great; through the diligence and at the expense of the monks of the same Order in the Holy Wonderworking Lavra of Pochaiv,
ISSUED IN PRINT.
In the year from the creation of the world according to the Greek chronographers: 7269;
And from the Nativity of Christ: 1761.[^4]

*(Vacat — Fol. 1v: Unnumbered blank verso) [Woodcut Headpiece: The Holy Martyr Justin the Philosopher]*

# MONTH OF JUNE

*Having 30 Days.*

*On the 1st Day: The Holy Martyr Justin the Philosopher; and Another Martyr Justin, and Those with Them:*
*Alleluia, or Troparion.*[^5]
*At "Lord, I have cried," if we sing [the service], we sing 3 stichera of the Theotokos:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
*And 3 of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben the same:*

When the ice of ignorance held fast all creation through the assaults of the enemy, and the multitude of idols was served, then, O glorious martyrs, with a fervent heart and with the warmth of divine faith ye brought this to an end, lovingly shedding your blood for Him Who openly poured out His blood upon the Cross.

When by divine beckoning ye separated yourselves for supernatural struggles, having cast off the corruptible body, then, being strengthened by the power of the Almighty, ye were not terrified of fire, nor of the cutting sword. Therefore, having bowed your necks unto God, O blessed ones, ye received your end with joy.

John the valiant sufferer, Valerian, and Chariton, and Charita with them, and divine Justin, Euelpistus, and glorious Hierax, having dyed their garment from their divine blood, and being clothed together, stand before the King of all in the heavens, before Christ the King with the Angels.

*Other stichera of the Theotokos, Tone and Podoben the same:*

Rejoice, star radiant as the sun; rejoice, all-immaculate cause of all good things! Rejoice, thou who didst contain the uncontainable God, who didst sprout forth the ear of immortality; rejoice, divine palanquin and all-radiant portal!

All-radiant portal! Rejoice, slaying of the ancestral curse, O Maiden, who art the mediatress of good things!

O Joy of all that sorrow, and protectress of the wronged, and nourisher of the poor, consolation of travelers, and staff of the blind, visitation of the sick, shelter and patroness of them that labor, and helper of orphans, Mother of God the Most High: thou art all-pure; hasten, we pray thee, that thy servants may be saved.

A protection and defense art thou unto us Christians, and a mighty refuge, O Mother of God the Most High. But now as always, through thy vigilant intercession, cease not to pray that thy servants may be saved from all tribulations; for next to God, all we Christians have thee as defense and refuge.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
No other mighty refuge, nor tower of strength, nor unassailable wall have we truly gained save thee, O all-pure one. Unto thee do we flee, and unto thee do we cry aloud: O Lady, help, lest we perish! Show forth thy grace upon us, and the glory of thy strength, and the majesty of thy tender mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the tree of the Cross, O Jesus, she that knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who bare Thee, O inaccessible Light of the unoriginate Father? Hasten and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory!

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: through their supplications save our souls!

*Canon, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of Egyptian bondage, for He hath been glorified.*

Adorned with the splendors of martyrdom, O martyrs, ye stand before the pure throne of the Master of all, granting the remission of our sins.

The pious champions of truth, having stepped forward with one mind before the tribunal of the tyrants, cast down the deception of the devil by spiritual power.

By wounds, and stripes, and tribulations, the divinely called great-martyrs purchased the kingdom of Christ, wherein they now dwell crowned.

*Theotokion:*
Having seen the creation of Thy hands alienated, O Thou Who art rich in God's mercy, Thou didst take up Thy dwelling in the womb of a Virgin who knew not man, and hast shown her unto us as a protectress.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing praise unto Thee.*

Bearing the foundation of Christ in thy heart, O Justin, thou didst manfully oppose those who interrogated thee, who most unlawfully commanded thee to break the law.

A divinely bestowed choir, a God-chosen army of holy martyrs, a holy assembly gathered today, hath conquered the bodiless foes together with the body.

Having given over their bodies unto stripes, the magnanimous ones rejoiced, foreseeing eternal enjoyment and future glory and brightness.

*Theotokion:*
Dispel the clouds of my soul, O light cloud of the Sun of glory, and with light guide my mind, which is darkened by the blackness of malice.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́лное сло́во):*
Justin, and Chariton, Peon, Euelpistus, Charita, Hierax, and Valerian: radiant luminaries who illumine the ends of the earth with the rays of their struggles, let them be praised with a loud voice in faith, as they pray unto Christ that we may be saved.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Fervent protectress and invincible, certain and unashamed hope, wall, protection, and haven of them that flee unto thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: with the Angels pray thou unto thy Son and God to grant peace unto the world, and salvation, and great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee hanging willingly upon the Cross between thieves, O Christ, Thy Mother, maternally torn in her inward parts, said: O sinless Son, how art Thou unjustly nailed unto the Cross like an evildoer, wishing as the Tender-hearted One to bring the human race to life?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I have glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.*

At the suffering of Justin, and Chariton, and Charita, and Peon, the angelic ranks were amazed.

Their bodies being shattered by wounds mediated divine blessedness for their souls.

Like spiritual coals ye burned up deceit with divine fire, and were revealed, O martyrs, as luminaries of the world.

At the courage of Euelpistus and Hierax, unbending martyrs, the princes of darkness were struck with fear.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin who in thy womb didst receive the fire of the Godhead, deliver me from fire and torment!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for beside Thee we know no other God.*

Filled with the Divine Spirit, O blessed martyr Justin, having suffered valiantly, thou didst drown the fierce spirits of malice in the streams of thy blood.

She who is named after grace was filled with divine grace; and forgetting woman's natural weakness, she trampled down the foe lawfully and received glory.[^6]

Having endowed thy soul with grace through sacred confessions, thou didst blunt the malice of the ancient enemy, O sufferer Chariton, precious ornament of divine passion-bearers.

*Theotokion:*
O pure one, who didst bear Christ the Savior, Who likened Himself unto earthly men through the surpassing multitude of the riches of mercy: pray unto Him as God to save thy servants.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God!*

Let us all glorify the most radiant memory of Justin, and Chariton, Peon, and Valerian, and Charita, and Hierax, and Euelpistus!

Without tempest, through divine governance, ye passed through the deep sea of torments, O martyrs, and reached the tranquil haven of the Kingdom of Christ.

Shining like the stars of heaven, by the precious confirmation of the Church, ye enlighten the whole universe with the radiance of your suffering.

*Theotokion:*
Deliver me from immeasurable hard-heartedness, me who am held fast by many sins, O Lady Mother of God, and who am liable unto Gehenna's fire!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
With the wisdom of thy divine words, O Justin, the whole Church of God, having adorned herself, enlightens the world with the brightness of thy life; and having received a crown for the outpouring of thy blood, standing with the Angels before Christ: pray unceasingly for all of us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The wise Children did not serve the golden image, but entered into the flame themselves, and derided their gods; amid the flame they cried aloud, and an Angel bedewed them: The prayer of your lips hath already been heard.*

With the river of your blood ye dried up the depth of deceit, and poured forth streams of miracles, O martyrs, quenching the coals of fierce passions for them that ever cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

With the fire of divine love, O wise ones, ye valiantly burned up the matter of malice; and having shone forth like fire, ye piously enlightened the hearts of all who cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Adorned with divine wisdom, wise Justin made foolish the pagan wisdom by grace, having confirmed [the faithful] to worship the Trinity and to cry aloud in an Orthodox manner: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, who knewest not wedlock, pure and blessed one, restoration of the fallen, forgiveness of sinners: save me, the prodigal, save me as I cry aloud unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Praise ye Him as Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages: God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew Children, and turned the flame into dew.*

Having been patiently tried in torments as in a fiery furnace, O passion-bearers of the Lord, ye truly shone forth divinely brighter than gold, crying aloud: We exalt Christ above all unto all ages!

With wise Justin let Charita, and Chariton, and Peon, and great Hierax be praised, together with Valerian, who through suffering drove away deceit and were united unto the angelic ranks.

Being led unto eternal life, O wise ones, with valiant wisdom ye all bowed your necks unto the sword in unanimity of soul, O martyrs, having offered yourselves as sacrifices unto God.

*Theotokion:*
Being the wellspring of life, in that thou didst bear the Water of Life, O Virgin Mother of God, give drink unto my soul, which is corrupted by the flame of sin, that I may glorify thee unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*God, God the Word, Who came by unutterable wisdom to renew Adam, who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating: from the Holy* Virgin, Who was ineffably incarnate for our sake: Him do we, the faithful, magnify with songs in one mind.

Shining with divine glory, O valiant sufferers, ye broke the chill of deceit, and became partakers of the sacred passions of Him Who took flesh for our sake, and with Him ye rejoice eternally in the heavens.

At the wisdom of Justin, and the courage of Charita, and truly at the boldness of Chariton, at the valiant struggles of Peon and Hierax, and at the great endurance of Valerian amid fierce torments, even the Angels marveled.

As upon a divine chariot having mounted, O glorious ones, ye joyfully ascended toward it by your blood; and from the life-bearing hand, O wise ones, ye received victorious crowns, unceasingly praying for us.

*Theotokion:*
Having borne without man the Word beyond word, O pure Virgin who knewest not wedlock: deliver us from assaults, and from all tribulations, and from soul-corrupting harm, and from passions, and from the everlasting flame and condemnation!

*[Decorative Headpiece]*

*In the Same Month, on the 2nd Day: Our Father among the Saints, Nikephoros, Patriarch of Constantinople, the Confessor:*
*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*[^7]

As the setting-up of truth, the confirmation of the faith, and the proclaimer of dogmas do we know thee, the harmonizer of piety, dwelling of purity, and chosen receptacle, fragrance of the Spirit, and surpassing great treasure of teachings, and foundation of Christ's Church, O divinely inspired sacred luminary! We praise thee, the seal of teachers and divine image, the mystagogue of Christ, O blessed bishop, and the God-flowing river of understandings that wisely drowns the thoughts of the lawless and of blasphemers!

Successor of the Apostles, of equal character with the sufferers, equal in zeal with the ascetics: having multiplied the talent of wisdom, O exceedingly rich one, thou wast accounted worthy of the joy of thy Lord, adorned with the grace of divine radiance and mentally with the beam of spiritual brightness, and now thou standest before the life-bearing right hand, ever illumined thence with rays, O glorious one!

*Other Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

Into the inner divine sanctuary of the unwaning light, of the true Tabernacle which the Lord pitched,[^8] as a lawful high priest thou didst enter, O venerable father, not with the blood of another, but with thine own, imitating Christ; thou didst not submit to the conspiracy of torment, by which thou didst please God unto the end, O father!

Adorned with word and deed, and with that sacred vesture, and with the rectitude of teaching, O all-wise Nikephoros, fosterer of wisdom: through confession of these things thou didst accept exile, and being examined and tried, thou didst endure fervently with strength of mind all tribulations that befell thee, O blessed one!

An instrument of the Spirit wast thou revealed to be, addressed from above with divinely inspired voices, proclaiming the ineffable mystery of the Savior and His divine, true, and distinguished truths, loudly preaching unto us the Incarnation of the Word, which is beyond comprehension and higher than all understanding and thought, O most honorable one!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Save me, O all-pure Lady, who didst ineffably bear Christ the Savior; for thee alone have I gained as protectress, and unassailable wall, and shelter, and rejoicing, and the divine consolation of my soul. Deliver me, therefore, from the unsleeping worm and from the everlasting fire, O Mother of Christ God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the Cross, O Jesus, and accepting passions willingly, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud: Woe is me! O sweet Child, how dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity and didst deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and image of meekness, a teacher of abstinence hath the truth of things shown thee unto thy flock; therefore through humility hast thou acquired lofty things, and through poverty rich things: O father Nikephoros, pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved![^9]

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing in hymns of Nikephoros as victor." Composed by Theophanes.[^10]*
*Tone 4, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The Red Sea's deep abyss with unwetted feet did Israel of old traverse dryshod, and by Moses' arms outstretched in the form of a Cross* vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

Action hast thou shown to be the stepping-stone unto contemplation; for having harmoniously attuned thy soul through active virtue, O exceedingly rich Nikephoros, thou didst soar aloft unto the supreme, preeminent contemplation.

Having put to death earthly thoughts through abstinence, and having directed thy soul with diligence, O divinely inspired Nikephoros, thou hast reached the havens of stormless enjoyment.

Having sustained the heresy of Mammon, the accursed lion Leo,[^11] dashed himself against thee, the firm rock, O God-receiver Nikephoros, and was swiftly shattered near Hades, the all-senseless tyrant.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the brokenness of mankind, O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear the Creator Who raised all of us who were cast down by the fall of our first parents, O Bride of God!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ: for there is none holy beside Thee, O Lover of mankind.*

Having adorned thy life with thy virtues, and having visibly become the keeper of the luminary of the word of life, thou didst guide Christ's all-holy flock.

Thy God-speaking, fiery, divine tongue burned up thorny heresies with the spiritual fire, O divinely wise, glorious father!

Having searched out the depth of wisdom through the love of wisdom, with the cords of thy teachings thou hast clearly drawn in them that rejected the image of Christ, O right marvelous one!

*Theotokion:*
Confessing the Son, Who was born in the last days from thy womb, O all-pure one, to be co-beginningless with the eternal Father, we truly glorify thee as the Mother of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*
The grace of thy wise words and the height of thy mystical teachings have become for us a divine ladder of ascent; for thou didst gain a spiritual trumpet, O father, and through it didst preach divine dogmas. Therefore hast thou also settled in a place of pasture, having driven away the beast with the sling of thy words, O blessed Nikephoros: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that lovingly honor thy all-honorable memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As Virgin and alone among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all we generations of men call thee blessed bless thee, all generations of mankind: for the fire of the Godhead took up its dwelling in thee, and as an infant dost thou nourish with milk the Creator and Lord. Therefore, both the angelic and the human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that with faith worship thy most holy childbearing.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb wept and cried out, and bitterly lamenting, she cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my inward parts are on fire, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou dost endure out of tender mercy, O longsuffering Lord! O abyss of mercy, and inexhaustible wellspring: have compassion, and grant remission of sins unto them that in faith hymn Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
By divine power, O sacredly revealed one, thou didst crush the jaws of the ungodly with thy firm word; having conquered them, thou didst teach [all] to honor the divine icon of the Savior.

*Troparion:*
By confession thou didst radiantly illumine the divine priesthood, O father Nikephoros, shutting the lion's savagery,[^12] O God-pleasing initiate of unutterable mysteries.

*Troparion:*
With youthful vigor didst thou oppose [the heretics], O Nikephoros, with the pious teachings of the honorable Church; by the wounding of thy words thou didst drive away the wolves that steer astray the upright faith.

*Theotokion:*
Having borne the Word of God incarnate Who before was bodiless, O all-immaculate one, thou who art full of grace hast renewed the world. Therefore, as the Bride of God, with Orthodox faith we honor thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance them that hymn Thee with faith.*

*Troparion:*
Having a pure mind ascending unto the heavens, O all-blessed one, thou didst find the unfading flower of understanding and wisdom.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst drop down as dew the healing of thy words upon Orthodox souls, O divinely inspired one, and didst overthrow the land of the senseless by thy teachings.

*Troparion:*
With active word and embracing life uncontained, shining like the sun, thou dost enlighten the honorable Church's confirmation.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the release from the ancestral mother's curse, O all-immaculate one, having conceived Him Who crowned us with divine blessings.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.*

*Troparion:*
Shining radiantly with spiritual beams, O divinely wise one, thou didst appear unto the churches as an immovable pillar and a cloud leading unto the heavenly inheritance.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst consecrate all thy life unto God; abiding as a leader over all the universe, thou wast glorified, O sacredly revealed, blessed father.

*Troparion:*
Thy divinely inspired word tears apart the webs of godless heresies with the two-edged sword of the Comforter, and showed forth the light of truth unto all.

*Theotokion:*
The laws of nature are marvelously undone: for a virgin bears, and the maiden nourishes without knowing wedlock, having conceived the Creator and Lord of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*[^13]
Having received from God a crown of victory from heaven, O exceedingly glorious Nikephoros: save them that honor thee with faith, as a sacred hierarch and teacher of Christ.

*Ikos:*
Like a city of God called from heaven upon a mountain, the great hierarch appeared as an established pillar, preserving the world by his prayers; having conquered the ranks of the godless, he remained unharmed, unentangled by the arrows of the foes, carrying within his heart the King and Lord of all. Therefore he also gave clarity unto them that honor Christ on an icon: from the height of virtues and the pillar of deeds, with a sling he hurls against the ranks of the foes, and opens the doors unto the merciful, who in faith sing praise unto him as the hierarch and teacher of Christ.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the Children of Abraham, burning with the love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
We know thee as a living church of the living God, having within, like a golden lamp, the knowledge of the Trinity: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cast down the blasphemous babblings of the heretics, assuring [all] to honor the icon of Christ, O Nikephoros, as they cry aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having the wealth of wisdom and a divine life, thou didst pass beyond all sense spiritually; now dost thou converse with Christ: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Neither dividing the united nor confusing [them], we glorify the one Christ, the Word incarnate of thee, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The childbearing of the Theotokos saved the pious Children in the furnace: then indeed prefigured, but now in deed, it stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Delivered by the blood of Christ, with understanding he surrendered to death for His sake; for thou didst bring the martyrdom of conscience, shining with the grace of confession, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Cleansed in body and soul, the Word was seen in thee; with word didst thou offer sacrifice, showing Him Who of old was bodiless and without beginning, and Who afterward became merciful man, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest attain the heavenly inheritance and unchangeable glory, O glorious one, thou didst leave behind fading glory, O father, manfully enduring exile and affliction, O wise one, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
As a single pure dove did the spiritual Bridegroom find thee, O all-immaculate one; a most fragrant lily, a most comely blossom, and a light-bearing bridal chamber, He led thee in as His bride. Therefore do we hymn thee as Virgin, and exalt thee above all unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A cornerstone cut without hand from an uncut mountain,[^14] O Virgin, was Christ, Who joined together the severed natures. Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.*

*Troparion:*
Thou hast found a blessed end, having passed beyond mirrors unto the manifestation of truth, by the vision of incorruptible beauty: feasting upon it now, remember us by thy prayers!

*Troparion:*
As we have seen the divine [memory] of the God-bearing Nikephoros shine forth, come, ye Christ-bearing people, with fervor let us hymn him as a victor, and with gladness let us celebrate his feast!

Having held fast every virtue, at the conclusion of thy struggles thou didst appear adorned with the crown of confession, O hieromartyr; embellished with it, thou now standest before thy Master.

*Theotokion:*
As the root and fountain and cause of incorruption do all we faithful know thee, O Virgin, and with praises do we honor thee; for thou hast gushed forth hypostatic immortality unto us.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "While the disciples looked on" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ зрѧ́щымъ):*[^15]
As an eminent victor over passions, O Nikephoros, thou didst receive a twofold crown from Christ the Savior, O hierarch and sufferer, as a champion of piety, O father, in that thou didst most wisely cast down the God-fighting heresy of them that reject the divine icons: pray therefore for us who honor thee, O blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Not leaving the Father's heavens, even though Thou didst take flesh from the Virgin, O Lord: to save me who was fallen, bowing the heavens Thou didst descend, wishing to snatch me from the mouth of the noetic beast and from his strangling hold. Therefore, delivered, I glorify Thy power.

*[Decorative Headpiece]*

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 3RD DAY:*
*The Holy Martyr Lucillian and Those with Him:*[^16]
*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всѣхва́льнїи мч҃єницы):*

Fierce youths, corrupt through envy, surrendered thee who wast a God-slayer,[^17] O glorious one; yet having acted manfully and invincibly, O Lucillian, thou didst receive the enjoyment of Paradise. Therefore pray thou that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

The all-consecrated children, obeying thee as a father, suffered steadfastly with thee, and the glorious and venerable Paula, the marvelously praised martyr; with whom thou now dwellest in the heavens: pray thou unto Christ that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

Thy reliquary ever goushes forth waters of healing unto them that hasten to it, O much-suffering martyr, and washes away passions and drowns demonic ranks by the operation of the Holy Spirit. Therefore pray thou that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls!

*Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone and Podoben the same:*

Rejoice, marvelous hearing, O pure one! Rejoice, sacred tree of Paradise planted by God! Rejoice, destruction of deceitful demons! Rejoice, two-edged weapon that didst cut off the enemy's head by thy marvelous childbearing, O all-holy and all-immaculate one: call back us who have become estranged!

Heal my soul, O all-holy one, which is grievously sick with deceitful passions, O thou who didst bear Christ the Physician and Savior of all, Who healed every wound of him that was wounded by the devil's malice, and Who ransomed us from death!

O most holy temple of God, broader than the heavens and holier than the Cherubim, O thou who art full of grace, all-holy Maiden: bestow grace upon my mind and enlighten the eyes of my heart, granting me by thy acceptable intercessions the forgiveness of all my sins!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Woe is me, what shall become of me, having defiled mind and soul and body with transgressions! What shall I do? How shall I escape the unendurable flame, the insoluble and eternal bonds? But O all-immaculate one, before the end pray thou unto thy Son to grant me remission!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the Ewe-lamb and spotless Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor comeliness, weeping she said: Woe is me! Whither hath Thy beauty departed, O sweetest One? Whither Thy majesty? Whither the gleaming grace of Thy countenance, O my most beloved Son?

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing the glory of the Martyr Lucillian." Tone 8.*[^18]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the water as dry land, and escaping from Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*

*Troparion:*
As the servant of God the Word and the glory of martyrs do I sing thy praise, O Lucillian; therefore by thy prayers grant me cleansing from evils and healing.

*Troparion:*
With all thy mind and soul thou didst cleave entirely unto the love of Christ, and didst wisely despise the deception and impotence of idols.

*Troparion:*
Desiring the tormentor's beheading rather than all earthly glory, O divinely blessed one, thou hast inherited incorruptible heavenly glory.

*Theotokion:*
The ladder which Jacob saw, O Theotokos, art thou; for through thee the Word was united unto them that are upon the earth, and drew up human nature unto the height.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit sings praise unto Thee.*

*Troparion:*
Judas of old betrayed the Deliverer Christ unto the murderers of God; and thou now hast been betrayed unto the lawless Jews.

*Troparion:*
When the deceiver attempted to beguile thee with flattering words, by thy words, O sufferer, he appeared as though struck with stones.

*Troparion:*
A most radiant luminary wast thou unto them that suffered with thee, radiantly guiding [them] unto the divine paths of the enjoyment yonder.

*Theotokion:*
I pray that He Who was born of thee may be merciful unto me through thy bold intercessions, O Lady, in the day of judgment.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*[^19]
Drawing near unto the fire, thou wast by no means burned, O Lucillian, valiant-minded sufferer; for thou didst acquire divine dew that refreshed thee. Therefore, rejoicing, thou didst accomplish struggles beyond nature together with the holy children, with whom remember us!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
O Hope of Christians, all-holy Virgin, whom thou didst bear as God beyond mind and word: unceasingly pray with the heavenly hosts to grant remission of all our sins and correction of life unto them that ever glorify thee in faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, the all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the tender creation of Thine all-pure hands?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.*

*Troparion:*
Thou dost shine forth by thy words and enlighten the world by thy sufferings; and the opposition of the tyrants thou dost drive away like an unsteady mist.

*Troparion:*
A new Abraham wast thou shown to be through faith, who of old was justified in Christ; for by the knowledge of thy Creator thou didst cast away all vanity.

*Troparion:*
Having gained thee, O glorious martyr, as the destroyer of deceit and the expeller of painful passions, we, the faithful, are ever filled with healings.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is the mystery of thy childbearing, O Ever-Virgin; for having conceived God in thy womb, by an ineffable word thou didst bring Him forth.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*
O Christ, only Physician of souls and bodies: by the supplications of the martyrs, heal the sicknesses of my passions!

*Troparion:*
A consecrated light by the operation of the Spirit wast thou revealed to be, O glorious martyr, offering forth fruitful virtues in thy sufferings.

*Troparion:*
Having the words of life in thy heart, O wise one, by thine opposition thou didst put to death the threats of the tyrants, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
An unassailable wall, and a haven, and confirmation, and hope, O pure Mother of God, and having thee as a divine armor, we are saved.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the abyss of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!*

*Troparion:*
Having mentally elevated the divine desire of thy soul, thou didst boldly rush toward the tormentor's fire, and by the dew of the Spirit thou wast refreshed with the children, O all-blessed sufferer.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by thy teachings, the wise martyr Paula contended patiently against the deceiver, and received the crown of righteousness from the hand of Christ the Prize-giver.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thine unbending mind, immovable as Mount Zion,[^20] the enemy could not endure it; but he was put to shame and trampled unto the earth by thee, O divinely wise one, in the struggle of suffering.

*Theotokion:*
Truly thou didst conceive the Divine Word in thy womb, and Him beyond nature didst thou bear, O all-pure one. Make Him merciful through thy prayers, to deliver thy servants from all tribulations.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Children that came from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
With Paul didst thou cry aloud, O martyr: Neither tribulation, nor distress, nor wounds, nor the sword shall separate me from the love of Christ![^21] Therefore didst thou also cry aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Upon the rock of divine confession thou didst establish thy feet, appearing unshakeable by the storm of tribulations. Therefore didst thou cry aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
We have all been delivered from passions and tribulations, and adversities, and multifaceted sicknesses, O Virgin, by thy supplications, O all-pure Mother of God, crying aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn, praise ye and exalt Him above all unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Planted by faith in the Lord truly at the divine outgoings of the waters,[^22] thou didst appear, O all-blessed one, as a life-bearing tree.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the beauty of the honorable confession of Christ, the sufferers worthily received crowns from heaven.

*Theotokion:*
Bow down with sicknesses and fallen, raise me up, O Mother of God, that I may glorify thee unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*We magnify thee, the all-immaculate Mother of Christ our God, whom the Holy Spirit overshadowed.*

*Troparion:*
Beholding today the fragrant martyrs of Christ as the blossoms of the spiritual Paradise, with praises we magnify them.

*Troparion:*
Truly light-bearing were the martyrs of Christ God shown to be unto the Church: they shine with the light of confession.

*Troparion:*
O Thou Who by the prayers of passion-bearers didst heal the sicknesses of passions, heal my sicknesses also now, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Thee as a wall and protection, and a convenient haven, O all-immaculate one, have we faithful gained, through whom also we are saved.

*[Decorative Headpiece]*

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 4TH DAY:*
*Our Father among the Saints, Metrophanes, Patriarch of Constantinople:*[^23]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having set aside all" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^24]

Wholly consecrated, a God-bearer wast thou revealed, a divine anointing:

clothed with the Holy Spirit and radiantly entering the higher things into the Holy of Holies,[^25] illumined by the primordial splendors, partaking of the grace of the holy mysteries, as a true and exceedingly glorious hierarch, praying with boldness for our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Thy life shone forth with the splendors of virtues: thou didst enlighten the faithful and didst disperse the darkness of deceit; for thou didst appear as a true, light-beaming sun, O most blessed hierarch Metrophanes! And now hast thou taken up thy dwelling where the unwaning Light shines, having become a son of the day by the grace of the Holy Spirit. Therefore, celebrating honorably thy divine and light-bearing memory, O ever-memorable one, in love we honor thee.

*Sticheron:*
By the nod toward God, O divinely wise one, thy mind adorned with faith appeared radiant, O all-glorious one: in a mortal and corruptible body, having most wisely meditated upon incorruption, thou didst gain the splendors of the bodiless hosts; and being outside passions, thou wast adorned with dispassion, O Metrophanes, father and all-wise hierarch, bright luminary and intercessor for them that in love honor thy memory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Rejoice, fulfillment of the law! Rejoice, temple of the Holy Trinity, incorruptible bride! Rejoice, divine chariot of the King of all! Rejoice, immaterial fire, bearing upon thine hands the Ember as with tongs![^26] O new Paradise, garden enclosed, most radiant divine table, undefiled dove, throne of the Most High, divine noetic couch, whom the Holy Spirit overshadowed, O Maiden!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When of old the spotless Ewe-lamb and all-immaculate Lady beheld her Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, maternally she cried aloud, and marveling she cried out: O sweetest Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How hath the ungrateful assembly delivered Thee unto the tribunal of Pilate, and condemns unto death the Life of all? Yet do I sing the praise of Thine ineffable descent, O Word!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A rule of faith and image of meekness, a teacher of abstinence hath the truth of things shown thee unto thy flock; therefore through humility hast thou acquired lofty things, and through poverty rich things: O father and hierarch Metrophanes, pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing the glory of great Metrophanes." Tone 6.*[^27]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the deep dryshod with his feet as on dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!*

*Troparion:*
Standing with the heavenly choirs as a hierarch, as a minister of God: pray earnestly for us, that by thy prayers we may obtain eternal good things!

*Troparion:*
Chosen to serve the Lord's honorable Gospel, O blessed Metrophanes, by thy most wise teachings, O father, thou didst rear the people in prudence.

*Troparion:*
Enriched with divine understanding, thou didst gush forth the living word from thy heart, giving drink unto souls possessed by passions, O all-wise hierarch of God, divinely blessed Metrophanes!

*Theotokion:*
The sacred prophetic choir made manifest from afar that thou, O pure one, wast to be the true Parent of God, higher than the Cherubim and all creation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.*

*Troparion:*
Gushing forth divine sweetness from thy lips, O father, thou didst dry up the drops of bitter godlessness, having given the pious to drink the beverage of divine understanding, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Chosen by the judgment of God, thou wast revealed to be a hierarch: offering unbloody sacrifices unto God Who sacrificed Himself for our sake, O father and hierarch Metrophanes.

*Troparion:*
The sepulchre wherein thy venerable body lies goushes forth fragrance like a paradise of God, wafting sweetness upon the faithful, O exceedingly glorious and all-honorable hierarch Metrophanes!

*Theotokion:*
To comprehend the ineffable depth of thy childbearing, O pure one, human mind is at a loss; for God, having humbled Himself out of tender mercies, in thy womb renewed me entirely.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^28]
In that thou didst preach the faith of Christ and didst preserve it, thou didst truly increase thy faithful flock into a multitude; therefore with the Angels, O Metrophanes, thou rejoicest together, unceasingly praying unto Christ for all of us!

*Then Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^29]
Having reigned over carnal passions, a hierarch wast thou shown to be Godlike, and the imperial city didst thou gloriously shepherd, driving off the heretical ranks, O venerable one, with the staff of divinely-inspired words. Therefore after thy demise thou didst worthily inherit unending grace, O blessed hierarch! Therefore we cry unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that honor thy most honorable memory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Having fallen into multifaceted temptations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of mine unnumbered transgressions: as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O all-immaculate one, earnestly pray unto Him Who was incarnate from thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, ever beseeching Him to grant remission of transgressions unto them that worthily sing thy glory!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud, weeping and bitterly lamenting, she cried out: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O longsuffering Lord, bottomless abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain: have mercy and grant remission of transgressions unto them that in faith sing of Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner befitting God, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.*

*Troparion:*
Being filled with the Holy Spirit, O consecrated father Metrophanes, thou didst drive away evil spirits from men, and didst make the faithful steadfast by thy spiritual teachings.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst theologize the uncreated Unity, divided in three Persons, indivisible and unconfused, enlightening the pious by thy sacred theologies.

*Troparion:*
Having first put to death carnal mindsets by abstinence and labors, a divine hierarch wast thou revealed, and an all-consecrated advocate of the Trinity.

*Theotokion:*
The ancestral curse was destroyed through thee, O Mother of God; for unto us, O all-pure one, thou didst bring forth the fountain of holiness of ever-existing life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*By Thy divine light, O Good One, the souls of them that rise early unto Thee with love do Thou illumine, I pray Thee,* to know Thee, O Word of God, as true God, calling [them] out from the gloom of sin.

*Troparion:*
Accomplishing the things of God in a divine manner, and touching the holy things with purest contemplation, blameless wast thou shown to be, serving God as a most consecrated hierarch of God.

*Troparion:*
Servants of idols became servants unto God through thy venerable teachings; snatching them away from unworthy things, thou wast shown to be a worthy servant unto God the Almighty.

*Troparion:*
Called by grace, thou didst destroy the godlessness of idols; by the shower of thy words, O venerable one, thou didst make hearts that had been possessed to be fruitful by the grace of God.

*Theotokion:*
After thy strange childbearing, thou didst remain a virgin as before; for God was the One born, Who transforms all things by His will, O unwedded Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O most Merciful One!*

*Troparion:*
Thy tongue, wisely sharpened by the Spirit like the pen of a quickly writing scribe,[^30] inscribed the word of grace into the hearts of the pious, O all-consecrated father, by the divine nod.

*Troparion:*
Instructed in divine things, as entering into the noetic Holy of Holies, thou dost perfect the faithful by the Spirit with the radiance of the Trinity, O consecrated father, most perfect Metrophanes!

*Troparion:*
Having dammed the turbid decrees of the ungodly by the streams of thy commandments, thou wast revealed as a peaceful river, watering the congregations of the faithful with piety, O all-honorable hierarch!

*Theotokion:*
The Word took up His dwelling in thy womb without seed, and appeared as perfect man, renewing nature in a manner befitting God, as He alone knows, O God-welcoming Maiden, all-immaculate one!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^31]
From infancy thou wast shown to be an honorable vessel, having been chosen as a hierarch unto God, unto Whom with gladness thou didst cry aloud: Equal art Thou, O Christ, unto the Father and the Spirit!

*Ikos:*
Such a High Priest became us,[^32] an initiate of the Word of God and a minister, as Paul calls out, a divine instrument of Christ, ever offering services unto God, as blameless and honorable, visibly clothed indeed with a corruptible body, yet conversing with the bodiless in the ministry, and taking away the transgressions of the people by a righteous measure, as the great hierarch Metrophanes hath now appeared unto us, proclaiming with boldness: Equal art Thou, O Christ, unto the Father and the Spirit!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Angel made the furnace pour forth dew for the venerable Children, while the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tyrant to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparion:*
Illumined by spiritual grace through participation in the divine nod, thou wast a light-giving star, enlightening them that wisely sing: Blessed is God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having become a shining church, illumined by virtuous deeds, thou didst have the uncreated Trinity dwelling within thee, O consecrated one, crying aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Casting away slumber from thine eyes by divine vigilance, thou didst receive divine splendors from the Angel of light, which made thee a pillar and support unto the faithful, O hierarch of the truth!

*Theotokion:*
He Who sits uncircumscribed in the bosom of the Father, now sits circumscribed in thy womb, O all-pure one, clothed in thy form, having become circumscribed for the salvation of Adam.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*From the flame didst Thou pour forth dew for the venerable, and the sacrifice of the righteous one didst Thou consume with water; for Thou, O Christ, makest all things solely by willing: Thee do we exalt above all unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
The proud serpent didst thou humble by thy humility, O blessed one, being elevated unto God by thy pure thoughts; therefore do we honor thee, exalting Christ above all unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst theologize the incarnate Word, and didst save the people from the deceit of slavery, driving away the impiety of idols, O father and hierarch Metrophanes, all-wise and God-bearer!

*Troparion:*
Having thy life befitting thy countenance, and having speech befitting thy life: being filled with spiritual days, O father, thou didst pass over into the resting-places on high.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered from the first curse by thy childbearing, O all-blessed Maiden, full of the grace of God, we send up unto thee the voice of Gabriel: Rejoice, cause of the salvation of all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.*

*Troparion:*
Into the land of the meek didst thou take up thy dwelling, O hierarch, having been meek and guileless, joining together with the hosts on high, wholly adorned with virtuous splendors and clothed with the light of virtues.

*Troparion:*
Thou beholdest the splendors of God and of the Angels, the beauty of patriarchs, and of martyrs, and of Apostles, praying with them unto the Lover of mankind to grant remission of sins and correction of life unto us who praise thee, O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Thou who didst first brightly adorn the throne of the presiding of all cities, wherein having lived as an Angel, and having strengthened it by the hierarch's office, by anointing thou didst sanctify it, perfecting the people divinely-wise.

*Theotokion:*
As a heavenly drop the rain came down into thy womb, O Virgin, and dried up the streams of corruption, O holy one: shower incorruption upon all men, and deliverance was granted through thee, O thou full of the grace of God!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^33]
As a much-radiant star of the Church do we know thee, O hierarch Metrophanes, splendor of patriarchs; for thou didst synodically cast down the blasphemy of Arius, making the Church clear by Orthodox teachings. Therefore in faith we sing thy praise.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bear the hypostatic Wisdom, and the consubstantial Word, and the Physician of souls and bodies: heal the grievous and longstanding putrefaction and sores of my soul, and quiet the sicknesses of my heart, O Virgin!

*[Decorative Headpiece]*

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 5TH DAY:*
*The Holy Hieromartyr Dorotheus, Bishop of Tyre:*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "Thy martyrs, O Lord" (Мꙋ́ченицы твоѝ гдⷭ҇и):*[^34]

By the teachings of the divine Spirit wast thou shown, O divinely blessed father, bearing divine teachings inscribed in a divine understanding; by their unfolding thou didst enlighten them that were in peril through lack of understanding, therefore by thy prayers ask great mercy for all of us, O father!

*Sticheron:*
Having dyed thy sacred vesture with the divine streams of blood, O Dorotheus, rejoicing thou didst enter into the heavenly temple, now to appear before our God Who grants crowns unto them that have suffered. Earnestly beseech Him to send down great mercy upon all!

*Sticheron:*
Thy life was equal to the Angels, and thy martyrdom was enlightened, whereby thou wast accounted worthy to dance with the Angels in joy, illuminating the earth with divine wonders and teachings, O father Dorotheus; therefore by thy prayers ask that great mercy be granted unto all!

*Other Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone and Podoben the same:*

Snatch me, O Lady, from the hand of the manslaying serpent who strives deceitfully to swallow me up completely; crush his jaws, I pray thee, and destroy his subtleties, that having been delivered from his claws, I may magnify thy power!

A barren tree am I, O all-immaculate one, bearing no fruit of salvation. Therefore do I tremble at the cutting down, lest I, the wretched one, be cast into the unquenchable fire. Therefore do I fall down before thee: deliver me from this, and by thine intercession show me fruitful unto thy Son, O pure one!

The deadly arrow of desire hath wounded my heart, and I was wounded through lack of understanding, and I am sick incurably. O Mother of God, despise not me who am despaired of, but grant me healing, as having borne the Lord and Savior, the only Merciful One!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Impure are my thoughts, and deceitful my lips, and all my deeds are defiled, and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O good Virgin, pray unto thy Son and Creator and Lord, that He may receive my spirit in repentance, as the only Tender-hearted One!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The spotless Heifer, beholding the Young Bull willingly nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously cried aloud: Woe is me, most beloved Child! What hath the ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O dearly Beloved?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
And partaker of the character, and successor unto the thrones of the Apostles having become, thou didst find deed, O divinely inspired one, as an ascent unto contemplation: therefore rightly dividing the word of truth, for the faith's sake thou didst suffer even unto blood, O hieromartyr Dorotheus: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "The gift of God, thee, O all-blessed father, do I honor." The composition of Joseph. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone hath been gloriously glorified.*

*Troparion:*
As a most pure gift unto God, O all-blessed one, thou didst offer thyself, by a perfect life and by venerable martyrdom, O Dorotheus.

*Troparion:*
Like a ripe cluster of grapes, O father, having pressed thy mind, thou didst set forth the cup of gladness of teaching unto the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stop the deceitful streams, O Dorotheus, by the currents of thy most wise tongue, and didst water the minds of the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
Shower upon me, O Virgin, I pray thee, drops of compunction that wash away the defilement that hath increased upon me, that I may glorify thee!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings praise unto Thee.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst drive away every night of vanity by the splendors of thy most wise preaching, O father and hierarch!

*Troparion:*
By wonders thou didst confirm the thoughts of all the faithful, and by sacred teachings, O wise one, as a consecrated shepherd.

*Troparion:*
The Lord anointed thee with sacred anointing, O father, and appointed thee chief-shepherd of His divine Church.

*Theotokion:*
Illumine this heart of mine with divine light, O portal of light, driving away the gloom of sin!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^35]
Ever shining with divine dogmas, a holy gift thou didst offer thyself, having suffered for the immortal King, O Dorotheus, and thou dwellest now, ever rejoicing, in the heavenly metropolis of the firstborn.[^36] O venerable father, pray unto Christ God to grant great mercy unto us!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
He was not separated from the divine nature, having become flesh in thy womb; but God, having become man, remained such, Who after childbearing preserved thee a Virgin Mother, as before childbearing, all-immaculate, the only Lord: earnestly beseech Him to grant great mercy unto us!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The spotless Ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him Who sprouted forth from her without pain hanging upon the Cross, maternally lamenting in fitting manner, cried aloud: Woe is me, my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, wishing to deliver man from shameful passions?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.*

*Troparion:*
The frost-bearing winter of godlessness didst thou spit upon, O venerable one, and by the warmth of the divine Spirit thou didst keep thy heart unharmed.

*Troparion:*
Demonic base mountains crumbled before thy face, O blessed Dorotheus, and the ragings of wicked men melted away.[^37]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endure being wronged, being driven from city to city; therefore thou didst obtain the blessed glory and splendor of martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
Save me from temptations and soul-corrupting passions, O Birthgiver of God, that in faith I may call thee blessed, O all-holy Maiden, Virgin Mother!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*From the night of ignorance, having enlightened the ends [of the earth] with the knowledge of God, enlighten me by the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Discerningly beholding the divine rewards, O Dorotheus, thou didst endure great afflictions and temptations.

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming the Trinity in the unity of Godhead, O Dorotheus, thou didst destroy the vain raging of polytheistic madness.

*Troparion:*
Refusing to bow down, O sufferer, unto the works of lawless hands, thou didst endure temptations and afflictions, and stripes and sicknesses, O Dorotheus.

*Theotokion:*
The East of the Sun that shone forth before the morning star wast thou;[^38] through Whom, O pure one, they that were in the darkness of grievous things beheld light.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a robe of light, Thou that clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most Merciful Christ our God!*[^39]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst acquire a heart inflamed by the breathings of the Spirit, destroying the onslaught of adverse spirits, O Dorotheus.

*Troparion:*
The lives of the saints didst thou make manifest by sacred writings, O divinely inspired father, enlightening the soul with divine understanding.[^40]

*Troparion:*
The proclamation of thy words and the power of thy beautiful teachings went out into all the earth, O all-blessed one, by divine grace.[^41]

*Theotokion:*
Beseech Him Whom thou didst bear as God, Who became man, to deliver me from fire and torment!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Shining with virtues, and more than the sun with divine teachings, and by sufferings, O blessed one, thou didst shine forth and didst enlighten the earth, O Dorotheus, driving away the gloom of polytheism and grievous heresies; therefore brightly do we celebrate thy memory.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew Children boldly trampled the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!*

*Troparion:*
A most radiant star wast thou revealed to be by the splendors of thy struggles, enlightening the souls of them that faithfully cry aloud, O Dorotheus: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Walking in the right paths, thou didst cast down the ravines of the deceiver, guiding unto the paths of life them that cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
By the showers of thy blood, O all-glorious one, thou didst quench the deceitful furnace, and didst take up thy dwelling by the water of life,[^42] crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Deliver my wretched soul from the bond of sin, O Parent of God, and yoke it completely unto the love of God, that in faith I may glorify thee, O Birthgiver of God, unto the ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The divinely speaking Children in the furnace, trampling the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*

*Troparion:*
Having humbled thyself in the Lord, O father, thou didst humble the serpent boasting in malice, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
In thine advanced old age,[^43] the Creator settled thee in the ageless places of rest, O consecrated father, adornment of martyrs!

*Troparion:*
Having an angelic life, together with the Angels thou now standest before the throne of thy Master, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Comely wast thou revealed, O Virgin, having brought forth the all-comely Word, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The childbearing of the Ever-Virgin, prefigured on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush, for the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns do we magnify.*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst guide the flock of Christ unto divine havens, preserving it unharmed from the tempest of adversaries, as a most excellent hierarch and a wise fulfiller of divine commandments, O father!

*Troparion:*
As a pure celebrant of sacred mysteries, as a valiant martyr, as a marvelous wonderworker, and as a scribe of divine words,[^44] as an unshakable pillar of faith, eternal glory hast thou obtained, O father!

*Troparion:*
Having undergone many labors and sicknesses at the tribunal, rejoicing thou didst pass over unto the painless and divine life, now purely partaking of deification, O worthy of wonder, martyr Dorotheus!

*Troparion:*
Thy light-bearing memory hath shone forth unto the world, illuminating the faithful with rays of gifts: show us who praise it to be partakers, O father, of the divine splendor that is in thee!

*Theotokion:*
Bearing in thine arms Christ, Who bears all things by His divine beckoning, O pure one: ever pray unto Him to direct the lifting up of our hands as incense before His face![^45]

*[Decorative Headpiece]*

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 6TH DAY:*
*Our Venerable Father Bessarion the Wonderworker; and the Venerable Hilarion the New, of the Dalmatian Monastery:*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^46]

O light of monastics, wise Bessarion: by virtuous rays and by the radiance of grace thou wast a most radiant luminary in the wilderness; warmly delivering them that honor thee from the mist of passions and the darkness of grievous evils, and from every tribulation and temptation, who in faith sing of thy divine falling-asleep!

By divine love, patiently enduring standing under the open sky,[^47] thou didst show forth an opposition equal unto the sufferers of thine unutterable struggles; thou dost sweeten the waters of the bitter sea, and dost give drink unto souls in thirst, O most wise one; thou walkest above human torrents, performing dryshod a passage upon the waters, O most praised one![^48]

Knowing thee clearly as an emulator of Elijah in the world,[^49] O Bessarion, all of us praise thee: by divine mercy granted unto thee, by thy venerable prayers thou didst bring down from heaven showers of water, and rainy fountains, and divine dew; and thou dispensest from above, O all-blessed father, unto all the faithful grace and power, and an unassailable shelter!

*Other Stichera of the Venerable Hilarion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^50]

An undefiled life, patience and meekness, and unfeigned love, boundless abstinence, all-night vigil, divine compunction, true faith and hope, having acquired with mercy, O father: as an Angel didst thou live upon the earth in the body, O blessed Hilarion, intercessor for our souls!

An earthly Angel and a heavenly man wast thou, O venerable one: a fountain of compunction, a stream of compassion, an abyss of wonders, a surety of sinners, truly a fruitful olive tree of God,[^51] anointing with the oil of thy labors the countenances of them that faithfully praise thee, O marvelous Hilarion!

Thy mind shone with divine understanding; thou wast above bodily passions, unmixed with base things, bearing the impress and imaging within thyself divine beauty, and known to be wholly light-resembling by the operation of the Spirit, O Hilarion our father, adornment of monastics!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Rejoice, ray radiant as the sun, throne of the unwaning Sun, who didst dawn the incomprehensible Sun! Rejoice, O mind gleaming with divine splendors, lightning-like ray that hast enlightened the ends of the earth, true gleaming of gold, all-good and all-immaculate one, who hast shone forth the unwaning Light unto the faithful!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, weeping the all-pure one cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things that Thou didst unto them? And they hasten to make me childless of Thee, O dearly Beloved! I marvel, O Tender-hearted One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness: take not Thy mercy away from us, but through their intercessions direct our life in peace!

*Canon of Saint Bessarion. The composition of Joseph. Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He hath been glorified.*

*Troparion:*
The Church, keeping festival, sings unto God today on thy memory, O God-inspired Bessarion, and in faith glorifies thy great ascetic struggles, O father!

*Troparion:*
Having shown thyself a servant unto God, by the powers of the soul thou didst enslave the bodily passions, and didst show thyself free from every malice, O all-marvelous father!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the beauties at the consummation of thy struggles,[^52] thou didst show thyself a vessel well-pleasing to the divine Spirit, containing His operation, O blessed Bessarion!

*Theotokion:*
The deceitful enemy drew me into the pit of destruction; but stretch out thy mighty hand unto me, and swiftly, O Birthgiver of God, lead me up unto the light!

*Another Canon of the Venerable Hilarion, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having passed through the water as dry land, and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*

*Troparion:*
Having run unto the immaterial and noetic light from the darkness of material things, O venerable Hilarion: by thy prayers unto God enlighten me!

*Troparion:*
From infancy attaining all virtues, by the labors of abstinence and by fasting sweats, thou didst reach unto a full-grown man, and unto the spiritual measure of the stature of Christ.[^53]

*Troparion:*
Having passed through the abyss of passions, and escaped the tempestuous surging of demons, thou didst hasten dryshod into the haven of dispassion, O venerable father!

*Theotokion:*
The Word, Who is without mother from the Father, without father at the last thou didst bear God-manfully,[^54] O only Mother of God, as one who art higher than all creation!

*Ode 3 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
*Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies, for my spirit has rejoiced when singing: There is none holy as our God, and there is none righteous more than Thee, O Lord![^55]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst ascend unto the height of dispassion; thou didst enter into the darkness of the vision of God;[^56] thou didst receive in thy heart the divine Law; strengthening thyself thereby, thou wast a most radiant luminary of monastics, O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
As a fruitful olive tree, openly planted in the divine house, thou didst show thyself, O father; with the oil of thy struggles make rich our faces, who praise thy wonders, O venerable Bessarion!

*Troparion:*
Having lived a life of poverty, O blessed one, like unto the birds, O father, and clad with the wings of dispassion, rejoicing thou didst reach the heavenly mansions; therefore in faith we call thee blessed![^57]

*Theotokion:*
Having spent my life in slothfulness, and having defiled my heart with passions, I draw near unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, with compunction of soul; and I pray thee to have compassion and save me, establishing me in the patterns of repentance!

*Ode 3 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the establishment of them that hasten unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.*

*Troparion:*
With the coal of abstinence, O venerable one, thou didst burn up the thorns of the passions, and thou refreshest the minds of the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired through the Spirit the sharpened arrows of virtue, like a mighty warrior thou didst wound the heart of the cruel adversary.

*Troparion:*
Armed with the weapon of abstinence, and with prayer as a breastplate, O venerable one, thou didst conquer the hosts of invisible enemies.

*Theotokion:*
I pray thee to be a mother unto me through thy bold intercessions, O Sovereign Lady, before Him Who was born of thee, in the Day of Judgment!

*Kontakion of Saint Hilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Within the fences of thy life-bearing pasture, like a shepherd thou didst preserve thy flock unharmed, and didst show thyself great by the height of thy deeds, O new Hilarion, having received many torments and tribulations for the sake of piety; therefore thou hast settled in the all-joyous life of the Sion on high: pray for us, O venerable one!

*Sessional Hymn of Saint Bessarion, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Having burned up the passions with the fire of abstinence, thou didst show thyself a radiant candle of dispassion, driving away the darkness of our souls by grace; therefore today we celebrate thy light-bearing memory with gladness of heart, O Bessarion our father!

*Glory... of Saint Hilarion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Thou didst consecrate thy life unto God, O all-blessed one, having become an excellent minister of holy things, truly a priest of Christ; therefore after many sweats and labors, rejoicing thou didst pass over into the immaterial mansions, and now thou pourest forth for us streams of healings!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear the transcendent God: with the bodiless powers ceaselessly pray unto Him to grant us remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end, who in faith and love sing of thee as is meet, O only all-hymned one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

O all-immaculate Virgin Mother of Christ God, a sword passed through thy most holy soul[^58] when thou didst behold thy Son and God voluntarily crucified; ceaselessly pray unto Him, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions!

*Ode 4 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
*I sing unto Thee, for I have heard report of Thee, O Lord, and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who have gone astray; therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Much-merciful One![^59]*

*Troparion:*
Drops of healings thou didst receive from the abyss of grace, wherefrom by thy prayer, O wise one, thou didst turn the sea water into sweetness, curing the thirst of thy disciple, O right-marvelous one!

*Troparion:*
In no wise even unto old age didst thou fail in life, O God-wise father, wandering voluntarily in the deserts and mountains,[^60] seeking God Who enlightened thee with splendors surpassing noetic wonders!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast humble and abased, lifted up by dispassion; therefore thou didst dash down the haughtiness of the demons, having become a luminary of monastics, O father, who piously praise thee in faith!

*Theotokion:*
Heal the passions of my soul that grow within me most wretchedly; enlighten my thoughts, driving far away the darkness, O pure one, of the demons that afflict me and ever set snares for me!

*Ode 4 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.*

*Troparion:*
Planted by the waters of abstinence,[^61] O blessed father, thou didst show thyself a fair-branching vine, offering clusters of piety!

*Troparion:*
Having become wholly divine and Spirit-bearing, thou didst depart entirely unto God, O all-blessed Hilarion; therefore thou dancest ever with the Angels!

*Troparion:*
Upon the rock of faith thou didst set the steps of thy heart, and thou didst remain unshaken, not fearing the assaults of demons!

*Theotokion:*
Thee Who art higher than the Cherubim and the Seraphim we praise, O all-pure one; for Him before Whom they tremble, our God, thou didst bear in the flesh upon thine arms!

*Ode 5 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
*Enlightenment of them that lie in darkness, salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: unto Thee do I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy brightness, for other God beside Thee I know not!*

*Troparion:*
The enlightenment of the Comforter, having taken up abode within thy soul, O all-blessed one, showed the path leading unto the Kingdom; walking wherein, thou didst turn aside from the affliction of demons and from spiritual darkness!

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as a new Joshua, O God-bearer,[^62] thou didst halt the course of the sun by light-shining supplication, adorned with divine words, O blessed one, illuminated by the radiant dawn of wonders, and beautified by many miracles of God!

*Troparion:*
In the book of life thou wast worthily inscribed,[^63] having accomplished all things written in the divine books; therefore I cry aloud unto thee: By thy supplications unto the all-seeing God, O father, tear in pieces the handwriting of mine evil deeds![^64]

*Theotokion:*
Following the steps of Him Who shone forth from thy womb by an ineffable birthgiving, O Virgin Mother, the choir of the venerable hath been joined unto the heavenly ranks; with whom pray, and ask cleansing for us who honor thee!

*Ode 5 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*Rising early we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God, beside Thee we know no other.*

*Troparion:*
The eye of the heart thou didst keep sleepless in the divine commandments, blamelessly, O glorious one!

*Troparion:*
Upon the mountain of virtues by abstinence, O God-bearer, actively didst thou ascend, unto ascents of the vision of God!

*Troparion:*
Pouring out oil unto the poor from the heart, O venerable Hilarion, thou didst draw celestial riches!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord of the Law showed thee forth as the ark of the Word, O Birthgiver of God, wherein He ineffably took up abode!

*Ode 6 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
*Unto the Lord out of the whale Jonah cried aloud: Lead me up from the depths of hades, I pray; that as unto the Deliverer, in the voice of praise and in the spirit of truth, I may sacrifice unto Thee![^65]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not grow weary, drawing near unto many struggles; for forty nights and forty days, O venerable one, thou didst remain standing,[^66] unbent by the pressing necessities of nature!

*Troparion:*
Having mortified thy members which are upon the earth,[^67] O marvelous one, with the living operation of healings thou wast enriched, and thou livest in the heavens, ever healing the passions of our souls that bring death!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst finish thy life ever weeping, for divine desire inflamed thee within, O father; and now in the heavens thou hast found comfort, joining with the choirs of the venerable!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord was born of Thee in the flesh, the Creator of all creatures: pray Him, O pure one, to deliver our souls from the conquering enemy!

*Ode 6 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depths of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation!*

*Troparion:*
The noetic Goliath, who puffed himself up in wickedness,[^68] by thine unarmed combat thou didst slay in faith with a sling, and tearing away his victorious weapons, thou didst cut down him who boasted exceedingly!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst emulate the great Hilarion in virtues,[^69] and following his spiritual footsteps by abstinence, O venerable one, thou didst show thyself supreme and an exemplar for the flock!

*Troparion:*
By ascetical sweats thou didst extinguish unto the end the coals of the enemy's arrows; and by the fire of divine faith thou didst consume the rages of heretical unbelief![^70]

*Theotokion:*
Truly thou didst conceive the Word of God in thy womb, and Him beyond nature thou didst bear, O all-immaculate one; Whom entreat by thy prayers on behalf of them that truly praise thee as the Birthgiver of God!

*Kontakion of Saint Bessarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Emulating the powers on high, O venerable one, thou didst live after the manner of the birds, consigning temporal things unto oblivion, drawn with ceaseless desire toward the heavenly beauties of Christ the King, until thou didst attain unto Him, O Bessarion: pray ceaselessly for us all!

*Ode 7 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
*The most wise children served not the golden image, but entered into the flame of their own accord, and mocked their gods; amid the flame they cried aloud, and an Angel bedewed them: The prayer of your mouth hath now been heard![^71]*

*Troparion:*
Having renewed thy soul with the pious plow of abstinence, O father, thou didst cultivate a fruitful ear of corn, preserved in the heavenly granaries, and thou feedest them that faithfully sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having mingled with the immaterial Light, thou wast a pure light, and thou didst pass through the rushing torrent of the river without wetting thy feet at all;[^72] and in upright paths thou didst walk, O God-wise Bessarion, escaping the turbid waters of sin!

*Troparion:*
O fearful wonder! How thou didst make water that was formerly undrinkable to be potable by the divine sign of the Cross, and didst cross the torrent of the river and didst hold back the course of the sun, O God-wise Bessarion, magnified with sacred tokens from God!

*Theotokion:*
The Hypostatic Wisdom of God was born of thee, O pure one, and destroyed the evil-believing philosophers, and made wise the multitude of monastics and venerable ones to praise with grace the blessed one who bore God in the flesh!

*Ode 7 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*The children who came from Judea in Babylon of old trampled underfoot the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Troparion:*
In the verdure of the Kingdom on high thou didst pasture thy flock, O father; and with the staff of teachings driving away the heretical wild beasts, thou didst sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Stretching forth the radiance of wonders, thou drivest away the gloom of the diseased; and thou dispensest today rays of health and cures unto them that cry: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal banquet of Christ the King, clothed in a garment worthy of Him that called thee;[^73] therefore thou didst recline at table, crying aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Beyond nature and word thou didst bear the Master of all, and thou nourishest from thy breasts Him Who prepares in due season a table for all;[^74] Whom earnestly entreat on our behalf, O all-pure one!

*Ode 8 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
