*Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace with the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew: sing, ye works, as the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Light has shone forth clearly for thee as for a righteous man,[^75] and joy and gladness, because thou didst weep diligently upon the earth, O father, and wast a stranger thereto by lack of possessions and by every suffering of affliction!

*Troparion:*
Wholly mingled from within with supreme desires, O venerable father, thou didst pass through cities and deserts in great compunction, until thou didst reach the Metropolis on high, radiantly adorned!

*Troparion:*
As the dawn thou hast shone forth unto us, enlightening our thoughts ever with radiant virtues; therefore today we celebrate thy light-bearing memory, O father Bessarion!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear a Son, O pure one, co-unoriginate with the unoriginate Father, and thou alone didst renew our nature which had grown old through grievous transgressions; therefore in unison we sing of thee, O Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 8 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant frantically kindle the furnace for the pious; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
With tearful streams thou didst drown the surgings of passions and restless thoughts; and thou wast an abyss of long-suffering, O venerable one, guiding unto a most peaceful haven them that cry aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having thy teachings of abstinence, O venerable Hilarion, like a patient adamant we establish our hearts and conquer the legions of passions, crying aloud in faith with our tongue: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
By abstinence, O venerable one, wisely exhausting the voluptuousness of the flesh by self-restraint, thou didst fill the soul's operation with the Spirit, and didst attain unto the paths of dispassion, crying: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Beneath the shelter of thy holy wings, O Sovereign Lady, all of us who are afflicted with grievous sins, trials, and tribulations flee; having thee as an intercessor, we cry aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9 [Canon of Saint Bessarion]. Heirmos:*
*God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who through eating fell grievously into corruption, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: with one accord in hymns, ye faithful, let us magnify!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst overthrow the strongholds of the deceiver, girded with spiritual strength,[^76] and didst show thyself mighty over the passions, O all-blessed one; therefore having received the grace of wonders from God, thou healest incurable diseases for the faithful!

*Troparion:*
Thy great glory, which is in God, was manifested unto all creation, illuminating the faithful by the sacred similitudes of thine immaculate life, O God-wise Bessarion, truly the adornment of monastics and summit of fathers!

*Troparion:*
With groanings of weeping and mourning, watering the earth ever with tearful compunction, O God-wise father, thou didst finish thy course, until thou didst find divine comfort in the City of God with all the saints O venerable one, having settled therein!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst blossom like a lily, O father,[^77] with the fragrant perfumes of virtue filling our souls with sweet odor; therefore celebrating thy memory, we ask through thee, O Bessarion, to be delivered from the foul stench of sins and from every malice of demons!

*Theotokion:*
Sinning with harmful mind, I remain unamended, callous through much insensibility; therefore I cry unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: Visit me now who am in such necessity, and heal the passions of my humble heart!

*Ode 9 [Canon of Saint Hilarion]. Heirmos:*
*Heaven was struck with awe thereat, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of Angels and men magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God!*

*Troparion:*
Christ showed thee forth truly as a fountain of wonders, O blessed one, cleansing the painful pollutions of passions and easily swallowing up the sicknesses of diseases; therefore all of us call thee a river of spiritual cleansings, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Having blossomed spiritually like a palm tree in the house of God,[^78] O most glorious one, thou didst bear the fruit of abstinence like sweetness, wherewith wisely thou didst sweeten the hearts of thy rational flock; therefore thou hast received the crowns of heavenly glory, O ever-memorable father!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show thyself a rule of abstinence, O father, and didst appear as an image of meekness, and a gentle guide unto all, O Hilarion; by thy supplications unto the Lord calm also my tempest of passions, and grant profound peace unto him that asks, that I may worthily call thee blessed!

*Theotokion:*
We have thee as a weapon in tribulations and trials, a haven and a wall in afflictions, a noetic shield in battles, and a great place of propitiation in grievous transgressions and assaults; therefore as is meet we honor thee as the Birthgiver of God, and we magnify thy birthgiving!

*[Decorative Headpiece]*

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 7TH DAY:*
*The Holy Hieromartyr Theodotus, Bishop of Ancyra:*[^79]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the celestial ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^80]

With a joyful heart and a steadfast mind, thou didst truly dare valiantly unto ascetic struggles, O martyr, not fearing the torments of diseases nor a violent death; therefore having lawfully suffered, thou hast been radiantly crowned, O Theodotus!

By the trials of thy flesh, O blessed one, thou didst wound the adversaries, and by thy reproofs thou didst pierce their hearts as with arrows; and by the drops of the outpourings of thy blood, thou didst dry up the torrents of godlessness unto the end, O all-blessed one!

Enduringly burned with torches and having thy back broken with wounds, O martyr, thou didst endure, crying aloud: Nothing shall separate me from the love of Christ,[^81] neither death, nor life, nor any other torment!

*Other Stichera of the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone and Podoben the same:*

Overcome by unexpected falls, O pure one, having thee as our only protector, O Virgin, we cry aloud thankfully: Cleanse us, O most holy Bride of God; for thou art the refuge of the world and the intercession for our race!

With the staff of thine intercession, O pure Birthgiver of God, swiftly drive away the bestial passions of my wretched soul, peacefully directing my life, and count me with the holy flock of thine elect sheep!

Submerged in the abyss of transgressions, I have fled unto the quiet haven of thy most pure prayer, O Birthgiver of God, and I cry unto thee: Save me, stretching forth thy mighty right hand unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having slipped through corrupt habits, I lie prostrate, and I hasten unto thy calm harbor, O Virgin: from the contrary tempest and from multiform temptations deliver me, that I may ceaselessly sing of thy grace, O Birthgiver of God, Mother Ever-Virgin!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His long-suffering, weeping the pure Mother said: Woe is me, O sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having become a partner in ways and a successor upon thrones unto the Apostles, thou didst find in active deed an ascent unto contemplation, O God-inspired one; therefore rightly dividing the word of truth,[^82] thou didst suffer for the faith even unto blood,[^83] O hieromartyr Theodotus: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon, whose Acrostic is:*
*Truly wast thou a gift of God, O blessed one.*[^84]
*The composition of Joseph. Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the deep on foot as if on dry land, Israel beheld Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, and cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!*

*Troparion:*
Having shown thee forth as a radiant star upon the height of His Church, the noetic Sun with the brightness of thy sufferings illuminated the whole earth, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Dedicated unto God from a young age, O Theodotus, with God-given virtues thou didst manifestly enlighten thy soul, and with honorable sufferings thou didst adorn the world!

*Troparion:*
Beholding the snares of the deceiver spread upon the earth, the glorious martyr went everywhere, establishing the souls of the pious by teachings through grace!

*Theotokion:*
Bowing the heavens out of exceeding mercy, the Son of God came down and was incarnate of thee, O pure one,[^85] saving man from the serpent's deceit!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy like Thee, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession!*

*Troparion:*
Ever gazing unto God with noetic eyes, O ever-memorable martyr, though encompassed on every side by torments, thou didst not feel the wounds approaching thy body!

*Troparion:*
In no wise, O martyr, wast thou shaken by painful assaults, nor by the flatteries of the lawless, firmly planted upon the rock of Christ's faith, O glorious Theodotus!

*Troparion:*
Crushing the body with many wounds, the persecutor was entirely unable to weaken thy spiritual strength, which was established by the divine love of the Savior, O blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Beholding from afar the depth of thy mystery, the divinely sounding prophetic choir with multiform voices proclaims thee as the pure Mother of God, O all-immaculate one!

pure Bride of God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
A star of exceeding brightness, illuminating creation with gifts of wonders and with sacred sufferings, O blessed Theodotus, truly wast thou; therefore celebrating today thy light-bearing memory, in gladness of heart we magnify Christ!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
O unwedded, pure Virgin Mother of God, only protectress and shelter of the faithful: from perils and afflictions and grievous distresses deliver all who have hope in thee, O Maiden; and save our souls by thy divine prayers!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hanging dead upon the Wood, the spotless Ewe-lamb, weeping, spoke, crying aloud with a mother's voice: How shall I endure Thine ineffable condescension, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O God most good?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Christ is my power, God and the Lord, the honorable Church in a manner befitting God sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord![^86]*

*Troparion:*
By the wisdom of thy words and the grace of thy deeds, O Theodotus, thou didst manifestly put to shame the champion of darkness, and having gloriously contended, thou didst take victory over him!

*Troparion:*
With the fire of thy sufferings thou didst visibly kindle and consume the deceit of polytheism; and like a luminary by the Holy Spirit, O martyr and initiate of God's mysteries, thou didst enlighten all under the sun!

*Troparion:*
Firmly scraped in the ribs with an iron claw,[^87] O glorious one, and truly encompassed on every side with severe pains, thou wast found with an unshaken mind for the sake of the faith, O ever-memorable one!

*Theotokion:*
Preserving thee incorrupt after childbearing even as before childbearing, O Virgin, the Creator took up abode in thy womb, and renewed those who had decayed through many transgressions!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate, I pray, the souls of them that rise early unto Thee with love, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest us out of the darkness of sin![^88]*

*Troparion:*
By thy patience, O wise martyr, thou didst visibly endure the assaults of the lawless, the afflictions of torments, and the burning of fire, protected by divine assistance!

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming the word of piety, journeys of a long way far off, O God-wise martyr, thou didst travel, and those led astray by the darkness of vanity thou didst turn toward the light!

*Troparion:*
Raised up like a lamb upon the wood, O martyr, thou didst endure the tearing of iron claws as though bodiless, O blessed one, putting off the coarseness of mortality!

*Theotokion:*
Truly the incarnate Word of the Father from thy pure blood, O pure Virgin, thou didst bear in two perfect natures and in one Hypostasis, O thou who art full of divine joy![^89]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^90]*

*Troparion:*
With thy sacred words thou didst anoint pure women unto ascetic struggles,[^91] and having cast down the enemy by courageous combats, O martyr, they wove incorruptible crowns!

*Troparion:*
Shielding thy soul with the strength of the Spirit, by enduring wounds thou didst cast down the spirits of wickedness; and from God thou didst receive the crown of victory, O worthy of wonder!

*Troparion:*
With drops of thy blood, O martyr of Christ, thou didst quench the flame of idolatry; and with the dew of thy sufferings thou didst refresh the souls of the pious, delivering them from the flame of deceit!

*Theotokion:*
He Who is wholly transcendent over all noetic and visible creation as God, truly took up abode in thy womb and became flesh, building up mankind, O Ever-Virgin!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*An Angel made the furnace bedewing for the venerable youths, but the command of God burning the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^92]*

*Troparion:*
With the fire of thy sufferings, O divinely blessed one, thou didst extinguish the kindle of idolatry; and with streams of blood thou didst submerge bodiless enemies, O Theodotus, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Like fragrant incense, O wise one, by divine grace thou wast offered unto Him Who set the contest, casting down foul-smelling deceit; therefore thou singest joyfully: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Observing thy words, the holy Fronto gave thee unto God-bearing Fronton,[^93] to erect an honorable temple for the purification of all the pious, and for the preservation of souls, O martyr Theodotus!

*Theotokion:*
The Mother and Virgin bore the Word possessing one Hypostasis, having two wills and His natural properties manifest; for He is God and man, Who for our sake was incarnate![^94]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous man Thou didst consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, solely by willing: Thee do we highly exalt unto all ages![^95]*

*Troparion:*
An unshaken pillar, an immovable rampart, an unconquerable wall, the foundation of faith, a gift divine, a champion of piety, a destroyer of deceit wast thou shown to be, O passion-bearer!

*Troparion:*
By the governance of divine grace, O most wise one, thou didst cross the great abyss of torments; and rejoicing, O glorious one, thou didst reach the calm harbor of the Kingdom on high!

*Troparion:*
Thy goings and thy paths were directed unto Christ the God of all, into Whose hands thou didst commit thy spirit, O thrice-blessed martyr and passion-bearer Theodotus!

*Theotokion:*
The tongue of rhetors cannot express the unfathomable depth of thy mystery; for beyond word thou didst bear the Word of God, O all-pure Virgin, unto the salvation of all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed!*

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest attain that glory, O passion-bearer, rejoicing thou didst despise visible things, and by divine beckoning joining the ranks on high, thou wast not dismayed by the sights of torturers and bitter death; therefore having contended, thou wast crowned with the martyrs!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be a pure temple of the Trinity, O Theodotus, richly endowed with the building of divine deeds and adorned with the bright radiance of sufferings; therefore, O wise one, by the divine power of the Comforter thou didst visibly overthrow the carved temples of demons!

*Troparion:*
A mountain dripping with divine sweetness,[^96] thou wast revealed as a fountain of wonders, an abyss of divine gifts, O glorious one, a bright stream of the Holy Spirit, watering souls and drowning passions, and interceding for all who piously sing joy unto thee, O martyr!

*Theotokion:*
At the voice of the Angel thou didst bear the unoriginate Word, Who received flesh possessed of mind and soul, O all-pure Virgin, from thy pure blood, and casting down bodiless enemies, and saving all of us who confess thee to be the true Birthgiver of God.

[Decorative Headpiece]

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 8TH DAY:*
*The Holy Great Martyr Theodore Stratelates:*[^97]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^98]
Wholly didst thou offer thyself unto Him Who gave thee perfect life, O all-blessed one, as a whole-burnt offering living and endowed with soul, and a sacrifice acceptable and most pure; therefore thou wast an acceptable intercessor, delivering from perils all who sing of thee in faith, O martyr, and call upon thee, O Theodore!

Having diligently cultivated the sown seed of the Word in thy pure soul, thou didst multiply it by the pains of suffering; and wisely laid up in heavenly granaries,[^99] thou didst find incorruptible delight: wherewith now being filled, O blessed one, by thy mediation unto Christ save them that faithfully sing of thee!

O martyr and passion-bearer of Christ, in diverse necessities save by thy supplications; driving away every affliction, and banishing soul-destroying grief, asking for us grace and mercy: that having been saved by thy prayers, rejoicing we all may sing of thy honorable struggles, O Theodore!

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ въ мꙋ́ченицѣхъ):*[^100]
A valiant athlete, * an unconquerable soldier * by the Holy Spirit thou didst show thyself to be, * having cast down the combatant * by the wisdom of thy words * and the patient manifestations of thy deeds, O wise one; * therefore thou hast received the crowns of victory, * and hast joined the choirs on high, * O great martyr Theodore!

A divine pillar wast thou, * O richly endowed in piety, * having loathed the shrines of the wicked, * and as a spotless sheep, * and an acceptable sacrifice, * thou wast purely brought unto Him Who was sacrificed for thy sake, * Who glorified thy holy memory * and gave thee to the spheres of the world as a treasure of wonders, O Theodore!

Lifted upon the cross, * scraped upon the flesh, * and with sharp arrows wounded, * and stretched upon the wood, * and severed with swords, * and encompassed with all manner of crafty torments, * thou didst show thyself unbending and unconquered, * by the power of Him Who was nailed upon the Cross, * O glory of martyrs, Theodore!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Today there has shone forth brighter than the morning star[^101] the honorable memory of the passion-bearer of Christ, invisibly enlightening the hearts of the faithful and driving away the clouds from souls by the operation of the grace of the Spirit; unto whom let us who love the martyrs cry aloud: Rejoice, thou who wast revealed unto the faithful as a God-given grace, extending a multitude of wonders unto them that flee unto thee, O blessed Theodore! Cease not to pray unto Christ that they who celebrate thy memory in faith may obtain eternal good things!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ):*[^102]
The Ewe-lamb of old, beholding her Lamb hastening unto the slaughter, earnestly followed Him, crying aloud these things unto Him: Whither goest Thou, my sweetest Child Christ? Why this swift course, O Long-Suffering One, dost Thou run without delay? O most desired and sinless Jesus, O Lord of great mercy, give a word unto Thy handmaid, my Son most beloved; overlook not in silence, O Merciful One, her that marvelously bore Thee; O God all-merciful, grant great mercy unto the world!

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 8, Idiomelon:*
As the namesake of the gifts of God and heir of His blessedness,[^103] all we faithful praise and worthily call blessed the noble Theodore, great martyr and champion of the universe: for he prays unto Christ God for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^104]
I cannot bear, O Child, to see Thee * asleep upon the Wood, * Who bestowest wakefulness upon all, * that unto them who of old fell into ruinous sleep * by the fruit of transgression, * Thou mayest grant divine and saving wakefulness, * the Virgin said weeping, * whom we magnify!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
In true enlistment, O passion-bearer, thou wast an excellent general of the Heavenly King, O Theodore; for with the weapons of faith thou didst wisely arm thyself, and didst conquer the hosts of demons, and wast shown to be a victorious athlete; therefore we ever call thee blessed!

*At Matins, both Canons of Christ from the Octoechos, and of the Saint on 6:*

*Canon of the Saint, whose Acrostic is:*
*I sing of thee, namesake of divine gifts, with divine praises. The composition of Theophanes.*[^105]
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness![^106]*

*Troparion:*
Visibly adorned with the splendors of martyrs, thou standest before Christ the Benefactor, embellished by Him, as one namesake of divine gifts, O martyr Theodore!

*Troparion:*
Arming thyself with divine weapons of the faith, O Theodore, thou didst firmly cut down the enemy's soul-destroying legions; and having become a victor, thou wast crowned with the martyrs!

*Troparion:*
Displaying the bright valor of thy soul unto the wicked king,[^107] O God-wise one, by the surpassing wisdom of thy words and the grace of thy deeds thou didst put him to shame, O Theodore!

*Theotokion:*
Without seed by the Father's will, from the Divine Spirit thou didst conceive the Son of God, and in the flesh didst bear Him Who was of the Father without mother, and for our sake was of thee without father!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment![^108]*

*Troparion:*
The wicked army, like a mighty general, thou didst conquer, and all the wiles of the crooked serpent thou didst trample underfoot!

*Troparion:*
As one zealous in soul, with pious mind, O glorious one, thou didst destroy the adulterous shrines of the wicked![^109]

*Troparion:*
By the strength of thy thoughts thou didst endure the assaults of deceitful ones, emulating the life-creating death of the Author of the contest!

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone for them on earth wast the mediatress of good things beyond nature, having become the Mother of God; therefore unto thee: Rejoice! do we cry aloud!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^110]
Having taken up the divine whole armor, and cast down idolatrous deceit, thou didst move the Angels to praise thy struggles; for having inflamed thy mind with divine love, thou didst valiantly dare unto a fiery death; therefore, as thy name signifies, thou givest divine gifts unto them that ask thee, O passion-bearer Theodore; therefore we cry aloud unto thee: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that with love honor thy memory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Falling into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of mine innumerable sins, and fleeing unto thy warm protection and my shelter, O pure one, I run unto the haven of thy goodness; therefore, O all-pure one, intercede earnestly with Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants who ceaselessly pray unto thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, ever beseeching Him to grant remission of sins unto them who worthily hymn thy glory!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Creator upon the Wood, the Virgin and Mother of Jesus groaned weeping, and was grievously wounded, rent in soul and body, smiting herself and bitterly crying out unto Him and shouting: Woe is me, O my Son! How shall I endure Thy suffering, the nails and spear, having given Thee birth without pain! But hasten to arise, that I may see Thee, my Son and God, and cease from mourning and pain; and they that sing of Thy sufferings shall receive remission of sins!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^111]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst wound the serpent that desired to wound thee, and by patient resistances thou wast revealed as a martyr, singing earnestly unto Him Who strengthened thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Wasting the flesh with many wounds, thou didst establish an unbending mind, O thrice-blessed one, singing with fervor unto Him Who strengthened thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast adorned again with sufferings, having joined thy precious sufferings, O most wise one; and thou wast accounted worthy of His brightness and desirable beauty!

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing wedlock thou didst give birth, O Virgin, and after childbearing thou wast shown forth virginal again; therefore with unceasing voices in unwavering faith we cry aloud unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: Rejoice!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance them that hymn Thee in faith![^112]*

*Troparion:*
By the sprinkling of bodily blood thou didst halt the demonic blood offered for destruction, O unconquerable Theodore!

*Troparion:*
O pious disposition! O valiant mind! O most fervent faith of the honorable passion-bearer, whereby he attained unto God!

*Troparion:*
Having a mind illumined with divine light, thou didst darken the serpent, the champion of malice, O God-bearing Theodore!

*Theotokion:*
Thee do we set forth as an unconquerable weapon against enemies; thee have we acquired as the establishment and hope of our salvation, O Bride of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side![^113]*

*Troparion:*
By bold fervor thou didst conquer them that cast thee down, stretched upon a cross and pierced with nails, O blessed one, portraying the saving Passion of the Creator![^114]

*Troparion:*
Unto thee who didst suffer lawfully, Christ appeared when shut up in prison, raising thee up as the Arbiter of the contest unto the struggles of combat against the enemies, O most glorious one![^115]

*Troparion:*
A pure, holy, and spotless sacrifice thou didst offer thyself, O passion-bearer Theodore, unto Him Who for thy sake willingly offered Himself as a sacrifice!

*Theotokion:*
Newest wonder of all wonders! For having conceived without knowing man Him Who holds all things, the Virgin contained Him in her womb without constraint!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^116]
Clothed in faith with courage of soul, and taking the Word of God as a spear into thy hand, thou didst conquer the enemy, O Theodore, greatest of martyrs: together with them ceaselessly pray unto Christ God for all of us!

*Ikos:*
The most radiant beauty of athletes, come, all ye faithful, let us crown Theodore with songs as with wreaths; for a great gift of God has appeared unto the world with the radiatings of wonders: having conquered the enemy Belial by honorable sufferings, instead of drops of blood he rains down torrents of healings; with all these he rejoices in Christ and grants unfailing peace; therefore we cry aloud unto him: Cease not to pray for all of us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned rather with love of piety than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^117]*

From the furnace of thy struggles, O glorious martyr, thou didst burn up the matter of wickedness, and wast a luminary of piety, singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
As a general, as one prudent and sober in deed, thou didst overcome the foolish and wicked king,[^118] and showed him to be powerless, having strengthened thyself with spiritual might!

*Troparion:*
The law of praise is overcome by the praises of thy majesty; for thou didst shine forth with most radiant flashings of martyrdom, crying unto thy Master, O Theodore: Blessed is the dominion of Thy Lordship!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, sanctified divine habitation of the Most High; for through thee joy was given unto them that cry, O Birthgiver of God: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Stretching forth his hands, Daniel shut the lions' gaping mouths in the den; and girded with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^119]*

*Troparion:*
Having hoped in Thee Who for my sake didst endure the Cross and death, I have been lifted upon a cross, O Master, wounded with arrows and touching grievous wounds, O Lord! thou didst cry aloud suffering, O martyr of valiant mind, Theodore!

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, O Theodore, thou didst offer thyself a pure sacrifice unto thy Creator; and departing gloriously unto the Heavenly Kingdom, with the martyrs thou criest sacredly ever: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Like a lily, like a noetic rose,[^120] with the fragrance of thy sufferings thou fillest us with sweet odor, by grace ever driving away the stench of our passions, and prompting us to cry with a fragrant soul: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone in all generations, O all-pure Virgin, wast shown forth as the Mother of God; thou wast the habitation of the Godhead, O all-immaculate one, unconsumed by the fire of the unapproachable Light; therefore all of us bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A cornerstone was cut without hands from the unhewn mountain, from thee, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God![^121]*

*Troparion:*
Now taking up thine abode with the blessed assemblies and the choirs of martyrs, adorned with sufferings, taking up thine abode and rejoicing together, bearing a crown thou standest before Him Whom thou didst desire, O Theodore!

*Troparion:*
Reckoning earthly things as nothing before the elevation of celestial height, thou wast accounted worthy of the end desired by thee, rejoicing as thou receivedst the very fulfillment of thy desires, O Theodore!

*Troparion:*
Having boldness toward God as a valiant and wise athlete, ask remission of sins for them that praise thee with love, delivering all of us from passions and afflictions!

*Troparion:*
Desiring Thee alone, the immortal Word, Who didst suffer in the flesh and die, the God-wise martyr, having obtained Thine immortality, settled in the heavens with the all-powerful Creator of all!

*Theotokion:*
Come, all ye people, with faith and love let us ceaselessly praise blessed Mary, the Virgin Mother of God, through whom we have found eternal rejoicing!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Having adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^122]
Comely in beauty, a most mighty general, and a glorious soldier among athletes, thou didst offer thyself unto Christ, and wast revealed as a divine gift unto the world, O Theodore!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Beneath thy sovereign shelter from the assault of enemies keep all of us, thy servants, unharmed, O pure one; for thee alone have we acquired as a refuge in necessities!

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 8:*
Having engaged in the combat of suffering, thou didst act manfully against deceit, O Theodore; with thy fire-breathing tongue thou didst put to shame the inhumanity of Licinius; therefore, rejoicing with the Angels, O blessed one, entreat Christ God that He may be merciful unto us in the Day of Judgment!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion.*
*Stavrotheotokion, written at Vespers at the Aposticha.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, or of the Octoechos; and of the Saint, Ode 3.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.[^123]*
*Verse: Hear, O God, my voice, when I make my prayer unto Thee.[^124]*
*Epistle to Timothy, pericope 292 [2 Tim. 2:1–10]:*[^125]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
*The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be increased like a cedar in Lebanon.[^126]*
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 36 [Matt. 10:16–22]:*[^127]
*Communion Verse:*
*In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous man be.[^128]*

[Decorative Headpiece]

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 9TH DAY:*
*Our Father among the Saints Cyril, Archbishop of Alexandria:*[^129]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "As one valiant among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Having enlightened thy mind with spiritual radiance, * thou wast a sun of radiant beams, * stretching forth the rays of doctrine * unto all ends, * and enlightening the fullness of the faithful, * O all-blessed one, * and driving away the darkness of heresies, O God-bearer, * by the power of Him Who shone forth from the Virgin!

By the eloquence of thy words, * O holy Cyril, * the whole Church is adorned * and piously embellished with fair beauties, * and sacredly honors * thy holy and renowned memory, * O praise of the Orthodox, * head of the fathers, * champion of the good at the Council![^130]

By thy fiery doctrines, * every brushwood matter of heresies * is consumed, O most wise one; * by thy depths of understanding, * the army of the disobedient and impious * is submerged; * while the Church of the faithful is ever glorified, * honoring thee with a loud voice!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O good and faithful servant, * laborer of the vineyard of Christ: * thou didst bear the burden of the day, * thou didst multiply the talent given unto thee, * and didst not envy them that came after thee;[^131] * therefore the heavenly gates have opened unto thee: * enter thou into the joy of thy Lord,[^132] * and pray for us, O Cyril our father!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion.*
*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Все ѿло́жше):*
When the spotless and blameless Sovereign Lady of old * beheld her own Lamb upon the Wood of the Cross, * with a mother's voice she cried aloud, * and marveling shouted: * O sweetest Child, * what is this new and most marvelous sight? * How has the graceless council * delivered Thee unto the judgment seat of Pilate, * and condemned unto death the Life of all? * Yet I sing of Thine ineffable condescension, * O Word!

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 6:*
O man of God, and faithful servant, * minister of the Lord, man of desires,[^133] * chosen vessel, pillar and foundation of the Church,[^134] * heir of the Kingdom, heir of the Kingdom, do not cease crying aloud for us unto the Lord!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, * she that bore Thee cried aloud: * What strange mystery is this which I behold, O my Son? * How upon the Wood diest Thou in the flesh, being suspended, * O Giver of life?

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Guide of Orthodoxy, * teacher of piety and purity, * luminary of the universe, * God-inspired adornment of hierarchs, * O most wise Cyril: * by thy teachings thou didst enlighten all; * O spiritual lyre, * entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saint on 6, whose Acrostic is:*
*Cyril is the lyre of divine visions. The composition of Theophanes.*[^135]
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the deep of the Red Sea with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness![^136]*

*Troparion:*
Having received light-bestowing grace from God, O Cyril, thou wast shown forth as a radiant luminary, flashing forth rational rays upon us by thy memory, that we may worthily hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast filled with spiritual manifestation of light, O most wise Cyril, as a keeper of Christ's commandments, cleansed from passions; truly wast thou a dwelling place of the super-divine Trinity!

*Troparion:*
As one filled with zeal and boldness, O glorious Cyril, contending for the right faith, thou didst truly expose all the God-opposing, harmful tongue-blasphemies of impious heresies!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst heal the crushing of mankind, O all-immaculate one, having given birth unto the Creator of all, Who raised up us who had been cast down by the ancestral fall, O Bride of God!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment![^137]*

*Troparion:*
Strengthened in Christ, O Cyril, with a steadfast mind thou didst rend asunder the wiles of the crafty serpent like a spider's web!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shake off the darkness of passions from thy soul, O Cyril, casting down the exaltation of every thought that exalts itself against Christ![^138]

*Troparion:*
As a successful combatant, O father, thou didst firmly turn away from all carnal voluptuousness, as vain and baneful!

*Theotokion:*
Thee who alone among women art blessed, as the divine dwelling place, in all comeliness let all of us glorify with joy!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
Having become a pillar and sacrifice of Christ's Church, O father, thou didst most wisely preserve her unshaken from the temptations of the crafty one; for thou didst overthrow all the malice of Nestorius,[^139] and didst proclaim the Birthgiver of God to be the Mother of God; therefore, having assembled, we honor thee, O Cyril, blessed of God! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
As the uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou didst bud forth the most beautiful cluster of grapes, pouring out for us the wine of salvation, making glad the souls and bodies of all; therefore ever blessing thee as the cause of good things, with the Angel we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The unwedded pure one and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee hanging dead upon the Cross, weeping with a mother's voice, said: What has the Jewish lawless and ungrateful assembly rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed so many and great of Thy gifts, O my Son? I sing of Thy divine condescension!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^140]*

*Troparion:*
Beholding that thou hadst conquered soul-corrupting passions and restrained carnal thinking, O God-bearing Cyril, Christ appointed thee, O father, as president of His divine Church!

*Troparion:*
Having inherited as a beloved son the paternal virtue of Mark,[^141] thou wast a successor unto his throne, following the footsteps of the divine Evangelist!

*Troparion:*
Having been tended like a sheep of Christ, like a shepherd thou didst tend the flock, O father, with thy soul-nourishing words, as with grasses and flowers, richly feeding them with grace!

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the Word of God, incarnate Who was bodiless before, O all-hymned and favored one, thou didst renew the world; therefore, O Bride of God, with Orthodox faith we proclaim thee!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance them that hymn Thee in faith![^142]*

*Troparion:*
Unto an exceedingly mighty torrent flooding rivers, thy word, O Cyril, was likened, turning aside the subtilties of the ill-renowned.

*Troparion:*
Alike the abominable division of Arius,[^143] O Cyril, and the foul confusion of Sabellius,[^144] thou didst cast out from the deep!

*Troparion:*
Wealth-producing words like gold hast thou left unto the Church of Christ, O Cyril, and treasures as thy precious stones!

*Theotokion:*
Let heaven drop down gladness and grace upon the earth;[^145] for gladness unto the height is now rendered unto her, the only Mother of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side![^146]*

*Troparion:*
With the tongs the Seraph offered the coal unto the Prophet;[^147] but unto the Church of Christ by thine own hands, O initiate of sacred mysteries, thou dost dispense the coal of purification from the divine Fire!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not destroy neighboring foreigners like Samson;[^148] but all the alien doctrines of the heterodox thou didst overthrow, and gavest dominion unto the Orthodox, O Cyril!

*Troparion:*
Having manifestly led the God-chosen Council,[^149] the impiety of Nestorius, his Christ-fighting boldness, O father, thou didst cast down by the zeal of the Spirit, in honor of the true Mother of God!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bud forth the Fruit of incorruptible understanding, O Mother of God, ever-living for the world, shown forth as the mediatress of incorruption for them that hymn thee in faith and love!

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Fulfilling the dispensation for our sake" (Е҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ):*[^150]
An abyss of teachings of theology hast thou manifestly poured forth unto us from the springs of the Savior,[^151] drowning heresy, O blessed Cyril, and preserving the flock unharmed from tempests; for thou art a guide unto all countries, O venerable one, manifesting divine things!

*Ikos:*
Fairly at morning the sun appears exceedingly sacred and light-bearing: darkness is driven away, and the moon withdraws, for night cannot endure; it brightens the day, illumines the air, shows us a fair sky, embellishing the earth, causing flowers to spring up; the sea gleams, and together it adorns the whole world: but brighter than all of this is Cyril, whose wise doctrines save the world under heaven; for he enlightens the souls of the faithful, and ever piously conquers heresies; for he is a guide unto all countries, manifesting divine things!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned rather with love of piety than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^152]*

*Troparion:*
Having entered into the gloom of old upon Mount Sinai, Moses received the Law that kills by the letter;[^153] but thou, O blessed Cyril, didst unfold the hidden beauty of the Spirit!

*Troparion:*
The veil of concealment given of old by enigmas of legislation as unto infants, O father,[^154] thou didst uncover like shards of understanding, explaining the types most clearly!

*Troparion:*
In unconfused divine union and indivisible distinction, O father, thou didst theologize three Hypostases, O Cyril, immutable in one Essence and Godhead!

*Theotokion:*
By thy childbearing, O Mother of God, the Angels beheld strange things: the corruptible nature of the earthborn race running back unto incorruptible life and heavenly glory!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Stretching forth his hands, Daniel shut the lions' gaping mouths in the den; and girded with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^155]*

*Troparion:*
Truly like a sea-wave did the fountain of wisdom flow forth unto thee; for ever-flowing rivers of pious doctrines ran from thy belly, O father,[^156] even as Christ foretold; for thy God-chosen words surpass the sand!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a wise shepherd and teacher of the Church, O Cyril, manifestly unfolding the covenants, O venerable one; for holding thy words as an anchor and paternal inheritance, she cries: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Unto the ineffable union of the Word Who was incarnate for our sake, the all-craftsman,[^157] indivisible and unconfused, thou didst instruct us, O Cyril, declining equally from either will,[^158] crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Incomprehensible in nature unto sight, the Son and Word of God took up abode in thee, O pure one; and having clothed Himself in our essence, as Merciful He appeared approachable in the flesh, O all-immaculate one, and took up abode with us who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve indeed, by the sickness of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, by the blossoming of thy fruit-bearing, didst bud forth blessing unto the world; therefore all of us magnify thee![^159]*

*Troparion:*
The exaltation that lifts itself against the knowledge of Christ and His Mother,[^160] O Cyril, thou didst cast down in all its power: the duality of godless Nestorius as a dream-weaver, and likewise the confusion of the headless natures![^161]

*Troparion:*
By the power of understanding and the enlightenment of grace, thou didst theologize the consubstantial Trinity, and the Son as God incarnate, O blessed one; and having appeared as champion of the Birthgiver of God, thou art now glorified on high!

*Troparion:*
From on high do thou mercifully watch over us thy singers, O ever-memorable father, granting victories unto the cross-bearing king,[^162] and by thy prayers exalting the horn of the Orthodox,[^163] brightening them that magnify thy memory!

*Theotokion:*
Straitened and hindered by many falls and by the severity of perils, now do I offer unto thee a sacrifice of praise, and earnestly cry unto thee: O holy Mother of God, help me, for in thy praise do I conclude this hymn!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "While the disciples looked on" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ):*[^164]
Having surpassed sensation and understanding, and learned the depths of the Spirit, as an initiate of divine mysteries let us sing of the initiate of the super-divine Trinity, as a luminary of the Lord and champion of piety!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Whoso kisses not thine immaculate icon with love, O blameless Mother of God, is cast into the fire of Gehenna like foolish Nestorius; but we, O all-holy one, embrace thee with love!

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos:*
*Glory... Tone 6:*
O heir of God, partaker of Christ, minister of the Lord, Cyril our father: according to thy name, such also was thy life; for understanding shone forth together with thy gray hairs, the brightness of thy countenance bore witness to the harmlessness of thy soul, and meekness and silent speech attested thy virtues; thy life was glorious and thy dormition with the saints: pray for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Standing by the Wood of old at the time of crucifixion, the Virgin with the virgin disciple weeping cried aloud: Woe is me! How sufferest Thou, O Christ, Son of all impassibility?

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, or of the Octoechos; and of the Saint, Ode 3.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 1:*
*My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding.[^165]*
*Verse: Hear this, all ye nations; give ear, all ye that inhabit the world.[^166]*
*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericope 318 [Heb. 7:26–8:2]:*[^167]
*Alleluia, Tone 2:*
*The mouth of the righteous shall meditate wisdom, and his tongue shall speak of judgment.[^168]*
*Verse: The law of His God is in His heart, and His steps shall not be tripped.[^169]*
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 11 [Matt. 5:14–19]:*[^170]
*Communion Verse:*
*In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous man be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^171]*

[Decorative Headpiece]

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 10TH DAY:*
*The Holy Hieromartyr Timothy, Bishop of Prusa:*[^172]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "As one valiant among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

By thy active teachings, * lifted up from earthly things * like an animated cloud, O blessed one, * by the thunders of thy wonders * and the terrible flashes of thy words, * thou didst cast down the fierce, crooked serpent.[^173] * Grace didst thou receive * to consume the fleshly serpents,[^174] * burning the innards of the enemies' torturers by a divine veil!

Holy Timothy, praise of the righteous, * champion of the universe * and luminary of the world, * adornment of the Church, * sacred sacrifice of the faith, * and honorable, * light-bearing embellishment of martyrs: * pray that they who in faith celebrate thy most honorable memory * may be delivered from corruption and perils!

A beautiful garment dyed in blood * thou didst weave by thy sufferings, O Timothy, * a heavenly garment from on high * of incorruptible purification; * and ineffably thou didst receive immutable life: * bearing it immaterially on high, * pray for all who piously praise thee, * O passion-bearer!

*Other Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone 4, Podoben the same:*

From condemnation deliver my humble soul, * O most holy Bride of God, * and from grievous transgressions, * and from death ransom me by thy prayers; * and grant me to obtain justification in the Day of Trial, * which the assemblies of the saints have obtained: * having cleansed me by repentance * and outpourings of tears, * show me forth before the end!

With showers of the Most Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, * bedew my mind, * thou who didst bear the Drop, Christ, * Who innumerably with compassions, washed away the measureless iniquities of men; * dry up the fountain of my passions, * and vouchsafe me unto the torrent * of living food ever, * by thy prayers![^175]

*Sticheron of the Theotokos:*
An excellent * and saving medicine of repentance, * O thou who didst bear God my Savior, * and streams of tears, and a human thought of the terrible and fearful, * unbribable judgment: * do thou grant me to escape by thy prayers, O Virgin, * from trembling torment, * and to obtain divine grace!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Ten thousand times, O all-pure one, have I promised * to make repentance for my sins; * but the habitual love of mine evil deeds leaves me not. * Therefore unto thee do I cry, * and falling down I pray: * do thou, O Sovereign Lady, deliver me from such tyranny, * guiding me unto better things, * and unto things that are near unto salvation!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When she beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Wood, * the ewe-lamb that bore Thee wailed, * and with a mother's voice spoke unto Thee: * O beloved Son, * how art Thou suspended upon the Wood of the Cross, O Long-Suffering One? * How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, * and Thou didst pour out Thy blood, O Master?

*Canon, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The mighty chieftains, He Who was born of the Virgin submerged in the deep of impassibility, the three parts of the soul, I pray: that unto Thee as on a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory![^176]*

*Troparion:*
Having an unconquerable might, an immutable dominion, and unchangeable as Good: by Thine ineffable power establish the weakness of my tongue in Thy hymns, and by the prayers of Timothy show mercy!

*Troparion:*
By the God-working strength of a holy root, thou didst sprout forth a sacred garden, O holy Timothy, and wast shown forth as a fair adornment of virtues; for thou didst blossom forth with abundant fruits of suffering, and didst bring them unto the Benefactor of all!

*Troparion:*
By purity of mind and brightness of soul, adorned with thy blood-dyeings, thou wast shown forth, O God-bearing Timothy; on high now thou reignest with the Master of creation, and standest before thy flock in faith!

*Theotokion:*
O animate, divine, and most radiant house, good Mother of God, joy of the world: cast me not far away from thee, but show mercy, and deliver me from all temptations, that I may dance within thy protection!

*Theotokion:*
...thy protection.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment![^177]*

*Troparion:*
Among the martyrs of Christ, Thou wast shown forth bright in torments, O glorious one; among wise hierarchs, Thou wast shown forth as the adornment of priests!

*Troparion:*
The temple of Christ, the treasure of wisdom, come today, ye faithful, let us hymn: the primate of the flock of Prusa!

*Troparion:*
With the sweats of virtue having mingled thy blood, thou didst offer a pure drink unto the Lord, divinely and fairly, O most wise Timothy!

*Theotokion:*
Mother of God, higher than the Cherubim, O pure one, and Sovereign Lady of all the earth: save me by thy divine supplications!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное сло́во):*[^178]
Watered with showers of blood, O hieromartyr of the Lord, thou didst bring forth fruit in the good soil of thy heart,[^179] imperishable food which thou didst receive from God; therefore we pray thee: deliver from perils them that celebrate thy memory!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Having found thee as a haven, and wall, and refuge, and hope, and shelter, and fervent protection, we the faithful run unto thee, and cry aloud earnestly, calling faithfully: Have mercy, O Mother of God, upon them that trust in thee, and deliver us from transgressions!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
By the Cross of thy Son, O thou who art full of divine grace, all idolatrous deceit was abolished, and demonic power was trampled down; therefore, we the faithful, as is our duty, ever hymn and bless thee, and truly confessing thee to be the Mother of God, we magnify thee!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^180]*

*Troparion:*
Having acquired an unobscured life, and wholly flashing forth with the thunders of the Spirit, thou didst burn up the all-ruinous serpent, O glorious one, strengthened by divine grace!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with contemplation and active deed in the levitical order,[^181] with a two-edged weapon thou didst cut down the inner serpents, O Timothy, with the veil of sacred loaves![^182]

*Troparion:*
Having known Christ the Great High Priest, not with alien blood,[^183] but with thine own, O martyr, being brought unto the Father as His companion, offering thy blood, thou didst sacrifice thyself thou didst sacrifice thyself!

*Theotokion:*
The many-eyed [powers] are overcome, O pure Mother of God, gazing insatiably upon thy purity; for as Sovereign Lady thou dost ever anticipate them, and ceaselessly foreseest thy servants!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance them that hymn Thee in faith![^184]*

*Troparion:*
Like a firm axe, like a mighty weapon, having joined battle against the legions of the lawless, thou wast crowned with victory!

*Troparion:*
A light-bearing day-luminary does it appear: thou didst enlighten the Church, which in the darkness of malice finishes her course, O glorious countenance!

*Troparion:*
In place of the defilement and mingling of idols, thou didst deliver thyself unto the shedders of blood like a lamb, O blessed one, and wast offered as a sacrifice unto God!

*Theotokion:*
O pure dove, having stretched out the wings of thy prayers and protection over thy servants who are in the world, O Virgin, despise not me also!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side![^185]*

*Troparion:*
The universe rejoices together today, being glad on thy memory, O blessed one; for from on high thou dost enlighten with rays of wonders the souls of them that hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth now, not as a minister of the holy Table on earth, but entering into the heavens, thou art fed at the ineffable Table; wherefrom grant unto us also thy food and delight!

*Troparion:*
Lawfully accomplishing the sacred contest with boldness, O God-wise one, thou didst expose the folly of the emperor,[^186] and didst overthrow the shrines of idols, O Timothy!

*Theotokion:*
With angelic voices thy Son is hymned, O Bride of God, the Creator of all, Who is God before the ages; and reigning together with Him, save me, O Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned rather with love of piety than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^187]*

*Troparion:*
As is meet, the God-working grace crowned Thee before Thy struggles as a rich treasure of virtues, and enlightening Thee with gifts of wonders, perfected Thee radiant in the world!

*Troparion:*
Beholding the weapons of threat and the torturer of fire-bearing flame, having armed thy mind and soul with faith, and with a fearless understanding, thou didst proclaim Christ, thy Maker and Creator and Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Having in thy womb, in a manner befitting God, Him Who holds all things in His palm, O Mother of God,[^188] thou dost preserve them that cry: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate one![^189]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Stretching forth his hands, Daniel shut the lions' gaping mouths in the den; and girded with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^190]*

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the power of divine faith, O wise Timothy, thou wast shown forth unto us as a new Daniel, casting down the all-destroying serpent, and standing upon the height, thou dost cry, singing ever: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Within, as a sacred servant, thou didst enter into the joy of thy Lord;[^191] for by thy pains thou didst multiply manifold the talent of faith, O glorious one, by sufferings and wonders, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Driving away diseases of men, with thine anointings thou dost perfume souls, O most wise one; by thy mighty gift thou takest away the sores of diseases, and from evil spirits dost deliver ever them that cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
As [our] nature received a glorious crown by thy divine childbearing, grant me to be a conqueror of enemies in battle, to be crowned with a bright crown of thy gifts, and to cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Eve indeed, by the sickness of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, by the blossoming of thy fruit-bearing, didst bud forth blessing unto the world; therefore all of us magnify thee![^192]*

*Troparion:*
We have seen truly marvelous things of wonders in thee, O God-bearing Timothy; for the shrine of thy relics pours forth fountains of healings for us, and stays the pains of diseases; therefore honoring thee we glorify thee!

*Troparion:*
Truly dancing in chorus, O Timothy, with the angelic hosts, rejoicing with the assemblies of martyrs and the companies with sacred ranks: do thou ceaselessly pray unto the Lord to save them that praise thee in faith!

*Troparion:*
Hear the voices of hymns and supplications, O all-honorable martyr of Christ; give ear unto desire and love, and look upon [the adversary] unconquerable in wrath; unconquerable in wrath; and from on high grant thy protection unto all that praise thee in faith!

*Theotokion:*
Exalt thy grace, O pure one, magnify thy supplication, and bring the angelic ranks unto the Sovereign Lady, the choirs of Apostles and Prophets, and all the venerable ones with the martyrs, that we who honor thee may be saved!

[Decorative Headpiece]

*IN THE SAME MONTH, ON THE 11TH DAY:*
*The Holy Apostles Bartholomew and Barnabas:*[^193]

*At Vespers we sing: "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 8, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "As one valiant among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

By the net of thy words, * O God-speaking Apostle, * out of the depth thou didst brightly draw * the ends of the world * from grievous ungodliness, * and didst bring them unto Christ God through faith, * Who granted unto thee such grace * as a divine disciple and teller of secret mysteries, * O Bartholomew the Apostle! *Twice.*

As a radiant beam, O most glorious one, * Christ our God, the Sun of Glory, * sent Thee forth into the fulness of the world, * mightily driving away the gloom of ungodliness, * and enlightening all * that sit in the darkness of ignorance, * whom He made heirs of light, * O God-wise Bartholomew! *Twice.*

Submitting to the beckoning of Him * unto Whose beckoning all things submit, * and assimilating thyself unto Him * as a Teacher of Righteousness, * the cup of death for the sake of the passion of the Cross * thou didst joyfully drink, * and standest before the Utmost of Desires, * dancing with angels and apostles, * O glorious Bartholomew!

*Other Stichera, of the Apostle Barnabas, Tone the same [Tone 4]:*
*Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

Thou didst receive, O Apostle, * unconquerable authority and power over demons * from the Name of Christ, * to drive away the rulers of darkness; * thou didst traverse the earth, * enlightening like the sun, * and having come unto renowned Rome, * first didst thou proclaim, O glorious one, * the saving advent of Christ!

Assimilating thyself unto the first Goodness * and the natural and super-divine life, * thou wast a good man, * excellent according to essence, * and named by likeness a son of consolation;[^194] * by the goodness of thy manners * and the purity of mind, * thou wast shown forth as a genuine disciple of Christ!

A well-attuned instrument * by spiritual working wast thou, O Barnabas; * thou wast entrusted with the calling of the Gentiles,[^195] * guiding them unto the knowledge of Christ * by words and deeds; * and thou didst enlighten all * to confess the true * hypostatic Godhead,[^196] * the Savior of our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6, by Byzantios:*[^197]
O all-praised Barnabas, * radiant vessel of the Holy Spirit, * as a son of consolation, * who before Paul wast the head of the Seventy disciples,[^198] * and with Paul again didst proclaim the Gospel unto the ends: * therefore having also fulfilled the word of grace by deeds, * thou didst offer thyself as a pure whole-burnt offering * unto thy Teacher, Christ; * pray ever, O glorious Apostle, * we pray thee, that our souls may be saved in peace!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
*Who will not call thee blessed.*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*
*Readings, three.*
*(Seek this month on the 24th day.)*[^199]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast; but if a fast, Stichera of the Apostles, Tone 1:*
*Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́лнїи мч҃нцы):*

Into all the earth went forth * your saving proclamation, * O God-beholding Apostles, * and it enlightened wandering souls, * and brought men unto Christ, * enlightened by grace. * Therefore pray, * that peace and great mercy * may be granted unto our souls!

*Verse: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^200]*

Radiant luminaries of Christ, * most pure vessels, * who by faith received the dawn of the Spirit, O most wise ones, * pillars of the Church, * most glorious heavens * declaring the glory of God: * entreat Him * that peace and great mercy * may be granted unto our souls!

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.[^201]*

Ye brought the nations unto Christ God, O blessed ones, * who were redeemed by His blood, * Who was well pleased to be born on earth, * and voluntarily endured death upon the Cross, O glorious ones: * entreat Him now to grant * peace and great mercy * unto our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Apostle of the Savior, * all-praised Barnabas, * receptacle of faith, * son of consolation, * calm of the storm-tossed, * and haven in memory having boldness on thy memory, pray that our souls may be preserved in peace!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
*O Virgin unwedded.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
Holy Apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He may grant remission of transgressions unto our souls!

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Apostles, twice.*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Thee who didst mediate...*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
The spiritual choir of the Apostles * was mystically sent into the world * from God on high, * and they were shown forth as physicians unto them that suffer from diseases; * single-mindedly invoking the Tri-hypostatic One,[^202] * they wisely wrote of the divine Incarnation of the Lord Emmanuel! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The bush which Moses beheld unconsumed,[^203] * the Mountain of God,[^204] * the Holy Cloud,[^205] * the Spotless Tabernacle,[^206] * the Table receiving God,[^207] * the Palace of the High King,[^208] * the all-radiant and impenetrable Gate:[^209] * O Virgin, we sing of thee!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ):*
With radiant beams, as stars ye enlighten the ends of the earth, * O heralds of the holy proclamation, * heavenly initiates of sacred mysteries, Apostles of the Lord! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Fervent protection of them that are in distress, * our help * and reconciliation unto God, * through whom we have been delivered from corruption: * we the faithful call the Mother of God blessed!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having drawn [men] up by the net of the word * out of the depth of ignorance in a manner befitting God, * thou didst bring the nations through faith; * and being as a fountain of spiritual waters, * thou didst water the whole universe with grace; * therefore, having been saved through thee, * with one voice we call thee blessed, as the close friend of Christ,[^210] * O Bartholomew, crying aloud unto thee: * Entreat Christ God * to grant remission of transgressions * unto them that celebrate thy holy memory with love! *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*
*Gradual Antiphons, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Their sound is gone out into all the earth:*
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God:*
*Let every breath:*
*Gospel according to John, pericope 67 [John 21:15–25]:*[^211]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 1:*
Into all the earth went forth the saving proclamation, O God-beholding Apostles, * and it enlightened wandering souls, * and unto Christ brought men enlightened by grace unto Christ: * therefore pray, * that peace and great mercy * may be granted unto our souls!

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Apostles, two canons, on 8.*
*Canon of the Holy Apostle Bartholomew, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The overpowering power once cast down the entire host of Pharaoh in the deep; and the incarnate Word abolished all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord, for He has been gloriously glorified![^212]*

*Troparion:*
The Knower of all things and God, beholding in God-beholding manner the mind of thy soul, chose thee, and joined thee unto the choir of the Apostles, O all-praised Bartholomew, having enlightened thy heart with grace!

*Troparion:*
Having ascended unto the height of virtues, thou wast enriched with the division appearing as fire by a tongue inspired with fire, as an Apostle of Christ, by the coming of the Comforter, O exceedingly wealthy one, burning up the brushwood of impiety and ignorance![^213]

*Troparion:*
Truly a God-bearing coal wast thou shown forth, kindled by the dawn of the Divine Spirit, burning up the hay of deceit, O all-praised Bartholomew, and enlightening with faith the hearts of them that sing of thee!

*Theotokion:*
O pure one, tell us how thou dost suckle, and how thou wast a Mother, shining with the seals of virginity? Seek not the abyss, which is unapproachable by nature; God alone and Lord of all searched it out, Whom also I bore in the flesh!

*Another Canon, of Saint Barnabas, Tone and Heirmos the same [Tone 2].*
*Troparion:*
As thou art a good man, and wast shown forth as a son of consolation, drive away the affliction of my soul, and the soul-destroying sorrow of sin, that being filled with gladness, I may hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
Possessed in mind with the radiance of Him Who sprouted from the tribe of Judah, O exceedingly wealthy one, proclaiming Christ, having come sacred from the Levitical kin, thou didst proclaim the change of the Law![^214]

*Troparion:*
Thy bright life, O most wise one, of the first Goodness and natural Essence showed thee forth as a second god,[^215] being thence called blessed with good things, O glorious Barnabas, offspring of divine consolation!

*Theotokion:*
The divine choir of maidens divinely hymns thee as the fair one among women, O Mother of God, Sovereign Lady, embellished with the beauties of the Godhead; for beyond word didst thou give birth unto the Word, the Well-Doer!
*Katavasia according to the Typikon.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The desert blossomed like a lily, O Lord, the barren Church of the Gentiles, by Thy coming, wherein my heart is established![^216]*

*Troparion:*
Like heavenly rain, O most wise one, thou didst water the whole universe dried up by the darkness of ungodliness, O God-seer, worthy of wonder!

*Troparion:*
A fountain of saving drink went forth, O all-honorable Bartholomew; and thou didst bring manifold fruit, worthy of wonder, unto the salvation of the nations!

*Troparion:*
With the salt of grace thou didst stop the corrupting putrefaction of idols, O all-wise one,[^217] sweetening the hearts of them that honor thee by the sweet word of faith!

*Theotokion:*
Thee who alone art Virgin and Mother do we honor, as having become for us the mediatress of salvation, O spotless one, and delivering the world by thy prayers!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
A willing cloud carrying rain for us from the fountain of grace wast thou seen, O glorious Barnabas, watering the torrent of delight![^218]

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee endowed with diverse splendors, the power of the all-powerful Spirit set thee apart, O all-praised one, as a minister of the mysteries of Christ![^219]

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the grace of Thy Cross, O Master, the all-glorious Apostles Barnabas and Paul, wise in God, brought the nations unto Thee through faith!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth in purity as a lily in the midst of thorns, O Sovereign Lady, shining with the rays of thy virginity, O all-pure Mother of God![^220]

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Today" (Въ дне́сь):*[^221]
Thou wast the all-true servant of the Lord, * and wast shown forth as the first among the Seventy Apostles; * thou didst also illumine thy proclamation together with Paul, * proclaiming Christ the Savior unto all: * therefore with hymnody do we celebrate thy divine memory, O Barnabas!

*Ikos:*
The Son of God, co-eternal with the Spirit, having in His good pleasure taken flesh on earth as man, and through His honorable passion granted impassibility, appointed thee, O blessed one, as an Apostle and initiate of the faith, wise in word and worthy of wonder, an honorable preacher, teacher of the nations, O blessed Barnabas, and true worshiper of the Trinity; therefore as a bright morning star has thy divine memory shone forth unto the faithful, which we worthily celebrate!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Having drawn [men] up by the net of the word * out of the depth of ignorance in a manner befitting God, * thou didst bring the nations through faith; * and being as a fountain of spiritual waters, * thou didst water the whole universe with grace; * therefore, having been saved through thee, O God-proclaimer, * with one voice we call thee blessed, as the close friend of Christ, * O Bartholomew, crying aloud unto thee: * Entreat Christ God * to grant remission of transgressions * unto them that celebrate thy holy memory with love!

*Glory, of Barnabas, Tone and Podoben the same [Tone 8]:*
By words wast thou shown forth, * declaring the heavenly glory of God,[^222] * by the brightness of wonders * and the rays of power, * driving away the night of ignorance by grace, * and flashing forth the rays of the knowledge of God unto the ends of the earth. * Therefore today we the faithful glorify thy light-bearing and holy memory, * and cry aloud unto thee: O all-praised Barnabas, * entreat Christ God * to grant remission of sins * unto them that celebrate thy holy memory with love!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
As the all-immaculate bride of the Creator, * as the unwedded Mother of the Redeemer, * as the receptacle of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: * since I am a foul dwelling place of iniquity * and a plaything of demons, having come to awareness, * make haste to deliver me from their evildoing, * and make me a bright dwelling place of virtues, O light-bearing and incorruptible one; * drive away the cloud of passions, * and vouchsafe me to partake of things on high * and of the unwaning Light, * by thy prayers!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast come from the Virgin, neither an ambassador nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and hast saved me, whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^223]*

*Troparion:*
Fenced about by the Cross of the Lord, thou didst abolish the demonic fury, O most excellent Bartholomew; their soul-destroying deceit thou didst drive away by thy preaching!

*Troparion:*
As the light of the world, the radiance of the divine glory,[^224] Christ showed thee forth, O God-receiving Bartholomew; for thou didst enlighten the whole creation with the knowledge of God, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst trouble the seas of the Gentiles, O Apostle, as an excellent horse, having become a chariot of the Word;[^225] and by faith thou didst extinguish the ends of bitter ungodliness!

*Theotokion:*
Healing Eve's ancient transgression, the Super-Divine One took up abode in thee, the spotless and all-pure one, and renewed me, fallen man, entirely!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same:*

*Troparion:*
Following the commandments of the Master, O all-praised Apostle Barnabas, thou didst distribute all thy possessions unto the poor,[^226] and wast a loud-voiced preacher of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst attain the uttermost brightness of virtues, and found Paul, the God-proclaiming Apostle; with him thou didst announce unto all the ineffable depth of the mysteries![^227]

*Troparion:*
Like the sun traversing the ends of the world, O all-honorable Barnabas, with radiant beams, following Paul, thou dost enlighten [men] by the word of grace!

*Troparion:*
Having become a partaker of the sufferings of the Savior, O all-praised Barnabas, thou art glorified with Him in the heavenly mansions, being filled with joy surpassing the mind!

*Theotokion:*
The curse of Eve the foremother was abolished through thee, O all-immaculate one; for thou didst give birth unto the incarnate co-eternal Word, Who crowned the world with blessings!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou hast become a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access unto Thy Father, the Originator of light, out of the night of ignorance![^228]*

*Troparion:*
Unto Christ, the Uncreated Light Who for our sake appeared among creations, unto the First-Fashioner thou didst converse, having come unto Him, O Apostle, and being enlightened by divine rays from Him!

*Troparion:*
Like divine dew granting healing unto men wast thou in the world, O most blessed Bartholomew, and prophetically didst thou overturn the land of impiety!

*Troparion:*
Having drawn near unto the First Light, thou wast a second light, enlightened by radiance and outpourings of light from thence, as an eyewitness of the Word, O all-wise Bartholomew!

*Theotokion:*
Remembering thy words, we now call thee blessed;[^229] for through thee, O spotless one, we have found blessedness, and have been enriched with honorable, true life!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Having passed beyond the shadow of the Law, and shining with grace, preaching the good tidings of the fulness of the Gentiles, thou didst enlighten them by the preaching of Christ, O God-wise Barnabas!

*Troparion:*
Manifestly girded with the mighty strength of the Holy Spirit, O Apostle, having cast down the spirits of wickedness, thou didst deliver the world from their malice!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast the voice of one crying in the barren Gentiles, O wise one, proclaiming unto all the incomprehensible mystery of the divine Incarnation, O exceedingly wealthy Barnabas![^230]

*Theotokion:*
Fire in thy womb, Christ God, beyond word didst thou hold, O all-pure Mother of God, even as Isaiah foretold;[^231] and beyond nature, O Birthgiver of God, didst thou give birth unto Him!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I invoke the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: from corruption, O God, raise me up![^232]*

*Troparion:*
Having received the grace of wonders and been enriched with the working of healings, O all-wise Bartholomew, thou didst catch the flock of the Gentiles!

*Troparion:*
Thy fiery tongue, O most glorious one, burned up all deceit like hay, and enlightened the hearts of the faithful by divine proclamation!

*Troparion:*
Having the Fountain of Wisdom, the unoriginate Word, as Teacher, O most glorious one, thou didst make foolish the wisdom of the world by grace, O preacher of God![^233]

*Theotokion:*
Beyond nature didst thou give birth, O Virgin, and remainest a Virgin, manifesting the images of the true Godhead of thy Son and God!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Christ God, the Sun of Righteousness and Master, shone forth upon the world,[^234] flashing forth Barnabas and Paul as shining rays!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast an axe cutting at the root, felling the dominating deceit by wonders beyond word, O marvelous Barnabas, proclaimer of God![^235]

*Troparion:*
