Thou wast shown forth as a lawgiver of grace, translating the figures of the Old Covenant unto the true manifestation, O God-receiving Barnabas![^236]

*Theotokion:*
He Who stretched out the heavens by His will showed thee forth as another earthly heaven, O all-pure Mother of God, and having shone forth from thee, He appeared!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Thou wast shown forth as a great sun unto the universe * by the radiance of thy teachings and awesome wonders, * guiding by light them that honor thee, * O Bartholomew, Apostle of the Lord!

*Ikos:*
As a heaven declaring the glory of God wast thou seen by the Holy Spirit,[^237] O God-beholding Apostle, and like a bright sun wast thou shown forth unto the world, driving away deceitful gloom by divine grace; therefore I pray thee, and make my supplication: by thy divine rays enlighten the blinded pupils of my soul, O exceedingly wealthy one, and grant my opening lips a word to hymn thee on the day of thy memory, wherein every believer rejoicing praises thee, O Bartholomew, Apostle of the Lord!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread spiritual dew over the pious youths, He Who is blessed and supremely praised![^238]*

*Troparion:*
The great mystery of the divine Incarnation, O Bartholomew, thou didst manifest unto the world: which is uncontainable and incomprehensible unto all, but containable unto the faithful alone, ineffable and believed!

*Troparion:*
As a most holy stone rolling purely, O Bartholomew, thou didst mightily overturn the fortress of deceit; and having been shown forth as the unconquerable foundation of the Church,[^239] thou dost preserve her unshaken!

*Troparion:*
With the color of virtues, O most blessed Bartholomew, thou didst portray the divine likeness; thence having become a divine Apostle, thou didst proclaim unto all the long-awaited coming of Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Beholding hanging upon the Wood the ripe Cluster of grapes which thou didst bud forth uncultivated, O pure one,[^240] thou didst cry aloud: O sweet Child, distil for me the sweet wine,[^241] whereby the drunkenness of the passions is taken away!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same:*
*Troparion:*
Thou didst proclaim, O Barnabas, Him Who rests in the holy of holies,[^242] the Son co-unoriginate with the Father and co-enthroned, Who became man, working salvation for us!

*Troparion:*
Rays flashing forth divine radiance, all-honorable Barnabas and God-receiving Paul, go forth as light, enlightening the entire inheritance of the Gentiles by the word of grace, preachers of Christ!

*Troparion:*
He that nurtured thee, O Barnabas, having sprouted the blossoming word of grace, rejoices today, O God-blessed one; having transformed it, deliver it from present captivity by thy prayers![^243]

*Theotokion:*
Directing my life, O all-honorable one, guide me unto thy calm haven, thou that didst give birth unto God, the Fountain of good things, Who grants ungrudging goodness unto all the faithful!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The fiery furnace in Babylon once divided its action by divine command, burning the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^244]*

*Troparion:*
The great and small, the abundant and humble Gospel of the Savior thou didst teach most wisely; great and most glorious things in structured words thou didst designate unto all, O God-wise Bartholomew!

*Troparion:*
Like a river going forth from Zion,[^245] thou dost water the churchly assemblies, O exceedingly wealthy one, and hast poured forth a torrent of divine delight, most manifestly crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Manifestly with the brightest and greatest of divine gifts, as an Apostle thou wast divinely adorned; the dignity of all good renown surpasses that which is among men, O blessed one, beholding it by grace!

*Theotokion:*
A passionless Mind was united in thy womb unto the human mind, O all-immaculate one; and He Who is above the ages became subject to time through the grossness of flesh: therefore with faith and love we glorify thee, O Mother of God!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same [Ode 8]:*
*Troparion:*
To trouble the sea of the Gentiles, Thou, as the Benefactor, didst bring Thy steeds, glorious Barnabas and marvelous Paul,[^246] driving away polytheistic deceit by the teachings of piety, O Giver of Life!

*Troparion:*
As God, O all-working Comforter Spirit, Thou didst place shining luminaries in Thy Church, preaching Thy power, and crying aloud unto the Son and the Father: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shine brightly like a sun in the world, having manifestly conversed with the noetic Sun, O God-receiver; for kindled by His radiance, thou wast a second light, manifesting unto all the power of the mystery!

*Troparion:*
Having enlightened the whole earth with thy teachings, thou didst worthily attain the blessedness that is beyond the mind, in the heavenly mansions, O all-honorable Barnabas, having received the crown of comeliness from the Master!

*Theotokion:*
Having destroyed the primal curse and the condemnation of death of the ancient foremother, the Word of God was born of the Virgin Mother of God, and granted incorruptible immortality unto all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, has appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened, and to gather together the dispersed; therefore the all-hymned Mother of God do we magnify![^247]*

*Troparion:*
Having been an eyewitness and minister of the Word, an apprentice and God-beholding preacher, where the Word now is, there truly wast thou vouchsafed to dwell, even as the Supremely-Good One Himself unlyingly promised![^248]

*Troparion:*
As thou now standest before the throne of thy Master, and art illumined by the rays from thence, O God-wise Bartholomew, looking down upon thy singers, thou deliverest them from passions and manifold adversities!

*Troparion:*
Because of faith thou wast vouchsafed to be a son unto God,[^249] and wast an heir of paternal treasures: O generous palm! O gift of the now transcendent gift of Christ Who glorified thee, O all-blessed God-seer!

*Theotokion:*
Having understood from afar with farsighted eyes, all the prophets proclaimed thee as destined to be the Mother of the Creator and Master of all; therefore the all-hymned Mother of God do we declare!

*Another Canon, Heirmos the same [Ode 9]:*
*Troparion:*
Having become light through virtue, O Apostles, and now standing before the Great Light, pray ye unto the Master to grant peace unto the world and to save our Church, which ye established!

*Troparion:*
The Scythian ferocity,[^250] by the flame of thy wisdom, O God-manifest Barnabas, Thou didst manifestly extinguish, having the fountain of divine radiances within; therefore thy most sacred festival do we honor!

*Troparion:*
Celebrating thy memory now in faith, O Apostle, we set thee forth as an intercessor before the Master, asking to receive remission of transgressions through thy mediation, O all-honorable Barnabas, sacred preacher of God!

*Theotokion:*
As morning dawn wast thou found in the night of life, shining with the rays of virginity, having shown forth unto us the Orient of the noetic Sun of Righteousness, O all-pure Mother of God!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Thou hast visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны̀):*
Come, ye people, let us hymn the Apostles of Christ, divine Bartholomew and wise Barnabas, preachers of the faith, as praying unto Christ for our souls! *Twice.*

*Glory, both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Pure, blessed, holy Mother of God, who alone art truly our valiant salvation and hope: entreat our Redeemer born of thee to save the world from deceit, perils, and afflictions!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "As one valiant among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Jesus our God, * the Sun of Righteousness, * placed thee in all the fulness of the world * as a most radiant beam, * O God-wise Apostle, * driving away the mist of malice, * and enlightening hearts darkened with folly * and the obsession of passions, * O exceedingly wealthy Bartholomew! *Twice.*

A pure vessel * wast thou shown to be, * illumined with the splendors of virtues, * and having received in thy heart, O Barnabas, * radiance from Him * and an illumination brighter than the sun, * brightly sending it forth unto the ends, * and enlightening them that sit in the darkness of folly,[^251] * O God-receiver!

Like clouds, O blessed ones, * ye drop heavenly rain unto all the ends, * and water the souls of the faithful * with divine grace, * having drowned the tares of grievous ungodliness, * and made human souls * fruitful: * therefore in praises do we honor you, * Apostles of the Lord!

*Glory... Tone 5, by Byzantios:*
The Wisdom of God, the co-eternal Word of the Father, even as He foretold in the Gospels: Ye are the abundant vines, O all-praised Apostles,[^252] bearing the ripe and fair cluster in your branches; which the faithful eating, we sweeten our taste unto gladness: O God-receiving Bartholomew, and Barnabas, son of consolation, diligently pray ye for our souls!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
*We bless thee, O Virgin Mother of God.*
*Great Doxology. And Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes from the Canon of Bartholomew, Ode 3, on 4; and of Barnabas, Ode 6, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^253] Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.[^254] Epistle: Acts, pericope 28 [Acts 11:19-26, 29-30]. Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord, and Thy truth in the church of the saints.[^255] Gospel: Luke, pericope 51 [Luke 10:16-21]. Communion Hymn: Their sound is gone out into all the earth...*

[Decorative Headpiece]

*In the Same Month, on the 12th Day::*
*The Venerable Father Onuphrius, and the Venerable Father Peter of Mount Athos:*[^256]
*(He that wishes to celebrate the Vigil for the Venerable Onuphrius, look for the service after this service.)*[^257]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * thou didst separate thyself * from worldly tumult; * thou didst ascend unto transcendent perfection; * desiring the Fountain of good things, * thither by true love didst thou attain: * illuminated by its radiances, O blessed one, * snatch us from the gloom of sin * by thy prayers!

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * the night-time frost and the daytime heat * through hope of future things, * O acceptable one, didst thou endure; * having mortified thy members upon earth,[^258] * thou didst attain heavenly life;

...joyfully didst thou enter into the bridal chamber, to behold the immeasurable beauty of thy Creator!

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * glorious Paphnutius found thee * hiding in the desert like a treasure,[^259] * and clearly related unto them in the world * the accomplishments of thy labors, * enriching the faithful with narratives * of thy God-pleasing life, O all-praised one: * make us also imitators thereof * by thy prayers, O glorious one!

*Other Stichera, of the Venerable Peter, Tone and Podoben the same [Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder"]:*

As namesake of divine Peter,[^260] * following him in truth * and piously arriving at zeal for the faith, * we know thee, O venerable one: * for thou didst love the good Law, * and didst desire his divine commandments, O Father, * and didst adorn thy soul with virtues: * therefore do we honor and call thee blessed!

O venerable, exceedingly wealthy Father, * having acquired true patience, * unfeigned love, * unwavering hope, * and perfect humility * in compunction of soul, * thou wast a temple of the Holy Spirit: * purely receiving Whose radiance, * thou wast seen as a second light unto earthly men * by the purity of thy life!

O venerable, God-inspired Father, * hidden in obscurity upon earth for many years, * thou wast made known by the Spirit * in signs and wonders, * and the sweet fragrance of myrrh:[^261] * for thou didst pour forth living brightness and sanctity, * showing unto them that love thee * the true grace of the divine Spirit!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Venerable fathers, into all the earth went forth the sound of your accomplishments:[^262] therefore in the heavens ye found the reward of your labors; ye destroyed the legions of demons and attained unto the angelic ranks, whose life ye blamelessly emulated. Having boldness before the Lord, ask peace for our souls!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
The sun grew dark, * beholding Thee nailed unto the Cross, O Giver of Light, * and the earth shook with fear; * but the lawless Jewish people showed no compassion at all![^263]

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having preserved that which is according to the image unharmed,[^264] ascetically establishing your mind against destructive passions, ye attained as far as possible unto that which is according to the likeness:

...for having valiantly compelled nature, ye strove to subjugate the worse unto the better, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit: therefore ye were shown forth as the summit of monastics, desert citizens, instructors of them that run well,[^265] the sure rule of virtues. And now in the heavens, the mirrors being dissolved,[^266] ye purely behold the Holy Trinity, praying together for them that honor you with faith and love!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Considering Thy seedless conception * and ineffable childbearing, I marvel greatly: * How is it that thus, like a malefactor, * Thou hast been well pleased to die, O Son? * the all-pure one cried weeping aloud.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, remove not Thy mercy from us; but by their prayers direct our life in peace!

*Canon from the Octoechos, one; and of the saints, two, on 8.*
*Canon of Saint Onuphrius, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*He that is mighty in battles cast Pharaoh's chariots and his power into the sea; let us sing a new song, for He is glorified![^267]*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the light of the tri-radiant dawn, snatch me from the deadly darkness of sins by thy prayers, O Father Onuphrius, that I may hymn thy memory!

*Troparion:*
Having renounced the world, thou didst cleave unto Christ; thou didst subjugate the flesh unto the mind, and wast shown forth as a receptacle of the Holy Spirit, O God-wise Father Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Guided by a pillar of divine light,[^268] thou didst leave passionate Egypt, and didst reach the height of passionlessness, and conversed with God!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto Him Who was begotten of the Father before thee, yet thou didst remain incorrupt and a Virgin after childbearing: therefore do we confess thee as true Mother of God, O all-hymned one!

*Another Canon, of the Venerable Peter, the composition of Joseph, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He is glorified![^269]*

*Troparion:*
As a second Peter the Church sets thee forth for us today, enlightening our thoughts by thy divine memory, O exceedingly wealthy Father!

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed the noetic eyes from the mire of passions, O venerable one, thou didst behold the immeasurable beauty of Christ the King Who glorified thee!

*Troparion:*
The unwaning light of the Tri-sunned Godhead shone forth upon thee, O all-blessed one, making thee a pure vessel of divine gifts, O God-wise Father!

*Theotokion:*
He Who for our sake was clothed in man out of thee, O Virgin, through His abundant goodness, is supremely glorified among the assemblies of the venerable who loved Him!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who establishest the thunder and createst the spirit, establish me, O Lord, that I may truly hymn Thee and do Thy will, for there is none holy as Thou, O our God![^270]*

*Troparion:*
Emulating the angelic sight, thou didst reach the wilderness; and having withered carnal pleasures by abstinence, thou didst live angelically therein, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Appearing as a planted tree by the streams of the waters of abstinence,[^271] in good season, O Father, thou didst truly provide the good fruit of virtues by deed, O Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
As an all-radiant star shining forth from the desert unto the universe, thou didst illumine the assemblies of the faithful with the rays of pious labors, O our venerable Father Onuphrius!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst destroy the grief of the forefathers, having given birth unto our joy, the Giver of Life and Redeemer, O all-holy Mother of God: entreat Him diligently to save thy flock!

*Another Canon, Heirmos:*
*Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us that hymn Thee![^272]*

*Troparion:*
Having mortified thy members that are upon the earth,[^273] O venerable one, by divine meditations thou didst vivify thy soul, and didst obtain eternal life and splendor!

*Troparion:*
Having lived well, O all-blessed one, thou wast united with the choruses on high, abiding in stillness and beholding divine beauty!

*Troparion:*
Manifestly hidden in the mountains, thou didst withdraw from human society, and didst wing thy whole mind toward heavenly beauty, rejoicing!

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee to be the fair palace of ineffable riches, O Virgin, we worthily glorify thee, having been saved by thy childbearing, O all-spotless one!

*Kontakion of the Venerable Peter, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Въ вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having withdrawn thyself from human cohabitation, in stony caverns and clefts didst thou dwell, by divine desire and love of thy Lord, O Peter, from Whom thou didst receive the crown: pray unceasingly that we may be saved!

*Ikos:*
Having fled worldly tumults and the tempest of life as fleeting and reproachful, thou didst reach impassable places, and apart like a bird, alone for many years didst thou dwell,[^274] hiding in caverns, chasms, and clefts, looking unto the Lord and fulfilling His commandments: from Whom having received grace, O venerable one, thou didst shine forth like the sun, O Father, praying unceasingly for us all!

*Sessional Hymn of Saint Onuphrius, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Quickly anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Thou didst withdraw thyself, O venerable one, and manifestly settled in the deserts, ever awaiting the Lord Who delivers thee from tribulations: therefore having well-pleased the Master, O all-blessed one, thou didst receive the unaging heavenly Kingdom, wherein now abiding, remember them that honor thee!

*Glory... of the Venerable Peter, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*
Thou didst bury all uprising of passions by the mortification of the flesh, O sacred Father, and didst receive endless life after death: therefore today Christ's Church celebrates thy wonder-worthy memory, O adornment of the venerable!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Guide us into the way of repentance,[^275] who have ever turned aside unto the pathlessness of evils and provoked the supremely-good Lord to wrath, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of desperate men, habitation of God!

*Or Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee stretched out dead upon the Cross, O Christ, the all-spotless Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast mercifully saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard Thy report, O God, and was afraid; I considered Thy works, and was filled with dread, O Lord, that the earth is full of Thy praise![^276]*

*Troparion:*
Having stripped off the corruptible garment of sin, O Father, thou didst clothe thyself in the robe of righteousness, O Onuphrius, and didst dwell within the bridal chamber!

*Troparion:*
By unceasing prayers thou didst gather the light of divine understanding into thy heart, O our Father; and thou didst flee the dark deceits of folly, O Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst walk the narrow and sorrowful path upon earth, O God-wise one, O God-wise one, and didst reach the broad place,[^277] and wast filled with the joy of heavenly delights, O venerable one!

*Theotokion:*
Thee do I call upon at every time and place for salvation: despise me not, O all-spotless one, who didst give birth unto God, together my Redeemer and Savior!

*Another Canon, Heirmos [Ode 4]:*
*Thou hast come from the Virgin, neither an ambassador nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and hast saved me, whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^278]*

*Troparion:*
Dwelling in mountains and caverns, O wise one, having set divine ascents in thy heart,[^279] thou didst dry up all torrents of passions by divine grace!

*Troparion:*
The shooting of demons, their arrows, and their huntings,[^280] thou didst flee unto the end, shielded by the shield of faith,[^281] O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Beholding cast down at thy feet the deceitful serpent that fiercely raged, made a laughingstock, O all-glorious Peter, we send up the majesty of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord born of thee, O all-spotless Lady, delivered all from the enemy that rules the world:[^282] and the venerable ones who loved Him, He magnified!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Shine forth upon me, O Lord, the light of Thy commandments, for unto Thee my spirit wakes at dawn and hymns Thee; for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace![^283]*

*Troparion:*
Paphnutius, finding thee in the desert as a fellow-dweller with angels, a great and equal-to-angels luminary, piously revealed unto all the accomplishments of thy life, O wise one!

*Troparion:*
The Lord Whom thou didst desire from thy soul shone forth upon thee, the unwaning light of passionlessness, O Onuphrius; and the whole world in the narratives of thy blameless life didst thou enlighten, O wise one!

*Troparion:*
As well-preaching, O blessed Onuphrius, that it is good to cleave unto our God,[^284] thou didst place thy hope in Him from thy soul, and hadst His praise ever in thy mouth![^285]

*Theotokion:*
Shine forth upon me the dawn of thy mercy, O Sovereign Lady, as I sit in the darkness of my transgressions, O Virgin; and guide me unto the light of repentance, I pray, and the salvation of life!

*Another Canon, Heirmos:*
*O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for besides Thee we know no other God![^286]*

*Troparion:*
Ascending the mountains of honorable commandments, like Moses, O venerable one, thou didst manifestly see God,[^287] as far as thou wast able to contain, and being enlightened in all spiritual senses, thou wast greatly glorified!

*Troparion:*
Ever adorned by divine nods, O wise one, thou didst remain unpartaking of any passion, O Peter, and didst clothe thyself in passionlessness as a garment, O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Thy body, hidden for many years, appeared for our enlightenment, pouring forth rivers of healings for us, and the fragrance of sweet myrrh![^288]

*Theotokion:*
With sacred voices we hymn thee, the beauty of Jacob,[^289] who didst carnally give birth unto the Light from Light for the world, which enlightens the assembly of the venerable!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*A tempest of thoughts having overtaken me drags me down into the depth of immeasurable sins; but do Thou, O good Helmsman, anticipate and guide me as the Prophet, and save me![^290]*

*Troparion:*
While thy flesh froze with frost, thy spirit was warmed by the warmth of the Spirit: and while scorched by heat, O wise one, heavenly dew cooled thee by grace!

*Troparion:*
As thou didst walk in the desert, thou wast guided by God with radiance of light, O glorious one, depicting beforehand the light that was destined to receive thee now, and the ever-abiding glory!

*Troparion:*
He Who was born in a cave and vouchsafed to be received into the arms of the Elder as an infant,[^291] appoints thee, an elder infant-like in good deeds, in a cave unto better things!

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the abyss of mercy, drown the abyss of my passions in the ocean of thy compassions, and grant me a shower of tears from my soul, O all-spotless Birthgiver of God!

*Another Canon, Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: from corruption, O God, raise me up![^292]*

*Troparion:*
Strengthening thyself by the power of the Spirit, thou didst humble all the enemy's power: and having become a victor, thou didst ascend unto Christ as a crown-bearer!

*Troparion:*
Following in the footsteps of supreme Peter, the rock of faith,[^293] wast thou, O venerable one: whose name also thou didst bear, and with whose faith thou wast richly enriched!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst fight the divine fight, O Father, and didst worthily keep the faith: henceforth there is laid up for thee the crown of good labor![^294]

*Theotokion:*
He took up abode in thy womb, and deified the whole of human nature, and sacredly drew the venerable company of the righteous.

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Enlightened by the radiance of the All-Holy Spirit, O God-wise one, thou didst leave behind the tumults of life, and having reached the desert, O venerable Father, thou didst make glad God and Creator over all: therefore Christ the Great Bestower of gifts glorifies thee, O blessed one!

*Ikos:*
Who can recount thy labors, O all-blessed one? Or who of the earthborn can worthily praise thy life, O Father Onuphrius? As bodiless didst thou live, nourished by herbs, and as food thou hadst Christ God, in Whom thou didst place thy hope, walking naked in the deserts,[^295] enduring cold and frost as though bodiless: therefore He, the Great Bestower of gifts, showed thee forth as a luminary, O blessed one!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst speak on the mountain with Moses, and revealed the image of virginity in the bush: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^296]*

*Troparion:*
He that on the mountain nourished Elijah by a raven,[^297] sends thee food by an angel, magnifying thee, O venerable one, who didst magnify Him!

*Troparion:*
Like a palm tree budding forth by the outgoings of the waters of abstinence,[^298] thou hadst dates for food, nourishing thyself with food for thy pains!

*Troparion:*
Expanding thyself in vigils, O Father, thou wast nourished with the pains of abstinence: and now thou hast obtained heavenly good nourishment, O Onuphrius!

*Theotokion:*
He that took up abode in the womb of the Virgin, and showed it forth as wider than the heavens: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Another Canon, Heirmos:*
*The most wise children did not serve the golden image, and went into the flame of their own accord, and derided their gods; in the midst of the flame they cried aloud, and an angel bedewed them: The prayer of your lips is already heard![^299]*

*Troparion:*
With a steadfast mind taking the Cross upon thy shoulder,[^300] thou didst follow Him Who was crucified, and as an ascetic monk, thou didst burn up the passions like brushwood, crying aloud, O all-blessed one: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
That which was hidden for many years as a treasure, the divine Word reveals secretly, O venerable Peter, for the manifest sweetness of all who in secret have acquired sinful defilements, having thee as a manifest intercessor clearly repenting!

*Troparion:*
Sweet-fragranced unto Christ by the vow of virtues wast Thou shown to be a censer, sweet myrrh being poured forth from thy precious relics, pouring it forth unto the wonder of all that behold it: for in the flesh Thou didst live an angelic life!

*Theotokion:*
Finding thee fair and good among women,[^301] O all-holy one, the most-fair Word took up abode in thy most holy womb, enlightening all the choirs of singers[^302] that cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Earth, and all that is therein, sea, and all springs, heavens of heavens, light and darkness, frost and heat, sons of men, priests: bless ye the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages![^303]*

*Troparion:*
Having passed over the earthly realm, thou didst acquire heavenly life, and wast numbered with the choirs of the bodiless while bearing flesh, O blessed Onuphrius; and thou didst inherit the land of the meek,[^304] which Christ granted unto thee unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst truly acquire drops of tears as an extinguisher of passionate kindle-heats; and through the love of divine fire, by the dew of passionlessness thou wast united unto Christ our God: therefore we honor thee, O God-wise Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Having emulated the angelic life by unceasing prayers, Christ showed thee forth, O Father, as a partaker of His mysteries by the hand of an angel:[^305] truly the Angel of Great Counsel,[^306] Whom we supremely exalt unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
O holy land, from whom Christ sprouted forth as the ear of life, and nourished human nature that was melting away with the famine of ancient wickedness, O all-spotless Mother of God: therefore we hymn thee unto the ages!

*Another Canon, Heirmos [Ode 8]:*
*Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew: sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^307]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast truly vouchsafed divine blessedness, O God-wise Peter, having voluntarily become poor, and ever wisely wept; and having become meek, thou didst inherit the land of the meek![^308]

*Troparion:*
Having a vigilant mind, thou didst put to sleep the rebellions of passions; and having fallen into the sleep that is now due unto the righteous,[^309] thou wast shown forth unto all that honor thee, O exceedingly wealthy one, as an unsleeping intercessor!

*Troparion:*
By winter cold and summer heat, O wise one, having conquered the demonic winter, by spiritual warmth thou dost manifestly consume it, and put to death carnal passions, shining with the radiance of passionlessness!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst settle on Mount Athos, even as of old on Mount Carmel, O God-wise Peter, Elijah the Tishbite did,[^310] zealously seeking God in stillness, O wonder-worthy one; and of His vision, as venerable, wast thou vouchsafed!

*Theotokion:*
Having a despondent mind, and a soul weighed down with pleasures, unto thee do I flee, O pure Parent of God, crying aloud unto thee: Raise me up and save me, despaired and condemned!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*For He that is mighty hath done to me great things, and holy is His name, and His mercy is from generation to generation on them that fear Him![^311]*

*Troparion:*
By unceasing contemplations directed toward God, O God-wise Father, deified by communion with Him, thou didst become a god;[^312] and serving with the angels, thou dost glorify Him continually!

*Troparion:*
Having mortified the flesh, thou didst conquer the enemy; thou didst pass over unto the unwaning dawn, having become an heir of heavenly good things, O Father Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Deliver continually by thy supplications them that celebrate thy holy festival with love from every surging storm of passions,[^313] as having found boldness with the Savior, O Onuphrius!

*Theotokion:*
Having acquired thee as hope and a wall, O all-pure one, we trust to obtain good things through thy prayers, O pure one, which Christ promised, Who was incarnate of thee for our sake!

*Another Canon, Heirmos [Ode 9]:*
*God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who by eating had fallen grievously into corruption, and was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin for our sake: with one accord, ye faithful, in songs do we magnify![^314]*

*Troparion:*
Girding thee with might and dominion,[^315] Christ God strengthened thee to conquer demonic cunning, O exceedingly wealthy one, as having loved Him with all thy heart and soul: therefore in faith and love do we call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth in the house of the Lord like an olive tree,[^316] O God-wise one, with the oil of Thy sweats anointing the faces of us that celebrate this Thy glorious and divine festival, O adornment of monastics, chief of the venerable!

*Troparion:*
Thy body that poured forth sweet-fragranced and fair myrrh, O all-glorious Peter, filled the faithful with sweet fragrance, for sweet-fragrance unto God both in the living and after falling asleep wast thou manifestly shown to be: therefore do we worthily call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
O sacred vessel unto the Lord our God, converser with martyrs, apostles, and angels: pray for all that honor thee on earth in faith, that we all may find remission of transgressions!

*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Lord, spare me, when Thou art about to judge me, and condemn me not unto the fire, neither in Thine anger rebuke me:[^317] the Virgin that gave birth unto Thee entreats Thee, O Christ, and the angelic choirs, and the assemblies of the venerable!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
The angelic ranks marveled at thy contests, which thou didst accomplish in the desert, O God-bearing Onuphrius: for sixty and three years, O Father, didst thou fast; and now, O all-blessed and venerable one, thou reignest with Christ unto unending ages!

*Glory... of Saint Peter, the same Tone:*
Let us all in loud voices worthily honor with hymns Peter, namesake of the supreme one,[^318] praise of ascetics, and glory of the venerable, heavenly pillar, flower of Athos, as an intercessor for us all!

*Theotokion:*
We confess thee in truth as Mother of God, O Sovereign Lady, who have been saved through thee; for ineffably thou didst give birth unto God, Who by the Cross destroyed death, and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable: with whom we praise thee, O Virgin!

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory... Tone 8:*
We honor you, all-honorable fathers, as instructors of multitudes of monks: through whom we have truly learned to walk the right path. Blessed are ye that labored for Christ, and cast down the enemy's power, well-pleasers of the Lord, fellow-partakers with the holy angels: with whom pray ye unto the Lord, that our souls may find mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
The unblemished heifer, * beholding the Lamb upon the Tree * nailed of His own will, * weeping piteously, * cried aloud: Woe is me, * O most beloved Child! * What has the ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee,[^319] * desiring to make me childless * of Thee, O Exceedingly-Beloved?

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of Peter, Ode 3; and of Onuphrius, Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^320] Verse: What* shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?[^321] Epistle: to the Galatians, pericope 213 [Gal. 5:22–6:2]. Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.[^322] Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.[^323] Gospel: Matthew, pericope 43 [Matt. 11:27–30]. Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous![^324]

[Decorative Headpiece]

*Service of our Venerable Father Onuphrius the Great:*
*At Small Vespers, Stichera, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́вное чꙋ́до):*

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * having become like the sun in spirit, * thou wast a settler far from worldly tumult; * thou didst enslave the flesh, having denied thyself; * and dwelling in the desert, rejoicing, O blessed one, * thou didst wing thy soul unto heaven, * where thou didst manifestly acquire thy citizenship![^325]

O venerable Father Onuphrius, * having acquired a sacred soul in God-wise manner: * thou didst endure temptations, * strengthened by divine faith, * and united unto God by love; * thou didst settle in the land of the meek,[^326] * adorned with the splendors of virtues: * therefore rejoicing, * we celebrate thy memory!

O all-blessed Father Onuphrius, * having received heavenly gifts that are beyond the mind, * piously dost thou impart unto them that desire them, O exceedingly wealthy one, * the narratives of thy fasting accomplishments: * thou didst hear the blessed voice, * being within the bridal chamber, * where now thou standest * before the throne of the King of all,[^327] * O wonder-worthy one!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Thou didst seek, O God-bearer, to behold the beauty of the Lord, and didst desire alone to converse with Him Alone:[^328] therefore, fleeing, thou didst leave the world, and abide in deserts and mountains; and having put on Christ,[^329] thou didst neglect garments, working for thyself the robe of incorruption: wherewith thou didst ascend into the heavenly bridal chamber, ever rejoicing, O venerable Onuphrius!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Thou wast shown forth from maternal swaddling-clothes * as a God-bearer, * a sacred vessel, * O our Father, * and a house of the All-Holy Spirit!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
Bearing in thy hand, O Onuphrius, * the Cross of the Lord, * demonic fantasies utterly, * O our Father, thou didst cause to vanish!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments shall he greatly delight.[^330]*
Having exposed the deceit * of evil demons * by mighty power, * thou didst clearly manifest the glory of Christ, * O Onuphrius, our Father!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Podoben the same [Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha"]:*
Lift up thy hands, O good one, * thy holy hands, * unto thy Son, * unto the Maker,[^331] * that He have compassion on thy servants!

*At Great Vespers:*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́вное чꙋ́до):*

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * thou didst separate thyself * from worldly tumult; * thou didst ascend unto transcendent perfection; * desiring that Fountain of good things, * thither by true love didst thou attain: * illuminated by its radiances, O blessed one, * snatch us from the gloom of sin * by thy prayers!

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * the night-time frost and the daytime heat * through hope of future things, * O venerable one, didst thou endure; * having mortified thy members upon earth,[^332] * thou didst obtain heavenly life; * joyfully didst thou enter into this bridal chamber, * to behold the immeasurable beauty * of thy Creator!

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * glorious Paphnutius found thee * hiding in the desert like a treasure,[^333] * and clearly related unto them in the world * the accomplishments of thy labors, * enriching the faithful with narratives * of thy God-pleasing life, O all-praised one: * make us also imitators thereof * by thy prayers, O glorious one!

O God-wise Father Onuphrius, * lead me who now dwell in the desert of passions * unto the right path of virtues, * unto the path of repentance by thy prayers: * unto the fulfillment of divine desires, * unto the inexhaustible reception of good things; * that rejoicing, I may celebrate thy memory, O all-praised one!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Venerable Father, into all the earth went forth the sound of thy accomplishments: therefore in the heavens thou didst find the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the legions of demons and attained unto the angelic ranks, whose life thou didst blamelessly emulate;

...having boldness before the Lord, ask peace for our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Who will not call thee blessed?*

*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three Readings.*
*A Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wisdom 5:15–6:3]:*
The righteous live unto the ages, and their reward is in the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High.[^334] Therefore shall they receive a kingdom of glory, and a crown of beauty from the Lord's hand; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take His zeal for a panoply, and shall arm the creation for revenge upon His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall don unfeigned judgment as a helmet. He shall take holiness as an invincible shield; He shall sharpen fierce anger for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the senseless. Right-aimed lightning bolts shall go forth, and as from a well-drawn bow of the clouds they shall fly unto the mark. And as from a sling fierce hailstones shall be cast in full; the water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall overwhelm them relentlessly. A spirit of power shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall scatter them. And lawlessness shall lay waste all the earth, and wickedness shall overturn the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O kings, and understand; learn, ye judges of the ends of the earth! Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and are proud of nations of peoples: for dominion is given unto you from the Lord, and power from the Most High!

*A Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wisdom 3:1–9]:*
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them.[^335] In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us for utter destruction, but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly rewarded; for God proved them, and found them worthy of Himself. As gold in the furnace did He try them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine forth, and shall run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign in them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy are upon His venerable ones, and visitation upon His elect!

*A Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Wisdom 4:7–15]:*
Though the righteous happen to die early, he shall be in rest;[^336]

...for honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor that is measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him; so that living among sinners he was translated; he was taken away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of naughtiness obscures things that are honest, and the wandering of concupiscence undermines the simple mind. He, being made perfect in a short time, fulfilled a long time: for his soul pleased the Lord: therefore hastened He to take him away from among the wicked. This the people saw, and understood it not, neither laid they up this in their minds: that His grace and mercy are with His venerable ones, and that He hath respect unto His elect!

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 4:*
Having left house and palace, thou didst settle in the desert shelterlessness, O all-blessed one, lovingly rejoicing in hills and valleys; in clefts and caves thou didst abide glad-heartedly, and invincible didst thou traverse impassable places; enduring frost and heat, thou wast not dismayed, but ever didst thou hasten to find the One Creator!

Strengthened with power from on high,[^337] O Onuphrius, through non-eating and pinching poverty thou didst come to know divine things; and through standing in prayer, thou didst love the flows of tears as heavenly Edenic fountains, divine springs: whence thou wast enriched, having acquired steadfastness in understanding, equality with angels in soul, and spotlessness in the flesh!

Shining like the sun with the brightness of thy miracles, thou dost enlighten the ends of the earth: giving light unto the blind, and walking unto the lame; and unto all that suffer in their limbs, thou dost grant relief and strength by thy prayers, O Father Onuphrius: for Christ, beholding thee, granted thee unto the world as a marvelous wonderworker: Whom pray thou unceasingly for us all!

*Glory... Tone 2, by the Studite:*
Let us honor Onuphrius, an angel upon earth and a man of God in the heavens; the world's fair adornment, the delight of good things and of virtues, the praise of ascetics: for planted in the house of God, he blossomed forth uprightly; and like a cedar in the desert,[^338] he multiplied Christ's flock of noetic sheep in holiness and righteousness!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Desiring the blessedness that is beyond the mind, thou didst divinely account sweet food to be abstinence, and poverty to be wealth; non-possessiveness to be true great possessions, and humility to be good renown; therefore didst thou obtain the desires that are in understanding, dwelling in the tabernacles of the saints, O Onuphrius!

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
Thou didst unswervingly finish the course of asceticism, * and didst keep the faith:[^339] * therefore also thou didst receive the crown of righteousness, O Father, * which Christ prepared for thee, * Who worthily grants recompenses, * and the honor of gifts, * and reward for labors: * Whom pray thou also to deliver us from afflictions now!

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments shall he greatly delight.*
Subduing all voluptuousness of the body, * thou didst deny thyself, O God-wise one, * embittering the senses with the labors of abstinence * and austere abidings; * and through patience in temptations * and steadfast standing in tribulations, * instead of these, * thou didst receive unending delight, * and ceaseless sweetness, * and ineffable joy!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having preserved that which is according to the image unharmed,[^340] ascetically establishing the sovereign mind against destructive passions, ye attained as far as possible unto that which is according to the likeness: for having valiantly compelled nature, ye strove to subjugate the worse unto the greater, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit: therefore ye were shown forth as the summit of monastics, desert citizens, instructors of them that run well, the sure rule of virtues. And now in the heavens, the mirrors being dissolved,[^341] ye purely behold the Holy Trinity, praying together for them that honor you with faith and love!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion.*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
With spiritual desire thou didst reach the desert, O God-wise Onuphrius, and as one bodiless didst thou struggle there for many years in loving labor, emulating the prophets Elijah and the Baptist; and from angelic hands wast thou fed with the divine Mysteries: and now in the light of the Holy Trinity, together with them make glad! Pray that we who honor thy memory may be saved!

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Zealously desiring the spiritual breath of divine and virtuous life, thou didst take upon thy shoulders the Cross of thy Lord,[^342] and leaving behind the care and passion of life, thou didst live by mortification and in the spirit: therefore thou didst fill creation with most glorious wonders, O venerable one, by the grace of the Spirit, O Onuphrius, our Father: pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that honor thy holy memory with love!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Let us sing of the heavenly gate and ark, the all-holy mountain, the light-bearing cloud, the unburnt bush, the noetic paradise, the recalling of Eve,[^343] the primal treasure of the whole universe: for in her salvation was wrought for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions: therefore we cry aloud unto her: Pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that piously bow down before thy childbearing!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having left all earthly things, though being in the world bodily, in spirit thou wast an earthly angel: for having mortified the oncoming passions of the flesh, thou wast shown forth as a well-pleaser of the Trinity, O blessed one: therefore thou dost heal the sufferings of the sick, and by a word dost drive away spirits by grace, O all-blessed Onuphrius: pray thou unto Christ to grant remission of transgressions unto them that honor thy holy memory with love!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Considering the flame of Gehenna and the unquenchable fire, the weeping therein, and the wailing, the most distressing and bitter separation from the choir of the saints and their joyful assembly: tremble and be afraid, O my soul, and groan; and hasten to blot out with tears of compunction thy debts written by myriad in the books,[^344] having the pure Mother of God as an unashamed helper: for through her remission of transgressions is given unto them that orthodoxly glorify her as Mother of God!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Emulating ancient Abraham, O Father, thou didst remove thyself from carnal kinsmen,[^345] obeying God our Savior Who spoke, O Onuphrius: therefore, having fasted, thou didst show forth thy soul brighter than the sun's rays, O God-wise and exceedingly wealthy one!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Without seed thou alone didst give birth unto God, a wonder beyond the mind, O Virgin Mother: therefore, bowing down, we glorify thee as Mother of God; for thou didst give birth unto the King of Glory:

...Whom entreat to give peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

*The Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^346] Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?[^347]*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel: Matthew, pericope 43 [Matt. 11:27–30].*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Venerable Father, thou gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids,[^348] until thou didst free soul and body from passions, and preparedst thyself as a dwelling-place for the Spirit: for Christ, coming with the Father, made an abode with thee;[^349] and thou wast a minister of the consubstantial Trinity, O great preacher Onuphrius: pray thou for our souls!

*Canon of the Mother of God, with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Saint on 8.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*He that is mighty in battles cast Pharaoh's chariots and his power into the sea; let us sing a new song, for He is glorified![^350]*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the light of the tri-radiant dawn, snatch me from the deadly darkness of sins by thy prayers, O Father Onuphrius, that I may hymn thy memory!

*Troparion:*
Having renounced the world, thou didst cleave unto Christ; thou didst subjugate the flesh unto the mind, and wast shown forth as a receptacle of the Holy Spirit, O God-wise Father Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Guided by a pillar of divine light,[^351] thou didst leave passionate Egypt, and didst reach the height of passionlessness, and conversed with God!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto Him Who was begotten of the Father before thee, yet thou didst remain incorrupt and a Virgin after childbearing: therefore do we confess thee as true Mother of God, O all-hymned one!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who establishest the thunder and createst the spirit, establish me, O Lord, that I may truly hymn Thee and do Thy will, for there is none holy as Thou, O our God![^352]*

*Troparion:*
Emulating the angelic sight, thou didst reach the wilderness; and having withered carnal pleasures by abstinence, thou didst live angelically therein, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Appearing as a planted tree by the streams of the waters of abstinence,[^353] in good season, O Father, thou didst truly provide the good fruit of virtues by deed, O Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
A most radiant star, having shone forth unto the universe from the wilderness, thou didst enlighten the assemblies of the faithful with the rays of pious ascetical struggles, O our Father, venerable Onuphrius!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst destroy the grief of the forefathers, having given birth unto our joy, the Giver of life and the Redeemer, O all-holy Mother of God; Whom diligently entreat to save thy flock!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Thou didst withdraw thyself, O venerable one, and manifestly settled in the wilderness, ever waiting for the Lord Who delivered thee from fierce evils; therefore, having well-pleased the Master, O all-blessed one, thou didst receive the unaging heavenly Kingdom, unto which settling now, be mindful of them that honor thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Direct us unto the way of repentance, who have ever turned aside unto the pathless ways of evils, and have angered the all-good Lord, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of despairing men, dwelling-place of God!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard, O God, report of Thee, and I was afraid; I understood Thy works, and was filled with dread, O Lord, for the earth is full of Thy praise![^354]*

*Troparion:*
Having stripped off the corruptible garment of sin, O Father Onuphrius, thou didst put on the robe of righteousness,[^355] and didst dwell within the bridal chamber!

*Troparion:*
By unceasing prayers thou didst gather the light of divine understanding into thy heart, O our Father; and thou didst flee the gloomy deceits of ignorance, O Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Having walked the narrow and sorrowful path on earth, O God-wise one, thou didst reach the broad way, and wast filled with the joy of heavenly delights, O venerable one!

*Theotokion:*
Thee do I call upon at every time and in every place as my salvation: despise me not, O all-immaculate one, who gavest birth unto God, my Redeemer and Savior!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Shine forth upon me, O Lord, the light of Thy commandments, for unto Thee my spirit wakes at dawn and hymns Thee; for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace![^356]*

*Troparion:*
Having found thee in the wilderness as a co-dweller with angels, a great equal-to-the-angels luminary, Paphnutius piously manifested the accomplishments of thy life unto all, O wise one!

*Troparion:*
The Lord, Whom thou didst desire from thy soul, shone forth upon thee as the never-waning light of passionlessness, O Onuphrius:

...and by the narratives of thine immaculate life thou didst completely enlighten the world, O wise one!

*Troparion:*
As thou didst well proclaim, O blessed Onuphrius, that it is good to cleave unto our God,[^357] thou didst place the hope of thy soul in Him, and didst ever have His praise in thy mouth!

*Theotokion:*
Shine forth upon me the dawn of thy mercy, O Lady, while I am in the darkness of my transgressions, O Virgin; and direct me who pray unto the light of repentance and the salvation of life!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The tempest of thoughts having overtaken me, pulls me down into the depth of immeasurable sins; but Thou, O good Helmsman, anticipate and steer me, as Thou didst the Prophet, and save me![^358]*

*Troparion:*
While thy flesh was freezing with frost, thy spirit was warmed by the warmth of the Spirit; and while thou wast scorched with heat, O wise one, heavenly dew cooled thee by grace!

*Troparion:*
As thou walkedst in the wilderness, thou wast escorted by God with radiance, O glorious one, prefiguring the light that was about to receive thee there now, and the ever-abiding glory!

*Troparion:*
He Who was born in a cave, and deigned to be received into the arms of the Elder as an infant,[^359] assigns thee, an elder being child-like in good deeds in the cave, unto better things!

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the abyss of mercy, having drowned the abyss of my passions in the flood of thy bounties, grant me a shower of tears from my soul, O all-immaculate Mother of God!

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*
*Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Enlightened by the radiance of the All-Holy Spirit, O God-wise one, thou didst leave the clamors of life; and having reached the wilderness, O venerable Father, thou didst make glad God and the Creator Who is over all; therefore Christ, the great Bestower of gifts, glorifies thee, O blessed one!

*Ikos:*
Who can recount thine ascetical struggles, O all-blessed one? Or who among earthborn men can worthily praise thy life, O Father Onuphrius? For thou didst live as one bodiless, fed upon herbs; and thou hadst Christ God as food, in Whom thou didst place thy hope, walking naked in the wilderness, enduring cold and frost as one bodiless: therefore He, the great Bestower of gifts, showed thee forth as a luminary, O blessed one!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst speak on the mountain with Moses, and didst reveal the type of virginity in the bush: Blessed art Thou* O God of our Fathers![^360]*

*Troparion:*
He Who fed Elijah on the mountain by a raven,[^361] sends food unto thee by an angel, magnifying thee, O venerable one, who didst magnify Him!

*Troparion:*
As a palm tree thou didst blossom by the streams of waters of abstinence,[^362] and thou hadst dates for food, being fed with the food of thy labors!

*Troparion:*
Expanding in contemplation, O Father, thou wast fed by the labors of abstinence; and now thou hast obtained heavenly good nourishment, O Onuphrius!

*Theotokion:*
He Who dwelt in the womb of the Virgin, and showed it forth broader than the heavens: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our Fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The earth, and all that is upon it, the sea, and all springs, the heavens of heavens, light, and darkness, frost and heat, ye sons of men, ye priests, bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages![^363]*

*Troparion:*
Passing beyond the earthly down below, thou didst acquire heavenly life; and bearing flesh, thou wast united with the choirs of the bodiless, O blessed Onuphrius, and didst inherit the land of the meek,[^364] which Christ gives unto thee unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst truly acquire drops of tears as an extinguisher against the inflammatory kindling of passions; and through love of the divine Fire, thou wast joined unto the dew of passionlessness in Christ our God; therefore do we honor thee, O God-wise Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Emulating the life of the angels by unceasing prayers, Christ showed thee forth, O Father, as a communicant of His Mysteries through the hand of an angel: truly the Angel of Great Counsel,[^365] Whom we exalt above all unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Holy earth, from which Christ blossomed as an Ear of Life,[^366] and renewed the human nature that had wasted away through the famine of ancient wickedness, O all-immaculate Mother of God: therefore do we hymn thee unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*For the Mighty One has done great things unto me, and holy is His name, and His mercy is from generation to generation unto them that fear Him![^367]*

*Troparion:*
By unceasing contemplation directed toward God, O God-wise Father, deified by communion with Him, thou becamest a god; and serving with the angels, thou dost ever glorify Him!

*Troparion:*
Having mortified the flesh, thou didst conquer the enemy; thou didst draw near unto the never-waning dawn of heavenly good things, having become an heir, O Father Onuphrius!

*Troparion:*
Deliver from every affliction them that celebrate thy holy festival with love from every surge of the passions, ever deliver by thy supplications, as having boldness before the Savior, O Onuphrius!

*Theotokion:*
Having acquired thee as hope and wall, O all-pure one, we trust to obtain good things by thy prayers, O pure one, which Christ promised, Who became incarnate from thee for our sake!

*Exapostilarion:*
*Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены́ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
The angelic ranks were amazed at thy struggles, which thou didst perform in the wilderness, O God-bearing Onuphrius; for three and sixty years,[^368] O Father, didst thou fast; and now, O all-blessed venerable one, thou reignest with Christ unto unending ages!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We truly confess thee to be the Mother of God, O good Lady, who have been saved through thee; for ineffably thou gavest birth unto God, Who destroyed death by the Cross, and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable; with whom we praise thee, O Virgin!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 1:*
Ascending the chariot of virtues that runs unto heaven, O marvelous one: through fasting thou didst reach the heavenly city, beholding from the wilderness the supermundane things of the Jerusalem on high; and receiving the worthy honors of thy labors and struggles, thou rejoicest with the heavenly chieftaincies, O all-blessed one, having become an heir of eternal good things and a citizen of the Kingdom; but pray, O God-bearing Onuphrius, unto the Savior of all, to give peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

Shielded by the unerring firmness of struggle, O blessed one, and armed with spiritual grace, thou didst conquer Amalek,[^369] the whole array of the Egyptian darkness; and having purified thy mind on high, thou didst expose the beguiler; and thou wast shown forth as victorious over spirits, O all-blessed one, and a co-heir of Christ, having become an imitator of the bodiless ones; but pray, O God-bearing Onuphrius, unto the Savior of all, to give peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

Having disdained transient and earthly things, O God-wise one, Thou didst love high and abiding things with divine desire; for Thou didst bear thy cross upon thy shoulder according to the Gospel,[^370] and didst love God; valiantly having conquered demonic legions by fasting, Thou rejoicest with the heavenly choirs as one invincible; and desiring to be numbered among the temple, Thou wast accounted worthy of endless life; but pray, O God-bearing Onuphrius, unto the Savior of all, to give peace unto the world, and to save our souls!

*Tone 2:*

Thou soughtest, O God-bearer, to behold the beauty of the Lord, and desiredst to converse alone with the Alone; therefore fleeing, thou didst leave the world, and didst abide in the wilderness and mountains; and having put on Christ,[^371] thou tookest no care for clothing, having fashioned for thyself a robe of incorruption, with which thou didst ascend into the heavenly bridal chamber, ever rejoicing, O venerable Onuphrius!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Today the great luminary, the foundation of hermits, the rule of all venerable monastics, the earthly angel and heavenly man, the great Onuphrius, passes most gloriously from earthly unto heavenly things, escorted by angelic ranks; therefore we also, assembled at his memory, hymn Christ with psalms and spiritual hymns, Who has thus worthily glorified him; and we pray: by his prayers may He save our souls, as He is the Lover of mankind!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion.*

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^372] Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?[^373]*
*Epistle: Paul to the Galatians, pericope 213 [Gal. 5:22–6:2].*
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.[^374] Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.[^375]*
*Gospel: Luke, pericope 24 [Luke 6:17–23].*
*Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, ye righteous![^376]*

*In the Same Month, on the 13th Day:*

*The Holy Martyr Aquilina; and the Holy Triphyllius, Bishop of Leucosia in Cyprus.*

*At 'Lord, I have cried', Stichera, Tone 4:*
*Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Knowing thee to be an incorrupt bride, * adorned by the Holy Spirit, * we celebrate thy holy memory, * O passion-bearing martyr; * and piously bowing down * before thy relics and holy shrine, * we ever draw forth healing for our passions, * O all-praised Aquilina, * as with faith we honor thee!

Thou didst bring as a gift, * the tearing of thy limbs, * unto thy Bridegroom, O all-honorable one; * therefore, having accounted thee worthy of most radiant bridal chambers, * the Pre-eternal One enlightens thee with the light of divine glory; * before Whom standing and rejoicing, * diligently pray for us pray, * for them that faithfully glorify * thy suffering, O Aquilina!

Held fast by the desire * of thy Creator, O all-lauded one, * thou didst portray His sufferings in the flesh, * having endured every surge of pains; * and now thou dwellest in the heavens, * possessing painless glory * and wearing an unfading crown, * and beholding that which the angelic ranks see, * O God-inspired Aquilina!

*Other Stichera [for St. Triphyllius], Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O glorious Father Triphyllius, * enlightened exceedingly * by many tears, * thou didst fly up unto the First Cause of good things; * and thou wast shown forth as a light-bearing pillar, * enlightening by words and miracles all that approach thee * with ever God-submissive thought; * therefore do we honor and call thee blessed!

O holy Father Triphyllius, * honorably adorned * with the hem of vestments * like a second Aaron,[^377] * thou now beholdest * the Holy of Holies, * having entered within the second veil;[^378] * O thy pure radiance beyond the mind, * divine adornment of hierarchs, * of which thou hast been made a partaker!

Having a merciful soul, * and a pure mind, * and an uncrooked heart, * and undefiled faith, * and unfeigned love, * thou wast honorably numbered among the patristic choirs * in the dignity of hierarchy, O Father; * therefore do we honor thee with sacred songs, * and piously do all of us call thee blessed and praise thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, enlightenment of souls, forgiveness of sinners, restoration of the negligent! Rejoice, consolation and joy, helper of the sorrowing! Rejoice, O good one, healing of infirm souls! Rejoice, reconciliation unto God and mediatress, being holier than all the seraphim, O pure one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee nailed * upon the Cross, O Jesus, * and willingly accepting passions, * the Virgin, Thy Mother, O Master, * cried out: Woe is me, * sweet Child! How dost Thou suffer wounds unjustly, * O Physician Who healedst human weakness, * and deliveredst all from corruption by Thy tender-heartedness?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Aquilina, O Jesus, * cries with a great voice: * Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, * and seeking Thee I endure suffering, * and am crucified and buried with Thee in Thy baptism,[^379] * and suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, * and die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; * but receive me as a spotless sacrifice, * offered with love unto Thee! * By her supplications, * as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*Troparion to the Hierarch, the same Tone:*
The truth of things revealed thee unto thy flock as a rule of faith, an icon of meekness, and a teacher of abstinence; therefore thou didst acquire high things through humility, and rich things through poverty, O Father Triphyllius; pray thou unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon from the Octoechos, one; and of the Saints, two, on 8.*
*Canon of the Saint [Aquilina]. A creation of Joseph. The acrostic whereof is: Receive the song, O beneficent Aquilina.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The severed sea parted for him that was not cut, and the sun saw land which it had not seen; the water drowned the fierce enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable deep; and a song was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified![^380]*

*Troparion:*
Most gloriously standing before Christ thy Bridegroom in the heavens, truly rejoicing; therefore, O honorable one, by thy prayers glorify them that piously glorify thee on earth, and make them communicants of thyself, who sing unto the Lord: For He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:*
The grace of the Spirit that dwelt within thee made thy soul pure, O glorious one; and by the wounds of thy body thou becamest fairer; therefore the Bridegroom of souls betrothed thee unto Himself, O all-lauded one, as being pure and incorrupt unto the end!

*Troparion:*
When a fierce winter filled the ends of the earth of old, being filled with the Spirit as a true martyr of the Lord Almighty, thou didst reach the life-giving spring, and didst win a victorious victory, O glorious, God-wise, and marvelous Aquilina!

*Theotokion:*
He Who is co-eternal with the unoriginate Father entered into thy womb, having become man, and saved us who were bowed down by malice and enslaved by the deceit of the enemy, O Mary, Bride of God; therefore do we call thee blessed with faith and love!

*Another Canon, of the Holy Hierarch Triphyllius.*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He is glorified![^381]*

*Troparion:*
With faith let us all offer praise unto God, Who adorned His well-pleaser with manifold virtues and raised him up as a second Moses![^382]

*Troparion:*
Ascending the mountain of virtues, thou didst receive the divine Law, O Father Triphyllius; and by the tablet of thy mind thou didst clarify the beauty of thy soul!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst manifestly appear fragrant with understanding, O venerable one; for thou didst sprout spiritual healing for all as a bodily medicine, O Father, having appeared as a God-inspired herb unto the faithful!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the wellspring of life, O pure Virgin Mother, having given birth unto the Prince and Lord, the Life of all, who intercedest for them that glorify thee with faith!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the establishment of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee![^383]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst weave a robe of light by grace, O glorious one, having sacredly crimsoned it with thy blood, O Martyr Aquilina!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast manifestly wounded with divine love, O all-honorable one; therefore thou didst steadfastly endure wounds and fierce pains!

*Troparion:*
The Savior and honorable Lord consecrated thee, O honorable Aquilina, who lovedst Him with all thy strength from thy soul!

*Theotokion:*
Thee, an unwedded bride, and pure, clean dwelling-place of Jesus, we know to be holier than the cherubim!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee![^384]*

*Troparion:*
Thy holy memory has shone forth brightly upon the world like the sun; and it enlightens the hearts of us who faithfully celebrate it by grace!

*Troparion:*
Having become a cluster of the Divine Spirit, which sprouted a divine cluster, thou didst noetically mix the wine of faith for us faithful!

*Troparion:*
Having taken the sword of divine grace, thou didst cut down the insolence of Beliar,[^385] and wast shown forth unto us as Christ's valiant victor, O holy one!

*Theotokion:*
Neither the tongue of the earthborn nor the mind of the bodiless can tell of thy childbearing; for beyond nature and mind, O Mother of God, thou gavest birth unto the Creator!

*Kontakion of the Hierarch, Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):*
Having received the purity of virginity through excellence of life, O Triphyllius, thou wast shown forth as the primate of Leucosia; and thou wast revealed unto them as a herald and teacher of the knowledge of God; therefore joyfully do we cry unto thee: Rejoice, adornment of hierarchs!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint [Aquilina], Tone 8:*
*Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Illumined with the divine light of virtues, Thou didst adorn thyself with a martyr's crown, O glorious and honorable one; and having become fair in beauty, by faith Thou wast truly betrothed unto the Master of all, O undefiled and incorrupt one; therefore Thou wast accounted worthy of the heavenly bridal chambers, having suffered well and conquered the beguiler, O God-wise Aquilina: pray Thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that honor thy holy memory with love!

*Glory... of the Holy Hierarch, Tone 3:*
*Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Having the zeal of faith, O all-blessed one, thou didst bud forth ripe fruits; for thou didst put to shame the legions of enemies, and slewest the devil, O Triphyllius, our venerable Father: pray thou unto Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Both now... of the Feast, or Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, who in purity surpassed the angels; cleanse me, who above all have become dust and am defiled with carnal transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting, O pure one, great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding Him Who sprouted from her without pain hanging upon the Cross, wailing maternally as was fitting, cried out: Woe is me, my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Godhead![^386]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cast down the proud serpent by the weakness of thy body, having valiantly waged war, and didst destroy the insolence of the tormentors!

*Troparion:*
By the brightness of martyrdom and the divine beauty of virginity, O Aquilina, thou becamest an excellent bride unto the Master!

*Troparion:*
The streams of thy blood quenched the fire of polytheism, and the currents of thy miracles wash away the infirmities of men!

*Theotokion:*
The slumber of despondency and the sleep of sin have overtaken me, O Maiden; by thy vigilant prayer, raise me up unto repentance!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*I heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind![^387]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a truly all-pure vessel, O all-blessed hierarch, and the Divine Spirit dwelt within thee!

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the Divine Spirit, thou didst transfer thy mind unto things on high, and strongly didst thou rise up against deceit, O Father!

*Troparion:*
Like lambs thou didst shepherd thy noetic sheep, O all-blessed one, and didst bring them unto Christ by thy words!

*Theotokion:*
Thee as an invincible wall and a mighty hope, O Mother of God, have we faithful acquired in afflictions ever!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Thou Who from the night of ignorance hast enlightened the ends of the world with the knowledge of God: enlighten me with the morning dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord![^388]*

*Troparion:*
When deceit formerly raged like a surging sea, thou wast shown forth being steered, O Martyr Aquilina, by the grace of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Looking unto the crowns, O martyr, thou didst steadfastly surrender the tearing of thy limbs and a violent death!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst adorn thyself with radiance, amazing the adversaries themselves with thy fair beauty and spiritual rising!

*Theotokion:*
All we who have been delivered from the ancestral condemnation by thy childbearing, cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace, all-hymned one!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord: guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for we know no other God save Thee![^389]*

*Troparion:*
Mightily robed in divine grace, O glorious one, thou didst cast down the power of the enemy; and thou didst worthily receive a crown from God, as an initiate of the mysteries, O Triphyllius!

*Troparion:*
Having lifted up the eyes of thy soul unto heaven, O glorious one, thou didst come to know the abyss of divine teachings, O Father, revealing the Trinitarian radiance unto men!

*Troparion:*
Having taken up the whole armor of God, O sacredly revealed one, Thou didst openly put to shame the hostile might; and having shattered their heads by divine teachings, Thou rejoicest ever!

*Theotokion:*
We faithful have known the Son, born of thee without seed, to be incarnate from thee, O Mother of God: true God and man by nature; therefore do we glorify thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who alone didst settle Jonah in the sea-monster, O Lord: me who am bound with the nets of the enemy, save as Thou didst him from corruption![^390]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst desire to follow Christ's glorious sufferings, O virgin, and didst courageously endure the lacerations and the tearing apart of thy holy body.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endure manifold pains, and didst make light of the pain of fire; therefore, O Aquilina, thou dost also lighten all our pains by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Looking from on high, the angelic choir marveled at thy patience, O glorious virgin: how, being in the flesh, thou didst conquer the bodiless foes through grace.

*Theotokion:*
That God might become man, God was seen as man, O Virgin, being ineffably born from thy womb, and remaining unchanged as that which He was.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy tender mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God![^391]*

*Troparion:*
As Enoch, thou wast translated in spirit from earthly things unto the heavenly, and didst worthily receive the crown of incorruption, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Leukosia[^392] had thee as a confirmation, O Father, and as a shepherd and initiate of grace, like a light-bearing sun enlightening the world with miracles.

*Troparion:*
O unshakable foundation of the Church: by thy prayers, O Father, establish now the chamber of my soul, shaken by all the wiles of the enemy!

*Theotokion:*
Thou art higher than the celestial powers and more holy, O all-immaculate one, having beyond nature contained in thy womb the uncontainable Word!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having pre-cleansed thy soul with the beauties of thy virginity, thou didst ascend unto the heights by martyrdom, O all-honored Aquilina, wounded with love for Christ thy Bridegroom, before Whom thou standest rejoicing with the angels; with them cease not to pray for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trod down the flame and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^393]*

*Troparion:*
When thy body suffered, O God-wise one, Thou didst take no heed, looking upon the rewards that are there, and the light and glory, O Aquilina, and the beauty of God, and divine rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
