Bearing an undefiled body, and united unto the Lord in heart, thou didst stand before the judge of torturers, O pure one, rebuking the senseless torturers, O God-wise one Thou didst exchange the corruptible world for transcendent, incorruptible life, and burdensome food for the abiding food, O virgin, and betrothed of the flesh for the immortal Bridegroom.

*Triadikon:*
With hymns do we sacredly revere the divine Monarchy, in persons indeed gloriously three-hypostatic, yet simple in nature, crying: O Lord God, blessed art Thou unto the ages!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*The most wise children did not serve the golden image, but went into the flame of their own accord, and scoffed at their gods; in the midst of the flame they cried out, and an angel bedewed them: the prayer of your mouths has already been heard![^394]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not wisely bow down before the image of passions by self-control, and the bodily furnace did not scorch thy dew, O Father; and being filled with the Holy Spirit, thou didst boldly cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
A steadfast shepherd of prime virtue wast thou shown forth, O wise one of Leukosia, and thou didst bring unto thy Master a multitude of people as a whole-fruit offering; therefore do we faithfully entreat thee: standing before God, pray for thy flock, that it may be delivered from all corruption.

*Troparion:*
The living spring of thy tears, O wise and most glorious one, amazes all, for enriched ever with streams of healings, O Triphyllius, we sing the grace of thy relics and say: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O steadfast haven, dread intercessor, and invincible wall unto them that are in distress amid necessities and afflicted in tribulations: having entreaties unto thy Son, O Mother of God, save thy servants from manifold temptations!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The God-speaking children in the furnace, treading down the flame with fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^395]*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the radiance of purity, and variegated with the shedding of blood, thou standest, O virgin Aquilina, before Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast offered as a pure sacrifice and a most radiant offering, and as fragrant incense, O God-wise Aquilina, unto Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
Thy holy body, lying in the tomb, O most glorious Aquilina, heals manifold diseases in men, and drives away demonic harm.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond speech thou didst give birth unto the incorruptible Word, Who delivered us from all corruption; therefore do all we glorify thee, O Virgin!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew, sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^396]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst open the way of salvation unto all that were in Leukosia, O wise one, having first sat upon the ecclesiastical throne faithfully, receiving these things worthily from the divine radiance, O glorious hierarch.

*Troparion:*
Cease not to pray unto Christ the Benefactor to deliver from tribulations and temptations of the enemy them that faithfully flee unto thy shrine, O sacredly revealed one.

*Troparion:*
The angelic choirs marveled at thy fasting, O wise one, and the demonic hordes took great trembling, and every creature was awestruck; the human race worthily honors thee.

*Theotokion:*
Forget not the voice of thy suppliants, O dread intercessor; but by thy prayers snatch us away from every disease and from the threatening of God; for thy maternal prayer inclines God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Truly do we confess thee to be the Mother of God, who have been saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.[^397]*

*Troparion:*
Unto the Savior Who was nailed upon the Cross, the virgin was co-crucified by an upright disposition, having trampled down the head of the serpent.

*Troparion:*
Fair is thy beauty, and great is the nobility of thy soul, O most wealthy Aquilina of God-like form, made manifestly apparent now!

*Troparion:*
Having joined with the choirs of the bodiless and with the assemblies of martyrs, O all-praised one, pray unto God for us!

*Troparion:*
Thy most glorious memory joyfully summons all, O Aquilina, to sing of thy glorious pains and sufferings, O martyr!

*Theotokion:*
We cry unto thee the voice of the angels, O all-pure one: Rejoice, salvation of all men, and divine crowner of the glorious martyrs!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Triphyllius]:*
*God the Word, Who from God with ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, and Who was ineffably incarnate for our sake of the Holy Virgin: with one mind do we faithful magnify Him in hymns![^398]*

*Troparion:*
Having lifted up the noetic eye unto the heights, thou didst come to know the Trinitarian dawn, and having forsaken earthly things, O wise one, thou didst divinely and richly endow all with divine enlightenment; therefore do we worthily magnify thee in hymns!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a noetic luminary unto Leukosia, O all-blessed one, traversing the surrounding regions, O wise one, and divinely adorning them with sacred knowledge of Christ as with animate rays, and immediately delivering them from the deceit of the world.

*Troparion:*
We faithful, having gathered together now, beseech thee, O Father, ever to pray unto Christ, that being mighty He may deliver us from famine, and from the destruction of foreigners, and from every misfortune; that we may worthily magnify thee in hymns!

*Theotokion:*
What word of earthborn men can tell of thine ineffable childbearing, the inexpressible gestation? For He that was born was an Infant and God; and she that gave birth was a handmaid and the Mother of the born Son, O Mother of God; therefore do we hymn thee and thy childbearing!

*The Same Month: The 14th Day:*
*The Holy Prophet Elisha; and our Father among the Saints Methodius, Patriarch of Constantinople:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Prophet, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Rejoice, O most wise Elisha! For having purified thy mind from bodily pleasures, O glorious one, thou didst receive the spiritual rays, and didst transmit them unto all the rest, and wast shown forth wholly light-like; therefore hast thou settled unto the never-setting Light, ever praying for us that praise thee.

The zealous Elijah truly shown forth as a charioteer of the air, having left thee behind, O blessed Elisha, enlightened in a twofold portion of grace;[^399] through whose likeness thou didst stay the swift currents of the river by grace, and didst cross over, O glorious one, joyfully adorned and magnifying Christ!

Of old, prayer bestowed the child of the Shunammite's faith,[^400] whom when he died thou didst raise up, sacredly marveled at as a divine prophet; and again by salt thou didst manifestly transform barren waters into childbearing,[^401] accomplishing measureless miracles by the grace of the all-creating Spirit, O blessed one; therefore do we hymn thee!

*Other Stichera of St. Methodius, Tone 8:*

*Tone 8, Podoben: The same [O most marvelous wonder] (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
O God-wise Father Methodius: like a lion by the roaring of thy most wise teachings, thou didst terrify the crafty earthen fox;[^402] and unable to endure thy rebuke, the impious one cut them with the robbery of the sword; yet thou didst not abandon the perishing flock of Christ, having become a champion for the Orthodox people.

The confirmation of Orthodoxy, the shepherd of the Church, the adornment of monastics, a river flooded with the waters of the Spirit, a chalice pouring forth a most sweet drink, a luminary enlightening the ends of the world, sharpened on both sides by grace and cutting down the hosts of the impious—this art thou, O Methodius!

An image of confession, a foundation of hierarchs, and a God-inspired instrument filled with singing the teachings of piety, a house of wisdom, an abyss of love, an ever-flowing stream of mercy, a vessel pouring forth the drink of compunction, and a flourishing paradise, O glorious one—this art thou, O Methodius!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
The divine coming of the prophets has shone forth today, mystically summoning the feast-loving souls: he who mounted the heaven-coursing chariot, the star of the heavenly Orient,[^403] together with Elisha the marvelous man, broadens the streams of the new Jordan, elucidating the preaching of piety, clearly depicting in twofold manner the unison glory of the Old and New, a twofold blessing unto them that faithfully celebrate their all-festal memory!

*Both now... Forefeast or Theotokion.*
*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the wood of the Cross, O Jesus, she that knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who gave Thee birth, O unapproachable Light of the unoriginate Father? Hasten and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine sufferings may obtain divine glory!

*At the Aposticha, of the feast or of the Octoechos.*
*Then we say the refrains:*
*Verse: Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy saints shall rejoice.* [LXX Ps. 131:9]
*And we sing the Sticheron, Tone 4:*
Joyfully today the Church of God is robed and rejoices, crying: My beauty has been made clearer than that of every city, for lo! the glorious Methodius, the great treasure of hierarchs, has made his journey unto the heavens! Come then, ye feast-lovers, assembly of the Orthodox, forming a choir together around the divine shrine, receiving streams of healings from it, to entreat Christ God to deliver the universe from every heresy!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O prophet, preacher of Christ, who wast never parted from the throne of majesty, and dost ever stand by each that is sick, serving in the highest, thou blessest the universe; being glorified everywhere, ask purification for our souls!

*Both now... Forefeast or Theotokion.*
*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding our Life hanging upon the wood, the all-immaculate Mother of God weeping maternally cried aloud: O my Son and my God, save them that sing unto Thee in love!

*Troparion of the Prophet, Tone 4:*
The angel in the flesh, the foundation of the prophets, the second forerunner of the coming of Christ, glorious Elijah, sent down grace from on high unto Elisha to drive away diseases and cleanse lepers; therefore he also pours forth healings unto them that honor him.

*Glory... of St. Methodius, the same Tone [Tone 4]:*
The truth of things revealed thee unto thy flock as a rule of faith, an icon of meekness, and a teacher of temperance; therefore hast thou achieved the heights through humility, and riches through poverty: O hierarch Methodius, entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion.*

*At Matins, one Canon of the Octoechos, and two of the saints, to 8.*
*Canon of the Prophet, whose acrostic is:*
*I sing joyfully of the grace of Elisha.*
*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people through the sea with dry feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his host, unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified![^404]*

*Troparion:*
Flashing forth with prophetic praises the hymns sung of old in God, whereby He that is praised is sung, I hymn Him that glorified him, for He has been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Having taken the mantle from the prophet's hand,[^405] the son was received, being transformed from the plow into a prophet by the spiritual dawn; for He has been glorified!

*Troparion:*
As Thou didst foreknow Elisha's purpose, O Christ, His calling unto that which is better, Thou didst enlighten him with the grace of prophecy, O all-powerful Savior; for He has been glorified!

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure Mother of God, [entreat] God Who was incarnate from thee, and was not separated from the bosom of the Father [O all-pure Mother of God, entreat] God [Who was incarnate from thee, and was not separated from the bosom of the Father], unceasingly to save from every affliction those whom He created.

*Another Canon of St. Methodius.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst strike Egypt and drown the tyrant Pharaoh in the sea, didst save the people from bondage, who sang with Moses a song of victory: for He has been glorified![^406]*

*Troparion:*
Subduing thy body in fasts, O venerable one, thou didst enrich thy soul with divine ascents, and didst receive the inexhaustible nourishment of the supreme Kingdom.

*Troparion:*
Armed with the spiritual sword of the Word, and clothed with faith as with a breastplate, thou didst courageously cut down the hosts of heretics, O all-blessed Father Methodius!

*Troparion:*
Thy divine mouth was an ever-flowing river unto the Church, truly drying up the heretical seas, O venerable Methodius, and watering the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
Let us honor in hymns the animate tabernacle, the ark, the table, and the mountain from which the Stone was cut without hands of man, the Lord of all—all-pure Mary!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind, that I may hymn and glorify Thy saving Ascension![^407]*

*Troparion:*
Having departed from earthly attachment, thou didst serve the fire-breathing one,[^408] conversing with the servants of the Master's pleasing.

*Troparion:*
In deed thou didst receive the twofold gifts of thy teacher, not missing his ascent through slothfulness.

*Troparion:*
Standing before Elijah with weeping, and rending thy garment, with his mantle thou didst part the streams of the Jordan.

*Theotokion:*
Unceasingly entreat Him Who came forth from thy loins, O pure one, to deliver from the deceit of the devil them that hymn thee, O Bride of God!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*Thou Who makest the thunder firm and createst the wind: establish me, O Lord, that I may truly hymn Thee and do Thy will; for there is none holy as Thou, O our God![^409]*

*Troparion:*
Thy tongue was a rain-bearing cloud, O Methodius; for watering the hearts of the pious with the word, it ever choked the tares of the impious.

*Troparion:*
Setting upright the word of faith, thou didst endure the trial of stripes, and wast condemned to sit in a dark prison, O Father; therefore hast thou passed over unto the never-waning Light, rejoicing!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst sing the sufferings of the martyrs, and didst partake of their inalienable joy, O Father, having suffered doubly with unceasing afflictions and wounds, O Methodius!

*Theotokion:*
Declaring "Rejoice!" unto the Virgin, Gabriel spoke: The Holy Spirit shall come upon thee, O Virgin, and thou shalt conceive the Savior and Lord in thy womb.

*Kontakion of St. Methodius, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm and God-proclaimed preachers" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Thou didst contend on earth as one bodiless, and didst inherit the heavens, O Methodius, having elucidated the veneration of icons unto the ends of the earth; for having remained more abundantly in labors and afflictions, thou didst not cease boldly rebuking them that reject the icons of Christ.

*Ikos:*
Grant me remission of sins, that I may worthily hymn thy struggles, O most glorious Methodius! For thou wast cast into a deep pit,[^410] so that thou mightest release souls previously buried in the depth of ignorance unto the radiance of divine light-shedding: having manifestly taught all under the sun to bow down unto the divine, truly ineffable representation of Christ, rebuking them that reject the icons of Christ.

*Sessional Hymn of the Prophet, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
By divine foresight the great Elijah took thee as a disciple, O wise Elisha, and fore-showed thee as a prophet shining with the Spirit; therefore today, celebrating thine all-holy memory, with pious mind we honor thee with him, O glorious one!

*Glory... of the Venerable One, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Thou didst shine forth as a bright sun, O Father, sacredly enlightening the whole universe with thy teachings, O Methodius, and driving away the heretical gloom.

*Both now... Forefeast or Theotokion.*
Stretching forth thine all-pure hands, O Virgin Mother, protect thy people that hope in thee and cry unto thy Son: Grant Thy mercies unto all, O Christ!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding thy Son lifted up upon the wood, O all-pure one, torn in maternal compassion, thou didst plaintively cry: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my timeless Light?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard, O Lord, of Thy Resurrection from the tomb, and I glorified Thine invincible power![^411]*

*Troparion:*
As of old Thou didst heal the waters, so now heal my barren mind, O Benefactor, by Elisha's prayers!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast divinely enriched with the revelation of the things on high, and by wild beasts thou didst take away the wickedness of the children.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with virginity, O glorious one, thou wast seen as a lamp shining from afar unto them that were in the darkness of delusion.

*Troparion:*
The tongue moved by the Spirit cannot keep silent concerning thy marvelous achievements in life, O Elisha!

*Theotokion:*
Truly do we glorify thee, O unwedded Mother of God, and unto thy haven do we faithful flee!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*I heard report of Thee, O God, and I was afraid; I understood Thy works, and was filled with dread, O Lord, for the earth is full of Thy praise![^412]*

*Troparion:*
Having dipped thy lips in the cup of noetic wisdom, O Father, thou didst pour forth unto us rivers of teachings, O venerable one, who piously celebrate thy memory.

*Troparion:*
A divine preacher, sweet in word, simple in disposition, exceedingly rich in spiritual gifts, wast thou shown forth, O venerable hierarch Methodius!

*Troparion:*
Having pierced with the noetic lance of the divine Word the insolence of impiety that was in heretical abomination, thou didst put an end to it, O Methodius!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O noetic gate, through which by an ineffable word the King of Heaven passed, and keeping thee blameless, left thee sealed again!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Waking at dawn we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, and we know no other save Thee![^413]*

*Troparion:*
The silent and meek disposition of thy soul, O God-inspired one, showed thee forth as a prophet for the sake of thy faith, O Elisha.

*Troparion:*
The divine operation of thy words enlightens me with spiritual sayings, O all-blessed Elisha!

*Troparion:*
Dead unto sin, as standing alive before the Lord, O prophet, they were accounted unto thee, O Elisha.

*Theotokion:*
Cease not to pray unto Him Whom thou gavest birth, O Mother of God, to save our souls who hymn thee, O all-pure one!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*Shine forth upon me, O Lord, the light of Thy commandments, for unto Thee my spirit wakes at dawn and hymns Thee; for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace![^414]*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the thrice-radiant Light, O glorious one, thou didst piously drive away the gloomy and dark mist of heresy, and wast a star, flashing with rays upon the faithful.

*Troparion:*
Sharpened by the Spirit, thy tongue was shown forth as the reed of a swiftly writing scribe,[^415] O Methodius, truly inscribing upon the tablets of the heart the divine law of grace.

*Troparion:*
Bearing streams of tears in all-night standings like pearls, O venerable one, by them thou didst dry up the torrents of sins, and wast shown forth as a pure receptacle of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
By thy prayers thou deliverest from every adversity them that flee beneath thy protection, O Mary, Mother of God; for after God, thou art the intercessor and hope of them that honor thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The abyss surrounded me, the sea-monster became a tomb unto me; yet I cried unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord![^416]*

*Troparion:*
Of old it filled the torrent-valleys:[^417] neither rain nor wind of the air, but the breeze of grace, O prophet, and thy word by the operation of the Spirit filled them!

*Troparion:*
As of old, O prophet, thou didst lavishly fill the widow's small vessel with oil,[^418] so pray, O Elisha, that divine mercy may pour forth unto me!

*Troparion:*
The honorable woman, sustained by faith, O prophet, received release from barrenness in old age through prayer, bearing fair offspring.[^419]

*Theotokion:*
As the unburnt bush, and the mountain, and the animate ladder, and the heavenly gate, do we worthily glorify thee, O glorious Mary, boast of the Orthodox!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*The tempest of thoughts having overtaken me, pulls me down into the depth of immeasurable sins; but Thou, O good Helmsman, anticipate and steer me, as Thou didst the Prophet, and save me![^420]*

*Troparion:*
Emulating the industrious bee, thou didst gather the flowers of writings; and as in a honeycomb, thou didst construct this in thy heart, pouring forth unto all the river of thy teachings.

*Troparion:*
Being filled with spiritual words, O most wise one, thy tongue cast down the babbling of the blasphemers, preaching that the Incarnation of Christ and His likeness in the flesh are to be honored.

*Troparion:*
First indeed the elder upon the seat praised the Lord upon the hierarchal throne; and at last thou thyself didst ascend, having tended in a manner pleasing unto God these chosen people, O Father!

*Theotokion:*
As the only Mother of our God, O Lady Mother of God, all creation knows thee; for thou alone didst ineffably contain God in thy womb, unconsumed by the divine fire.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Thou didst appear as a prophet of God, having received a double portion of grace, truly worthy of thee, O blessed Elisha; for thou wast a companion of Elijah, with him unceasingly praying unto Christ God for us all.

*Ikos:*
Let us all hymn with songs of praise the wise Elisha, who shone forth in the Law of the Lord; for he is full of miracles in twofold images, pouring forth streams of healings unto us: for he sprinkles the souls of the faithful, and saves from misfortunes, even as of old for the childless people he sweetened the waters with salt, proclaiming the grace of baptism,[^421] praying unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save from the fiery furnace the children who sang praises: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^422]*

*Troparion:*
Having been filled with superior wisdom, O Elisha, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
As a servant of life, O prophet, thou didst straightway show forth unto the mother her dead son alive.[^423]

*Troparion:*
Feeding of old the sons of the prophets when they were without provision, thou didst make harmless the deadly food.[^424]

*Theotokion:*
Blessed is the God of our fathers, Who was born of the Virgin, Whom He made the Mother of God!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths, scorched rather by love of piety than by the flame, cried out: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^425]*

*Troparion:*
With the dew of divine love, O most glorious one, thou didst extinguish the ignited furnace of passions; and having been shown forth through trials as gold in a crucible, thou shinest like the sun.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst possess an adamantine heart in afflictions and in persecutions, O Methodius, and now thou shinest with heavenly rays and with fire-breathing lips; therefore do we praise thee.

*Troparion:*
As an insurmountable pillar, thou didst abide unmoved by the assaults of temptations, casting down the affirmation of delusion, O hierarch, by the power of thy words, O most blessed Methodius.

*Theotokion:*
Thou most marvelous chamber, thou alone didst give birth unto God, Who united Himself unto humanity, and thou didst remain a virgin, O Bride of God, Mother Mary the Virgin!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who bedewed the furnace and preserved the children in the midst of the burning flame: O youths, bless; O priests praise; O people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Unto Him Who wrought cleansing at the command of the prophet:[^426] the youths sing, the hierarchs bless, the people supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed thy mind from matter, possessed by desire, thou didst immaterially behold the Immaterial One. Therefore, leaping for joy, thou didst cry: Supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Foreseeing the future by grace, thou seest the most pure Cross prefigured on the wood;[^427] therefore, rejoicing, thou didst cry, O God-taught prophet: Supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having seen a multitude of chariots round about, O marvelous one, thou didst cast down [the foes]; with darkness thou didst unstintingly strike them, rebuking their folly, O all-blessed one,[^428] crying: Supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Angels glorify thy childbearing, O pure Bride of God; with them all we sing: Bless, O youths; hymn, O priests; supremely exalt Him, O people, unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*The earth, and all that is therein, the sea, and all springs, the heavens of heavens, light and darkness, frost and heat, ye sons of men, O priests: bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^429]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cast down the haughty strivings of heretics, which were truly impious, and didst lift up the horn of the Church of the faithful, O Father, instructing that the icon of Christ be honored, O Methodius; therefore do we sing to thee and honor thee with love.

*Troparion:*
Christ the Sun showed thee forth as an all-radiant star upon the firmament of the Church, O most wise one; and by the beams of thy venerable teachings, O Father, thou hast enlightened the ends of the earth unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thy word resounded like thunder upon the earth, O Methodius; having bound the coworker of mammon and father of delusion like a serpent, thou didst banish him unto eternal darkness, as being its heir.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the One Only-begotten on high, and the same One below: without a mother on high from the Father, and without a father from a mother below; taking form in her, having impoverished Thyself, O Word, into what was alien, into my nature humbled by sin.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thee Who beyond mind and word art the Mother of God, Who in time ineffably gavest birth unto the Timeless One: we the faithful with one accord magnify!*

*Troparion:*
Drawing up the iron with a cutting stick, the prophet taught that the clay nature of men is lifted up by the Cross.[^430]

*Troparion:*
That the Author of life might show forth the resurrection of bodies, He raises a dead man who had no breath at all in the flesh, showing forth the prophet's most honorable [power].[^431]

*Troparion:*
Now in divine rest, O marvelous one, thou prayest unto God that they who ever call thee blessed may obtain beatitude.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of God, Mother of Christ God! Entreat Him Whom thou didst bear to grant remission of transgressions unto them that sing of thee in faith.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of St. Methodius]:*
*For He that is mighty has done to me great things, and holy is His Name; and His mercy is on them that fear Him from generation to generation.[^432]*

*Troparion:*
The beauty of hierarchs, the ornament of monastics, the pattern of all the faithful, the shepherd of the Church, and the bulwark of the Orthodox art thou, O Methodius.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired the zeal of Elijah, thou didst fill with shame the lawless priests who bow not down before the icon of the Master's Incarnation, O wise Methodius.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing with the angels before the face of the Redeemer, and enjoying His glory: by thy prayers deliver us from all temptations, O Methodius.

*Theotokion:*
We sing thy childbearing and glorify Him Who was born of thee, as both God and Man, O pure Virgin Mother: entreat Him earnestly to have mercy upon us.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Having adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Thou didst receive twofold grace from God, O God-blessed Elisha; and unto them that ask of thee with faith, thou doubly givest healings, unto both souls and bodies.

*Glory... of the hierarch. Podoben: "As the disciples looked on" (Оу҆чн҃кѡ́мъ зрѧ́щымъ):*
Come, ye faithful, let us sing of the divine memory of Saint Methodius today, who was cast into a nethermost pit,[^433] that he might deliver souls buried in darkness from evil impiety.

*Now and ever... of the feast, or Theotokion:*
O all-hymned Virgin and Mother of God, who ineffably gavest birth unto God: earnestly entreat thy Son for us thy servants, to show compassion and save them that praise thee with love.

*On the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory..., Tone 6:*
A star of piety shone forth from the west of the visible sun: the great Methodius among hierarchs; and having cut through the mist of the ill-believing, he came to rest in the true East, of the spiritual Sun, Christ our God; and there with the bodiless taking up thy dwelling with the bodiless choirs, and standing before the Trinitarian throne, as a venerable one, as a martyr, as a hierarch, as a patriarch, by action and contemplation: ask great mercy for us who perform his holy memory in faith.

*Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion.*

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Contemplating thy seedless conception and ineffable childbearing, I marvel greatly: How, O my Sun, hast Thou deigned to die as an evildoer, O my Son? the All-pure one cried out.

*At the Liturgy:*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek.[^434] Verse: The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand.[^435]*
*Epistle: The General Epistle of James, pericope 57 [Jas. 5:10–20].[^436]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: Moses and Aaron among His priests, and Samuel among them that call upon His Name.[^437]*
*Gospel: Luke, pericope 14 [Luke 4:22–30].[^438]*
*Communion Verse: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.[^439]*

*The Same Month, on the 15th Day:*
*The Holy Prophet Amos:*
*Alleluia, or Troparion.*

*Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
The spiritual dawn found the purity of thy mind, O prophet, shining clearly like a mirror, and enlightened the world, flashing with the radiance of divine knowledge, and prefiguring the images of divine mysteries: grace being about to be bestowed upon all men, O most marvelous one!

Having become the mouth of God, thou didst openly rebuke the workers of impiety; with an inescapable judgment thou didst condemn them, O most rich one, following the former teachings and the divine judgments; therefore, seeing the elevation of thy most wise voices, O blessed one, we worthily praise thee in hymns.

Standing before the Master's throne, and being filled with ineffable and divine glory, beholding supermundane beauties, and being filled with light, O Amos, speaker of God: be mindful of all them that honor thee with faith, asking for them the salvation of the soul and the remission of transgressions, O God-pleasing one!

*Other Stichera of the Mother of God, Tone 2. Podoben: The same [When from the Tree] (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Rejoice, O star bright as the sun! Rejoice, O cause of all good things, all-immaculate one! Rejoice thou that didst contain the uncontainable God, who didst bud forth the Ear of Immortality! Rejoice, divine conveyance, and all-radiant gate! Rejoice, slaying of the ancestral curse, O maiden, being the mediatress of good things!

Joy of all that sorrow, and protectress of the wronged, and nourisher of the poor, consolation of strangers, and staff of the blind, visitation of the sick, shelter and protectress of them that labor, and helper of orphans, Mother of God Most High, art thou, O All-pure one: hasten, we pray, to save thy servants![^440]

Thou art a shelter and protection for us Christians, and a mighty refuge, O Mother of God Most High; but now as ever, by thy vigilant prayer, cease not to pray that thy servants may be saved from all tribulations; for after God, all we Christians have thee as protection and refuge!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Truly we possess no other mighty refuge, or tower of strength, or insurmountable wall, save thee, O All-pure one! Unto thee do we flee, and unto thee do we cry: O Lady, help, lest we perish; show forth thy grace upon us, and the glory of thy strength, and the greatness of thy tender mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the undefiled Ewe-lamb saw her Lamb willingly dragged unto slaughter as a man,[^441] weeping she said: Dost Thou hasten now to make me childless, O Christ, who gave Thee birth? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness that is beyond mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*Canon, Tone 7.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Unto God Who drowned Pharaoh in the Red Sea, let us sing a song of victory, for He has been glorified![^442]*

*Troparion:*
Having purified my mind from the dark thought of passions, illuminate me, O Master, to hymn Thy prophet Amos.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as an initiate of the mysteries of God which are beyond mind, and wast accounted worthy to foresee things to come, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as an undefiled and pure resting-place of God, having received the divine operation of the Spirit, O Amos.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst prophetically raise up the fallen tabernacle of Adam,[^443] O All-pure one, having borne the Savior and God in thy womb.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The Church of Christ has been established in faith; for unceasingly singing she cries out in hymns: Holy art Thou, O Lord, and my spirit hymns Thee!*

*Troparion:*
Rebuking the lawless, O Amos, thou didst show forth the righteousness of the Lord,[^444] crying aloud, O God-blessed one: Holy art Thou, O Lord, save our souls!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a pillar of the New Covenant, O prophet, taking away its veil, and crying in faith: Holy art Thou, O Lord, save our souls!

*Troparion:*
Having learned divine things, O prophet, speaker of God, O divine Amos, enlightening the people, thou didst cry aloud: Holy art Thou, O Lord, save our souls!

*Theotokion:*
I was established, having confessed thy divine childbearing, and I richly call thee protectress, O Virgin: Rejoice, O Bride of God, my hope and help!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
*Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
A divine instrument of the Comforter, attuned by His grace ever clearly, O blessed one, wast thou as a prophet; for thou proclaimest the manifestation of unknown things, and dost enlighten them that flee with faith, O glorious Amos: entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory... Both now... of the feast, or Theotokion:*
Not parting from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb, but God became man and remained [God], Who after childbirth preserved thee a Virgin Mother all-immaculate, even as before childbirth, the One Lord: Him earnestly entreat to grant us great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled Ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing Him Who sprouted from her without travail hanging upon the Cross, maternally wailing, cried out: Woe is me, my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard Thy report, O Lord, that Thou wast seen upon earth in order to save us; therefore do we cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^445]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst rebuke the people of Israel who served deaf idols;[^446] therefore thou didst declare, O worthy of wonder, that they would be taken captive!

*Troparion:*
God, Who of old showed thee forth as a shepherd, having taken thee, showed thee forth as a prophet, O consecrated Amos;[^447] therefore do we all bless thee in faith!

*Troparion:*
Having received the mirror of the Holy Spirit, O Amos, gleaming with light, thou dost reveal piety unto all, rejoicing!

*Theotokion:*

*Theotokion:*
The Word dwelt in thee, O pure one, reforming my nature, which had fallen of old through transgression.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Unto Thee do I wake early at dawn, and unto Thee do I cry, O compassionate Lord: enlighten my soul darkened by sins with the light of Thy commandments, and guide me![^448]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst rebuke them that were slain by the arrow of food, O blessed one,[^449] since their inner parts were about to fall into thoughtlessness, and their pleasure was fleeting and corrupt.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with rich outpourings of light, thou wast shown forth as a speaker of God and a companion of God, proclaiming unto all the true path of piety.

*Troparion:*
Entreat, O blessed one, to deliver all them that hymn thee from deprivations and the snares of the enemy; and that they may be illumined with the heavenly light of Christ's Kingdom!

*Theotokion:*
May we who confess thee to be the Mother of God, O Virgin Mother, be accounted worthy through thee to obtain the unending Kingdom and its delight.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Jonah cried out from the belly of hades: Bring up my life from corruption![^450] And we cry unto Thee: O all-powerful Savior, have mercy upon us!*

*Troparion:*
Shining with the radiant beams of truth, thou didst buffet the prophet of falsehood,[^451] strongly rebuking him, and foretelling his destruction. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst darken the flame of delusion, O prophet; for like a sun the dawn of piety shone forth upon all, having received the radiance of the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
I sing of the ineffable and venerable childbearing of thy most pure virginity, O Virgin, and I glorify thee, O All-pure one, through whom we have been saved from corruption and death.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся єсѝ дне́сь):*
Having purified thy radiant heart by the Spirit, O glorious prophet Amos, having received the gift of prophecy from on high, thou didst cry aloud with a great voice in the lands: Lo, this is our God, and none other shall be compared unto Him![^452]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The venerable children cast into the fiery furnace turned the fire into dew, crying aloud thus in song: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^453]*

*Troparion:*
That which thou didst foretell, the divine salvation, was made manifest, O marvelous prophet Amos; having enlightened the world with the illuminations of piety, thou didst cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Preserved by divine power, thou wast unharmed by the God-opposing foes; enduringly didst thou abide like adamant, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord *God of our fathers!*

*Theotokion:*
We glorify the incomprehensible childbearing of the Virgin, through which we were delivered from death; therefore, having been born into incorruption, we cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The one unoriginate King of glory, Whom the heavenly hosts bless and the angelic ranks tremble before: praise, ye priests; O people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst ascend wholly unto the heavenly heights, and didst learn the understanding of ineffable things, and becamest a proclaimer of the incarnation of the Word, O Amos; therefore do we hymn thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
By the sharpness of thy mind, as much as is possible, thou wast accounted worthy to understand the Master of all, Who revealed thee unto the understanding of ineffable things, crying: Praise, ye priests; O people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Beatitude and ineffable joy, and the heavenly Kingdom hast thou obtained as a prophet, O beholder of heavenly things, crying piously: O people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
In two natures, in a manner befitting God, without seed, O Mother of God, thou gavest birth unto the Savior of the world, One of the Trinity; therefore do we the faithful supremely exalt thee in love unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*O radiant one, higher than the heavens, who didst conceive the unoriginate Word without seed, and gavest birth unto God incarnate for men: therefore do we all magnify thee!*

*Troparion:*
Having become a God-moved tongue by prophetic manifestation, speaking divine things, thou didst proclaim the divine tender mercy unto all, O Amos; therefore do we all ever call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Ineffable is the prophetic blessed glory: for the Holy Spirit dwelt in them, showing them forth as partakers of His outpouring of light, from whom all we faithful are enlightened.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst take away my garment of deadness and corruption, O Virgin, having given birth unto the saving garment of the incarnate God for men; therefore do we all ever magnify thee.

*The Same Month, on the 16th Day:*
*The Holy Wonderworker Tikhon of Amathus:*

*Stichera, Tone 8. Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*
Possessing an angel-like life through the casting off of pleasures, thou wast shown forth as a vessel of God; therefore, O wise one, He worthily appoints thee as a divine hierarch unto the people, and shows thee forth, O God-inspired Tikhon, as a pillar and confirmation of the faith, pasturing thy flock upon the waters of Orthodoxy, O holy one!

Filled with divine understanding, thou wast shown forth unto the rational flock as an all-wise shepherd, piously rearing them upon the pasture of true teachings; therefore do we honor thy holy celebration today, with a loud voice glorifying the Lord Who glorified thee, O God-wise, all-blessed Tikhon: pray that our souls may be saved!

God glorified thee with miracles, O Father, Who ever glorifies them that glorify Him; for at the time of thy venerable and divine memory, O wise one, a ripe cluster of grapes is seen, a most glorious miracle unto them that sing;[^454] partaking of which, the faithful receive sanctification and benefit, worthily glorifying thee!

*Other Stichera of the Mother of God, Tone 8. Podoben: The same [What shall we call you] (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Strange and dreadful is the mystery of thy seedless childbearing, O pure Virgin, surpassing in greatness every human thought; for Him Whom the many-eyed ones cannot bear to behold, and all the ranks of holy angels fear, thou gavest birth unto us in the flesh, without departing from the essence of the Father: we glorify thee who gavest Him birth, O Bride of God!

Unto whom hast thou likened thyself, O wretched soul, in no wise looking up unto repentance, and fearing not the fire that awaits the wicked? Arise, and call upon the only one for protection in affliction, and cry out: O Virgin Mother, entreat thy Son and our God to deliver me from the snares of the crafty one!

O protection of all, Mother of God: I am bowed down by a dark abyss, and am ever fiercely drowned by the storm of life; send down a drop of thy mercy, and stretch forth the hand of thy help unto me, and account me worthy of the portion of the elect *and the righteous: as having given birth unto the Abyss of the Lover of mankind!*

*Glory... Both now... Podoben: The same [What shall we call you]:*
Having given birth unto the unendurable Fire That burns up sins and bedews the faithful: by thy prayers, O all-immaculate, pure one, burn up the material stuff of my measureless transgressions; and with dew refresh my soul melted by passions, that I thy servant may magnify thy mercy and power with a loud voice!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the Ewe-lamb saw her Lamb stretched out willingly upon the wood of the Cross, she cried out maternally, suffering with weeping: O my Son, what is this strange sight? Thou Who as Lord givest life unto all, how art Thou put to death, O longsuffering One, granting resurrection unto earthborn men? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
A dweller of the wilderness, and an angel in the flesh, and a wonderworker wast thou shown forth, O our God-bearing Father Tikhon; having received heavenly gifts through fasting, vigilance, and prayer, thou healest the sick, and the souls of them that run unto thee in faith: Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who works healings for all through thee!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saint to 4. A composition of Theophanes. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The wonderworking rod of Moses of old drowned the chariot-driver Pharaoh, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, and saved Israel that fled on foot, as they sang a song unto God![^455]*

*Troparion:*
Filled with visions beyond mind, O blessed Tikhon, and saturated thence with the divine dawn: grant a voice of grace unto them that hymn thee, whereby we may appear worthy to recount thy virtues!

*Troparion:*
Choosing the better path from youth, thou didst pass by all the ancestral wealth like dust, and distributing it unto the poor, thou didst find a hundredfold reward,[^456] believing the words of thy Lord, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
He Who rests in the highest, having found thy heart to be a house of virtues, dwelt therein and took His rest in thee; for with the Father (even as He said) He made an abode in thee,[^457] pre-purified by the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
God became man from thy womb, O All-pure one, and preserved thee again a Virgin after childbirth; having divinized the nature that was contrary to His likeness, He granted it its first dignity.[^458]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens in understanding, and founded the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for none is holy save Thee, O Thou only Lover of mankind![^459]*

*Troparion:*
Gold and silver thou didst count as trodden earth, and worldly glory as refuse; for unswervingly thou didst lift up thy mind unto incorruptible things; and having attained them, thou didst take up thine abode therein, O God-wise, exceedingly rich Tikhon.

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest obtain the better life that abides eternally, O consecrated Tikhon, thou didst despise corruptible life as a martyr, with all thy resolve giving thyself over unto the work.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand of old before the tyrant's tribunal, confessing Christ the King with boldness, fearing neither wounds nor death, O marvelous one; by thy words thou didst ensnare the enemies, making them rich [in faith].[^460]

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst not cast off, O Christ, having clothed Thyself in him that was foul-smelling and far from Thee through sin; but receiving my poverty from the virginal womb, Thou dost show kindness and save [me] in Thine abundant goodness.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Desiring to obtain eternal life,[^461] thou didst truly cleanse thy soul from passions, O our God-wise father; therefore thou didst receive the dawn of consecration, and pourest out gifts of healings unto them that run with faith unto thy protection, O blessed Tikhon.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou only one that gavest birth unto the Creator of all, thou only one that didst adorn mankind by thy childbearing, O Bride of God: deliver me from the snare of the deceitful enemy; set me upon the rock of Christ's desires, fervently entreating Him Whom thou didst incarnate.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
A sword pierced thy most holy soul, O all-immaculate Mother of Christ God, when thou sawest thy Son and God willingly crucified; Whom cease not to entreat, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^462]*

*Troparion:*
Having received the strength of the Spirit, O father, thou wast shown forth unconquerable and untamable; thou dost strengthen [the faithful] as a military commander strengthens soldiers, summoning them unto the onslaught; therefore, having established thy victory without blood, thou didst receive the crown of martyrdom.

*Troparion:*
Sanctifying [thyself] unto God, O Tikhon, thou didst live fulfilling the sacred laws; not as a hired shepherd, but laying down thy life for the flock; therefore, having slain the wolves, thou didst guide Christ's flock unto life-bearing pasture.

*Troparion:*
The island of Cyprus truly honors thy venerable memory, dancing in chorus with the whole earth; and recounting the outpouring of miracles within it, it offers for contemplation now likewise unto them that desire to behold the garden of most glorious budding.[^463]

*Theotokion:*
Light shining forth from Light timelessly, O Son of the Father, in the last times Thou didst come forth from virginal loins, righting in Thyself the whole Adam who of old was slain; therefore truly honoring Thy Mother as the Mother of God, we the faithful proclaim her.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Never-setting Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, wretched that I am? But convert me, and guide my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^464]*

*Troparion:*
Having become filled with true vision, O glorious Tikhon, thou didst rebuke the lovers of vain wisdom, driving away demonic delusion and all fables, and the abominable deeds of Cyprus.

*Troparion:*
Possessing a pure life, performing in deeds that which is beyond mind, in an all-consecrated manner Thou didst stand before Christ's divine table; therefore by Thy prayers, O God-wise Tikhon, Thou didst overthrow the demonic temples.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast crucified unto the world like Paul, and the world was crucified unto thee, O exceedingly rich one; thou didst not live for the flesh, but for God and the Spirit, O God-bearing Tikhon; therefore thou hast obtained those things which thou didst desire: remember also us thy servants!

*Theotokion:*
Fashioned like the heavens wast thou shown forth, O Mother, brighter than the sun; for who from of old has heard of a virgin giving suck? But upon thee all things have been accomplished in a manner befitting God, O Virgin Mother, who gavest birth unto thy Creator.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and bring me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation![^465]*

*Troparion:*
Driving away the demonic snare from thy flock, O marvelous one, and banishing the idol-deceit of polytheism, thou wast shown forth as terrible unto adversaries, O God-proclaiming, all-wise Tikhon.

*Troparion:*
God glorified thee, He Who was glorified by thee, granting thee unto us who faithfully call thee blessed as one awesome in signs and great in miracles, O God-bearing Tikhon.

*Troparion:*
While living, O wise one, thou didst sanctify thine honorable land; and departing from it unto the heavenly reception, thou didst glorify it with most glorious miracles, O Tikhon, sincere friend of the Master.

*Theotokion:*
The fruit of slaying of old in paradise was by nature for me; but thou, O divine paradise, O Mother of God, didst bud forth the Fruit of Life, nourished by Whom I shall no longer be enslaved unto death.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Abiding in God-pleasing ascetic fasting, O holy one, thou didst receive the power of the Comforter from on high, to cast down the delusions of idols, to save the people, to drive away demons, and to heal infirmities; therefore do we honor thee as a friend of God, O blessed Tikhon.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean furnace enflamed with fire was bedewed by the Spirit, by the divine presence, and the children sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^466]*

*Troparion:*
Having become divine lips, thou didst make men worthy from unworthiness,[^467] O most marvelous Tikhon; therefore Christ truly glorified thee with miracles, even after thy repose.

*Troparion:*
Yearning after those hopes, O father, thou didst wisely renounce all worldly attachment; therefore also like a prophet, O wise Tikhon, thou didst foretell thine own demise.

*Troparion:*
Come together, ye lovers of the feast, let us traverse Cyprus with mystical vision, and let us behold the most glorious cultivation of the garden, from which the world embraces pure salvation.

*Theotokion:*
The Fruit of thy womb, O Virgin Mother of God, wells forth immortality unto them that partake of It; for having given birth unto God, thou didst loose the ancient debt.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a better power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^468]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst weave thine all-consecrated vesture, O most lauded Tikhon, adorning with the beauties of virtues; therefore, having declared thy repose as a manifestation of fragrance, and through pipes proclaiming round about thee, thou didst dispose [the people] to glorify the Savior, O blessed one: Supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Forgetting the law of the flesh, thou didst run as one bodiless whither the crown of the high calling was prepared for thee, O God-wise one; for not unto flesh and blood didst thou promise to live, O Tikhon; therefore thou beholdest the beauties of Christ's Kingdom, reigning with Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a victorious one over all Christ's enemies, holding fast by the martyrdom of conscience, and by voluntary disposition offering thyself as a sacrifice, O wise Tikhon; for before all the torturers thou didst cry out: Christ is God, and let the memory of falsely called gods perish unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Ever-present Sovereign Lady, helper of the world, mighty refuge of them that sorrow, only protectress of thy servants in distress: deliver thine inheritance from tribulations and various sorrows, that with faith we may cry: Rejoice, joy and salvation of the world!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Every ear trembled at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, becoming man from the virginal womb; therefore, we the faithful magnify the All-pure Mother of God![^469]*

*Troparion:*
Receive this sacred song offered unto thee from unworthy lips, O Tikhon, granting in return deliverance from sins by thy prayers unto God; for we that faithfully honor thy memory offer thee as an intercessor.

*Troparion:*
The pure and all-holy Spirit anointed Thee with the oil of gladness,[^470] O most glorious one, adorned the multitude of thy gifts, and accounted Thee worthy to reign with Him gloriously, O all-consecrated Tikhon, adornment and praise of priests.

*Troparion:*
Concerning the goodness of thy miracles, O consecrated Tikhon, human tongue cannot express my sight; for from a previously dry shoot of the vine, now by a swift bidding, thy branch most gloriously brought forth ripe grapes.

*Troparion:*
Grant me victory over passions by thy prayers, I pray, and account me worthy, O Tikhon, who warmly bless thee in love, to obtain the loving-kindness of God, and the enjoyment of good things, and to receive His beauty.

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O Mother of God, who didst give birth unto the Salvation of the world, by thy prayers delivering me from the manifold temptations and tangled trials that come upon me; for upon thee have I, thy faithful servant, placed the hope of my salvation.

*The Same Month, on the 17th Day:*
*Commemoration of the Holy Martyrs Manuel, Sabel, and Ismael:*

*Stichera, Tone 4. Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Like many-lighted stars, O all-lauded passion-bearers, with divine brightnesses ye illumine the ends of the universe, delivering [them] from demonic darkening, corrupting passions, and afflictions; therefore, having come together today, let us praise your bright, light-bearing, and holy festival, O glorious ones!

Let marvelous Manuel, blessed Sabel, and all-wise Ismael be honored with sacred hymns; for sacredly proclaiming the uncreated Trinity before enemies, they extinguished the delusion of polytheism with outpourings of blood, and received unfading glory.

The lawless emperor who lawlessly commanded to bow down and offer honor unto irrational, soulless gods, did ye wisely put to shame, O blessed, renowned Manuel, Ismael, and Sabel; and having suffered patiently and lawfully, ye wove crowns of victory, praying for the world.

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Purely having loved Thee, O Word of God, the glorious martyrs, forsaking the service of fire and disregarding the Chaldean land, were illumined with Thy light; and arming themselves with the weapon of faith, glorious Manuel, marvelous Sabel, and exceedingly rich Ismael put to shame the tyrant Julian; hymning Thee with the Father and the Spirit, they pray that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
What is this sight seen, which is beheld by mine eyes, O Master? Thou Who upholdest all creation art lifted up upon the Tree, and diest, Who givest life unto all!—the Mother of God, weeping, said, when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her as God and Man, lifted up upon the Cross.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for possessing Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: by their supplications, save our souls!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saints to 4. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Unto Him Who shook the tyranny of Pharaoh in the sea, and guided Israel through dry land, let us sing unto Christ, for He has been glorified unto the ages![^471]*

*Troparion:*
Let us all sing a song unto the Creator and Almighty God, Who worthily honored the passion-bearers, glorious Manuel and them that suffered with him, unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Rejecting the ancestral service of fire-worship, they curbed the onslaught of the persecutor, and became sufferers through their venerable confession in the Trinity.

*Troparion:*
Being equal in number to the Trinity, having suffered for Her sake, ye were crowned in a manner befitting your honor, O martyrs; pray therefore unceasingly to deliver all of us from tribulations.

*Theotokion:*
The birth from the Virgin being above nature, He Who was born concealed Himself from the prince of darkness, even as it is written, and opened Eden unto them that confess her to be the Mother of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as the Lord, and there is none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous save Thee, O Lord![^472]*

*Troparion:*
It is not possible, O holy ones, worthily to praise your sacred suffering, whereby ye were adorned with unwoven crowns, praying unto God our Deliverer that we may be saved.

*Troparion:*
Scorched by fire, unfailing in mind, bound by wounds, sharpened by afflictions, ye became unforgeable swords against the ungodly enemies, O martyrs.

*Troparion:*
The tormentor, the apostate and lawless one, was put to shame, having fallen with a dreadful fall, when Christ strengthened the triad of martyrs valiantly to take up arms against his impiety.

*Theotokion:*
Her that gave birth unto Thee in the flesh do we bring unto Thy dominion for supplication, O Christ our God, together with the assemblies of martyrs: by their supplications give peace unto Thy world!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*
Like a thrice-radiant lamp, divinely kindled with Trinitarian fire, ye burned up the darkness of the Persians who serve fire, and with boldness ye proclaimed Christ Who shone forth from the unwedded Virgin; and ye received a blessed end, O three in number martyrs.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Guide us into the way of repentance, who have ever turned aside into the pathlessness of evils and provoked the all-good Lord to anger, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of desperate men desperate men, dwelling-place of God.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, the all-immaculate Mother cried: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands, O Compassionate One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, even from the only Mother of God, the Prophet in God-beholding manner foresaw that Thou wouldest become incarnate, and with fear he glorified Thy power![^473]*

*Troparion:*
From a magical root, the blessed triad of martyrs, brothers sprouted, having an ungodly father but a pious mother, and they brought forth fruits of piety.

*Troparion:*
Ye were undeceived stars of the faith, like the sun manifestly shining upon the world, a thrice-radiant triad, light-bearing Manuel and Sabel with Ismael.

*Troparion:*
Pray ye three as one unto the Trinity, that cleansing and remission of sins may be given unto them that celebrate your memory with true faith, O holy martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast truly shown forth as a beautiful palace of the King of all, the throne of the Most High, higher than creation, O Mother of God, and intercessor for our souls.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*From the night of ignorance, Thou Who didst enlighten the ends [of the earth] with the knowledge of God: enlighten me with the morning of Thy loving-kindness, O Lord![^474]*

*Troparion:*
As is fitting, let us all celebrate the memory of the martyrs; for by their blood all the earthly air has been sanctified.

*Troparion:*
The church of the faithful, once dark in night, did these martyrs enlighten with the dawn of their right faith.

*Troparion:*
The bosom of Abraham received the martyr-souls, by whom, even as a depiction is painted, the future Kingdom is proclaimed.

*Theotokion:*
Having truly given birth unto One of the Trinity incarnate as God, O Mother of God: pray that thy servants may be delivered from tribulations.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a radiant robe, O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God![^475]*

*Troparion:*
The holy martyrs escaped noetic defilements, being saved without tempest, guided by the helmsmanship of the Cross.

*Troparion:*
The earth opening wide covered your blood, O holy ones, and suddenly your relics were hidden in the earthly treasuries.

*Troparion:*
Give peace unto the world, O God, and as Thou art compassionate, save Thy flock unharmed, by the prayers of Thy glorious passion-bearers.

*Theotokion:*
Conceiving God without seed, thou gavest birth incorruptibly unto the Creator of nature: Him entreat, O Mother of God, that we may be saved.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Wounded with the faith of Christ, O all-blessed ones, and having faithfully drunk His cup, ye cast down Persian worship and audacity to the ground, O ye who are equal in number to the Trinity, making supplications for us all.

*Ikos:*
O Jesus Giver of life, Who art from the beginning, manifestly illuminate me with Thy divine beauty, that I may hymn the martyrs who strove for Thee even unto death, and valiantly cast down all the enemy's strength, and rejected the idol-delusions, being strengthened by the power of the Cross; for having endured tortures, they received Trinitarian glory: do ye therefore pray unto Him for all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who in the beginning didst found the earth, and established the heavens by Thy Word: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers![^476]*

*Troparion:*
Three lamps from the One Light did Christ's light-bearing Church kindle, crown-bearers and victorious ones, glorious Manuel and them that suffered with him.

*Troparion:*
Forsaking their Chaldean homeland, they fled magical beliefs, three honorable armor-bearers of the Trinity, crying: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The three in the fire with Azariah escaped the Chaldean worship, and the three armor-bearers from Persia with Manuel suffered for Christ's sake.

*Theotokion:*
Among women no virgin has given birth, save thee alone, O Virgin Mother of God; for the Infant had no father below and no mother above: for Christ was twofold.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Hymn the Lord Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and by fire in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^477]*

*Troparion:*
Strengthening the body with abstinence, bracing the spirit with piety, Christ's passion-bearers united their sufferer's full self-mastery against the invisible princes of darkness.

*Troparion:*
Crowned with martyrs as with flowery wreaths, Christ's Church was clothed in bridal vesture dipped in blood, as with the purple dyeing of the incorruption of martyrs.

*Troparion:*
O Christ, Thou didst account them that confess Thee to be the One Son of the Father and of the Virgin worthy to obtain the portion and lot of the saved and of Thy martyrs, and unfailing glory.

*Theotokion:*
As having given birth unto One of the Trinity, cease not to pray, O Mother of God, that we may be delivered from our iniquities, and be accounted worthy of His manifestation and Kingdom.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The birthgiving of the Ever-Virgin, manifested of old on the mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush for the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns we magnify![^478]*

*Troparion:*
With a threefold cord did grace unite you, Manuel, Sabel, and Ismael, gathering together with unshakable faith the like-minded disposition of your confession in Christ.

*Troparion:*
Foul Julian the apostate, the deceitful tyrant, did ye conquer with a thrice-woven sling, O armor-bearers of the Trinity: ye three were together crowned with wreaths.

*Troparion:*
Memorable is the miracle of the martyrs: for after their end the earth, opening wide, covered them; and a little later, like radiant stars, they shone forth from the earthly depths.

*Theotokion:*
O Mother of God and Virgin among mothers, who ineffably in a manner beyond word gavest birth from thy womb unto the Superessential Word from the Unoriginate Father: save the souls of them that hymn thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
The marvelous and thrice-woven, all-consecrated memory of the martyrs in golden bonds has arrived, truly of great Manuel, Sabel, and glorious Ismael; and it illumines the hearts of all the faithful with light and gladdens their souls.

*Theotokion:*
As possessing tender mercy and great compassion, look down, O all-hymned Virgin Mother of God, upon my lowliness; dispel the storm of passions and the temptations of life, O Maiden, and deliver me from the fire of Gehenna by thy prayers!

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*God is marvelous in His saints.*
*Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.*
*Epistle to the Ephesians, pericope 233 [Eph. 6:10-17].[^479]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [Ps. 33:18 LXX].*
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 66 [Luke 12:32-40].*
*Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise is meet for the upright [Ps. 32:1 LXX].*

*The Same Month, on the 18th Day:*
*Commemoration of the Holy Martyr Leontius:*

*Stichera, Tone 4. Tone 4, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*
What shall we call thee, O glorious one? An offering of Hellas, as having set forth from her; a purification of Phoenicia, as having been slain in her;[^480] a lamp, as having shone forth in the darkness; a valiant one, as unconquered by them that waged war? Manifold are thy noble titles, O all-blessed Leontius: pray that our souls may be saved!

What shall we proclaim thee, O passion-bearer? A soldier of Christ, as destroyer of enemies; a king over passions, as sufferer for piety; a feeder of the hungry, as a lover of the poor; a righteous one, as a God-wise lover of virtues? Manifold are thy sufferings, brighter than all hymns: pray that our souls may be saved!

Who shall not marvel at thee, O Leontius? For having received within thyself the wellspring of incorruption as an inhabitant, thou gushest forth rivers of beneficence unto the faithful, and unto all freely, without begrudging, thou offerest gifts unto them that thirst, and thou makest glad them that partake of the waters of goodness: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O most lauded passion-bearer Leontius, neither fire, nor wounds, nor the sword could separate thee from the love of Christ;[^481] but having suffered manfully in the midst of torturers, thou didst destroy the sacrifices of idols, O God-wise one; and now, crown-bearer, thou dancest in chorus in the heavens: fervently pray for our souls!

*Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His longsuffering, the pure Mother weeping said: Woe is me, sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God?—that Thou mightest save mankind.

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos. Glory... Tone 4:*
Let heaven be brightly glad today, and let earth faithfully rejoice in the memory of the martyr Leontius; for the day that has dawned is crowned not with irrational sacrifices, but with the sacrifice of praise from spiritual flowers; for standing with us, adorned, offering the unbloody service unto the Crown-giver, he brightly offers supplication, [praying that we may] attain unto the heavenly courts [praying that we may] attain unto the heavenly courts, who faithfully celebrate his memory.[^482]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the All-pure one weeping cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst unto them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most dearly beloved? I marvel, O Tender-hearted One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Leontius, O Lord, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for possessing Thy strength, he cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: by his supplications, save our souls!

*Canon, the work of John the Monk, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast led Thy people out of Egypt's bondage, and covered the chariots and might of Pharaoh.[^483]*

*Troparion:*
Thy virtue falls not away from the law of praised ones, O servant of Christ Leontius; for this is thy praise and an inalienable wealth.

*Troparion:*
Desiring things above nature, O blessed one, having adorned thy pure soul and thy body with virginity, thou didst beautifully order thy course toward the upward path.

*Troparion:*
Taking dominion over delusion, praising the martyr's honor of Him Whom thou ever desired, O venerable and glorious Leontius.

*Theotokion:*
Unto thee do we all run, O Mother of God, as unto a firm defender, stirring thee unto supplication, to deliver thy flock from every distress.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty has become weak, and the weak have girded themselves with power; therefore is my heart established in the Lord.[^484]*

*Troparion:*
Adorned with common wisdom, and having acquired grace from God, O Leontius, thou wast shown forth as a true sufferer and an excellent martyr.

*Troparion:*
Commit your souls unto the living God, thou didst proclaim unto thy fellow soldiers, O Leontius: serve as soldiers unto the King Who abides eternally!

*Troparion:*
Thy honey-flowing and God-loving tongue poured forth the words of piety, casting down the blasphemers, the slaves of impiety.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother who knewest not wedlock, who contained God the Word in Thy womb, and gavest birth unto Him incarnate as God and Man together.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Taking up the Cross as a weapon, thou didst prepare thyself for the battle against invisible foes, and thou didst suffer valiantly; therefore, having destroyed his power, thou didst receive the grace of miracles as recompense, entreating that we may be saved, O most glorious martyr Leontius.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure, all-immaculate one that knewest no man, who alone didst bear in time the timeless Son and Word of God: entreat Him together with the holy and venerable patriarchs, martyrs, prophets, and righteous ones, to grant us cleansing and great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, Thine all-pure Mother, lamenting maternally, said: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, willing as Compassionate One to give life unto the dead?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thy virtue has covered the heavens, and the earth was filled with Thy praise, O Christ; therefore we faithfully cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^485]*

*Troparion:*
Possessed by demonic fury, he mercilessly wounded thee who wast held by divine love, O martyr and passion-bearer Leontius.

*Troparion:*
Unto him who desired to hunt and seize thee, thou didst with boldness promise the truth, showing that the unrighteousness of the deceiver is wretched, O martyr and passion-bearer Leontius.

*Troparion:*
He Who in the flesh was nailed to the Tree for thy sake, beholding thee bound upon the tree for His sake, filled thee with strength and grace.

*Theotokion:*
Higher than the cherubic ranks, who in the flesh didst carry God sitting upon thine arms: Rejoice, O all-hymned Mother of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who hast acquired us as a chosen people by Thy blood, O Lord, grant us Thy peace, preserving Thy flock in oneness of mind.[^486]*

*Troparion:*
Brought as a sheep ready for slaughter, O all-glorious Leontius, thou didst voluntarily present thyself before the tyrant's tribunal for the sake of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Endeavoring to cast down the strength of thy soul, he that was subject to delusion humiliated the crucified Lord as an evildoer.

*Troparion:*
Let the gods perish who clearly did not make earth and heaven; for He that was crucified is the Creator of all, didst thou say, O martyr.[^487]

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice O most holy, pure, and unwedded one, holy Mother, Bride of God, for thou didst bear God, Who made our race heavenly.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried out, praying in the whale: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers![^488]*

*Troparion:*
Gentle indeed unto them that hasten with faith, O Leontius, and having thy word seasoned with divine salt,[^489] yet severe didst thou show thyself unto Christ's enemies.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing indeed, thy faithful servant Leontius gave himself over unto the testimony of Thy Kingdom; but the enemies endured not Thy truth, O Christ.

*Troparion:*
Enkindled with fury like wild beasts, the lawless ones laid hands upon the martyr as upon prey, rushing forward mercilessly to tear the unconquerable one apart.

*Theotokion:*
We have no protectress save thee, O All-pure Lady, who singest thy most holy childbearing; therefore we pray: make it abide favorably for thy servants.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Thou didst put to shame the deceitful machinations of the torturers, and didst refute the godless worship of the Greeks; thou didst shine forth the knowledge of God unto all men by teachings of piety, O God-wise martyr: therefore we honor thy memory with love, O all-wise Leontius.

*Ikos:*
I call upon the Mediator between God and men,[^490] the Lord Who received our earthly poverty from the pure Mother of God who knew not man, and was crucified and raised humanity up from corruption, to grant [grace], that I may now praise the wise Leontius, and recount his bright and noble sufferings which he valiantly showed forth, armed with courage, faith, and love: therefore we honor the wise Leontius with love.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire and slew the Chaldeans, whom justice righteously snared: all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^491]*

*Troparion:*
With firmness of mind, even as the captive children, unconquerable Leontius trampled down deceit together with the flame of temptations, singing unto Thee: O Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Having become dispassionate toward the wicked flesh, thou didst firmly endure wounds for Christ as an unconquerable martyr, singing unto thy Creator: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
By wisdom and love of Christ, firmly curbing the corruptible firmly curbing the corruptible union [of flesh and spirit], thou didst choose life-bearing death, singing, O Leontius: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Theologizing the one tri-hypostatic essence of the Word and the Spirit with the Father, the unswerving and unconquerable martyr cried: All-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Her that without seed in her womb and above nature received the immutable God, Who in tender mercy united Himself unto men, let us piously hymn as the Mother of God unto the ages.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who was nailed in the flesh upon the Cross, and showed unto us a weapon for salvation: ye children, supremely exalt Christ our God unto all ages![^492]*

*Troparion:*
The martyr endured bodily wounds, and mightily conquered the incorporeal enemy, singing: Supremely exalt Christ our God unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Brightly didst thou stretch forth thy mind unto the Master in the prickings of thorns, O martyr, crying aloud in love: Supremely exalt Christ our God unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ is victory unto thee, O martyr, Whom thou didst glorify by bodily wounds, crying: Ye children, supremely exalt Christ our God unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thee, O pure Mother of God, most gloriously do all earthly generations call blessed; for through thee have we been lifted up unto heaven, O Virgin Mother.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thy childbearing was shown forth incorrupt: God came forth from thy loins, appeared upon earth as a bearer of flesh, and lived with men; therefore, O Mother of God, we all magnify thee![^493]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a servant of the Trinity, having served blamelessly, O Leontius, Him before Whom every knee bows, of things in heaven, and things on earth, and things under the earth;[^494] Whom singing, we worthily magnify.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to behold the glory of the Lord immaterially with unveiled face,[^495] he put off principalities and powers, having proclaimed the one tri-hypostatic Godhead.

*Troparion:*
