Thou didst rejoice in thy confession, and the Savior worthily confessed thee before the Father;[^496] Whom entreat, O passion-bearer, that all who with faith magnify thy memory may be saved.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast become higher than the heavenly ranks, O Virgin Mother of God, for thou alone wast known as the blessed dwelling-place of the Most High; therefore we all proclaim thee to be the Mother of God.

*Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Thou Who hast adorned the heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Having been tried Having been tried like gold by fire and torments, thou wast shown forth as a worthy gift of God Most High, O all-glorious passion-bearer: pray now for us!

*Theotokion:*
By thy mighty protection, keep us, thy pure servants, unharmed from the assault of enemies; for we have acquired thee as our only refuge in necessities.

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord [Ps. 63:11 LXX].*
*Verse: Hear my voice, O God, in my prayer [Ps. 63:2 LXX].*
*Epistle from the Acts, pericope 24 [Acts 10:34–43].[^497]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree [Ps. 91:13 LXX].*
*Gospel according to John, pericope 52 [John 15:17–16:2].*
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be [Ps. 111:6 LXX].*

*The Same Month, on the 19th Day:*
*Commemoration of the Holy Apostle Jude, Brother of the Lord:*

*At Vespers, we sing: "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera on 8, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
O all-lauded Jude, thy sound went forth like lightning unto the ends of all the earth, O wise one,[^498] guiding unto the never-setting Light those darkened by the deceit of godlessness; shining with Whom now, entreat great mercy for our souls! *(Thrice)*

O marvelous Jude, having become as Christ's chariot, thou didst cast down idol-pride, and mystically didst lead captivity captive by the word of God unto incorruptible life, O glorious one;[^499] which enjoying now, entreat peace and great mercy for our souls! *(Thrice)*

O Apostle of Christ Jude, having become a disciple of our incarnate God, by Whom thou wast truly sent as a sheep in the midst of wolves,[^500] by thy word transforming their malice unto piety and unto divine understanding of the Holy Trinity: therefore do we praise thee! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Having received the manifold grace of the Holy Spirit, thou didst become a partaker of the sacred apostolic choir, O Apostle Jude; therefore, inhaling the fiery breath once borne from heaven with a fiery tongue inhaling it, thou didst burn up the thorny heathen godlessness: O preacher of Christ God, pray that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... "The King of heaven..." Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings.*

*Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Jude. Chapter 1:*
*Jude, the servant of Jesus Christ, and brother of James, to them that are sanctified by God the Father, and preserved in Jesus Christ, and called: Mercy unto you, and peace, and love, be multiplied. Beloved, when I gave all diligence to write unto you of the common salvation, it was needful for me to write unto you, and exhort you that ye should earnestly contend for the faith which was once delivered unto the saints. For there are certain men crept in unawares, who were before of old ordained to this condemnation, ungodly men, turning the grace of our God into lasciviousness, and denying the only Lord God, and our Lord Jesus Christ. I will therefore put you in remembrance, though ye once knew this, how that the Lord, having saved the people out of the land of Egypt, afterward destroyed them that believed not. And the angels which kept not their first estate, but left their own habitation, He has reserved in everlasting chains under darkness unto the judgment of the great day. Even as Sodom and Gomorrha, and the cities about them in like manner, giving themselves over to fornication, and going after strange flesh, are set forth for an example, suffering the vengeance of eternal fire. Likewise also these filthy dreamers defile the flesh, despise dominion, and speak evil of dignities (they tremble not). Yet Michael the archangel, when contending with the devil he disputed about the body of Moses, durst not bring against him a railing accusation, but said: The Lord rebuke thee! But these speak evil of those things which they know not: but what they know naturally, as brute beasts, in those things they corrupt themselves.[^501]*

*Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Jude:*
*Woe unto them! for they have gone in the way of Cain, and ran greedily after the error of Balaam for reward, and perished in the gainsaying of Core. These are spots in your feasts of charity, when they feast with you, feeding themselves without fear: clouds they are without water, carried about of winds; autumn trees without fruit, twice dead, plucked up by the roots; raging waves of the sea, foaming out their own shame; wandering stars, to whom is reserved the blackness of darkness for ever. And Enoch also, the seventh from Adam, prophesied of these, saying: Behold, the Lord comes with ten thousands of His saints (angels), to execute judgment upon all* upon all, and to convict all that are ungodly among them of all their ungodly deeds which they have ungodly committed, and of all their hard speeches which ungodly sinners have spoken against Him. These are murmurers, complainers, a blemish-bearing portion, walking after their own lusts in ungodliness and transgression; and their mouth speaks great swelling words, having men's persons in admiration because of advantage.[^502]

*Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Jude:*
*But ye, beloved, remember the words which were spoken before of the apostles of our Lord Jesus Christ, how they told you: In the last time there shall be mockers, walking after their own lusts and ungodliness. These are they that separate themselves from the unity of faith, sensual, not having the Spirit. But ye, beloved, building up yourselves upon your holy faith by the Holy Spirit, praying, keep yourselves in the love of God, looking for the mercy of our Lord Jesus Christ unto eternal life. And of some have compassion, making a difference; and others save with fear, pulling them out of the fire, reproving them with fear, hating even the garment spotted by the flesh. Now unto Him that is able to keep you without sin and without blemish, and to present you faultless before the presence of His glory with exceeding joy, to the only wise God our Savior, (through Jesus Christ our Lord,) be glory and majesty, dominion and power, before all ages, and now, and unto all ages. Amen.[^503]*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Thou didst receive unconquerable authority and power over demons, O apostle, and in Christ's name to drive away the principalities of darkness; thou didst traverse the earth, enlightening it like the sun, and teaching all countries, O glorious one, thou didst proclaim Christ's first saving coming.[^504]
*Verse: Their sound went forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world [Ps. 18:5 LXX].*
Imitating the primal Goodness and the natural, all-radiant life, thou wast a good man, O Jude by nature, and called brother of the Lord; by the goodness of thy character and the beauty of thy conduct, thou wast shown forth as an intimate disciple of Christ.
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands [Ps. 18:2 LXX].*
An instrument well tuned by divine governance, O Apostle Jude, unto the manifestation unto the manifestation of calling unto the nations thou didst commit thyself; guiding unto the knowledge of Christ by words and deeds, thou didst enlighten all to confess the true Godhead of Jesus, the Savior of our souls.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Filled with divine zeal, O Apostle Jude worthy of marvel, thou wast zealous with zeal for God the Almighty, and He showed thee forth filled with divine miracles; for possessing marvelous zeal, thou didst acquire conduct by action: therefore, being in the highest, taking up thine abode with the assemblies that were before, pray, O seer of God, unto Christ God the Word, to grant us great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion: "O what a new wonder!..."*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
Knowing thee as a kinsman of Christ, O Jude, and a firm martyr, we sacredly praise thee, who didst trample down delusion and keep the faith; therefore, celebrating today thy most holy memory, we receive remission of sins through thy prayers.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the Troparion of the Apostle twice.*
*Glory... Both now... "When Gabriel announced..."*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
Having caught the nations, the glorious apostle, and taught the ends of the earth to bow down unto Thee with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God; therefore establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O only Merciful One and Lover of mankind! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
We have become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin; for thou didst bear for us the incarnate God: therefore as is meet, we all piously magnify thee.[^505]

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Christ sent thee forth like a sun of righteousness to enlighten all the earth, O glorious Apostle Jude; by thy divine prayers thou illuminest and with divine never-setting light enlightenest all who with faith accomplish thy holy memory. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Unashamed hope of them that trust in thee, who alone above nature gavest birth in the flesh unto Christ our God: entreat Him together with the holy apostles to grant the universe cleansing of transgressions, and unto us all amendment of life before the end.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
With the net of the word having drawn up the nations from the depth of ignorance in a manner befitting God, Thou didst bring them in faith with faith, and being as a fountain of spiritual waters, thou didst water the whole universe with grace; therefore we who have been saved through thee, O proclaimer of God, call thee blessed as a close friend of Christ, O Apostle Jude, crying unto thee: Pray unto Christ God, to grant remission of transgressions unto them that honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: "The heavenly ranks hymn thee..."*

*Gradual Antiphon, 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Their sound went forth into all the earth [Ps. 18:5 LXX].*
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God [Ps. 18:2 LXX].*
*Let every breath... Gospel according to John, pericope 67 [John 21:15–25].[^506]*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Into all the earth went forth thy saving sound, O apostle and seer of God; and it enlightened the souls of them that were lost, and brought men enlightened by grace unto Christ: therefore pray to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

*Canon of the Mother of God, with the Heirmos to 6; and of the Apostle to 8, whose Acrostic is: "I strive to sing Jude, seer of God." The work of Theophanes, Tone 5.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Horse and rider into the Red Sea, He Who shatters battles with a high arm, Christ shook out; but Israel He saved, who sang a song of victory.[^507]*

*Troparion:*
O initiate of heavenly mysteries, all-wise Jude, disciple of the Savior, and partaker of the true life: move my tongue, and guide my word unto thy praise, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Bearing the light yoke of the Savior, O apostle, and cutting the furrow renewed by grace, thou didst sow the seed and brought forth much fruit unto Him Who called thee, O most glorious one.[^508]

*Troparion:*
Having found the incarnate Word as Teacher, and enlightened by His beams, O seer of God, Thou wast a second light, conformed by the radiance of the first, O one worthy of marvel.

*Theotokion:*
Let faith alone tell of the wonder which is above mind, not human explanation, O all-pure Mother of God; for thou didst bear the incomprehensible God the Word, clothed in humanity.

*Katavasia according to the Typikon.*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and hung suspended that which was weighed down uncontrollably: establish Thy Church upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, Thou only Good One and Lover of mankind![^509]*

*Troparion:*
The beauty of Christ's disciples was lifted up above every majesty for they were His friends and companions, kinsmen and neighbors, and proclaimers of mysteries.[^510]

*Troparion:*
Thy brethren praise thee, O Jude, as shown forth to be the brother of Him Who unfeignedly appeared in the flesh, the pre-eternal Word Who shone forth from the ever-existing, unbegotten Father.

*Troparion:*
Having mortified thy members upon earth,[^511] thou didst live by the Life of all, Christ, O exceedingly rich one; and thou wast a herald of life-bearing life unto the whole universe, proclaiming the words of life.

*Theotokion:*
Behold, thou more than all wast called blessed, O pure one; thou didst surpass all in purity, thou wast supremely exalted above all, and wast shown forth higher than all the heavenly powers, having become the Mother of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Thou wast shown forth as a companion of Paul, O apostle; with him thou didst proclaim unto us the preaching of divine grace, O mystic speaker, blessed Jude: therefore we cry unto thee: Cease not to pray for us all!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Of the Master Who was born, and became a Brother unto all the chosen, O blessed Jude, thou wast a brother; and from Him thou wast sent as an apostle unto all ends, O all-wise one, sowing the word of faith unto all, and enlightening them that were enslaved in the darkness of ignorance unto the evil ruler of this world: therefore we cry unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*
*Or Theotokion:*
As all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as unwedded Mother of the Deliverer, as receptive dwelling of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: hasten to deliver me, who am an abominable dwelling-place of iniquity and who consciously became a plaything of demons, from their evildoing, and make me a radiant dwelling-place of virtues, O light-bearing and incorrupt one; drive away the cloud of passions, and account me worthy of the communion from on high and of the never-setting Light by thy prayers!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Perceiving Thy divine condescension foreseeingly, Habakkuk cried unto Thee with trembling, O Christ: For the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed ones, art Thou come![^512]*

*Troparion:*
The great apostle of the Savior, accounted worthy of the manifestation of the Spirit, was beloved, having seen the radiance His radiance and having seen His beauty.

*Troparion:*
Being filled with grace and with gifts bestowed by God, O apostle: guide unto the haven of salvation those who hymn thee in song!

*Troparion:*
Fenced about with the invincible power of the All-Holy Spirit, O minister of ineffable things: thou drivest away the spirits of wickedness by the word of grace!

*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably conceived thy Creator and God, O all-immaculate one: pray that we be saved from afflictions, and that salvation of soul be granted unto them that piously hymn thee!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, unto Thee I rise early, and unto Thee I cry: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou alone art compassionate![^513]*

*Troparion:*
Having left behind the shadows of the law, thou didst radiantly proclaim the image of truth, having acquired the Truth Himself as teacher.[^514]

*Troparion:*
Fulfilling the command of the Word, O apostle, teaching all nations by thy doctrines, thou didst traverse the earth, baptizing them by the invocation of the Trinity.[^515]

*Troparion:*
Like heavenly rain, as dew from on high, thy divine utterance, O preacher of God, dispelled the mist of polytheism through the preaching of the one God!

*Theotokion:*
Put to death my passions, O Birthgiver of God, and raise up my soul which is slain by sinful pleasures, O Mother of the true Resurrection!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Calm the sea of passions raging with a soul-corrupting tempest, O Master Christ, and lead me up from corruption, as Thou art compassionate![^516]*

*Troparion:*
Radiantly shining by the teaching of contemplation and by the deeds of life, O apostle of Christ: thou didst enlighten them that were in the deep of ignorance!

*Troparion:*
Thou sendest forth unto all men an epistle radiant and filled with the teaching of the Spirit, O divinely revealed and all-marvelous one![^517]

*Troparion:*
The beholder of God proclaims heavenly things, as it were declaring a certain spiritual glory of God, Who for our sake appeared in the flesh and by miracles.[^518]

*Theotokion:*
O all-holy Sovereign Lady, Mother of God: deliver me from corruption, and still the turbulence of passions, O thou who didst bear the Source of dispassion!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
With a firm mind thou wast shown forth as a chosen disciple, and an invincible pillar of the Church of Christ; unto the Gentiles thou didst preach the word of Christ, saying to believe in the one Godhead: glorified by Whom, thou hast received the gift of healings, to heal the infirmities of those who flee unto thee, O all-praised Apostle Jude!

*Ikos:*
Let us worthily praise the preacher of true piety and mighty champion of truth, as an emulator and witness of Christ; for he manfully received His sufferings upon his body, as a disciple of the Word and all-praised apostle, Jude.[^519]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Lord God of our fathers, Who is supremely exalted, quenched the flame and bedewed the Children who sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God![^520]*

*Troparion:*
Having become an eye-witness of the holy Godhead, let us all hymn Jude, singing in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Troparion:*
Thy tongue, moved by the Spirit and divinely inspired, converted the world through the preaching of Christ, unto Whom we all sing: Blessed art Thou, O God![^521]

*Troparion:*
The Lord gives thee the heavenly inheritance and an all-radiant throne; resting upon which, thou divinely singest: Blessed art Thou, O God![^522]

*Theotokion:*
With lips and understanding we joyfully proclaim thee as the Birthgiver of God and Sovereign Lady, for thou didst bear God, unto Whom we all sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Unto Thee, the Creator of all, the Children in the furnace, having woven a universal choir, sang: All ye works of the Lord, sing praise unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^523]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst receive the wealth of the Gentiles and the honor of kings as thy fair calling, crying aloud, O apostle: All ye works of the Lord, sing praise unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst receive this radiance of the Comforter that descended upon thee, O blessed one, crying, O glorious one: All ye works of the Lord, sing praise unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^524]

*Troparion:*
Having hated corruptible things, thou didst choose the glory of heaven, knowing it to be abiding, O apostle; therefore thou didst cry aloud: All ye works of the Lord, sing praise unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Come, let us hymn the beauty of Jacob, most pure Mary, singing in unison: All ye works of the Lord, sing praise unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^525]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Rejoice, O Isaiah! A Virgin hath conceived in her womb and borne a Son, Emmanuel, God and Man; the Dayspring is His name: Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed![^526]*

*Troparion:*
Having been illumined, O glorious one, with the radiance of the light-pourings of the Spirit, thou sendest forth upon all supernatural flashings of miracles, as a disciple of Emmanuel: Whom magnifying, we call thee blessed, O beholder of God!

*Troparion:*
Standing with the angelic ranks, O divinely seeing eyewitnesses and beholders of the Only-begotten, Who for our sake became poor in the flesh: fervently pray ye ever unto Him to save our souls![^527]

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to the Creator of creatures, O all-immaculate one, thou bearest the Principle of the Parent in every respect, having an acceptance incomparable and exalted; therefore, worshiping thy childbearing, we glorify thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "In spirit in the sanctuary" (Дꙋ́хомъ во ст҃или́щи):*
Having turned back the course of thy fair feet, O Apostle Jude, thou didst ascend rejoicing unto the heavenly journey, and standing before the Trinity, in the Father thou beholdest the Son and the Divine Spirit; therefore in faith we celebrate thy most consecrated and divine memory! *(Twice)*[^528]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
I call to mind the hour of the dread trial; the multitude of my evil deeds terrifies and frightens me; but have compassion, O most pure one, through thy fervent prayer, and grant me salvation: for whatsoever thou desirest, thou canst do!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4.*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Прпⷣбне ѻ҆́ч҃е):*
Obeying the divine nod, O most blessed one, thou didst preach unto all the incarnate Word, Who was made like unto us without change; having become His venerable disciple, having become His brother according to the flesh, and having been accounted worthy to behold His glory with a pure, divine mind: therefore we honor thee, O Jude of divine voice, and we celebrate thy memory, faithfully crying unto thee: Pray ever unto Christ to grant the universe oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy! *(Twice)*[^529]

*Sticheron:*
A mountain dropping down sweetness of divine understanding do we call thee, O Apostle Jude: a peaceful river rushing forth from spiritual, immaterial sources, drying up the seas of godlessness, and mystically watering the hearts and thoughts of the pious; a melodious trumpet proclaiming the divine manifestation of the Word Who delivers the world: Whom do thou now beseech, unto Whom pray thou ever, to grant the universe oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy![^530]

*Sticheron:*
O blessed Jude, thy brethren praised thee, who wast shown forth as the brother of the Lord and didst become a God-befitting vessel of the Spirit: being purely enlightened with Whose brightnesses, thou wast revealed as a live coal burning up deceit, and enlightening the faithful with the flashings of pure doctrines therefore we honor thee as an unsetting morning star, and we celebrate thy holy memory, faithfully crying unto thee: Pray ever unto Christ to grant the universe oneness of mind, peace, and great mercy!

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Having forsaken earthly things, thou didst follow Christ, and being sealed by the breathing of the Holy Spirit, thou wast sent by Him unto the perishing Gentiles, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostle Jude; and having finished the struggles of thy divine passion and of diverse torments, thou didst commit thy soul unto Christ: Whom do thou beseech, O all-blessed one, to grant us great mercy![^531]

*Both now... "Save from afflictions..." Great Doxology. And Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, at the Beatitudes: of the Apostle, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^532]*
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*
*Epistle: pericope 77 (Jude 1:1–10).[^533]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord, and Thy truth in the congregation of the saints.[^534]*
*Gospel from John, pericope 48 (John 14:21–24).[^535]*
*Communion Hymn: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth...[^536]*

*Month of the Same, the 20th Day:*

*The Holy Hieromartyr Methodius, Bishop of Patara, who consumed the Origenist heresy.[^537]*

*Stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Having arrived for us as a saving mastery, O hierarch Methodius, we radiantly hymn thy memory in song, proclaiming thy radiant struggles and contests, whereby thou didst vanquish the crafty and wicked one, and didst weave a crown of victory, rejoicing![^538]

*Sticheron:*
With fair blossoms thou didst cause the fullness of the Church to flourish, O divinely blessed Methodius; and by the brightness of thy sufferings thou didst drive away the gloom of polytheism, and didst pass over unto the unsetting light, O hieromartyr; therefore we celebrate today thy most festal and light-bearing triumph, being illumined with piety!

*Sticheron:*
With blood thou didst dye thy sacred vesture, O God-bearing Methodius, with which thou didst enter rejoicing into the Holy of Holies, O blessed one, and thou beholdest radiantly the Trinitarian divine brightness, having been taught most clearly that which is beyond mind and understanding, and having been pre-eminently deified, O most wealthy hierarch![^539]

*Other Stichera to the Theotokos.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Rejoice, sun-like ray, throne of the unsetting Sun, who didst cause the unfathomable Sun to shine forth! Rejoice, lightning-like dawn shining with divine illuminations, who didst enlighten the ends of the earth, true gleaming of gold, all-good and all-immaculate one, who didst flash forth the unsetting light upon the faithful![^540]

*Sticheron:*
Wash away the defilement of my passionate heart, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God, and cleanse all its wounds and corruptions that come from sin, O pure one; and still the instability of my mind, that I, thy wretched and unprofitable servant, may magnify thy power and great protection!

*Sticheron:*
Turn the weakness and paralysis of my soul into strength and power, O Virgin Mother all-immaculate, with fear and love both to perform and to do the ordinances of Christ: that I may escape the unendurable fire, and through thee receive the heavenly inheritance and life unpassing, rejoicing ever!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Bowed down by demonic assaults and cast into the pit of destruction, have compassion on me, O Sovereign Lady, and establish me upon the rock of virtues; and destroying the snares of the enemy, account me worthy to perform the commandments of thy Son and our God, that I may obtain remission in the day of judgment!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His ribs pierced with a lance, the most pure one, weeping, cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst do for them? And art Thou hastening to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion![^541]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy blood, O wise one, mystically cries unto God from the earth like that of Abel, O divinely wise hierarch Methodius, who didst clearly proclaim the divine Incarnation; therefore thou didst put to shame the deceit of Origen, and didst pass over into the heavenly bridal chamber: pray unto Christ God that He save our souls![^542]

*Canon, the composition of Theophanes.*
*Tone 7. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*By Thy nod was the previously easily-pourable watery nature transformed into an earthly form, O Lord; therefore Israel, journeying on foot dryshod, sang a song of victory unto Thee Ode 1. Heirmos (concluded):*
*...previously easily-pourable watery nature, O Lord; therefore Israel, journeying on foot dryshod, sang a song of victory unto Thee![^543]*

*Troparion:*
Showing the swift pathways beckoning toward God unto those who pass through, teach me, O hierarch and sacred initiate: that I may obtain unending life by thy prayers, O glorious one!

*Troparion:*
For thou didst shine forth in a light-bearing manner unto the Church of Christ, beaming with twofold graces, O divinely revealed one; therefore with twofold crowns Christ sacredly crowned thee![^544]

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the crown of martyrdom and with the anointing of the priesthood, O blessed one, thou didst flourish with both; therefore thou hast received the true and God-beholding inheritance!

*Troparion:*
As a blameless sacrifice, and consecrated unto the Master, both living and ensouled, thou wast offered up, O glorious one; therefore thou wast received upon the heavenly altar, O Methodius![^545]

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear for us the Deliverer of mankind, the Liberator and Savior Christ, O Virgin pure Mother; therefore, clearly knowing thee to be the Mediatress of eternal life, we glorify thee!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord and Savior, Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens by Thy all-powerful Word, and all their host by the all-effective and Divine Spirit: establish me upon the immovable rock of Thy confession![^546]*

*Troparion:*
Seeing the blazing delusion of Origen, as an excellent shepherd thou didst swiftly consume all his darkness with the divine fire, flashing forth the ray of God's wisdom within Thee, O divinely received one![^547]

*Troparion:*
Having learned honorable conduct and the beauty of purity in character, O glorious one, by thy wisdom and the grace of thy words thou showest forth the beauty of all these, O divinely wise one, and their eternal brightness!

*Troparion:*
The lightning of thy words, the trumpet of understanding, and the auspicious utterance of thy doctrines went forth into all the earth, O venerable one, summoning all the faithful, O most blessed one, unto the communion of heavenly delights![^548]

*Troparion:*
Being filled with the stronger meat of thy spiritual draughts, O all-wise one, they take delight in that true food which abides, ever flowing without ceasing and increasing for its partakers!

*Theotokion:*
With divine Gabriel, O blessed Virgin, we ever cry "Rejoice!" unto thee: for thou wast the cause of joy and true gladness for us, having borne in the flesh the Deliverer and Savior of all![^549]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

A fair altar didst thou make thy heart, sacredly offering unbloody sacrifices unto God; and having suffered manfully and having been sacrificed, thou wast offered as a victim, O Methodius, unto Him Who was sacrificed for our sake: Whom do thou fervently beseech ever to save all who hymn thee with love![^550]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The multitude of my unclean thoughts and the surges of improper imaginations, who can declare, O all-immaculate one? And the assaults of my bodiless foes and their malice, who can tell? But by thy supplication, O good one, grant me deliverance from them all!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
She who in the last times bore Thee in the flesh Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried aloud: Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by the Angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O my Son? I hymn Thee, O Long-suffering One![^551]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Without leaving the Father's bosom and having descended unto earth, O Christ God: I heard the mystery of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, the only Lover of mankind![^552]*

*Troparion:*
Unto the height of virtues, having finished the course of martyrdom, thou didst run, receiving the bountiful reward of thy sacred ministry, O most blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Occupying a hierarch's throne and possessing a holy life, thou didst proclaim the Orthodox faith, O all-venerable one, and didst instruct thy flock![^553]

*Troparion:*
The soul-profiting sweetness of thy doctrines issues forth like a fountain, gladdening the partakers and sweetening the senses of our souls!

*Theotokion:*
Without departing from the Father's bosom and having become incarnate of the Virgin, O Christ God: preserve Thy flock which venerats the divine representations of Thy dispensation![^554]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Lord my God, rising early from the night unto Thee, I pray: Grant me remission of my transgressions, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my pathways, I beseech Thee![^555]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst clothe thyself with life-bearing mortification, O father, before thy death; and being struck with the sword of thy martyrdom, thou didst pass over unto the manifoldly multiplied, better life, O glorious one![^556]

*Troparion:*
O initiate of ineffable mysteries and partaker of the choir on high: save them that honor thee with love, O all-glorious one, destroying the onsets of grievous temptations by thy prayers!

*Troparion:*
Having boldness before the Master, O father, Whose sufferings thou didst zealously emulate: by thy struggles entreat divine peace and tranquility for the faithful, O all-wise one!

*Troparion:*
First thou didst sacredly minister the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world; and afterwards, being slain, thou didst offer thyself unto Him as a spiritual and living sacrifice, O Father Methodius![^557]

*Theotokion:*
O pure Birthgiver of God, being the receptacle of the indestructible Light and temple of the divine radiance: enlighten my darkened soul with thy light, I beseech thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Voyaging amid the tumult of the cares of life, sinking with the ship through sins, and cast before the soul-corrupting beast, as Jonah, O Christ, I cry aloud unto Thee: Lead me up from the death-bearing depths![^558]*

*Troparion:*
Slumber to thine eyelids and sleep to thine eyes thou didst not give, O most blessed one, until thou didst free thyself from all passions, and didst make thyself a spacious temple for the beam-shining lightning of the Spirit![^559]

*Troparion:*
Wholly united unto the divine and God-beholding radiance, O divinely wise one, thou wast a rule of the divine priesthood, and wast shown forth as an intercessor before God for the whole Church of the faithful, O Methodius!

*Troparion:*
Having joined the radiant revelation of beings unto a radiant life, O speaker of God, it showed Thee forth as a radiant luminary unto the world, driving away heretical mockery and the darkness of godlessness!

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee to be the Queen of all creatures, for thou didst bear God Who made all things out of nothing, O Mother of God: with the Angel Gabriel we cry a joyful song of praise unto thee!

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Thou wast a sacred initiate of the Holy Trinity, a preacher of the divine commands that surpass the mind, and an establishment for the Orthodox, O Methodius; for the sake of the right faith thou didst refute evil-minded notions, having been shown forth as a hieromartyr by thy blood: and standing before Christ with the Angels, pray that we be saved![^560]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Cast into the fiery furnace, the venerable Children transformed the fire into dew, crying aloud in song thus: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^561]*

*Troparion:*
Consecrated, O divinely wise one, and shining with the blood of martyrs of the truth, with them thou singest unceasingly: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Established in faith, thou didst extinguish the idolatrous flame by the streams of thy holy blood, O martyr, crying aloud thus: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^562]

*Troparion:*
Revealed by the supreme divine vision, thou didst all-wisely clarify thine understanding, crying aloud with them that faithfully sing, O father: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
With hymnody all creation glorifies thy childbearing, knowing Him indeed as God, and honorably worshiping Him it cries: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The only unoriginate King of glory, Whom the heavenly powers bless and the ranks of Angels tremble before: praise Him, ye priests; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^563]*

*Troparion:*
As seeing the invisible One, the divinely wise one endured the wounds of the torturers, and was blessed with a crown, singing: Praise Him, ye priests; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^564]

*Troparion:*
Radiantly desiring the unshakable Kingdom of Christ, O hierarch, which was procured for thee by the straight path, crying unto Christ: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ granted thee a manifoldly multiplied reward for thy labors, O God-bearer, surpassing every thought, as thou piously singest: Supremely exalt His Kingdom unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
We know thee to be an abyss of teachings, a tablet of instructions, a treasury of gracious contemplation, and a throne of the senses, as we piously sing: Supremely exalt Christ the King unto all ages![^565]

*Theotokion:*
As a servant, O all-immaculate one, I come unto thee in faith, who didst conceive the Master of all: save me now from persecutions and perils, as I piously hymn God Who became incarnate of thee, O Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*O Mother of God and Virgin, who gavest birth and yet remainest virgin: this is not a work of nature, but of divine condescension; therefore, as the only one accounted worthy of divine wonders, we unceasingly magnify thee![^566]*

*Troparion:*
Pray ever unto Christ, O divinely blessed father, to halt the crafty plots of heresies against us, and drive away from our midst the present gloom: for as a powerful hierarch thou hast been an intercessor!

*Troparion:*
From earth thou didst pass over unto the heavenly life, receiving the prize of thy ministry and suffering, where, having become a partaker of incorruptible life, thou wast accounted worthy to be with Christ forever![^567]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst find the fruit of the tree of life O most blessed one, having gained the Author of immortal life for men; richly enjoying Whose joy, pray thou for them that hymn thee in faith![^568]

*Troparion:*
Stretching forth thine entire mind through the goodness of life, O venerable one, thou wast accounted worthy of the vision of Christ and of fair gladness, as a hieromartyr, an expounder of piety, and its champion!

*Theotokion:*
We call thee the Ark of the New Law and the God-inscribed tablet, wherein the Word of God was written, having become man in order to save the world from deceit, O thou who art full of grace, all-hymned Birthgiver of God![^569]

*Month of the Same, the 21st Day:*

*The Holy Martyr Julian of Tarsus.[^570]*

*Stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Perceiving thee, O blessed martyr Julian, as a light-bearing sun illuminating the fullness of the world with noetic splendors and dispelling the night of godlessness: we celebrate thy light-bearing and divine memory, and we venerate the shrine of thy relics, drawing forth health for our souls![^571]

*Sticheron:*
Beaten with stripes, grievously torn, shut up in prison, O blessed one, afflicted as thou wast dragged from place to place, enclosed with wild beasts, brought near unto the abyss and drowned in the sea, and receiving a blessed end: thou didst not deny the Master of masters, O glory of martyrs, most wealthy one![^572]

*Sticheron:*
When a venerable woman, guided by the Spirit, beheld thee being drawn forth from the bosom of the sea, she faithfully received thee, O Martyr Julian, and gave over unto burial thy blameless and much-suffering body, which had conquered the devil's tyranny by the operation of grace![^573]

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Having drawn forth the inexhaustible drink of blameless faith, O blessed one, thou didst extinguish the service of idols, and victoriously didst accomplish thy contest; being enlightened by the dew as an unsetting star, illumined by the ever-shining Sun, Christ; and sending forth upon each city the rays of thy martyrdom, a blessed end by the sea thou didst receive, and as a crown-bearer thou didst outrun [all] before the face of Christ; Whom entreat, O most glorious Julian, that they who accomplish thy memory in faith may be saved![^574]

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the unblemished Ewe-lamb of old, the all-pure Lady, beheld her Lamb upon the Tree of the Cross, maternally crying aloud and marveling, she cried: O sweetest Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How hath the ungrateful assembly delivered Thee unto Pilate's tribunal, and condemns unto death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word![^575]

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 6:*
Come, ye lovers of the martyrs, let us piously glorify Christ's passion-bearer, who was shown forth as a fellow citizen with Paul, an inmate with him, and who finished the course together with him, the glorious Julian; for entering into the sack with the beasts of the water, he shattered the serpent's head; and having sanctified the earth with his blood, and the sea by his traversing, he passed over from the things here unto the eternal mansions, and received from the Almighty hand the prize of his struggles: he asks cleansing for our souls, and great mercy![^576]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Standing of old by the Tree at the time of the Crucifixion, the Virgin, with the virgin disciple, weeping cried aloud: Woe is me! How dost Thou suffer, O Christ, Who art the impassibility of all?[^577]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord, Julian, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for possessing Thy strength, he cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: by his supplications, save our souls![^578]

*Canon, the work of John the Monk. Whose Acrostic is: "I crown the martyr Julian with songs." Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Unto that God, Who led His people through the Red Sea, as the only One, let us sing, for He hath been gloriously glorified![^579]*

*Troparion:*
With sacred songs let thy glorious sufferer and thy most honorable memory be praised: for with unapproachable glory hath he been glorified!

*Troparion:*
By stumbling thou didst not fall, striving as a martyr, O soldier of Christ, in the weakness of the flesh even unto blood more than the radiance of gold, thou wast adorned and didst shine forth with martyr's wounds, and with precious stone, O holy one![^580]

*Theotokion:*
O pure Birthgiver of God, who above nature gavest birth unto the incarnate, ever-existing, and pre-eternal Word: we hymn thee!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the establishment of them that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee![^581]*

*Troparion:*
It was not commanded me to serve stones, the works of hands, as God: Julian answered the mind-damaged judge!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand before the tribunal of the tyrant, O martyr Julian, standing before Christ as the Judge of the living and the dead!

*Troparion:*
I was not senseless in confessing God, said Julian, Who is one in Trinity, indivisibly hymned!

*Theotokion:*
Grant us help through thy prayers, O all-pure one, driving away the assaults of grievous tribulations!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
By the drowning of the waters thou didst receive a blessed end, O wise one, having suffocated the multifaceted serpent in them, thou didst take the victory; therefore celebrating with love thy praiseworthy memory, we pray: entreat Christ for us, we beseech thee![^582]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Guide us into the path of repentance, who have ever turned aside unto paths of evil, and have provoked the most good Lord to anger, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of despairing men, dwelling-place of God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee dead, stretched out upon the Cross, O Christ, Thine all-unblemished Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thine all-pure hands, O Tender-hearted One?[^583]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead![^584]*

*Troparion:*
Julian surrendered his body unto manifold wounds, yet felt not the pains, for he was preserved by Christ's love!

*Troparion:*
Desiring the heavenly robe, O most glorious Julian, thou didst leave the earthly one unto the persecutors, as a pleasing servant of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Not bodily strength, O most wealthy one, but unconquerable longing for Christ's passions made Thee a vanquisher of demonic deceit!

*Theotokion:*

...Grant cleansing unto our negligences, as Thou art without sin, and pacify Thy world, O God, through the prayers of her that bare Thee![^585]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Rising early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord, save us: for Thou art our God, and beside Thee we know no other![^586]*

*Troparion:*
Conforming thyself in all things unto the Master, O all-lauded one, thou didst stand before the tribunal of the lawless, being condemned!

*Troparion:*
Having striven lawfully, thou wast slaughtered for the Master like an innocent lamb, by the hands of the lawless, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Neither earthly lineage nor rank didst thou esteem higher than thy Christ-bearing name, O glorious martyr!

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, unwedded Birthgiver of God, make vain the hope of enemies, and make glad them that hymn thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a radiant robe, O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O plenteous in mercy, Christ our God![^587]*

*Troparion:*
Thine illustrious servant was not ashamed of Thee nailed unto the Tree, O Christ, but accounted it a treasure of glory!

*Troparion:*
The defilements of deceit were silenced, and by God-proclaiming tongues the divine mysteries of Christ's martyrs were adorned!

*Troparion:*
Ask healing of all passions and remission of transgressions, O glorious sufferer of Christ, for them that hymn Thee!

*Theotokion:*
Offer our supplication unto thy Son, O all-pure one: for thou art the praise and protection of our race!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayer" (Въ мл҃твахъ):*
An unconquerable soldier of piety, and a harmonizer and armor-bearer of truth, let us all worthily praise Julian today, and cry out unto him: Pray unto Christ God for us all![^588]

*Ikos:*
In the path of the Lord didst thou walk from infancy, O thrice-blessed one, and didst piously desire His testimonies; and having taken the Cross upon thy shoulder with zeal, O God-wise Julian, thou didst destroy the cruel deceit of idol-frenzy, shattering the temples of the enemy, and their snares and flatteries: therefore with faith we run unto thy protection, crying aloud, O most wealthy one: Pray unto Christ God for us all![^589]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled down the flame, and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^590]*

Sweet-smelling myrrh drops forth from thy praiseworthy and glorious memory, O soldier of Christ, unto them that unceasingly sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^591]

*Troparion:*
Ineffable glory crowns them that are gathered unto thy praise, O soldier of Christ, who unceasingly sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Not obeying the word of the torturer, thou didst not serve creation, O unconquerable one; but unto the one living God didst thou cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst loose Eve's curse, having taken up Thy dwelling in the all-unblemished Virgin, O Spring of blessing, who didst bud forth unto them that cry: Blessed is the fruit of thy womb, O Lady![^592]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*When the musical instruments sounded in harmony, and innumerable people bowed down unto the image in Dura, the three children, not obeying, hymned and glorified the Lord unto all ages![^593]*

*Troparion:*
The deceiver indeed flattered thee, and summoned thee by torments unto deceit, O Julian; but thou didst cry aloud: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
More mighty like a lion after torments, casting down the torturer, O Julian, thou didst cry out joyfully: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The fury of the fire shall not separate me, nor shall the cutting of the sword tear me away from God the Creator! He cried, exclaiming: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^594]

*Theotokion:*
As her that bare the Lord of all, and who remained a virgin after childbearing, O Mary, the faithful worthily hymn: Rejoice! we cry unto thee, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Let us people gloriously honor the pure Birthgiver of God, who received the fire of the Godhead in her womb without being consumed: let us magnify her with songs![^595]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cast down the haughty eye, O soldier of God, struggling manfully in the flesh against the bodiless deceiver, and ever magnifying Christ!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst escape the wrath of the serpent, the author of evil, being cast into the depths of the sea, O blessed and wise one; therefore do we magnify thee with songs!

*Troparion:*
As thou didst strive well through sufferings for the Master's sake, so wast thou fairly crowned by the right hand of the Ruler; therefore do we worthily call thee blessed!

*Theotokion:*
Moses beheld thee, O pure one, in the bush upon the mountain, receiving the unendurable fire of the Godhead without being consumed; therefore do we all magnify thee![^596]

*Exapostilarion:*
Like a light-bearing sun with marvelous rays, thou dost guide all creation, O most glorious passion-bearer; therefore, as we accomplish thy memory, we ask to be delivered from all perils![^597]

*Theotokion:*
Establish Thine inheritance in warfare, O Word, granting victories unto the rulers over barbarians, through the supplications of the Birthgiver of God, whom Thou hast granted as a protectress unto Christians!

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*In the saints that are in His earth [Ps. 15:3 LXX].*
*Verse: I beheld the Lord ever before me [Ps. 15:8 LXX].*
*Epistle to the Ephesians, pericope 233 [Eph. 6:10–17].[^598]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [Ps. 33:18 LXX].*
*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous [Ps. 33:20 LXX].*
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 106 [Luke 21:12–19].[^599]*
*Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be [Ps. 111:6 LXX].[^600]*

*Month of the Same, the 22nd Day:*

*The Holy Hieromartyr Eusebius, Bishop of Samosata.[^601]*

*Stichera, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*
What shall we name thee, O glorious one? A true hierarch and a sacred teacher, the confirmation of the Orthodox and the Tserkovne Oko, a luminary who shone forth noetic light, a fellow of the glorious martyrs, a champion of the truth, a great refuter of falsehood: pray that our souls may be saved![^602]

*Sticheron:*
What shall we call thee, O hierarch? A river flowing forth from noetic Eden, watering the earth with spiritual dew, a cup full of divine water, submerging the like-minded partisans of Arius, a pillar of fire leading the new people by divine grace: pray that our souls may be saved![^603]

*Sticheron:*
What shall I call thee, O Eusebius? A dispenser of piety and a destroyer of impiety, the beauty of passion-bearers and the rejoicing of priests, a sickle cutting down tares and gathering heavenly wheat, a fountain of miracles flowing forth, taking away the burning heat of diseases: pray that our souls may be saved![^604]

*Other Stichera of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God, in the Same Tone and Podoben:*
A strange mystery, O pure one, is that of thy seedless childbearing, marvelous and dread, surpassing by its greatness every human mind; for Him Whom the many-eyed [Cherubim] endure not to behold endure not to behold, and all the ranks of holy angels tremble at, thou didst bear in the flesh for us, yet not departing from the Father's essence: we glorify thee who gavest Him birth, O Bride of God![^605]

*Sticheron:*
Unto whom hast thou become likened, O wretched soul, not rising up at all unto repentance, and fearing not the fire that awaits the wicked? Arise, and call upon her who alone is speedy unto protection, and cry aloud: O Virgin Mother, pray unto thy Son and our God, to deliver me from the snares of the crafty one![^606]

*Sticheron:*
O protection of all, Lady, as I am held fast by an abyss of darkness and ever submerged by the grievous needs of life: send down a drop of thy mercy, and give me the hand of thy help, and account me worthy of the portion of the elect and venerable ones, as thou didst bear God, the Lover of mankind![^607]

*Glory... Both now... in the Same Tone and Podoben:*
Having given birth unto the unendurable Fire that burns up sins and bedews the faithful: burn up the material [wood] of my transgressions by thy prayers, O all-unblemished one, and refresh my soul, [inflamed] with passions, with dew, that I may [praise] thy mercy and power![^608]

*Stavrotheotokion, in the Same Podoben:*
When the Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb stretched out voluntarily upon the Tree of the Cross, maternally crying aloud, she wept in anguish: O my Son, what is this strange sight? Thou Who as Lord givest life unto all, how dost Thou die, O Long-suffering One, granting resurrection unto the earthborn? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God![^609]

*Canon, whose Acrostic is: "I honor the namesake of piety." The work of Joseph. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*That which was cut severed the uncut [sea], and the sun beheld the earth which it had not seen; the water drowned the cruel enemy, and Israel passed through the impassable: and a song was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He hath been gloriously glorified![^610]*

*Troparion:*
Illumined with perfect light, and ranked with the sacred choirs, O blessed one, and adorned with the martyrs' goodly glory: preserve them that celebrate thy memory in faith, O blessed Eusebius, making Christ merciful [unto us]!

*Troparion:*
Sweetly enkindled from youth with longing for Christ, thou didst subject the flesh unto the soul with sacred powers, O all-blessed one, and wast shown forth as a sacred vessel; in holiness didst thou shepherd the people, O venerable one, having been anointed with the myrrh of grace The Holy Spirit sets thee forth as a chief shepherd, destroying the gloom of heretics with thy light-bearing words, O wise one, and clearly showing the right path unto them that make haste to enter therein, O great Eusebius, luminary of our hearts![^611]

*Theotokion:*
O blessed one who gavest birth unto God, bless them that now bless thee, O all-pure one, and guide [us] unto good entrances; and fill [us] with divine good things, strengthening [us] to sing: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He hath been gloriously glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Plant Thy fear, O Lord, in the hearts of Thy servants, and be Thou our establishment, who in truth call upon Thee![^612]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast meek and gentle, humble-minded and calm, and filled with divine love; at thine end thou wast adorned with the crowns of martyrdom!

*Troparion:*
Shining in garments of blood, O sacred Eusebius, thou didst ascend into the holy places, radiantly crowned with the glory of the saints!

*Troparion:*
Rebuking the like-minded partisans of Arius, O blessed one, thou didst endure persecutions and a violent death; therefore wast thou accounted worthy of immortal glory![^613]

*Theotokion:*
By the Father's will didst thou bear the Word through the overshadowing of the Divine Spirit, O all-pure Virgin Mother, the glory of hierarchs; therefore do we hymn thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Having made bright thy life with the beauties of virtues, thou wast named a shepherd and an honorable hierarch, O most blessed Eusebius; and having adorned thyself with the blood of martyrdom, thou didst pass over on high unto the immaterial choirs; with them, O father, entreat Christ to have mercy on our souls![^614]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure, all-unblemished, and unwedded one, who alone in time gavest birth unto the timeless Son and Word of God: entreat Him together with the holy and honorable patriarchs, martyrs, prophets, and venerable ones, to grant us cleansing and great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, maternally lamenting she said: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, willing as Compassionate One to give life unto the dead?[^615]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*From the overshadowing mountain, the Prophet foresaw Thee, the Word, intending to become incarnate of the only Birthgiver of God* intending to become incarnate of the only Birthgiver of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power![^616]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst halt the putrefaction of deceit, O wise one, by the salt of thine excellent teachings; and them that were sick with the heresy of evil faith thou didst heal, O blessed Eusebius!

*Troparion:*
Having directed the people unto the haven of salvation, thou didst submerge Arius's unbelief by the flooding of thy blood, O shepherd and martyr, adornment of hierarchs!

*Troparion:*
A bright star didst thou shine forth in the world with sacred radiance, enlightening the thoughts of the Orthodox, O all-blessed one; therefore do we faithfully call thee blessed!

*Theotokion:*
With pure lips, tongue, and heart, I confess thee as the Mother of the Creator, and I pray thee: enlighten my soul, darkened by transgressions!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who hast enlightened the ends [of the world] from the night of ignorance by the knowledge of God: enlighten me with the morning of Thy love for mankind, O Lord![^617]*

*Troparion:*
Confirming the mind of the Orthodox by thy teachings, O blessed one, thou didst endure banishment for the sake of piety![^618]

*Troparion:*
We know thee as a pillar of Orthodoxy, a fountain of healings, O all-wise hierarch, and the confirmation of the Church!

*Troparion:*
Confirming the lawless by divine ordinances, O Eusebius, thou didst endure an unjust death at the hand of the lawless![^619]

*Theotokion:*
A radiant candlestick, O Birthgiver of God, bearing the Divine Candle that enlightens all the ends [of the earth], wast thou known to be!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my griefs; for my soul is full of evils, and my life has drawn nigh unto hades; and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^620]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst proclaim the consubstantial Word and Son, co-unoriginate with the Father; thou didst destroy the worldly mindset of the abominable frenzy of Arius, O Eusebius, unshakeable and firm bulwark of the pious!

*Troparion:*
By the sprinkling of thy precious blood, the Church dyed a purple robe for herself; thou didst enter with gladness, O father, into the heavenly Kingdom, to stand before the King of the ages, O all-blessed Eusebius!

*Troparion:*
Like a rose, like a sweet-smelling lily, like the paradise of God wast thou shown forth, having in the midst the Tree of Life, the Creator of all and Lord, O Eusebius: now thou fillest us with divine sweet fragrance!

...of the Holy Spirit.[^621]

*Theotokion:*
Perceiving the immeasurable depth of thy mystery, the God-proclaiming prophets clearly foreannounced this, O Maiden, by riddles and divine prophecies; for above speech didst thou in the flesh give birth unto the Word of the Father, O pure one!

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Having piously lived in hierarchal ministry and completed the course of martyrdom, thou didst extinguish idol sacrifices, O hierarch Eusebius; but as thou hast boldness toward Christ God, pray that our souls may be saved![^622]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled down the flame, and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^623]*

*Troparion:*
Occupying thyself in songs and supplications, O hierarch, thou didst put passions to sleep, and wast shown forth unto us the faithful as a watchful guardian, who piously hymn thee and celebrate thy memory!

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as a martyr and a hierarch, thou didst divinely inherit heavenly things, and standing before the throne of the Creator, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shine forth as the morning, proclaiming Christ Who shone forth from the Father before the morning star, O luminary of the Church, adornment of hierarchs, beauty of sufferers![^624]

*Theotokion:*
Thou renewest the laws of nature most gloriously by thy childbearing, O Virgin, and cleansest us who were corrupted by evil transgression; therefore as is meet do we praise thee!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The God-proclaiming children in the furnace, trampling upon the flame with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^625]*

*Troparion:*
Together with the hierarchs and martyrs, entreat the Savior of all, as a hierarch and a martyr, O sacred proclaimer Eusebius, that we may find mercy!

*Troparion:*
Having lived with a goodly disposition, thou didst shepherd thy sacred flock with the staff of the word, and didst drive away heretical beasts, O all-blessed one!

*Troparion:*
All them that were weighed down with the drunkenness of deceit, the heretics, thou didst rebuke, O Eusebius; and by them wast thou slain, having suffered firmly![^626]

*Theotokion:*
As higher than the heavenly powers, thou didst bear the most high Word, Who lifted up from the earth the fallen nature of mankind, O all-hymned one!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Truly we confess thee to be the Mother of God, who are saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs![^627]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast accounted worthy, O father, to behold things far off, supernatural things for thy sacred soul; and rejoicing, thou didst take up thy dwelling in the never-setting radiance!

*Troparion:*
Fair didst thou show thyself through divine speech and good deeds, O Eusebius, and through most honorable sufferings, O blessed one, [shining] with splendors!

*Troparion:*
Let us form a joyful choir, praising the sacred Eusebius, who is adorned with the beauty of piety!

*Troparion:*
Thy shrine gives forth the grace of healings, and thy holy memory, O Eusebius, enlightens the world like a great sun!

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin lover of good, who gavest birth in the flesh unto the most good God: make good my soul, mistreated by sins!

*Month of the Same, the 23rd Day:*

*The Holy Martyr Agrippina.[^628]*

*Stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Rome offers thee as a flower-bearing, sweet-smelling rose, O much-suffering Agrippina, making fragrant the thought of the faithful with virtuous odors, and ever driving away the foul stench of passions by grace, O adornment of martyrs, confirmation of the Church, praise of virgins, and abyss of miracles![^629]

*Sticheron:*
As a precious treasure, Christ our God gives thee unto Sicily, who didst suffer in Rome; where, having arrived, O glorious martyr, thou drivest away the crafty multitude of demons by thy protection; therefore do we call thee blessed, and celebrate today thy holy suffering, O much-suffering Agrippina![^630]

*Sticheron:*
Bassa and Paula bore thee upon their shoulders by the command of Him Who upholds all things, traversing diverse places and the breadth of the deep, O martyr Agrippina, working dread miracles by divine grace: and thou didst come to rest in the place where God ordained, having become rest for the weary, O all-lauded one![^631]

*Other Stichera of the Birthgiver of God, in the Same Tone and Podoben:*

*When thou singest Alleluia, speak these stichera first:[^632]*

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice, sun-like ray, throne of the unsetting Sun, who didst dawn forth the inscrutable Sun; rejoice, illuminate the mind with divine splendors, O lightning flash that hast enlightened the ends of the earth, true radiance of gold, all-good and all-immaculate one, who hast shone forth the unwaning light upon the faithful!

*Sticheron:*
Wash away the filth of my passionate heart, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God, and cleanse all the wounds and sores thereof which are from sin, O pure one, and stay the instability of my mind, that I, wretched and unprofitable servant of thine, may magnify thy power and great protection!

*Sticheron:*
The weakness and paralysis of my soul, as all-immaculate Mother, turn into strength and might, through fear and love to keep and perform the statutes of Christ, that I may escape the unendurable fire, and through thee inherit the heavenly inheritance and life that passes not away, ever boasting in thee!

*Glory, and now, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Bowed down by demonic assaults and cast into the pit of destruction, have compassion on me, O Sovereign Lady, and establish me upon the rock of virtues, and having destroyed the snares of the enemy, vouchsafe me to do the commandments of thy Son and our God, that I may obtain remission in the day of judgment!

*Staurotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Beholding Thee, Christ the Lover of mankind, lifted upon the cross, and Thy side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one wept and cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for all the good things that Thou didst unto them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Agrippina, O Jesus, cries aloud with a great voice: Thee, O my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering, and am crucified and buried with Thy baptism; and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; but as a blameless sacrifice, accept me who with love have sacrificed myself unto Thee.[^633] By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*The Canon, whose acrostic is: "I praise Christ's bride Agrippina." Tone 4.[^634]*
*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*
*I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen and Mother, and I will appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I will sing her wonders.[^635]*

*Troparion:*
By the radiance of grace that shone upon thee from on high, drive away the darkness of my ignorance, and grant me grace by thy prayers, O Agrippina, to hymn thy wonders, O martyr!

*Troparion:*
Brighter than the sun in truth didst thou shine forth upon the firmament of the Church of Christ, and with the rays of thy contest and thy miracles, O martyr, thou didst enlighten the ends of the world!

*Troparion:*
Desiring the immortal Bridegroom, the Giver of life, thou didst offer suffering unto Him as a gift; and thou didst receive the heavenly kingdom and an incorruptible crown, O glorious martyr!

*Troparion:*
Governed by the hand of thy Master, thou didst cross the unstable sea of impiety, O honorable one, and didst hasten to the haven of high splendor, O God-blessed Agrippina!

*Theotokion:*
The Daystar has shone forth in the hearts of them that with faith honor thee as the Mother of God, O all-immaculate one; and the light-bearing day, even Christ Who shone forth from thy womb, O all-hymned one, has illuminated them!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty has grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with power; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.[^636]*

*Troparion:*
Being beaten with rods, thou didst rejoice, crushing thereby the bones of impiety and crying aloud: Nothing shall separate me from Thy love, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Having exposed the nakedness of the enemies, O martyr, thou didst strip thyself of garments; therefore did Christ give thee the vesture of incorruption!

*Troparion:*
The mouths that spoke lawlessly against thee were lawfully shut, O martyr of Christ, as thou didst proclaim the majesty of the Savior!

*Troparion:*
Having stretched out thy body upon the earth, the minister of impiety raged with fury; but thou didst have thy mind stretched out toward the Master!

*Theotokion:*
The voice of Isaiah was piously fulfilled: for a Virgin gave birth to the incarnate Giver of life and Savior of our souls!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Go thou before" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*
They that served stones through much delusion weighed thee down with stones as thou didst confess Christ, the unconquerable Rock; therefore having finished thy course, O glorious Agrippina, thou hast shone forth a ray of most marvelous wonders, enlightening the souls of us who praise thee![^637]

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
O only one who gavest birth to the Creator of all, only one who didst adorn humanity by thy childbearing, O Bride of God: deliver me from the snare of the deceitful enemy, and establish me upon the rock of the desires of Christ, earnestly entreating Him Whom thou didst incarnate!

*Staurotheotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the cross, O Word of God, she wept maternally and spoke: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste death, desiring to bring the dead to life, as Thou art compassionate?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine came upon a light cloud, by His incorruptible palm, and saved them that cry: Glory, O Christ, to Thy power![^638]*

*Troparion:*
Performing the will of them that fear Him, by an angel the Master looses thee from bonds and distress, O martyr, who didst break all impiety by the bond of the love of Christ, thy Bridegroom!

*Troparion:*
Having been put to death for the sake of the love of Him Who put to death all the power of death by death, thou dost pour forth ever-living grace, healing them that are slain by passions, O Agrippina, adornment of Christ's martyrs!

*Troparion:*
Having surpassed all carnal love through the love of thy Bridegroom, thou didst endure firmly when painfully beaten, crying aloud: The threat of torments shall not separate me from Thy love, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Who shall declare the courage of Bassa and Agrippina, pure in mind, who established a blessed union through threefold desire? By which they mightily showed the power of idols to be weak!

*Theotokion:*
The Virgin Queen, adorned with a golden garment, now stands before the King, her Son, surpassing the angels beyond comparison, crying: Glory, O Christ, to Thy power!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Thou, O my Lord, didst come as a light into the world, a holy light, turning back from gloomy ignorance them that hymn Thee with faith.[^639]*

*Troparion:*
The unsetting Light, having taken up His dwelling in thy body, showed thee unto them that beheld thee in the night as in the day, emitting a ray of miracles!

*Troparion:*
An undefiled lamb, following the footsteps of the Shepherd, as an immaculate sacrifice was she divinely offered unto Him!

*Troparion:*
Having a radiant mind filled with prophecy, thou, O glorious Bassa, wast accounted worthy to foretell future things as though present!

*Theotokion:*

...O Mother of God, heal the affliction of my soul, who didst bear the most good Word Who blesses all!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.[^640]*

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth from Rome like an unsetting sun, Sicily, having received the body of the holy martyr, is delivered from the fierce darkness of demons!

*Troparion:*
Courageously the thrice-rich holy women martyrs, who were dead before death in the contest of martyrdom, bore thy dead, life-bearing body![^641]

*Troparion:*
The weapon of the enemy grew weak against thee, for like a city having no foundation didst thou cast down his pride, O Agrippina, by the toil of thy glorious contests!

*Troparion:*
By the touch of thy body the woman with an issue of blood is healed, and lepers are cleansed as they run with faith; and every other sickness flees away at the invocation of thy holy name, O martyr!

*Theotokion:*
Having now received the words of Gabriel, O all-immaculate Virgin, we cry aloud: Rejoice, only blessed Mother! Rejoice, gate that hast the Sun of righteousness!

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
The light-bearing day of thy radiant contests has arrived, on which the divine Church, honoring them, summons all joyfully to cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O virgin and martyr Agrippina, all-honorable![^642]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously ensnared: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers![^643]*

*Troparion:*
Like a golden dove protected by the weapon of the Cross, thou didst destroy the Hagarenes who drew near by night unto thy city, saving the faithful from their foul murder, O martyr![^644]

*Troparion:*
Having endured manifold afflictions for the sake of Christ, O martyr, thou didst find in Him painless and immortal delight, crying: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The marvelous priest, shining with the working of miracles, beholding thy wonders, O Agrippina, magnified God and rejoicing sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
With steadfast purpose bearing thy steadfast body unto sufferings, thou didst in no wise perceive womanly weakness, crying: virgin unto the Master: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto God, thou dost remain a virgin after childbearing, and dost maternally nourish with milk the Nourisher of the world, as is meet, O all-immaculate one; unto Whom singing we cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The pious children in the furnace the childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved; then indeed prefigured, but now in actuality, it moves the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^645]*

*Troparion:*
Night appeared as day unto them that bore thy relics, O martyr; every place that received thee became fragrant; the demonic host was greatly driven away; drops of healings are poured forth upon them that exalt Christ unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Desiring the immortal Bridegroom, thou didst run after His sweet odors, imitating His passions and death, O glorious martyr, and crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Possessing unhesitating faith, having trimmed thy lamp with the oil of thy blood, O martyr, thou didst enter within the bridal chamber, singing the heavenly joys: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages!

*Troparion:*
Desiring to behold the beauty of the Lord and to visit His temple, having mounted as upon a chariot of thine own blood, thou didst attain unto this: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
The bush which of old prefigured the strange mystery of thy childbearing remained unconsumed, O all-immaculate one; for thou didst give birth to the fire of the Godhead, and didst remain unburnt, O Maiden; therefore do we hymn thee unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*A stone cut without hands from the unquarried mountain, even thee, O Virgin, was cut off as the cornerstone, even Christ Who joined together the parted natures; therefore rejoicing do we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God![^646]*

*Troparion:*
By the nodding of Him Who creates all things, Agathonica, together with Bassa and Paula, earnestly hastening to the end of martyrdom, as truly befriended, found the summit of desire![^647]

*Troparion:*
Having taken up thy dwelling in the heavenly bridal chambers, O virgin, and contemplating unseen beauties, receiving divine splendor, make things bright for them that hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
A crown of graces was placed upon thy head, O Agrippina, for thou didst finish thy course and keep the faith unstolen; and the ranks of the righteous beautifully received thee!

*Troparion:*
As thou standest before the Creator and Master with boldness, O martyr Agrippina, together with all the saints from of old, ask forgiveness of transgressions for them that hymn thee, O good virgin!

*Theotokion:*
Carrying in thy hands Him Who upholds all things, maternally as is meet, O Virgin Mary: deliver me from the hands of the combatant, that, being saved, I may hymn thy majesty!

*Month of the Same, the 24th Day: The Nativity of the Honorable and Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John:*

*At Little Vespers, stichera on 4, Tone 4:*[^648]
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Thou wast born of a barren womb, having loosed the barrenness of our hearts, and granting the word of fruitful children in faith and truth unto barren souls, O glorious forerunner of the coming of Christ; the well-signifying voice of the Word, the loud-voiced foreteller of repentance, the divine mediator of the Old and New, manifestly recognized; whose nativity we celebrate with love, rejoicing![^649] *(Twice)*

*Sticheron:*
Now the fatted calf of self-control is born of the barren woman, showing unto us the Lamb born of the Virgin Maiden, Who takes away the sins of the world; the desert-loving turtledove has manifestly shone forth, announcing the divine spring, whereby the fierce winter of godlessness has ceased; and the close friend of the Bridegroom, John, has come radiantly, praying that our souls may be saved!

*Sticheron:*
Thou who wast born according to the divine promise: when the most marvelous and great Archangel in the temple announced thy birth unto thy father as he prayed, then unto the priest who did not believe and contradicted, as it is written, Gabriel brought speechless silence and deafness until the birth; but thou, O forerunner, having been born of thy father, didst loose the bond of his tongue, O blessed one, and dost pray for us all![^650]

*Glory, Tone 1. Idiomelon:*

Sweet fragrance is fitting for John; the beauty of songs is fitting for the Baptist; for he proclaimed the beginning of our salvation, leaping in the womb, and crying aloud in the wilderness: Repent!—the warrior of the King, the forerunner of grace, heralding the Lamb and entreating the Savior for our souls![^651]

*Other Theotokion: Receive into thy hands:*

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Make thy tongue clear and bright, O Zechariah, crying aloud: The child will be the Prophet of God and the forerunner of the divine Word![^652]
