*Verse: Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, for He hath visited and wrought redemption for His people.[^653]*

Thou wast shown forth from thy mother's womb, O John, sanctified unto God; for there arose not a greater among men, nor any above thee anywhere![^654]

*Verse: And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest.[^655]*

Thou didst loose, O most blessed father, his speechlessness, as well as the bonds of barrenness of her that bore thee, having been born of divine grace!

*Glory, and now, Tone 8:*
Behold Elizabeth saying unto the Virgin Mary: Why hast thou come to me, the mother of my Lord? Thou bearest the King, and I a warrior; thou the Lawgiver, and I the law-decreer; thou the Word, and I the voice proclaiming the Kingdom of heaven![^656]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O prophet and forerunner of the coming of Christ, we who honor thee with love are at a loss to praise thee worthily; for the barrenness of her that bore thee and the speechlessness of thy father were loosed by thy glorious and honorable nativity, and the incarnation of the Son of God is proclaimed unto the world![^657]

*Glory, and now, Theotokion: The mystery hidden from the ages:*

*At Great Vespers, after the customary psalm we sing: Blessed is the man, 1st antiphon.*

*On "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 8, Tone 4. Idiomela of John the Monk:*
John being born looses the silence of Zechariah; for it was not fitting that the father should keep silence when the voice had come; but as the tongue was bound when he did not believe at first, so when he appeared he gave freedom to the father; unto whom it was announced, and there was born the voice of the Word and the forerunner of the Light, praying for our souls![^658]

*Sticheron:*
Today the voice of the Word looses the paternal voice held bound for unbelief, and reveals fruitful children unto the Church, loosing the maternal bonds of barrenness; the lamp of the Light goes before the ray of the Sun of righteousness announces His coming, unto the renewal of all and the salvation of our souls!

*By Anatolius:*
When the Word of God was about to be born of the Virgin, an angel came forth from aged loins, great among them that are born of women, and higher than the prophets; for it was fitting that the beginnings of divine things should be most glorious: offspring beyond normal age, and conception without seed. O Thou Who workest wonders for our salvation, glory to Thee![^659]

*By Andrew of Crete:*
Today the great forerunner has appeared, coming forth from the barren womb of Elizabeth, a prophet greater than all prophets, and there is no other, nor has any arisen; for the most radiant light followed the lamp of the forerunner, and the Word followed the voice, and the Bridegroom the friend of the bridegroom, preparing an elect people for the Lord, and pre-cleansing them with water; the branch of Zechariah and goodly child of the wilderness, the preacher of repentance, the cleansing of transgressions, announcing the resurrection from the dead unto them that are in hades, and praying for our souls![^660]

*Sticheron:*
Thou wast shown forth as a prophet and forerunner from the womb, O John, baptizer of Christ, leaping and rejoicing in the womb of thy mother, beholding the Queen come unto the servant, bearing the Ageless One, Who is from the Father without a mother, unto thee who didst shine forth according to the promise from a barren woman and an old man; entreat Him to have mercy on our souls!

*Sticheron:*
O most marvelous wonder! He that believed not the word of the angel saying that Elizabeth would conceive and bear a son, saying: How shall she bear, since I am grown old and her members are deadened?—he who was condemned to silence for his unbelief, today sees the promised one being born, and is loosed from silence, and enters into joy, crying: Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, for He hath visited and wrought redemption for His people, granting great mercy unto the world![^661]

*Sticheron:*
O all-praised John, universal apostle, announcement of Gabriel, and offspring of the barren woman, goodly child of the wilderness, and sincere friend of the Bridegroom Christ: entreat Him that our souls may find mercy!

*Glory, Tone 6. By Byzantios:*
Today the lamp of the Light prepares the way for the coming of God the Word, as a bright star; today the tongue of Zechariah was made clear, having kept silence as the angel commanded; for thus it was fitting for the father not to keep the voice in silence, once it had come forth having come forth from the barren womb, and announcing the redemption of the whole world with great boldness!

*Now, the same Tone:*
Elizabeth conceived the forerunner of grace, and the Virgin the Lord of glory; both mothers embraced, and the infant leaped; for within, the servant praised the Master; and the mother of the forerunner, marveling, began to cry aloud: Whence is this to me, that the mother of my Lord should come unto me, that He Who has great mercy might save a despairing people?[^662]

*The Entrance. The Prokeimenon of the day.*

*The Reading from Genesis (17:15–19; 18:11–14; 21:1–8):[^663]*
God said unto Abraham: As for Sarai thy wife, her name shall not be called Sarai, but Sarah shall her name be. And I will bless her, and give thee a son also of her; yea, I will bless her, and she shall become nations; kings of peoples shall be of her. Then Abraham fell upon his face, and said in his heart: Shall a son be born unto him that is a hundred years old? And shall Sarah, that is ninety years old, bear? And God said unto Abraham: Yea, Sarah thy wife shall bear thee a son indeed; and thou shalt call his name Isaac: and I will establish My covenant with him for an everlasting covenant. Now Abraham and Sarah were old and well stricken in age. And Sarah laughed within herself, saying: It has not happened unto me even until now, and my lord is old. And the Lord God said unto Abraham: Why did Sarah laugh within herself, saying: Shall I truly bear, who am old? Is any word impossible with God? And the Lord visited Sarah as He had said. And she conceived, and bore unto Abraham a son in his old age, at the set time of which the Lord God had spoken to him. And Abraham circumcised his son Isaac the eighth day, as the Lord God had commanded him. And Abraham was a hundred years old, when his son Isaac was born unto him. And Sarah said: The Lord God has made laughter for me; for whosoever hears will rejoice with me. And she said: Who will announce unto Abraham that Sarah suckles a child? For I have born him a son in my old age. And the child grew, and was weaned; and Abraham made a great feast the same day that Isaac was weaned.[^664]

*The Reading from Judges (13:2–8, 13–14, 17–18):[^665]*
In those days, there was a certain man of the tribe of Dan, whose name was Manoah; and his wife was barren, and bore not. And the angel of the Lord appeared unto the woman, and said unto her: Behold, thou art barren; but thou shalt conceive, and bear a son. Now therefore beware, and drink not wine nor strong drink, and eat not anything unclean. For lo, thou shalt conceive, and bear a son; and no razor shall come upon his head, for the child shall be a Nazirite unto God from the womb. Then the woman came and told her husband, saying: A man of God came unto me, and his countenance was like the countenance of an angel of God, very terrible. And he said: Behold, thou shalt conceive, and bear a son; and now drink no wine nor strong drink, neither eat any unclean thing; for the child shall be a Nazirite to God from the womb even to the day of his death. Then Manoah entreated the Lord, and said: O my Lord, let the man of God whom Thou didst send come again unto us, and teach us what we shall do unto the child that shall be born. And the angel came unto Manoah, and said: Of all that I said unto the woman let her beware; she may not eat of anything that comes of the vine, neither let her drink wine or strong drink. And Manoah said unto the angel of the Lord: What is Thy name, that when Thy words come to pass we may do thee honor? And the angel of the Lord said unto him: Why dost thou ask thus after my name, seeing it is secret? And the angel of the Lord appeared no more unto Manoah and to his wife.[^666]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (40:1–3, 9; 41:17–18; 45:8; 48:20–21; 54:1):[^667]*
Thus says the Lord: Comfort ye, comfort ye My people, says God; speak ye, O priests, to the heart of Jerusalem, entreat her, for her humility is multiplied; for her sin is loosed, for she has received from the Lord's hand double for her sins. The voice of one crying in the wilderness: Prepare ye the way of the Lord, make straight the paths for our God! Get thee up into the high mountain, O thou that bringest good tidings to Zion; lift up thy voice with strength, O thou that bringest good tidings to Jerusalem! Lift it up, be not afraid: I the Lord God, I will hear the poor of Israel, and will not forsake them. But I will open rivers in high places, and fountains in the midst of the valleys; I will make the wilderness a pool of water, and the dry land springs of water. Let the heaven rejoice from above, and let the clouds shower righteousness; let the earth shine forth, and let mercy blossom, and let righteousness spring up together! With the voice of joy declare ye, let it be heard! Utter it even to the ends of the earth; say ye: The Lord has redeemed His servant Jacob! And if they thirst in the desert, He will bring forth water for them out of the rock. Rejoice, thou barren that barest not; break forth and cry, thou that didst not travail with child: for more are the children of the desolate than of her that has a husband![^668]

*At the Litiya, stichera, Idiomela, Tone 1:*
Let the mountains drop down sweetness, and let the hills leap like lambs; for he who is to dwell with us has been born of Elizabeth: the forerunner of the Lord, who in his birth loosed the speechlessness of his father; therefore do we also cry unto him: O baptizer of Christ, pray that our souls may be saved![^669]

*Sticheron:*
O portentous voice, lamp of the Light, forerunner of the Lord, witnessed to by Christ, first among the prophets, offering prayer for the world: remember especially thine own flock, to save it unharmed!

*Sticheron:*
Thou wast a preacher of the Lamb and Word of God, O John, prophet and forerunner: thou didst foretell things to come, and didst declare beforehand unto the ends of the earth: Behold the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sin of the world, and grants great mercy unto all![^670]

*Glory, Tone 5. By Andrew of Crete:*
Today Elizabeth brings forth the boundary of the prophets and beginning of the apostles, the earthly angel and heavenly man, the voice of the Word, the soldier and forerunner of Christ, who leapt beforehand according to the promise, and preached before the birth of the Sun of righteousness; and she rejoices; and Zechariah is amazed in old age, having put away silence as a bound bond, and as the father of the voice he prophesies most clearly: For thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest, and shalt go before His face to prepare His ways.[^671] Therefore, O angel, prophet, apostle, soldier, forerunner, baptizer, preacher of repentance, and teacher, as the voice of the Light of the Word, unceasingly pray for us who perform thy memory with faith!

*Now, the same Tone: Thou art the temple and gate:*[^672]

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Idiomela, Tone 2:*
Let us praise with psalms and hymns and spiritual songs the prophet born of a prophet, the offspring of the barren woman, the highest of all born of women, the citizen of the wilderness, the glorious John, crying aloud unto him: O baptizer and forerunner of the Savior, as thou hast boldness in thine honorable nativity, entreat Christ to grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Verse: Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, for He hath visited and wrought redemption for His people.[^673]*

The voice of the grace of the Word has arrived, the preacher of the Sun, born today of the childless barren woman according to the promise: John the forerunner. Ye peoples, rejoice! He has come to prepare for us the salvation, unto Whom also, leaping in his mother's womb, he bowed down: unto the Lamb Who takes away the sins of the world, and grants great mercy unto us![^674]

*Verse: And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest.[^675]*

He who was sanctified from his mother's womb, and received the fulfillment of prophecy, today is born of the barren woman, clearly proclaiming the coming of the Lord: Repent ye, for the Kingdom of heaven is at hand!

*Glory, Tone 8. By the Nun Cassia:*[^676]
Now is the voice of the Prophet Isaiah fulfilled today in the nativity of John, greatest of the prophets; for behold, he said: I send My angel before Thy face, who shall prepare Thy way before Thee.[^677] This soldier running before the heavenly King truly made straight the paths for our God; a man indeed by nature, but an angel by life; for having entirely embraced purity and chastity, he possessed indeed what is according to nature, but he strove above nature for the divine things that are beyond nature; unto whom all we faithful, imitating his virtues, pray that he intercede for us, that our souls may be saved!

*Now, the same Tone:*
Behold Elizabeth saying unto the Virgin Mary: Why hast thou come to me, the mother of my Lord? Thou bearest the King, and I a soldier; thou the Lawgiver, and I the law-decreer; thou the Word, and I the voice proclaiming the Kingdom of heaven![^678]

*At the Blessing of the Loaves, the troparion, Tone 4, twice:*
*(Written at Little Vespers):*[^679]
*And Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: once.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," the troparion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory, and now: The mystery hidden from the ages:*

*After the 1st Kathisma, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Now has the fruit of Zechariah budded forth for us, and spiritually gladdens the minds of the faithful: the adornment of the desert and degree of the prophets; therefore was he shown forth as the forerunner of Christ, and a true witness of His coming. With spiritual songs, therefore, let us cry with one accord unto the baptizer: O prophet and preacher of truth, pray that we may be saved! *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Joseph was amazed, beholding that which was beyond nature, and understood in his mind the rain upon the fleece, in thy seedless conception, O Birthgiver of God: the bush unburnt by fire, the budded rod of Aaron; and thy betrothed and guardian bore witness, crying aloud unto the priests: A virgin gives birth, and after childbearing remains again a virgin!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
As the beginning of the coming of Christ, thou wast truly born most gloriously, O all-praised John: the head of the prophets; and as the voice of the Word, being such, thou didst cry aloud: Repent ye, for the Kingdom of heaven is at hand! Therefore, having prepared the way of the Lord, thou wast shown forth unto the ends of the earth as the forerunner of grace, O baptizer and apostle; pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that celebrate thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
O all-holy Virgin, Mother of Christ, heal the cruel passions of my soul, I pray thee, and grant forgiveness unto my transgressions, which I have foolishly committed, having defiled both soul and body, wretch that I am. Woe is me! What shall I do in that hour when the angels shall separate my soul from my passible body? Then be thou my helper and most fervent protector, for such hope have I, thy servant!

*At the Polyeleos, the Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное):*
Let the father rejoice, and let the mother be glad, for thou hast born a prophet upon earth today: the divinely-called forerunner according to the promise; the barren woman suckles the infant baptizer, and Zechariah rejoices at him who is born, saying: My tongue is loosed by thy coming upon the earth, O lamp of the great Light! Truly a most glorious wonder! *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
We ever give thanks unto thee, O Birthgiver of God, and magnify thee, O pure one, and worship, chanting thy childbearing, O full of grace, crying unceasingly: Save us, O all-merciful Virgin, as thou art good, and snatch us from the dreadful accounting of demons in the hour of trial, that we, thy servants, be not put to shame!

*Then the Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest.[^680]*
*Verse: Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, for He hath visited and wrought redemption for His people.[^681]*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*The Gospel according to Luke, pericope 3 (1:24–25, 57–68, 76, 80): Elizabeth, the wife of Zechariah, conceived... Ending: Till the day of his manifestation unto Israel.[^682]*
*After Psalm 50, the sticheron, Tone 2: He who was sanctified from his mother's womb: (Written at the Aposticha).*
*Two canons: the first by John the Monk, Tone 4:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*He Who was born of the Virgin: drown Thou, I pray Thee, the tripartite nature of my soul in the deep of impassibility, having drowned the mighty captains; that in the mortification of the body, as upon a timbrel, I may sing a song of victory unto Thee![^683]*

*Troparion:*
As a beauteous dawn running before the Sun, the offspring of the barren woman clearly proclaims the childbearing of the Virgin; now the illumination of piety and grace shines unto all the ends of the universe!

*Troparion:*
Thou shalt truly be called the prophet of the Highest, for thou shalt go before the face of Christ; borne by the Holy Spirit, Zechariah cries aloud unto thee his son, O all-praised John: to prepare a way for the Creator!

*Troparion:*
Having heard the words of Gabriel, of the divine utterance, Zechariah was shown to be disobedient, and was condemned to silence; but he is suddenly loosed from this; for the voice of the Word, John the forerunner, is born!

*Theotokion:*
O city of God, Queen of all, divinely-received habitation, precious treasure, all-immaculate Birthgiver of God: preserve thine inheritance, which ever praises thee and honors thy childbearing in faith!

*Second Canon, the composition of Andrew, the same Tone:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Unto Him Who of old guided Israel as they fled from the bondage of Pharaoh, and nourished them in the wilderness: let us sing unto our Deliverer God, for He hath been glorified![^684]*

*Troparion:*
The old man's silence bears the image of the mysteries of the written Law; for when grace arrived, Moses was silent; for when hidden Wisdom was revealed, it was fitting for all to be silent!

*Troparion:*
While the priest was censing in the temple, an angel stood before him from on high, crying aloud: I have come bearing unto thee, O elder, the good tidings of a strange childbearing; for thou shalt receive from the barren woman the fruit of generation: the baptizer of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Why art thou amazed, O elder? Why dost thou stand disbelieving him who speaks these things unto thee? For it is an angel who speaks, though he bears a human form; therefore be dumb for a time, until the birth of the voice of the Word!

*Troparion:*
He Who made the silence of Zechariah a figure of the silence of the written Law, and thereby showed me the preacher of repentance, crying aloud, grants the voice of one crying, as an unplanted power!

*Troparion:*
O the most glorious wonders of the forerunner of Christ! He who before swaddling-clothes recognized Him Who loosed our bonds and cleansed the nations being born today of Elizabeth, looses the voice of his father!

*Theotokion:*
Thee, the receptacle of God and spiritual ladder whereby God descended and took form, and lifted up our nature unto heaven, do we all praise as the mediatress of salvation!

*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth:[^685]*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*We glory not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for none is holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind![^686]*

*Troparion:*
The birth from the Virgin is indeed more excellent; but that of the beloved servant from an aged and barren mother is befittingly wrought; for a wonder goes before a wonder!

*Troparion:*
The aged and barren woman embraces the Virgin Mother, recognizing this childbearing to be without falsehood: that the bond of barrenness was loosed by the divine will!

*Theotokion:*
Having unweddedly given birth unto God incarnate, establish me who am storm-tossed by the assaults of passions; for there is no helper, O all-pure one, besides thee!

*Second Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who dost establish the thunder and create the spirit: establish me, O Lord, that I may hymn Thee in truth, and do Thy will; for none is holy as Thou, O our God![^687]*

*Troparion:*
Elizabeth, who formerly was barren, prefigured Thy Church from the Gentiles, O Christ; and having given birth most gloriously, she shows her who once appeared barren to be again fruitful with many children!

*Troparion:*
Having made straight the ways of the Lord and prepared the path, thou didst show forth unto men the fruits of repentance, and didst teach the path of life, O prophet and preacher of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Today Elizabeth exulting carries thee, O forerunner, in her aged arms, and cries aloud boasting: There is none holy as Thou, O Lord our God!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst loose the sorrow of the forebears, having born joy for us: the Giver of life and Deliverer; Whom do thou earnestly entreat, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, that thy flock may be saved!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное):*
The voice of the Word, the forerunner, being born, clearly loosed the voice of Zechariah, and showed forth unto all the barrenness of the Law, crying aloud: Repent ye that are upon the earth, for behold, Jesus is come and has appeared, desiring to deliver all from the first curse, enlightening by baptism! Truly a most glorious wonder! *Glory, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́ся):*
Like a bright sun, the son of Zechariah shone forth for us from the womb of Elizabeth, and loosed the speechlessness of his father; and in great boldness he cried aloud unto all peoples: Make straight the way of the Lord; for He shall liberate and save them that flee unto Him! Entreat Him Whom thou didst preach, O John, that our souls may be saved!

*Now, Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou didst renew the mortal nature of the earthborn which had decayed in passions, and didst raise up all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore, as is meet, we all bless thee, O most glorified Virgin, even as thou didst foretell![^688]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the transcendent God is come upon a light cloud, with His incorrupt hand, and hath saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^689]*

*Troparion:*
The mystery of ineffable mysteries goes before, with new teachings, proclaiming the loosing of the pangs of the natural law: this is the setting-right, renewal, and enlightenment, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Isaiah prophesied from the Father unto the Son Who was about to become incarnate: Behold, I send an earthborn angel, equal to the angels, before Thy face, crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^690]

*Troparion:*
I was born to serve the Master as a servant; for unto this am I come, to announce His coming: that the aged woman who was barren, having most gloriously brought forth, might confirm beforehand the childbearing of the Virgin!

*Theotokion:*
Holy Birthgiver of God, in whom the transcendent Word of the Father was well pleased to dwell as in a fragrant house: she was not corrupted in womb, nor suffered pain; for she gave birth unto Emmanuel, God and Man!

*Second Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God: that Thou wast born of the Virgin, that Thou mightest deliver from deception them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^691]*

*Troparion:*
By thy birth, O forerunner and preacher, thou didst open repentance; for thou alone didst preach, crying aloud: Repent ye, for the Kingdom of heaven is at hand!

*Troparion:*
Virginity is established, and chastity triumphs; the wilderness rejoices, and the world keeps feast by thy birth, O forerunner!

*Troparion:*
Elizabeth rejoices, and Zechariah speaks again: both indeed are renewed after old age and by the voice of John they are immediately enlightened!

*Theotokion:*
The lawgiver of old beheld thee as the unburnt bush, and Daniel contemplated the holy mountain, O only Virgin Mother, Birthgiver of God![^692]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Now will I arise, prophetically says God; now will I be glorified, now will I be exalted, taking up him who fell from the Virgin, and lifting him up unto the noetic light of My Godhead![^693]*

*Troparion:*
The earth has brought forth a true preacher, proclaiming the voice unto all, by the tongue of the Spirit: the Son of the Virgin, the righteousness looking down from heaven upon us in bodily substance![^694]

*Troparion:*
The Lord has truly set thee as the lamp of Christ, enlightening all, clothing those who are at enmity with Him in shame as with a garment, preaching without falsehood the Son, the Word of God![^695]

*Troparion:*
All creation rejoices divinely in thy birth; for thou, O forerunner, hast appeared as an earthly angel and heavenly man, proclaiming unto us the heavenly God become incarnate!

*Theotokion:*
From thee is born, from the Father the Son, ever with Thee, the co-eternal and indivisible Word, as the Only-begotten Son; and in the last days from the same Virgin, He is incarnate of the Holy Spirit!

*Second Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Shine forth upon me, O Lord, the light of Thy commandments, for my spirit rises early unto Thee, and hymns Thee; for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace![^696]*

*Troparion:*
Thou hast prepared a star beforehand, O Sun of righteousness: John Thy baptizer, born today according to the promise, who loosed the voice of him who begot him!

*Troparion:*
Speak not, speak not against, O consecrated elder; for Gabriel, the first among the archangels, speaks unto thee, declaring the mysteries of God, and making known His descent unto us!

*Troparion:*
Disbelieve not, O elder, for God promises: that in old age thou shalt bear a son, at whose birth many shall rejoice; for he shall come in the power of Elijah![^697]

*Troparion:*
O prophet, preacher, forerunner, offspring of the barren woman, herald of repentance, sheep of the wilderness, lamp of the Light: pray for all who honor thee in faith!

*Theotokion:*
As the gate through which none may pass, the unburnt bush, and the unhewn mountain, from which the Stone was cut, Who is incarnate of thee: do we hymn thee, O Birthgiver of God![^698] O Birthgiver of God, Mother of the Creator of all!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I came into the deeps of the sea, and the tempest of many sins overwhelmed me; but as God, lead up my life from the deep, O Plenteous in mercy![^699]*

*Troparion:*
As a prophet thou didst recognize God the Word in thy mother's womb, and using her tongue thou didst speak theology, in a dark bridal chamber, being deified by unapproachable light!

*Troparion:*
As the voice of one crying, cease not to pray without silence unto the Deliverer of the world, O baptizer, to loose the spiritual barrenness of those who sing thy birth!

*Theotokion:*
Thy pure womb was shown to be the habitation of the unapproachable Godhead, O Birthgiver of God, upon which the heavenly ranks cannot gaze without trembling!

*Second Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*A tempest of thoughts having overtaken me, drags me down into the deep of immeasurable sins; but do Thou, O good Pilot, anticipate and guide me as the Prophet, and save me![^700]*

*Troparion:*
Today the citizen of the wilderness, John, is born: the preacher of repentance, the true witness of grace, the forerunner of the Word, and the star that shines before the Light!

*Troparion:*
Today the ax is forged, setting forth the felling of barrenness for souls, and planting the fruits of virtue; it goes forth firmly in thy birth, O forerunner![^701]

*Troparion:*
The Jordan rejoiced most gloriously, and plays, having heard that John is born of a barren womb; and the sea dances in watery play!

*Troparion:*
The lamp of the Light, running before His coming, preached the Lamb of God, the Savior, the Light Who shone upon the earth, spiritually foreseen by all nature!

*Theotokion:*
O thou that didst give birth unto the abyss of mercy: having drowned the abyss of my passions in the ocean of thy compassions, grant me a shower of tears from my soul, O all-immaculate Parent of God!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
She that was formerly barren today gives birth unto the forerunner of Christ, and he is the fulfillment of all prophecy; for having laid his hand in the Jordan upon Him Whom the prophets preached, he was shown forth as the prophet, preacher, and forerunner of God the Word!

*Ikos:*
Let us now praise the forerunner of the Lord, whom Elizabeth bore unto the priest from a barren womb, yet not without seed; for Christ alone passed through the unapproachable receptacle without seed; a barren woman bore John, yet not without a man did she bear him; but Jesus, by the overshadowing of the Father and the Spirit of God, the pure Virgin bore; yet to the Seedless One was he shown forth from the barren woman as prophet and preacher, and together forerunner!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The three youths in Babylon, turning the tyrant's command into foolishness, cried aloud in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^702]*

*Troparion:*
Though all the nature of the earthborn was formerly in darkness, O forerunner, yet thou hast appeared as the dawn, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thy glorious birth from the barren woman healed all nature that had been overcome, teaching it to sing, O forerunner: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast born of the barren woman, O forerunner, for the all-true One is come to the barren Law, singing unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Do thou who art divinely blessed pray for us who pray unto thee; for in thee do we all place our hope, and unto thee do we cry: O Lady, despise not thy servants!

*Second Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burned rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^703]*

*Troparion:*
Zechariah, having been silent for a time, announced by his silence the written Law; and having spoken again at thy birth, O forerunner, he most gloriously announces the grace of the Spirit!

*Troparion:*
The all-honorable Elizabeth, barren and childless, is likened unto the Church; who formerly indeed was blackened with sacrifices and abominations, but now boasts both in beauty and in childbearing!

*Troparion:*
By the birth of the forerunner the spiritual ax is sharpened today, whereby all the surgings of passions are cut down, and the fruits of repentance mystically blossom forth!

*Troparion:*
As the mediator of the Old and New, O forerunner, and the one making straight the paths of Christ, Who cleanses the threshing-floor of sins with the spiritual fan, do we honor thee, O John![^704]

*Troparion:*
Let us all sing of the glorious John, who ran before the Sun, Christ our God, as the star and forerunner, the preacher of repentance, who came forth from the barren woman!

*Theotokion:*
Him Who dwelt seedlessly in thy womb, O Virgin, and was born for the sake of ineffable and utter humility, and became poor for our sake: cease not to entreat for all, O Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*O almighty Deliverer of all: descending into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew the pious ones, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless the Lord, praise Him unceasingly![^705]*

*Troparion:*
Moses the lawgiver was a servant, but Jesus is the God of the New Covenant; and another, the forerunner of both, sings as a mediator: All ye works, bless the Lord, praise Him unceasingly!

*Troparion:*
The turtledove of the empty womb is come, as a God-planted grove has the Church now brought forth the forerunner of Christ, and chants: All ye works, bless the Lord, praise Him unceasingly!

*Troparion:*
Ye God-bearing people, holy nation, be ye conformed unto the turtledove of Christ, and sweetly chant, living in chastity: All ye works, bless the Lord, praise Him unceasingly!

*Trinity:*
Illumined by the tri-hypostatic Light, let us honor with unceasing lips the uncreated single Godhead, crying in orthodox manner: All ye works, bless the Lord, praise Him unceasingly!

*Second Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Thou, O Master, didst fashion human nature by Thy wisdom, and again didst establish the earth, as Thou knowest its bottom, having set its foundation upon the boundless waters; therefore singing we all cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord unceasingly![^706]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst recognize Him Whom thou didst worship before birth and before thy swaddling clothes, O forerunner: Christ the Giver of life; and by leapings thou didst point Him out, having called Him thy Lord, borrowing thy mother's tongue for the praise of Christ our God!

*Troparion:*
The ends of the universe keep feast beforehand today; the angels and the spirits of the righteous rejoice; the living are glad, and the dead likewise at the birth of John, having received the preaching of the Savior and Lord of all!

*Troparion:*
Conformed today unto John, the Jordan played most gloriously with watery currents, hearing that he who is equal to the angels in life is born of an aged barren woman: he that makes straight the paths and ways of the Lord, and baptized the world!

*Troparion:*
Today Zechariah inscribed upon a tablet the divinely-named name of the prophet and forerunner; and being silent, he cried aloud: Let John be the name of him who was born unto me in old age! It is befitting for him who was born according to the promise to have a strange appellation![^707]

*Theotokion:*
As a firm consolation, and unashamed hope, an invincible wall, and divine protection do thy people possess thee, who glorify thee, O Virgin, and are saved and being saved, they diligently cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, unceasingly![^708]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The hidden, ineffable mystery of God is manifestly accomplished in thee, O all-pure Virgin; for God was incarnate of thee out of tender mercy; therefore do we magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God.[^709]*

*Troparion:*
Lo, the voice of the forerunner is present, crying unto barren and desolate hearts: Prepare now the way of Christ, for He comes in glory; Whom obeying, we magnify![^710]

*Troparion:*
As of old by the all-holy Spirit thou didst manifestly proclaim the Son, O forerunner, the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world: ask forgiveness of transgressions for thy flock![^711]

*Triadikon:*
Singing in orthodox fashion, ye faithful, let us glorify the Unity in three persons, the consubstantial Trinity, which illuminates us with divine origin and fills our souls with the splendors of the unsetting Light!

*Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady of all creation, grant unto thy people the victory of conquest, and subdue the rebellious under the feet of the king,[^712] that we may magnify thee as the Birthgiver of God!

*Other Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*For He that is mighty has done to me great things, and holy is His name, and His mercy is from generation to generation upon them that fear Him.[^713]*

*Troparion:*
In thee, O priest, God has wrought this great and most glorious sign; for in old age and after the deadening of members thou dost beget a son, the forerunner!

*Troparion:*
Let the barren hear, and let them hymn God; for lo, Elizabeth cries: In old age and after the deadening of members I bring forth a son, the forerunner!

*Troparion:*
Make merry, O Zechariah, and rejoice now in God; for lo, Elizabeth nourishes with her breasts: in old age and after the deadening of members she brings forth a son, the forerunner!

*Troparion:*
Glory to Him Who gave fruit in old age unto the barren woman, a son unto the elder and prophet, preparing for Him a perfect people, the divine forerunner![^714]

*Theotokion:*
Taking up His dwelling from thee, He Who is in the bosom of the Father was born without passion and corruption, and through tender mercy brought us unto the Father and the divine Spirit![^715]

*Exapostilarion:*
*Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оуслы́шите):*
The joy-bearing nativity of the forerunner today looses the sorrow of his father's speechlessness and the barrenness of her that bore him, and proclaims present joy and gladness; therefore does all creation radiantly keep festival thereof!

*Theotokion, the same Podoben:*

The prophets proclaimed, the apostles taught, and the martyrs in God-wise manner clearly confessed Thy Son, O Birthgiver of God, the God of all, O all-pure one; with them do we magnify thee, having been delivered through thee from the ancient condemnation![^716]

*On the Praises, stichera on 4:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*
O most marvelous wonder! From an aged mother, John, the herald of the Word of God, hastens today to go forth; a bound tongue, by his nativity, is shown most clearly to speak. O Thine ineffable dispensation, O Master, whereby, O Christ, save our souls, as Thou alone art compassionate![^717]

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*
O most marvelous wonder! Among them that are born of women, the prophet who is higher looses the condemnation of maternal barrenness, who came in the spirit and power of Elijah to make straight the way of the Lord. O Thine ineffable lovingkindness, O Master, whereby, O Christ, save our souls, as Thou alone art compassionate![^718]

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*
O most marvelous wonder! He who proclaimed the emptying of Christ unto men is shown forth above all by His voice; and by divine power he looses the barrenness of her that bore him and the tongue of Zechariah. O Thy great wonders, O Master, whereby, O Christ, save them that honor Thy great forerunner!

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чудесѐ):*
O most marvelous wonder! Among them that are born of women the prophet and forerunner is shown to be higher; for he was the harbinger of the coming of Christ, having leaped beforehand in the maternal womb. O Thy very great gifts, O Lover of mankind, whereby, O Christ, save our souls, as Thou art all-powerful![^719]

*Glory, Tone 6. By Anatolius:*
The star of stars, the forerunner, from a barren womb is born on earth today, John the God-beloved; and he reveals the dawn of Christ, the Dayspring from on high, for the upright walking of the faithful![^720]

*Both now, Theotokion: O Birthgiver of God, thou art the vine:[^721]*
*The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the forerunner from the First Canon, Ode 3 on 4; and from the Second Canon, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, the Troparion of the temple of Christ, and of the Birthgiver of God, if there be one, then the Troparion of the forerunner. After this, the Kontakion of the temple of Christ. Glory, of the forerunner; Both now, of the temple of the Birthgiver of God. If there be no temple of the Birthgiver of God: Both now, the Kontakion of the temple of Christ. If there be no temple of Christ, we say the Troparion first of the forerunner; Glory, his Kontakion; Both now:*

...*O Protection of Christians unashamed.[^722]* The Prokeimenon, Epistle, and Gospel we read only for the forerunner; likewise the Communion Verse.

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.[^723]
*Verse: Hear, O God, my voice, when I make supplication unto Thee.[^724]*

*Epistle to the Romans, pericope 112 [Rom. 13:11b–14:4].[^725]*

*Alleluia, Tone 1:*
Blessed is the Lord God of Israel, for He hath visited and wrought redemption for His people.[^726]
*Verse: And thou, child, shalt be called the prophet of the Highest.[^727]*

*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 1 [Luke 1:1–25, 57–68, 76, 80].[^728]*
*Forasmuch as many have taken in hand to set forth in order... The end: in pericope 4: till the day of his manifestation unto Israel.*

*Communion Verse:*
In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^729]
*The regular Epistle and Gospel of the day we read ahead.*

*It is meet to know; behold:*
*If the feast of John the forerunner falls on a Sunday, all the resurrectional hymns precede.*

*Month of the Same, the 25th Day. The Holy Venerable-Martyr Febronia:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the forerunner, Tone 4. Idiomela:*[^730]
John being born looses the silence of Zechariah; for it was not fitting that the father should keep silence when the voice had come; but as his tongue was bound when he did not believe at first, so when he appeared he gave freedom to the father; unto whom it was announced, and there was born the voice of the Word and the forerunner of the Light, praying for our souls!

*Sticheron:*
Today the voice of the Word looses the paternal voice held bound for unbelief, and reveals fruitful children unto the Church, having loosed the maternal bonds of barrenness; the lamp of the Light goes before, the ray of the Sun of righteousness announces His coming, unto the renewal of all and the salvation of our souls!

*By Anatolius:*
When the Word of God was about to be born of the Virgin, an angel came forth from aged loins, great among them that are born of women, and higher than the prophets; for it was fitting that the beginnings of divine things should be most glorious: offspring beyond normal age, and conception without seed. O Thou Who workest wonders for our salvation, glory to Thee![^731]

*Other stichera of the saint, the same Tone [Tone 4]:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Thou didst endure suffering twofold by thy struggle, O all-praised Febronia: the sweats of self-control having mingled with the blood of martyrdom having mingled them together; therefore also did the Benefactor bestow a twofold crown upon thee, unto Whom thou didst ascend radiantly adorned, as an all-immaculate virgin and as an invincible martyr!

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
With the beauty of the body, the divine beauties of thy soul met together: like a lily didst thou shine forth, whitened in the dwellings of the venerable, and dyed red with streams of blood, O all-immaculate bride; therefore the fair heavenly bridal chamber and indestructible palace received thee as a virgin and martyr!

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Round about thee, O all-praised Febronia, came the delivering angel; for from infancy thou wast shown forth fearing the Lord, and offered as an honorable offering preserved for the Almighty; therefore didst thou trample underfoot the madness of Selenus,[^732] and didst eagerly run unto Christ Thy Bridegroom!

*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*
Behold Elizabeth saying unto the Virgin Mary: Why hast thou come to me, the mother of my Lord? Thou bearest the King, and I a warrior; thou the Lawgiver, and I the law-decreer; thou the Word, and I the voice proclaiming the Kingdom of heaven![^733]

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*

*Glory, both now, Tone 6:*
Elizabeth conceived the forerunner of grace, and the Virgin the Lord of glory. Both mothers kissed each other, and the babe leaped; for within, the servant praised the Master. And the mother of the forerunner, wondering, began to cry aloud: Whence is this to me, that the mother of my Lord should come unto me?[^734] That He may save the desperate people, He Who has great mercy!

*Troparion of the forerunner, Tone 4:*
O prophet and forerunner of the coming of Christ, we who honor thee with love are at a loss to praise thee worthily; for the barrenness of her that bore thee and the speechlessness of thy father were loosed by thy glorious and honorable nativity, and the incarnation of the Son of God is proclaimed unto the world![^735]

*Troparion of the saint, the same Tone [Tone 4]:*
Thy lamb Febronia, O Jesus, cries aloud with a great voice: Thee, O my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering, and am crucified and buried with Thy baptism; and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; but as a blameless sacrifice, accept me who with love have sacrificed myself unto Thee.[^736] By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*At Matins, one canon from the Octoechos; and of the forerunner on 4, and of the saint on 4.*

*Canon of the forerunner, by John the Monk, Tone 8.[^737]*
*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped from the wickedness of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God![^738]*

*Troparion:*
Having an angel designating thy glorious and divine nativity: an earthly angel and heavenly man wast thou shown forth, O forerunner![^739]

*Troparion:*
By the height of a life above nature, a heaven wast thou shown forth, and a noetic firmament, having Christ indeed as the Sun in the midst, and the virtues as stars!

*Troparion:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is thy death, O wise one, Who sacrificed Himself for our sake; and being of the male sex, as a rational lamb art thou borne![^740]

*Theotokion:*
The Deliverer, having taken up His dwelling from thee, O Maiden, drove away the truly lamentable sin that had dwelt within me, and showed me forth a dweller of paradise!

*Other Canon of the saint. The work of Theophanes, whose acrostic is: "With hymns I praise thee wisely, Febronia." Tone the same [Tone 8].[^741]*
*Ode 1. The same Heirmos:*
*Troparion:*
O champion of the glory of Christ, grant me grace, O Febronia, and thine illumination, that I may joyfully praise thy light-bearing festival, O pure one!

*Troparion:*
Continually enlightening thy soul with the meditation of death, O martyr Febronia, thou didst ascend unto the height of martyrdom, having inclined thyself unto Christ through tortures!

*Troparion:*
Having visibly renewed the field of thy heart with the pangs of self-control, O martyr, thou didst receive the crowns of martyrdom, singing in martyric contest unto our God!

*Theotokion:*
He Who made all things by His nod was incarnate of thee, O Mother of God; Whom the maiden Febronia piously desiring, was martyricly brought unto Him!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Supreme Fashioner of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind![^742]*

*Troparion:*
O many-flowered offspring of a barren womb, show my barren soul to be fruitful in good shoots, growing like a vine pouring forth the sweet cluster of virtues!

*Troparion:*
From a truly sacred root and pure seed, the beautifully sacred branch budded forth; and having budded forth, O blessed one thou didst baptize the life-bearing Branch, Christ, Who budded forth from the divine root of Jesse![^743]

*Troparion:*
Unceasing groanings, and pure tears, and a contrite heart, and a humble spirit give unto me, O all-praised one, with sorrowful weeping, and warm compunction, and continuing sickness of soul![^744]

*Theotokion:*
Having budded forth from an earthly root, O pure Sovereign Lady, the ever-blossoming and fair Shoot of nature, thou didst blossom forth Christ, the Blossom of the Godhead; for the rod of Aaron prefigured thee![^745]

*Other Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the confirmation of them that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.[^746]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast strengthened by the Spirit, O most glorious one, to cast down the savage cruelty of the tormentor, and the godless polytheism, O Febronia!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast now established with a mighty hand, O Master, the glorious Febronia, who contested martyricly!

*Troparion:*
Thy body being cut for Christ, O pure and all-wise one, martyricly wrought paradise for thee as a delight!

*Theotokion:*
The glorious martyr Febronia found thee to be her help, O all-pure Mother of God, and put the tormentor to shame!

*Kontakion of the saint, Tone 6:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Protection of Christians" (Застꙋ́пнице):*
My sweetest Bridegroom, Christ, cried Febronia: It is not hard for me to run after Thee; for the sweetness of Thy love has winged my soul with hope, and the beauty of Thy mercy has sweetened my heart to drink the cup of sufferings for Thy sake; that Thou mayest account me worthy to rejoice with the wise virgins in the bridal chamber concerning Thee. Therefore, O venerable passion-bearer, honoring the struggles of thy labors, we entreat thee: Pray that the doors of the bridal chamber be not shut against us also![^747]

*Sessional Hymn of the forerunner, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное):*
The fruit of Zechariah, the flower of the barren woman, has been born for us today according to promise: the honorable passion-bearer John, the forerunner of the Lord; and leaping in his mother's womb, even before his birth he proclaims the Creator of all. He revealed the ineffable and saving manifestation of God; him do all of us honor![^748]

*Other Sessional Hymn of the saint, the same Tone [Tone 8]:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having bound thy soul with the love of Christ, corruptible and temporary things, O wise martyr, thou didst pass by with forgetfulness, as a disciple of the Word; having first mortified the passions in fasting, secondly through martyrdom thou didst put the deceiver to shame. Therefore also the Creator of the Creator, thou wast accounted worthy of twofold boldness, O glorious one, having obeyed Him, O most rich Febronia; pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto them that with love honor thy holy memory![^749]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
As an all-immaculate bride unto the Creator, as an unwedded Mother unto the Deliverer, as a receptacle of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: since I am a defiled dwelling of lawlessness, and have become in understanding a plaything of demons, hasten to deliver me from their wickedness, and make me a radiant dwelling of virtues, O light-bearing and incorrupt one; drive away the cloud of passions, and account me worthy of communion on high and of the unwaning Light, by thy prayers!

*Staurotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest out of tender mercy of a Mother, O longsuffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and unemptying Fountain! Have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto them that with faith hymn Thy divine passions![^750]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty; therefore with the prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^751]*

*Troparion:*
Human lips are not sufficient to praise thee whom the lips of Christ praised, which are sweet and honey-flowing, and full of life, and granting the word unto all; nevertheless, accept my hymn, knowing my love, and that this very supplication is inescapable!

*Troparion:*
Pure was thy heart shown forth, O forerunner, a receptacle of myrrh of the Holy Spirit, breathing the fragrance of virtues, censing the understanding, sweetest of benefactions; for blessed are the pure in heart, for they, He said, shall see God![^752]

*Troparion:*
The striking of the ax at the root draws near; it is testified that every tree that brings not forth good fruit is cut down with the ax and cast into the fire of Gehenna; therefore tremble, O my soul, and bring forth fruits of repentance![^753]

*Troparion:*
As having thy citizenship in the heavens, the city that has unshakable foundations received thee; for a city here below thou didst not desire, but the abiding Jerusalem[^754] even Sion that is manifestly free, which became the mother of Paul![^755]

*Theotokion:*
Warm faith is proclaimed, and not an exposition, O Maiden, of thy fearful mystery; for when contemplated, it can in no wise be recounted, and when seen, it is incomprehensible; for with thee it passes by, and when apprehended it flees away, and when appearing it is but a little revealed!

*Other Canon. Ode 4. The same Heirmos:*
*Troparion:*
There is no blemish in thee at all, O all-praised Febronia; for in both respects thou didst hasten to be well-pleasing unto thy Deliverer and desired Lover, adorned with the pangs of self-control and with the sufferings of martyrdom, O God-wise and most rich one![^756]

*Troparion:*
From tender youth, O martyr, thou didst desire the love of the ever-flowing Fountain, desired by all rational beings; of Which having been filled, thou didst endure the pangs of martyrdom, burned with fire, and deprived of members, O Febronia, praise of virgins![^757]

*Troparion:*
Nurtured on the words of God, O God-wise one, sacredly handing over the saving word of the sacred books unto them that desire it, thou didst divinely stretch it forth, O martyr, and didst receive the reward of teaching, having been enriched with the glory of the martyrs![^758]

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as the door of light unto all, O all-immaculate one, having shone forth the uncircumscribed Light circumscribed in the flesh unto them that are in darkness, even Christ the most ancient Sun; strengthened by Whose rays now, Febronia acquired the glory of the martyrs!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Therefore hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O unsetting Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, wretched one? But convert me, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.[^759]*

*Troparion:*
A flourishing tongue, and the words of them that thunder in philosophical wisdom, and words of power, flowing with number and testament, and clapping rhetoricians cannot worthily praise thee with their understanding!

*Troparion:*
A rain-bearing cloud, sprinkling the rain of understanding, stretched out as a refreshing dew unto them that are melted with the flame of ignorance and perishing, did Christ show thee forth, O forerunner!

*Troparion:*
With incorruptible gifts, and divine benefactions, and bounties do thou enrich me, who am impoverished and naked of every virtue; and clothe me with thy saving protection as with a divine garment!

...garment![^760]

*Troparion:*
Having sprouted foul thoughts and corrupting imaginations, O blessed one, my soul is overcome with the thorns of passions; burning up this matter with the fire of thy prayers, do thou cleanse it, and plant the fear of God in it entirely!

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin called after the Lord, named the city of Christ the King, made glad by the rush of the noetic river, where the dwelling of all that rejoice is: establish my wretched soul there![^761]

*Other Canon. Ode 5. The same Heirmos:*
*Troparion:*
Adorned with purple woven for thee from thine own blood, O martyr, thou wast stripped in body, and didst cast off the garment of passions, standing before the tribunal of the tormentors, O Febronia, imitating the nakedness of Christ!

*Troparion:*
Inflamed, O martyr, with the love of thy noetic Bridegroom, and kindled with His incorruptible beauties, thou didst endure the severance of thy hands and the pain of wounds, O Febronia, praise of martyrs![^762]

*Troparion:*
The streams of thy blood quenched the flame of polytheism; and thy uprooted teeth tore out all the deception of idols, and wrought for thee, O Febronia, the delight of eternal beauty![^763]

*Theotokion [Troparion with Theotokion ending]:*
Thy severed feet walked the path of divine tortures, and passed over unto Him Who was born of the Virgin, walking joyfully in paradise, O Febronia, virgin bride of God![^764]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^765]*

*Troparion:*
As one bodiless and immaterial, O Baptist, thou didst marvel at the immortality of the immaterial ones in thy mortal and material flesh; and thou wast adorned with supreme philosophy of character, and didst become a partaker of both the angelic and the human nature![^766]

*Troparion:*
Most radiant among the stars is the sun, and among the seasons the sweetest is spring; and unto the whole choir of saints thou art as the sun unto the stars: with indisputable majesty thou wast enriched according to every nature!

*Troparion:*
O great protector of Christians, fervent comforter of sinners, orator of the heavenly Kingdom, introducer into divine baptism, preacher of repentance: confirm me in the ways of repentance!

*Theotokion:*

Thou art the ladder equal to heaven, O Sovereign Lady, stretched out from earth unto heaven; thou art truly the impassable door, yea rather the all-golden jar, and the bridge leading up to heaven and opening the ascents of life![^767]

*Other Canon. Ode 6. The same Heirmos:*
*Troparion:*
The godless and proud cruelty of the impious Selenus was cast down by God's providence over all, which avenged thy blood, O glorious one; and by thy patient endurance the divine Lysimachus was saved![^768]

*Troparion:*
The wise and God-bearing Bryene showed thee forth as a luminary unto the Egyptians, having instructed thee in teaching, O maiden; and she was in no wise mistaken in her hope, for she presented thee unto Christ, O Febronia, as an undefiled bride![^769]

*Theotokion:*
The animate bridal chamber of the Master, shining radiantly like a lily with the bright rays of virginity in the midst of the thorny mingling, the all-pure and God-befitting Birthgiver of God and Virgin is glorified![^770]

*Kontakion of the forerunner, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
She that was formerly barren today gives birth to the forerunner of Christ, and he is the fulfillment of every prophecy; for having laid his hand in the Jordan upon Him Whom the prophets proclaimed, he was shown forth as the prophet, preacher, and forerunner of the Word of God![^771]

*Ikos:*
The prophets of old, and Isaiah, saw Christ our true God in figures and shadows; but this one became a greater prophet than they, as it is written; for God the Word Whom he foretold, he saw in the flesh and touched; and before his birth he proclaims joy unto the whole world by his leapings, so that he was shown forth first as the prophet and preacher, and forerunner of the coming of the Word![^772]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The youths that came from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^773]*

*Troparion:*
The summit of prophets, the seal of apostles, the beginning of ascetics, the perfection of the righteous, the confirmation of monastics, did Christ show thee forth, O blessed one, He Who is the true Head of the whole Church!

*Troparion:*
From thy mother's womb thou wast filled with the Holy Spirit, O forerunner; and wine and strong drink thou didst not drink, as the angel said unto thy father; therefore wast thou called great before God![^774]

*Troparion:*
He Who baptized men in the fire of the Holy Spirit first, will afterward baptize with the fire of Gehenna only them that believe not in Him; from whose bitter baptism[^775] baptism, O Baptist, deliver me.[^776]

*Theotokion:*
The inanimate jar of old betokened thee as the animate jar, O Birthgiver of God; for thou didst bear Christ, the Manna of life, Who takes away the bitterness of death, the true Sweetness and Illumination of the world.[^777]

*Other Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew youths in the furnace trampled down the flame with boldness, and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^778]*

*Troparion:*
Thou pourest forth streams of healings unto them that run with faith unto thy tomb, O Febronia, and unto thy Bridegroom cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
O the goodly merchandise of thine! By the bitterness of blood, O God-wise one, thou didst purchase the Kingdom of God, crying, O all-praised one: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
With the choirs of virgins thou dancest, O most marvelous maiden Febronia, crying unto the Virgin Mother of God: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the Fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^779]*

*Troparion:*
Sweet and drinkable water did the hard, dry, and unhewn rock pour forth for the disobedient and senseless Judean people by the striking of the rod; but for us from barren and childless loins flowed forth a river of healings.[^780]

*Troparion:*
With praises, O prophet, is thy memory: the assembly of the righteous, the choir of the prophets, the armies of the martyrs, and the company of the fasters, the ranks of the venerable, and of all the saints; for the memory of the righteous is truly with praises.[^781]

*Troparion:*
Let the animate heaven, adorned with virtues as with stars, the gold-shining and gold-beaming sun, the lamp of grace, the treasure of the Spirit, the great preacher of the Kingdom, the God-inspired orator, the thunder-voiced tongue, the great trumpet, the forerunner, be hymned!

*Theotokion:*
Strange, marvelous, and fearful are the narratives of thy marvelous and most glorious childbearing, O all-hymned Mary; for the Lord of glory, Whom the Cherubim and Seraphim attend, One of the Trinity, without mingling and without change didst thou bear without seed incarnate of thee.[^782]

*Other Canon. Ode 8. The same Heirmos:*
Light-manifesting is thy memory now celebrated, O Febronia, shining with light-like lightnings; for thou didst draw near unto the Light and becamest light-bearing, crying unto the Fashioner and Giver of light: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast marvelous at first in self-control, O most praised one, and afterward thou wast adorned with martyric struggles; for thou didst run after thy Lover, O radiant maiden: both thy breasts, better than wine, having been cut off, O Febronia, for the sake of piety; and the fragrance of thine ointments sheds sweet savor upon the faithful.[^783]

*Troparion:*
Burdened with transgressions, O all-praised Febronia, and bowed down by the surging billows of passions, I flee unto thy protection, seeking deliverance from both; but as a marvelous and chosen maiden, despise me not that cry aloud, but by thy prayers, O virgin, snatch me away from the soul-corrupting bonds![^784]

*Theotokion:*
Eloquently speaking tongues cannot hymn thee, O Mother of God, Mary, Bride of God; for thou didst bear God Who is over all, O pure Maiden, from a virginal and unwedded womb; unto Whom now Febronia singing cries: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Heaven was terrified at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.[^785]*

*Troparion:*
Unto thee, my master and fervent protector of my soul, have I brought this poor offering, wisely fulfilling a servant's ministry, O blessed one; give ear unto it, loving the intention before the deeds, and the good will before the gift![^786]

*Troparion:*
With unclean and defiled lips do I, that am myself unclean, defiled, and wretched, entreat thee, the all-honorable kinsman of the all-pure Virgin, O blessed one: wash my body, cleanse my spirit, and sanctify my soul![^787]

*Troparion:*
O initiate of the heavenly bridal chamber, true friend of the Bridegroom Christ, standing and worthily hearing the joyful voice of the Bridegroom: show me also to be a hearer of that good voice, an heir of the Kingdom with the blessed, when God summons unto the inheritance![^788]

*Theotokion:*

...O all-pure Virgin Mother of God, animate tongs of the immaterially burning Coal,[^789] and flame-bearing throne of Him Who is borne by the Cherubim: burn up the matter of my soul with the immaterial fire, and deliver me from the unquenchable fire![^790]

*Other Canon. Ode 9. The same Heirmos:*
O divine dove of the Master, gilded with childish radiance, having enlightened thy body by fasting, gilded on thy wings with gold-like flashes of thy confession and of thy divine blood: enlighten them that hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
Having conversed in mind with bare intellect, O God-wise Febronia, thou didst trample desires down unto the end, and wast accounted worthy to obtain a blessed end; and now thou reignest with Christ, radiantly with thy Bridegroom, abiding in gladness in ineffable bridal chambers!

*Troparion:*
Thou pourest forth healings like rivers unto them that run with faith unto thy precious reliquary, O God-like Febronia, pillar of virginity, fair flower of nature, king's daughter, having within the blessedness of divine glory![^791]

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst right the stumbling of women, O Virgin, having given birth unto the Word, Who raises up on high them that have fallen, as He is good and mighty in powers; Whom glorious Febronia having fervently desired, ran after thee, and rejoicing cried aloud!

*Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Hear, ye women" (Жєны̀ ꙋ҆слы́шите):*
The joyful birth of the forerunner today looses the sorrow of the father's speechlessness and the mother's barrenness, and announces present joy and gladness; therefore does all creation celebrate it brightly![^792]

*Theotokion:*
O thou that didst bear the hypostatic Wisdom, the transcendent Word, and the Physician of souls and bodies: heal the festering sores and wounds of my soul, grievous and temporal, and calm the pains of my heart, O Virgin![^793]

*At the Aposticha, stichera from the Octoechos:*
*Glory, Tone 2:*
The prophet from a prophet, the offspring of the barren woman, the highest of all born of women, the citizen of the wilderness, glorious John, let us praise with psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, crying unto him: O baptizer of the Savior and forerunner, having boldness in thine honorable nativity, entreat Christ to grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls![^794]

*Both now Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the wood of the Cross, O Jesus, the unwedded Maiden, weeping, said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone that gave Thee birth, O unapproachable Light of the pre-unoriginate Father? Hasten and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory![^795]

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^796]*
*Verse: In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.[^797]*
*The Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 181 (1 Cor. 12:7–11).[^798]*
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
*Alleluia, Tone 4: I waited patiently for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me.[^799]*
*The Gospel according to Luke, pericope 33 (7:36–50).[^800]*
*Communion Verse:*
*In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be: Alleluia.[^801]*

*The Same Month, on the 26th Day:*
*The memory of our venerable father David of Thessalonica.[^802]*

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O all-blessed father David, having pre-eminently enlightened thine intellect by self-control, thou didst take wing unto the Primal Cause of good things; and thou wast shown forth as a light-bearing pillar, enlightening all that approach thee with words and wonders, with an understanding ever God-befitting; therefore do we honor and bless thee![^803]

*Sticheron:*
Like a sweet-singing bird in the ascent of an orchard, thou didst pitch a shelter, O father, frozen by cold and scorched by heat; whence thou didst receive the golden wings of impassibility and perfection, and didst fly up unto the height on high, ever praying for us who praise thee![^804]

*Sticheron:*
Having burned up the carnal pleasures with the divine coal of impassibility, thou didst remain unburnt; holding the coal upon thy hand before the emperor's face, O God-inspired and venerable one, he marveled at thy radiance; therefore a great petition was granted unto thee by God, having acquired thee as an intercessor through grace, O blessed one![^805]

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Venerable father, into all the earth went forth the report of thy right deeds; therefore in the heavens hast thou found the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the hosts of demons, and didst attain unto the angelic ranks: whose blameless life thou didst emulate; having boldness unto Christ God, ask peace for our souls![^806]

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Meditating upon Thy seedless conception and ineffable birth, I marvel greatly: how hast Thou thus, as an evildoer, been well pleased to die, O my Son? the all-pure one, weeping, cried aloud.[^807]

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 8:*
The multitudes of monastics honor thee as instructor, our father David; for by thy path have we truly learned to walk uprightly; blessed art thou, having labored for Christ, and having exposed the power of the enemy, converser with angels, companion of the venerable and the righteous; with them pray unto the Lord, that our souls may be saved![^808]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
The undefiled heifer, seeing the Calf willingly nailed upon the Tree, bitterly lamenting, cried out: Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What hath the thankless Hebrew assembly rendered unto Thee, desiring to leave me childless and bereaved of Thee, O most beloved One?[^809]

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
In thee, O father, was preserved with certainty that which is according to the image; for taking up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by deed didst teach to despise indeed the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, an immortal thing; therefore with the angels rejoices thy spirit, O venerable David![^810]

*Canon, the composition of Joseph.[^811]*
*The acrostic whereof is: I sing thy life, O blessed one, with divine words.[^812]*
*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The horse and the rider into the Red Sea: He Who shatters wars with a high arm, Christ shook off, but Israel He saved, who sang a song of victory.[^813]*

*Troparion:*
With hymns having assembled, let us honor thy light-bearing and divine memory, O David most blessed, having known thee by the Divine Spirit as an heir of light and of the day, O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Having mortified thy bodily members upon earth, O father, thou didst have dwelling in thy heart the Life, Christ, Who destroyed the power of the devil that had put humanity to death!

*Troparion:*
Submitting unto the laws of the Master, O venerable one, thou didst take thy cross upon thy shoulder, and didst follow His footsteps, having humbled the haughtiness of the grievous enemy.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst appear adorned with the beauty of virtues, and didst bear the fair Christ, O Virgin, Who adorned men with the fair beauties of the Godhead, O Ever-virgin.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and didst suspend it gravitationally unsupported: upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments establish Thy Church, O Christ, Who alone art good and the Lover of mankind![^814]*

*Troparion:*
Having withered bodily beauty by self-control, thou didst receive the radiance of the Divine Spirit, and didst shine forth with the light-beaming rays of impassibility and gifts of healings, O father namesake of the prophet![^815]

*Troparion:*
Following the life of the saints, O most rich one, thou wast wholly sanctified by divine actions; therefore dost thou mystically sanctify them that celebrate thy holy festival in faith, O God-bearing father, most blessed!

*Troparion:*
Having put the carnal pleasures to sleep, O venerable one, by prayers and vigils and struggles, O father, having fallen asleep in truth in peace, thou didst rest; therefore have we that honor thee with love acquired thee as an unsleeping guardian!

*Theotokion:*
More honorable than the angels hast thou appeared, O Virgin, having given birth unto their Creator; therefore I pray thee, O pure one: sanctify my mind and enlighten my heart, driving away the cloud of passions!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
By self-control having subjected the carnal passions unto the soul, O wise father, thou didst appear as an angel with a body, O blessed one; and having pitched a shelter like a sweet-singing bird upon an orchard tree, thou didst wing thine intellect unto the height; therefore enriched by the operation of wonders, thou didst pass over unto the Lord Whom thou didst love from infancy, O most glorious David; pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto them that honor thee![^816]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Having fallen into many-entangled temptations, buffeted by the tempest of visible and invisible enemies and of mine unnumbered transgressions, and as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I run unto the harbor of thy goodness; therefore, O all-pure one, unto Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed pray fervently for all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, praying Him ever to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto them that worthily sing thy glory![^817]

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest out of tender mercy of compassion, O longsuffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and unemptying Fountain! Have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto them that with faith hymn Thy divine passions![^818]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Perceiving Thy divine emptying beforehand, O Christ, Habakkuk cried trembling unto Thee: For the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed, art Thou come![^819]*

*Troparion:*
Having listened unto the life-flowing voices of the Spirit, having left behind the salty sea of life, with the drops of thy tears thou didst dry up the streams of passions![^820]

*Troparion:*
Having been filled with understanding and grace, O father David, in stillness didst thou seek the Benefactor of all, and didst receive His illuminations![^821]

*Troparion:*
Having radiantly shown forth thy life beyond the constraint of nature, by the co-operation of the Spirit thou didst deliver men from grievous distresses, O venerable one![^822]

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, O all-hymned Sovereign Lady, who didst ineffably give birth unto the most praised God, and incomparably excelled the angelic choirs in beauty!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Thou that clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, unto Thee do I rise early, and unto Thee do I cry: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou alone art tenderhearted![^823]*

*Troparion:*
Stretching forth thine eyes and thoughts unto Him Who was stretched upon the Tree, thou didst endure upon an orchard tree, scorched by heat, O father, and frozen by cold![^824]

*Troparion:*
Wholly lightning-like didst thou appear, O venerable one, holding fire in thy hands, O David, and censing the emperor with a marvelous censing, O all-blessed one![^825]

*Troparion:*
Now not in riddles, nor by imagination, dost thou behold the divine beauty, O all-blessed one, but face to face rather, O venerable one, the mirrors being done away![^826]

*Troparion:*
A most fair cluster of the divine vineyard wast thou shown forth, O venerable David, pouring forth the wine of compunction unto them that have grown foolish from the drunkenness of passions, O father![^827]

*Theotokion:*
Direct my life and intellectual movements, O Mother of God, unto the divine will of Him Who ineffably came unto us from thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Calm the sea of passions raging with a soul-corrupting tempest, O Master Christ, and lead me up from corruption, as Thou art tenderhearted![^828]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast known as a temple of the Divine Spirit, O venerable David, shut up in a small dwelling, and by the narrow path purchasing the spacious![^829]

*Troparion:*
Unto the laws of the Master didst thou bow thy soul, O father, and wast filled with light, and becamest a receiver of the gifts of the Spirit![^830]

*Troparion:*
Adorned in truth with wonders did thy life appear unto the ends of the earth, O God-bearing father; therefore do we praise thee with faith![^831]

*Theotokion:*
From thy precious blood flesh was woven beyond nature for Him Who fashioned man, O Birthgiver of God; therefore do we worthily hymn thee!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй):*
An ever-blossoming orchard bringing forth the fruits of virtues didst thou appear upon an orchard tree, like a sweet-singing bird; or rather, having received paradise, the Tree of life, the Lord, into thy heart, as having cultivated it, O God-wise one, whereby thou dost nourish us with grace: ever pray for us, O David all-blessed![^832]

*Ikos:*
The furnace of my passions kindles for me eternal flame, O venerable David: quench it by thy prayers; as with thy copious tears, having kindled the coal of love of my Creator, O blessed one, as of old carrying it in thy hand like an offering, thou didst amaze the emperor; that I may sing of thee, O light-bearer, David all-blessed![^833]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers quenched the flame and bedewed the youths, who sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God![^834]*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand unwavering upon the branches of the orchard, O venerable one, watered with the drinking of thy tears, often watering them, and singing unceasingly: Blessed art Thou, O God![^835]

*Troparion:*
All thy desire didst thou stretch forth unto God; all thy heart didst thou make a receptacle of divine gifts, O God-bearer, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God![^836]

*Troparion:*
Seeing thee become light-bearing, O father, holding the coal in thy hand, the emperor was amazed, and bowed down at thy feet![^837]

*Theotokion:*
