By thy childbearing was the human curse loosed, O only all-blessed one; therefore do we glorify thee as good, O Birthgiver of God, unto the ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

*Unto Thee the Fashioner of all, the youths in the furnace having woven a worldwide choir sang: All ye works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^838]*

*Troparion:*
An intellectual morning hast thou appeared unto them that are in the darkness of life, O blessed one, illuminating the light of understanding unto all that honor thee with love and supremely exalt Christ unto all ages![^839]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast upright, humble, and meek, faithfully imaging thy namesake David; therefore with him hast thou inherited the land of the meek, O father, unto all ages![^840]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast confirmed as a pillar of monastics, O blessed David; a second divine prophet hast thou appeared, foretelling things to come, the divine manifestations of the Divine Spirit![^841]

*Theotokion:*
Thee, the beauty of Jacob which God loved, which He chose, O Virgin, do we all hymn as the only blessed one unto all ages![^842]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin was with child in the womb, and gave birth unto a Son, Emmanuel, God and man, whose Name is the Orient; Whom magnifying, we bless the Virgin.[^843]*

*Troparion:*
Having assembled today, ye faithful, let us all celebrate the sacred solemnity of the sacred father who shone forth by self-control, and wholly consecrated himself unto God by a life of perfection!

*Troparion:*
Like a tree wast thou planted beside the waters, O venerable one, bearing fruits of currents of spiritual virtues; and having pitched a nest like an eagle upon an orchard tree, O father, thou didst wing thine intellect unto the heavenly things![^844]

*Troparion:*
Having prophetically foreseen thine own departure, O David, thou didst proclaim it unto these people, when thou hadst calmed the great indignation of the emperor which had come upon them, O wise one![^845]

*Troparion:*
Thy holy memory sanctifies us today that celebrate it with faith, O venerable David; for holily didst thou pass thy life, O holy one, and after thy falling asleep thou dost ever rejoice with the saints!

*Theotokion:*
A light-bearing door did Ezekiel foresee thee to be, through which passed the true Light, Christ our God; Whom do thou earnestly entreat, O pure one, to open unto me the doors of repentance, I pray thee![^846]

*Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Hear, ye women" (Жєны̀ ꙋ҆слы́шите):*
From worldly gladness as from defilement didst thou turn aside, and having withered the flesh by abstinence, thou didst renew the strength of the soul, O venerable one, and wast enriched with heavenly glory, O glorious one; therefore cease not to pray unto the Lord for us![^847]

*Theotokion:*

Truly do we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, O Sovereign Lady, who through thee have been saved; for ineffably didst thou bear the Lord Who destroyed death by the Cross, and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable; with whom we praise thee, O Virgin![^848]

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 6:*
Venerable father, thou gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, until thou didst free soul and body from passions, and preparedst thyself as a dwelling for the Spirit; for Christ, coming with the Father, made an abode in thee; and having become a pleasing servant of the consubstantial Trinity, O great preacher, our father David, pray that we may be saved![^849]

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйся по́стникѡвъ):*
When thou, who art the vine, didst see the unplanted Cluster, Whom thou didst bring forth, hanging upon the Tree, and pierced in His divine ribs with a spear: What is this, cried she, O my Son and God? How dost Thou, Who healest all infirmities and passions, endure passions, being impassible according to Thy divine nature? What have the thankless people rendered unto Thee, the Benefactor, instead of good deeds? cried the all-pure one: but by His passions pray unceasingly that I may be freed from passions, that I may glorify thee![^850]

*The Same Month, on the 27th Day:*
*The memory of our venerable father Sampson the Hospitable.[^851]*

*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льніи мꙋ́ченицы):*
O all-lauded father Sampson, with the burning heats of self-control thou didst wither the carnal impulses; thou didst take up thine abode in the wilderness like Elijah of old, having purified thine intellect by unceasing nods unto God; and now pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls![^852]

*Sticheron:*
O God-wise father Sampson, thou wast numbered among the fasters, adorned with impassibility, and didst take up thine abode in the habitations of the venerable, where is the unsetting light, and where is the Tree of life; and now pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls![^853]

*Sticheron:*
O God-inspired Sampson, thou wast a luminary, enlightening the world under the sun with the rays of miracles, driving away the darkness of soul-corrupting diseases, dispelling demonic gloom; and now pray that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.[^854]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*
Woe is me! What shall become of me, having defiled my mind and soul and body with transgressions? What shall I do? And how shall I escape the unendurable flame, the unyielding and eternal bonds? But O all-immaculate [Lady], before the end pray to thy Son to grant me remission.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*
When the ewe-lamb and undefiled Mistress beheld upon the Cross her Lamb, having neither form nor comeliness, woe is me! lamenting, she said: Whither hath Thy beauty departed, O sweetest One? Whither Thy fair comeliness? Whither the radiant grace of Thine image, O my most beloved Son?

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
In thy patience thou didst acquire thy reward, O venerable father, persevering unceasingly in prayers, loving the poor and satisfying them; but pray to Christ God, O Sampson, merciful and blessed one, that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: I sing to thee, O Sampson, faithfully in divine words. Joseph. Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song to Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He hath been glorified.[^855]*

*Troparion:*
Unto me who strive to praise thy most radiant solemnity with songs, send down light, O all-blessed one, who standest crowned before the Master's throne.

*Troparion:*
Desiring only that which leads thee unto life, thou didst pass by the fair things of the world and didst acquire a supermundane heritage; therefore, having come together, O Sampson, we honor thee.

*Troparion:*
Submitting unto the divine law, O richly praised one, rejoicing thou didst ascend to the highest virtue, having become merciful unto all; therefore we now call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Thee, who beyond word didst bear God, as the mighty protection of those vanquished by misfortunes, O all-immaculate one, we unceasingly glorify and call blessed.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death...[^856]* sin, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing to Thee.

*Troparion:*
Let us praise the sacred Sampson; for imitating the loving-kindness of God, he was merciful to the sick, and clothing unto all that were naked.

*Troparion:*
Having spent thy divine life holy and honorably, thou wast united with the saints and didst reach the habitations of the venerable, being accounted worthy of ineffable illumination.

*Troparion:*
Blessed art thou, and it shall be well with thee, who hast kept the divine laws of our Lord and God, O father; pray to Him that thy flock may be saved.

*Theotokion:*
Drive away the darkness of my soul and mind, O pure one, and grant me to live as in an appointed day, performing saving commandments.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Having shone forth through the Spirit as a radiant luminary, O all-blessed Sampson, thou dost honorably enlighten us in thy house; therefore, O father, thou dost distribute to those who are in diverse diseases the healings given thee by God. Therefore, by thy supplications deliver us from passions.[^857]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou hast renewed the mortal nature of the earthborn that had decayed in passions, and hast raised up all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore, as is due, all of us call thee blessed, O Virgin most glorified, even as thou didst foretell.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God being crucified willingly; whom cease not to implore, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions.[^858]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.[^859]*

*Troparion:*
As a most radiant star, thou dost enlighten the hearts of all of us who worthily call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Having cast off slumber from thine eyes, thou didst watch unceasingly, and didst put bodily pleasures to sleep.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast sufficient unto the poor for Christ's sake, and didst receive the heritage on high, O venerable one, rejoicing.

*Theotokion:*
Thee, O all-immaculate Mistress, radiant palace of the Word, do we hymn and call blessed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Giver of light and Creator of the ages, guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for we know no other God beside Thee.[^860]*

*Troparion:*
The most pure Light that rested in thee made thee entirely radiant, O father, enlightening the faithful with the bright rays of thy virtues.

*Troparion:*
By much compassion thou wast wealth unto the poor, and manifestly didst arise as a physician unto those who were in infirmity, taking away diseases through the healing of them.

*Troparion:*
Having cleansed the mire of passions, thou didst show thyself a pure temple of the most holy and honorable Spirit, thyself in truth, O God-bearing father; therefore we praise thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thee, O most holy and pure one, the comeliness of Jacob, do we hymn in faith with divine voices, who hast borne for us the flesh-bearing God, before Whom all things tremble.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the fathomless abyss of Thy loving-kindness: from corruption, O God, lead me up.[^861]*

*Troparion:*
Thou wast enriched with heavenly treasures, loving compassion, O glorious Sampson, and with a mature mind didst open thy heart to the poor.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst erect a sanctified temple to the glory of our God, running unto which the faithful are cleansed from manifold diseases, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst spend thy life purely, and didst take up thine abode venerably with the venerable; pray for us who honor thee in faith, O God-inspired one.

*Theotokion:*
A high heaven wast thou, O all-immaculate one, and a pure palace, and a radiant bridal chamber of God the King of all, by divine grace.

*Kontakion, Tone 8. Podoben: "As first-fruits" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
As unto an excellent physician and acceptable intercessor, running unto thy divine shrine, O God-wise, venerable Sampson, having come together in love, rejoicing with psalms and hymns, we glorify Christ Who grants thee such grace of healings.[^862]

*Ikos:*
Let us praise the venerable Sampson, who accomplished an aged, gentle, meek, and blameless life, who splendidly received the firm and ineffable grace of miracles—the nourisher of the hungry, the helper of those in need, and a great defender, who erected for the sick a divine temple of healings.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*When the golden image was served on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths took no heed of the godless command; but cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^863]*

*Troparion:*
Having the mighty strength of Christ in temptations, O all-blessed one, thou didst pass through the tempest of life without sinking, O venerable one, and singing didst reach the calm haven: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Moved by much wisdom and spiritual love, thou didst build a great church, O father, running unto which at all times, all receive healing, crying out and saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
A radiant lampstand of virtues, enlightened by divine light, didst thou appear, O Sampson, and didst illumine the ends of the universe; therefore, having come together in faith, we call thee blessed, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
The fleece of old prefigured thy pure womb, which received the heavenly rain that bedews all that are held by the thirst of sin, O all-hymned one; blessed art thou who in the flesh didst bear God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew: hymn, ye works, as the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^864]*

*Troparion:*
Moving thy tongue joyfully in divine songs, O father Sampson, by self-control thou didst enslave carnal passions unto the soul, and didst show thyself a sincere servant unto Christ and an heir of the city on high.

*Troparion:*
Thy pure mind, adorned with divine splendors, appeared most radiant, and receptive of honorable divine rays, O all-blessed one; therefore we honor thee, O Sampson, unto the ages.

*Troparion:*
Having a holy life, O most glorious, all-blessed Sampson, thou wast manifestly glorified, having passed over unto God; for behold, thy memory and thy righteousness abide unto the ages.[^865]

*Theotokion:*
Calm the fierce tempest of my passions, O pure and blessed one, and restrain all the enemies that unsparingly war against my poverty without cause, that in faith I may sing to thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*God the Word, Who from God with ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who through eating had grievously fallen into corruption, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: with one accord in faith* with songs we magnify.[^866]

*Troparion:*
Behold, thy memory, O father, like the sun hath shone forth upon all, brightly enlightening those who therein glorify thee as a servant of Christ, an heir of His Kingdom, and a partaker with all the venerable.

*Troparion:*
How radiant is thy life, how great thy glory, how rich thy tomb, O wise one, wherein thy precious body lay! It pours forth health unto those who in faith have recourse unto it, and who at all times ask thine aid.

*Troparion:*
By thy most glorious healing, O father, which thou didst acquire through afflictions, we pray thee, fail not to visit us ever, unceasingly standing before us in great compassion, and lightening the illnesses and labors of our souls.

*Troparion:*
Shining sun-like rays upon those who honor thee, O father, enlighten the senses of our souls, and make us partakers of divine brightness through thy prayers, O blessed one, we beseech thee.

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee as a radiant cloud of the unsetting Sun, O pure one, in faith we cry unto thee: Rejoice, glory of the apostles, crown of glorious martyrs, and salvation of the faithful, O unwedded Virgin!

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "The sky with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Thou didst distribute thy wealth to the poor in a manner pleasing unto God, and didst wholly dedicate thyself unto the Lord, O Sampson; pray to Him to deliver us from all passions.[^867]

*Theotokion:*
By thy mighty protection, from the assault of enemies, preserve all of us, thy servants, unharmed, O pure one; for thee alone have we acquired as a refuge in needs.

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^868]  
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2).[^869]*  
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 67 (Luke 12:32–40).[^870]*  
*Communion Verse:*
In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be: he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^871]

*JUNE 28:*
*The Same Month, on the 28th Day:*
*Translation of the Relics of the Holy Wonderworkers and Unmercenaries Cyrus and John.[^872]*

*Stichera on 6, Tone 4. Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Let us all praise with songs the pair of combatant martyrs who possess Trinity-like brightness, the foundation of faith, breathing forth flowers, the true sweet fragrance of the knowledge of Christ: Cyrus and with him great John, as they pray unceasingly unto the Lord for us.

*Sticheron:*
By brightness of life and fasting didst thou shine forth, and afterward by suffering didst thou adorn thy soul, O glorious Cyrus; and thou, O wise John, didst leave behind earthly warfare and didst find the heavenly, both of you imploring the Savior for those who call your memory blessed, O blessed ones.

*Sticheron:*
Ye showed yourselves as physicians unto the infirm, O blessed ones, and unsetting luminaries, defenders agreeing in the confession of the divine faith, partakers with the martyrs, having truly received crowns from Christ, O glorious Cyrus and wise John; unceasingly pray to the Savior for those who praise you in faith.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Today the pair of martyrs hath shone forth upon us, healing our spiritual diseases, the wonderworking Cyrus and John: the one having embraced the solitary life equal to the angels, living therein unto the end, was joined unto Christ by martyr's blood; and the other, having passed his life in military ranks, is enrolled among the citizens of the heavenly host. Therefore in their memory they grant healings unto those who celebrate it in faith, praying for our souls.[^873]

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder!" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*
I cannot bear, O Child, to see Thee fallen asleep upon the Tree, Who grantest vigilance unto all, that unto those who of old through the fruit of transgression fell asleep in the sleep of perdition, Thou mightest grant divine and saving vigilance, the Virgin said, weeping: whom we magnify.

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 2:*
Come, O ye company of the faithful, today let us crown with songs Cyrus and John: fertile fields of grace, and ungrudging givers unto those that ask; for in manifold ways they have appeared as most radiant physicians unto all[^874] radiant; therefore do they also pray for our souls.[^875]

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the undefiled Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly drawn as a man unto the slaughter, weeping, she said: Dost Thou now hasten to make me childless, O Christ Who gavest birth unto me? What is this that Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all? Yet I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness which is beyond mind and word, O Lover of mankind.[^876]

*Troparion, Tone 5:*
Thou Who hast granted us the wonders of Thy holy martyrs as an invincible rampart, O Christ God: by their supplications scatter the counsels of the heathen, strengthen the sceptres of the realm, for Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

*At Matins, both Canons from the Octoechos, and the Canon of the Saints on 6.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us sing unto Christ, Who drowned the tyranny of Pharaoh in the sea, and guided by His very power, for He hath been glorified unto the ages.[^877]*

*Troparion:*
Unto Him Who showed forth in the world wonderworkers and physicians, honorable Cyrus and John the martyr, healing the passions of all by grace, let us sing, O ye faithful, and let us honor the memory of the martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Having rejected the present things, lovers of the future, by blood they were conformed unto the true Lover; therefore, suffering also in wounds, they bravely endured.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened with boldness and having established their soul, instructed in Christ, they suffered manfully with tortures, and lawfully wove crowns.

*Theotokion:*
Save me, O Mother of truth, who am grievously held by passions and often submerged; and direct me, O pure one, unto the salvation of the calm haven.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy like the Lord, and there is none righteous like our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord.[^878]*

*Troparion:*
With faith Thou didst sharpen their hearts, O Christ, with hope Thou didst forge them, with love Thou didst enkindle them; armed with these three, Thou didst weave for them a thrice-woven crown.[^879]

*Troparion:*
Nobly did the invincible martyrs stand against the ungodly tormentors, having passed unharmed through the snares of delusion; for having armed themselves with Christ, they also rejoice with Him.

*Troparion:*
As God-built pillars and firm foundations, they fixed the ascents of faith upon the rock, having drunk from the rock the ever-living water which is from the cup of Christ[^880] thou becamest a dwelling for the Spirit, O pure one, having received the fullness of all the Godhead bodily; and having given birth unto the Savior and Lord, and the Deliverer of our race.[^881]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having trampled underfoot the downward-dragging passions, unto the divine height of martyrdom, O passion-bearers, ye were brightly lifted up by grace, O Cyrus and John, luminaries of the universe; therefore we pray you: deliver us from the darkness of sin and from diseases, praying unto God Who is over all.[^882]  
*Twice.*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Being a lover of sin, I pray thee who didst bear the sinless God Who takes away the sins of the world, O all-pure one: have compassion upon my much-sinning soul, and cleanse my many sins; for unto sinners thou art cleansing, and salvation unto the faithful, and protection.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, she who at the last gave birth unto Thee in the flesh, Who wast born of the unoriginate Father, woe is me! cried aloud, O sweetest Jesus: How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O longsuffering One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*From the overshadowed mountain, the prophet foresaw Thee in divine vision, O Word, about to be incarnate of the only Birthgiver of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power.[^883]*

*Troparion:*
Body and soul and mind, and all the faculties of the heart, did ye sacrifice unto Christ by faith; and having overcome the cruelty of the tormentors, lawfully were ye crowned, O holy ones.

*Troparion:*
Having surrendered your body to sword and fire, ye became acceptable sacrifices unto God, and for a sweet-smelling savor ye were mystically offered upon the table.

*Troparion:*
An unquenchable radiance hath shone forth upon the world: the relics of Thy martyrs, O Benefactor; whereby the darkness of folly was driven away, and in its place the knowledge of God was introduced.

*Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, gate of the Divine Word: cover and preserve from all evils those who are gathered together in thy church and praise thee, the all-pure one, with songs.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Rising early, we cry unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, beside Thee we know no other.[^884]*

*Troparion:*
Having acquired virgins as your fellow-travelers, O passion-bearers, ye took the perfect crown of victory, rejoicing[^885] enlightened by the dawn of divine radiance, O all-lauded martyrs, ye ever enlighten the world.[^886]

*Troparion:*
Having become conformed unto the passions of the Lord, O passion-bearers, ye endured sufferings, invincible in noble-mindedness.

*Theotokion:*
Calm the unsteady tempest of my passions, O thou that didst give birth unto God, the Helmsman and Lord.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a radiant robe, Thou that deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God.[^887]*

*Troparion:*
Having armed yourselves well with the armor of faith, O martyrs, ye firmly wounded the adversary, the enemy and foe.

*Troparion:*
The memory of Thy holy ones do we glorify in songs, O Christ; and in faith we offer adoration unto Thee, our God.

*Troparion:*
Emulating Thy passions, O Christ, the glorious martyrs endured tortures; by their supplications, O God, save all of us.

*Theotokion:*
The church of God, and the ark, and the animate bridal chamber, and the heavenly gate, O Birthgiver of God, do we the faithful proclaim thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 3. Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Having received the gift of wonders from divine grace, O holy ones, ye work wonders unceasingly in the world, invisibly cutting off all our passions by surgical craft, O God-wise Cyrus, with glorious John; for in truth ye are divine physicians.[^888]

*Ikos:*
Having dedicated yourselves unto God, O holy ones, for His sake ye endured every cruel trial, eagerly dying, O noble martyrs; and after your end ye pour forth divine gifts unto all that are in diverse diseases, and heal from many evils, whereof I the wretched one am one and the first: for in body and soul I suffer from cruel wounds, and in faith I cry unto you: Heal me, for ye are divine physicians![^889]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save the youths from the fire by an angel, and didst change the thundering furnace into dew: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^890]*

*Troparion:*
Having become lovers of heavenly glory, Thy sufferers, O Lord, Cyrus and John, left behind temporary and perishing glory.

*Troparion:*
Surrendering their body as a reasonable sacrifice, and pouring out blood unto Thee, O God, as a ministry, Thy sufferers, O Jesus, were made whole-burnt offerings.

*Troparion:*
How fearful is the assembly of the martyrs, for even after death[^891] it casts out spirits, and grants healing unto the sick.[^892]

*Theotokion:*
Beyond cause thou didst give birth unto the Cause of all things, Who through extreme goodness became man; therefore with one accord, O pure one, we call thee blessed.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who was begotten of the Father before the ages, and in the latter times was incarnate of the Mother: ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^893]*

*Troparion:*
Having abhorred idol sacrifices, the noble martyrs by their suffering offered themselves as spiritual sacrifices upon the heavenly altar table.

*Troparion:*
Celebrating mystical services, they offered the consecration of their members unto Him as acceptable sacrifices: body and blood upon the altar table and cup.[^894]

*Troparion:*
Having escaped the stumbling-block of the grandmother of our race, ye found the stream of delight, and tasted of the divine tree, O maidens, having adorned yourselves with sufferings.[^895]

*Theotokion:*
The divine prophecies of the prophets received their fulfillment in thee, O most holy one, convincing the whole world worthily to sing to thee, O pure one.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*We magnify thee, the Mother of God, and we glorify thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, as having given birth unto the Savior of our souls.[^896]*

*Troparion:*
The armor-bearers of Christ, physicians unto the sick, Cyrus and John, having received the grace of wonders from God, heal all of us.

*Troparion:*
Standing before us, the invincible Cyrus and John completely drive away spiritual passions by faith and by the power of Christ.

*Troparion:*
O God the Creator, Whom Cyrus and John preached of old: have compassion upon all who celebrate the memory of their martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
Let us all divinely honor the Mother of our God; for through her have we who hymn her been accounted worthy of honor on high.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "The sky with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
O wonderworking luminaries, Cyrus with John, heal our spiritual and bodily diseases, having received the grace of healings from the Lord.[^897]

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the cause of the good things given by God unto the world, O Birthgiver of God; but even now pray for the common salvation unto God Who is easily reconciled.

*At the Aposticha, from the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 4:*
With praiseworthy songs, O ye faithful, let us magnify Cyrus and John, who in spirit are blood-brothers[^898] kinsmen and brotherly in mind according to the flesh; with whom, praising noble Athanasia and her offspring, Theodota, Eudoxia, and Theoctiste, passion-bearers and venerable ones, who pray to Christ for our souls.[^899]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one wept and cried aloud: "What is this, O my Son? What have the thankless people rendered unto Thee in return for the good things Thou hast done for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most dearly beloved? I marvel, O Compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!"

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Octoechos; and Ode 3 of the Saints.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* To the saints that are in His earth.[^900]
*Verse:* I foresaw the Lord always before me.
*Epistle to the Corinthians, composite 153 [1 Cor. 12:27–13:8].*
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous cried.
*Verse:* Many are the afflictions of the righteous.
*Gospel according to Matthew, composite 34, from the midpoint [Matt. 10:1, 5–8].*
*Communion Hymn:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.

*The same month, on the 29th day.:*
The Holy, Glorious, All-Praised, and Preeminent Apostles Peter and Paul.
We celebrate a vigil.

*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 4, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*

Thou hast given praise unto Thy Church, O Lord: Thine honorable Apostles, wherein the spiritual beacons shine forth, Peter and Paul, like rational stars illumining the universe, through whom Thou hast enlightened the western darkness, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls.

Thou hast given confirmation unto Thy Church, O Lord: the steadfastness of Peter, the intellect of Paul, and radiant wisdom, their true theology dispelling Hellenic delusion;[^901]

therefore, mystically guided by them both, we hymn Thee, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls.

Thou hast given a pattern of conversion unto those who sin, in both of Thine Apostles: the one having denied Thee, indeed, in Thy Passion, and repented; the other having opposed Thy proclamation, and believed; and both being the foremost of the assembly of Thy friends, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls.

*Glory, Tone 8.*
*By John the Monk:*
Thou wast an insulter and persecutor of the Church, O all-blessed Paul; but having been called from heaven, thou didst defend her most gloriously. Pray now for her,[^902] that she may be delivered from tribulations and that our souls may be saved.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thy protection, O Virgin Mother of God, is a spiritual medicine-house; for fleeing unto it, we are delivered from spiritual infirmities.

*Prokeimenon of the day.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

O Peter, foundation of the Apostles, rock of Christ's Church, beginning of Christians, shepherd the sheep of thy sheepfold gloriously; preserve thy lambs from predatory wolves; deliver thy flock from cruel misfortunes; for we have truly acquired thee as an unslumbering intercessor before God, and boasting in thee we all are saved.

*Verse:* Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. [Ps. 18:4 / LXX 18:5]

O Paul, catcher of the nations, champion of Christians, light of the universe, unceasing mouth of Christ the living God, who like the sun didst traverse all the ends of the earth by the preaching of the divine faith: loose from the bonds of sin all who praise thee and who adorn themselves with thy prayers.

*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. [Ps. 18:1 / LXX 18:2]

O Peter, preeminent disciple, and Paul, fulfillment of the Apostles, first heralds: pray now with boldness earnestly unto God, and send down together upon us who grieve speedy help and deliverance from misfortunes, that we may boast in faith, and now in your shadow glorifying your protection in our works.

*Glory, Tone 4.*
*By John the Monk:*
Christ having chosen thee first, O Peter, as the foundation of the faith, the Master of all foretold unto thee, crowning thee with God:

foretold: "Blessed art thou, Simon bar Jonah, for flesh and blood has not revealed it, but My Father Who is in heaven has made thee My sacred herald. Pray unto Him, O divinely blessed Apostle, that He grant us great mercy."

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Deliver us from misfortunes...

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O foremost enthroned of the Apostles, and teachers of the universe: intercede with the Master of all, that He grant peace to the universe, and to our souls great mercy.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from the ages...

*At Great Vespers, after the customary Psalm, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 8, Tone 2. Automelon:*
*By Andrew Pyrrhus:*[^903]
*Tone 2, Automelon: "With what crowns of praise" (Кі́ими похва́льными вѣнцы̀):*

With what crowns of praise shall we wreath Peter and Paul, divided in bodies and united in spirit, the foremost champions of the divine heralds: the one, indeed, as the leader of the Apostles, and the other as having labored more than the rest? For them truly, with crowns of immortal glory, Christ our God does crown, Who has great mercy.
*Thrice.*

With what melodies of song shall we garland Peter and Paul: the wings of the knowledge of God, who flew across the ends of the earth and ascended unto heaven; the hands of the Gospel of grace; the feet of the true proclamation; the rivers of wisdom; the horns of the Cross, through which Christ cast down the demonic arrogance, He that has great mercy?
*Thrice.*

With what spiritual songs shall we praise Peter and Paul: who slew the uncloseable mouths of godlessness; the dread swords of the Spirit; the radiant adornments of Rome; the nourishers of the whole universe; the divinely written tablets of the New Testament; which Christ proclaimed as spiritual in Sion, He that has great mercy?
*Twice.*

*Glory, Tone 4. By John the Monk:*
By the threefold questioning, "Peter, lovest thou Me?", Christ repaired the threefold denial. Therefore Simon also said unto the Knower of secrets: "Lord, Thou knowest all things, Thou understandest all things; Thou knowest that I love Thee." Therefore the Savior said unto him: "Feed My sheep, feed My chosen ones, feed My lambs, which I have purchased with My blood unto salvation." Pray unto Him, O divinely blessed Apostle, that He grant us great mercy.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
He Who for thy sake...

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*

*Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Peter:*[^904]
Brethren: Blessed be the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, Who according to His great mercy has begotten us again unto a living hope, by the resurrection of Jesus Christ from the dead, unto an inheritance incorruptible, and undefiled, and that fades not away, reserved in heaven for you, who are kept by the power of God through faith unto salvation ready to be revealed in the last time. Wherein rejoice, though now for a little while, if need be, ye are in heaviness through manifold trials: that the trial of your faith, being much more precious than of gold that perishes, though it be tried with fire, might be found unto praise and honor and glory at the appearing of Jesus Christ; Whom having not seen, ye love; in Whom, though now ye see Him not, yet believing, ye rejoice with joy unspeakable and full of glory, receiving the end of your faith: the salvation of souls.

*Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Peter [Chapter 1]:*
Beloved: Having girded up the loins of your mind, be sober, and hope to the end for the grace that is being brought unto you at the revelation of Jesus Christ; as obedient children, not fashioning yourselves according to the former lusts of your ignorance; but as He Who called you is holy, so be ye holy in all your manner of life. Because it is written: "Be ye holy, for I am holy." And if ye call on the Father, Who without respect of persons judges according to every man's work, pass the time of your dwelling here in fear; knowing that ye were not redeemed with corruptible things, as silver or gold, from your vain manner of life received by tradition from your fathers, but with the precious blood of Christ, as of a lamb without blemish and without spot.

*Reading from the Catholic Epistle of Peter [Chapter 2]:*
Beloved: I beseech you as strangers and pilgrims, abstain from fleshly lusts, which war against the soul; having your conversation honest among the Gentiles, that, whereas they speak against you as evildoers, they may by your good works, which they shall behold, glorify God in the day of visitation. Submit yourselves therefore to every ordinance of man for the Lord's sake: whether it be to the king, as supreme, or unto governors, as unto them that are sent by him for the punishment of evildoers, and for the praise of them that do well. For so is the will of God, that with well doing ye may put to silence the ignorance of foolish men: as free, and not using your liberty for a cloke of maliciousness, but as the servants of God. Honor all men. Love the brotherhood.

Fear God. Honor the king. Servants, be subject to your masters with all fear, not only to the good and gentle, but also to the froward. For this is thankworthy before God, if a man for conscience toward God endure grief, suffering wrongfully. For what glory is it, if, when ye be buffeted for your faults, ye take it patiently? But if, when ye do well and suffer for it, ye take it patiently, this is acceptable with God. For even hereunto were ye called, because Christ also suffered for us, leaving us an example, that ye should follow His steps: Who did no sin, neither was guile found in His mouth; Who, when He was reviled, reviled not again; when He suffered, He threatened not; but committed Himself to Him that judges righteously; Who His own Self bore our sins in His own body on the tree, that we, being dead to sins, should live unto righteousness.

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 2.*
*Idiomela.*
*By Andrew of Jerusalem:*[^905]
Come today, ye assemblies of the faithful, a laudable and fair gathering: let us wreath the chosen craftsmen of grace, Peter and Paul, with fitting praises! For they ungrudgingly proclaimed the word unto all; and by these things they enriched the world with the gifts of the Spirit; and being branches of the true Vine, they cultivated for us a ripe cluster that gladdens our hearts. Unto them, with unveiled face and a pure conscience, let us cry aloud, saying: "Rejoice, destroyers of irrational things, and servants of reasonable things! Rejoice, fair chosen ones of the Creator and Provider of all! Rejoice, intercessors for good things and expellers of deceitful things; whom let us beseech to pray continually unto the Creator and Teacher, that He grant steadfast peace to the world, and to our souls great mercy!"

*By Arsenius:*[^906]
Let us praise the disciples of Christ and foundations of the Church: the true pillars and walls, and divine trumpets of Christ's teachings and sufferings, the preeminent Peter and Paul, as protectors of the whole world! For these, having traversed the breadth of the whole earth, as with a plow sowed the faith, and planted the knowledge of God in all, manifesting the word of the Trinity. O Peter, rock and foundation, and Paul, chosen vessel, who as yoked oxen of Christ drew all unto the knowledge of God: the nations, cities, and islands, and brought the Hebrews again unto Christ; and they pray that our souls may be saved!

*By Germanus:*[^907]
O Peter, preeminent of the glorious Apostles, rock of the faith; and Paul and marvelous Paul, orator and light of the holy churches: standing before the divine throne, intercede with Christ for us!

O Paul, mouth of the Lord, foundation of teachings, once indeed a persecutor of Jesus the Savior, but now become the foremost enthroned of the Apostles, O blessed one! Therefore, O wise one, thou didst behold unspeakable things, having ascended even unto the third heaven, and didst cry out: "Come with me, and we shall not be deprived of good things!"

*Tone 3. By John the Monk:*
Citizens of the Jerusalem on high, rock of the faith, orator of the Church of Christ, Trinity-bearing pair, catchers of the world: having today left behind the things on earth, they have departed through suffering unto God, and with boldness they pray unto Him that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, Tone 5.*
*By Byzantios:*[^908]
The Wisdom of God, the co-eternal Word of the Father, as He foretold in the Gospels: "Ye are the fruitful vines, O all-praised Apostles, who bear in your branches the ripe and fair cluster, eating of which, we faithful sweeten our taste unto gladness." O Peter, rock of the faith, and Paul, boast of the universe: confirm the flock which ye acquired by your teachings!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
We bless thee, O Mother of God...

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera,*
*Tone 1.*
*By Andrew of Crete:*
Who shall tell of thy bonds and tribulations in every city, O glorious Apostle Paul? The labors, afflictions, and vigils; the hardships in hunger and thirst; the trials in cold and nakedness; the basket, and the beatings with rods; the stonings, and the journeyings; the deeps of shipwreck? Thou wast a spectacle unto angels and men! All things, therefore, didst thou endure in Christ Who strengthened thee, that thou mightest win the world in Christ Jesus thy Lord. Therefore we beseech thee, who faithfully celebrate thy memory: pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved!

*Verse:* Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. [Ps. 18:4 / LXX 18:5]

Who shall recount thy bonds and tribulations in every city, O glorious Apostle? Or who shall set forth thy struggles and labors, wherewith thou didst labor in the Gospel of Christ, that thou mightest win all, and bring the Church unto Christ? But ask that she preserve thy good confession even unto her final breath, O Paul, Apostle and teacher of the churches.
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. [Ps. 18:1 / LXX 18:2]

Let us praise Peter and Paul, the great beacons of the Church: for they shone forth more brightly than the sun upon the firmament of the faith, and with the rays of proclamation they raised up the nations from ignorance. The one, indeed, being nailed to the cross, made his journey unto heaven, where he received the keys of the Kingdom from Christ; the other, being beheaded with the sword, departed unto the Savior; worthily are they blessed, and they both proclaim unto Israel that they wrongfully stretched out their hands against the Lord Himself. Therefore, by their prayers, O Christ our God, cast down those who rage against us, and confirm the faith of the Orthodox, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Glory, Tone 6.*
*By Ephrem of Caria:*[^909]
A joyful feast has shone forth unto the ends of the earth today: the all-venerable memory of the all-wise and preeminent Apostles, Peter and Paul! Therefore Rome also rejoices together, making festival in songs and chants; let us also, brethren, celebrate, having accomplished this all-honorable day: "Rejoice, O Apostle Peter, intimate friend of thy Teacher, Christ our God! Rejoice, O most dearly beloved Paul, herald of the faith and teacher of the universe!" As ye have boldness, O divinely chosen yoke-pair, pray unto Christ our God that our souls may be saved.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
My Maker and Redeemer...

*At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion:*
*Tone 4. Twice:*
Written at Small Vespers: "O foremost enthroned...", and "O Virgin Mother of God, rejoice..." once; and a reading.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Apostles, twice:*
*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from the ages...

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having abandoned the chief part of bodily strength,[^910] receiving from the Father the divine revelation from heaven of the incarnation of the Word, thou didst faithfully cry aloud with boldness unto thy Creator: "I know Thee to be the consubstantial Son of God!" Therefore, according to thy worth, thou wast truly shown to be the rock of the faith, and keeper of the keys of grace, O Apostle Peter. Pray unto Christ God that He grant forgiveness of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "When the stone was sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Let us the faithful worthily praise Peter, the rock of the Church, the all-praised, and Paul the defender, the worldly net, as possessing the heavenly keys; for by them the universe was enlightened with faith with faith in the Trinity. Glory to Him Who has glorified you! Glory to Him Who has strengthened you! Glory to Him Who for your sake has granted eternal life!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Thy divine hands, wherewith thou didst bear the Creator, O all-pure Virgin, Who was incarnate in His goodness: stretch them forth and beseech Him to deliver us from temptations, passions, and tribulations, who praise thee with love and cry aloud: "Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who has delivered us through thy childbearing!"

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having received the call from heaven from Christ, thou wast shown to be a herald of the light; thou didst graciously illumine all with thy teachings; for having stripped away the service of the letter of the Law, thou didst cause the understanding of the Spirit to shine forth upon the faithful. Therefore thou wast also worthily caught up on high unto the third heaven, and didst attain unto Paradise, O Apostle Paul: pray unto Christ God that He grant forgiveness of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
With light-beaming rays, like stars, ye illumine the ends of the earth with the teachings of the holy proclamation, O heavenly initiates of mysteries, Apostles of the Lord.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
As a fervent protection for those in tribulations, and our helper, and reconciliation unto God, through whom we have been delivered from corruption: we the faithful bless the Mother of God.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Let us praise Peter and the all-wise Paul, who appeared as the preeminent among the disciples, the great and radiant beacons; for having shone forth with the fire of the divine Spirit, they burned up all the gloom of deception. Therefore they were also worthily shown to be armorbearers of the Kingdom on high, and enthroned companions of grace. For this cause we cry aloud: "O Apostles of Christ God, ask forgiveness of transgressions for those who celebrate your holy memory with love!" Twice.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
As the all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as the unwedded Mother of the Redeemer, as the receptacle of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: seeing that I am a defiled dwelling-place of iniquity and have consciously become a plaything of demons, hasten to deliver me from their evildoing, and make me a radiant dwelling-place of virtues, O light-bearing and incorruptible one! Drive away the cloud of passions and vouchsafe me communion with the things on high and with the unwaning light, by thy prayers.

*The Anabathmoi: The 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. [Ps. 18:4 / LXX 18:5]
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God. [Ps. 18:1 / LXX 18:2]
*Let every breath.*
*Verse:* Praise God in His saints. [Ps. 150:1]
*Gospel according to John, composite 67 [John 21:15–25].*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
The true heralds of piety and most radiant stars of the Church, let us honor with laudable songs: Peter the rock of the faith, and Paul the true teacher and initiate of Christ's mysteries; for having sown the true word in the hearts of the faithful, they bestow fruitfulness upon all, and they pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Two Canons, the composition of John of Damascus.*[^911]
*The First, of the Holy Apostle Peter, Tone 4, with the Heirmos on 8: the Heirmos twice, Troparia on 6.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
There is none like unto Thee, O all-glorious Lord, for with a strong hand Thou hast delivered the people whom Thou hast acquired, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Today, let us worthily hymn the foremost of the Apostles, as the first-called of Christ, with divinely inspired songs.

Knowing thee beforehand, the Pre-eternal One appointed thee, O all-blessed Peter, as protector of His Church, and foremost-enthroned.

Neither flesh nor blood, but the Father inspired thee, O Apostle, to declare Christ the true Son of the Most High God.

*Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the holy mountain, the most exalted chariot, the Mother of God, and true Virgin after childbirth.

*Another Canon, of the Holy Apostle Paul, Troparia on 6. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
That which was cut severed the unseverable, and the sun beheld the earth which it had not seen; the water drowned the fierce enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable; and a song was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*
Calling things that are not as though they were, Christ, by His divine intellect, O all-blessed Paul, chose thee from thy mother's womb to bear His divine Name, which is above every name, before the nations; for He is gloriously glorified!

Proclaiming beforehand unto thee the future radiance of piety, O Paul, and the cleansing of deception, Christ appeared on the mount shining like lightning shining like lightning, darkening indeed the bodily eyes, but enlightening those of the soul with the understanding of the Trinity; for He is gloriously glorified!

Accomplishing, indeed, the circumcision on the eighth day, and being a zealot for the ancestral traditions, O Paul, of the seed of the Hebrews, of the tribe of Benjamin, and appearing according to the Law as a Pharisee: thou didst count all things as refuse, and didst gain Christ; for He is gloriously glorified!

*Theotokion:*
O pure Mother of God the Almighty, having sprung from a royal tribe, O Sovereign Lady, and having borne in the flesh, beyond nature, the one God Who reigns over all: deliver me from misfortunes, who sing unto thy Son; for He is gloriously glorified!

*Katavasia:* I shall open my mouth...[^912]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
We boast not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth, but in Thee, the Father's hypostatic Wisdom, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
The sweet lips of Christ God showed thee to be blessed and a firm treasure of the Kingdom; therefore we hymn thee, O Apostle Peter.

Upon the rock of thy theology the Master Jesus established the Church unshakable, wherein we glorify thee, O Apostle Peter.

As greater than angels, Peter was in the flesh; for Christ God said that in His radiant coming he should be a judge and an assessor.

*Theotokion:*
O unwedded one, who didst bear God in the flesh: establish me who am bowed down by passionate assaults; for there is none pure, save thee, as a helper.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of those in darkness, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast laid as a foundation stone for faithful souls, precious and cornerstone, of the Savior and Lord.

O all-blessed Paul, upon thy foundation of all-praised virtues, I pray that the brightness of the soul may be builded.

Ever carrying about in thy body the dying of Jesus in perfection, O Paul, thou wast counted worthy of the true life.

*Theotokion:*
We Christians have acquired thee as a refuge and our wall; we unceasingly glorify thee, O unwedded one.

*Hypakoe, Tone 8:*
What prison had thee not as a captive? What church has thee not as an orator? Damascus boasts greatly of thee, O Paul, for it saw thee overcome by the light Rome, receiving thy blood, also boasts; but Tarsus rejoices yet more, and loves to venerate thy swaddling bands.[^913] O Peter, rock of faith, O Paul, boast of the universe, having come together from Rome, confirm us!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
This is our God, incarnate from the Virgin, and having deified our nature, unto Whom singing we cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
As a fisher of men, as Christ promised, He made thee of His divine Church, having entrusted the helm to thee, the foremost.

Through thee we entreat Jesus the Giver of Life, Who gave thee authority, O Peter, to bind and to loose:[^914] may He be merciful unto me!

O Peter, fervently pray to open the Kingdom of Christ unto those who venerate thy divine memory with unhesitating faith.

*Theotokion:*
O Lady, all-hymned Birthgiver of God, having cleansed my thoughts by thy prayers, show me to be fruitful, O Mother of God of all.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*
Thou didst mount Thine horses, Thine Apostles, O Lord, and Thou didst take their reins into Thy hands; and Thy riding became salvation unto those who faithfully sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Crouching like a lion, fiercely destroying Christ's Church, Saul of old: but having been arrested by the divine voice of the Lamb of God, Whom he was persecuting, He entrusts the flock unto him as shepherd.

He that was to enlighten the universe is darkened; and Ananias is sent unto him, to give enlightenment of soul and body, from the divine manifestation having shown him to be a chosen vessel.

Damascus is worthily watered with a miracle: for from him, as of old from Paradise, this great fountain of divine waters went forth, and manifestly watered the whole earth with the knowledge of God.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord hath put down the mighty from their seats, as the Virgin and Mother said; and He hath filled with divine good things those who hunger, crying in faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Having acquired us as a chosen people by Thy blood, O Lord, grant Thy peace unto us, preserving Thy flock in oneness of mind.

*Troparia:*
Having received boldness toward God out of love, he was worthily marvelled at, a fisherman and an unlettered man, working wonders most gloriously by grace.

Neither gold for himself, nor silver, O Christ, did Thy divine Apostle [desire];[^915] but having acquired virtue, he was enriched with the power of wonders.

The feet and ankle bones of the lame were made straight by thy effectual voice: for by the Holy Spirit most glorious things were accomplished.

*Theotokion:*
God was united according to hypostasis, O pure one, being joined in flesh from thee, remaining immutable, bodiless according to His divine essence.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*
Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant Thy peace unto us, O Lover of Mankind.

*Troparia:*
Thou hast chosen the true boast, O Paul, ever bearing the cross of Christ the King as a trophy of victory, O Apostle.

For thee to live was Christ, and to die an exceeding gain: for out of love thou wast co-crucified with Him Who was crucified for our sake, O all-glorious Paul.

Truly thou rejoicest in the Lord, O venerable Paul, having departed from the body and gone unto Christ God the Giver of Life.

*Theotokion:*
Ever pray thy Son and our God, O unwedded Mary pure, to send mercy unto us the faithful.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Jonah, spending three days in the whale, prefigured Thee the Immortal One, O Christ, as dead of Thine own will, spending three days in the heart of the earth.

*Troparia:*
As of old, O Christ, by Thy right hand Thou didst save Peter walking upon the water on foot: save me also who am submerged by the tempest of grievous temptations, as Thou art compassionate.

Thou didst leave behind the things that are not, O Peter, and didst attain unto the things that are, like a certain merchant: and manifestly didst catch the Pearl of great price, Christ.

Those who foolishly conspired to tempt the Holy Spirit, O Peter, thou didst put to death:[^916] Whom thou hadst first theologized, manifestly confessing Him as God, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou gavest birth to the Word of God, higher than every essence, incarnate for us; therefore we proclaim thee as Mother of God with lips and soul.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and to Him will I proclaim my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto hades, and I pray like Jonah: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*
Having disregarded all the beautiful things of the world, wounded with love for the Master and with desire for the common salvation, having suffered with infirmities to bear witness unto Him, O blessed Paul: pray now for the universe!

As an excellent imitator of the Master, and clothed in Him, O Paul, thou wast truly made all things to all men, that thou mightest gain all and save the people; and thou didst truly save them, catching the ends of the earth for Christ.

Worthily did Christ grant thee citizenship in heaven, O Apostle Paul; for thou didst not desire the city that abides here, O blessed one, having been a faithful minister and steward of His mysteries.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord looked upon thee, renewing my nature, as the Mighty One, having done great things [for thee], O Mother of God, as thou didst say, O all-immaculate one; and my God saved me through thee from corruption, as He is compassionate.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^917]
The firm and God-proclaimed preachers, the summit of Thine Apostles, O Lord, Thou hast received into the enjoyment of Thy good things and into rest; for Thou hast accepted their labors and death above every whole burnt offering, O Thou Who alone knowest what is in the heart.

*Ikos:*
Make clear my tongue, O my Savior, having opened wide my mouth and filled it; soften my heart, that I may follow what I say, and that what I teach I may first do. For every one who does and teaches, He said, this one is great. For if I speak and do not do, I am accounted as sounding brass. Therefore grant me to speak what is fitting and to do what is profitable, O Thou Who alone knowest what is in the heart.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the beginning the unoriginate Word, with the Father and the Spirit, the Only-begotten Son: Blessed art Thou and exalted above all, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Instructed by the divine providence of Christ to emulate the Merciful One, thou wast permitted, before the Passion, O Peter, to fall under the surge of denial.

Unto thee, the first-called and greatly beloved, as the glorious president of the Apostles, does Christ appear first, having risen from the tomb.

Healing thy threefold denial which took place before the Passion, the Master by a threefold God-spoken question, O Peter, confirms [thy] love.[^918]

As witness of thy love for Christ, O Peter, thou didst offer the all-seeing Word as God; therefore in love He entrusts His flock unto thee.

*Theotokion:*
He Who shone forth before the ages from the Father, God the Word incarnate from thee, all we bless, as the Mother of God of all.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*
In the plain of Dura of old, the tyrant set up a furnace for the torment of the God-bearers; wherein the three youths hymned the one God, singing triply, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Thou wast, O Christ, the seal and crown of Thine Apostles, who at the last of times wast called, yet in diligence didst surpass all; with whom the people of the Church sing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Even though he persecuted Thy Church at first, Paul the prisoner, yet he put aside his former boldness through Thy zeal in the end; for he gathered the nations, O Christ, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Preaching the Gospel unto all from Jerusalem, and having traversed around all the earth, O Paul, even teaching unto the borders of Illyricum,[^919] thou didst cry out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Taken up in ecstasy, thou didst reach the third heaven, O exceedingly rich one, and having heard unspeakable words,[^920] thou criest: Glory to the transcendent Father and to the Son, and to the co-enthroned Radiance of the Spirit of God Who manifestly searches the deep things!

*Theotokion:*
As rain upon the fleece, Christ God came down upon thee, O Virgin, and came forth bearing flesh from thee, uniting what before was separated, granting peace upon earth and in heaven: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
By Thy divine, unapproachable power, O Christ, Thou didst bedew Thy venerable youths in the flame, who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
They shall stretch forth thy hands, and shall gird thee unto the cross,[^921] the Master foretold, O Peter, commanding thee to follow Him, as thou criest: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

By the word of grace, thou didst heal Aeneas who was grievously paralyzed, and by working a wonder didst raise Tabitha who was dead,[^922] crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

O Christ Who didst declare the Gentiles cleansed unto Peter: by the spiritual dawn cleanse my thoughts also, as I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
O radiant Virgin, who in the flesh gavest birth unto God the Word: having driven away the darkness of my soul by thy prayers, enlighten me as I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush through fire revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin unto Moses: sing ye and exalt the Lord above all unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Paul was mingled with Thy love, and by a good change was transported; for the glorious one lived no longer for himself, but had Thee, O Compassionate One, living in himself unto all ages.

Thou didst betroth the Church to present her as a bride unto the Bridegroom Christ; for thou wast shown forth as her bridal leader, O God-bearing Paul: therefore she glorifies thee unto all ages.

Thou hast fought the good fight, and having finished thy course lawfully, thou hast kept the treasure of the faith; therefore, O Paul, thou hast been deemed worthy of the crowns of righteousness.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O fiery throne of the Lord; rejoice, O Bride unwedded, Virgin; rejoice, O cloud that didst cause the Sun of Righteousness to dawn, Whom we exalt above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, the all-glorious Bride and all-holy Mother of God, who didst give birth to the Creator of invisible and all visible things, in hymns we magnify.

*Troparia:*
Thy surpassing grace is worthily glorified: for thy shadow drove away the infirmities of the sick, O Peter;[^923] therefore we magnify thee.

Having cast down by ineffable divine power Simon the Sorcerer,[^924] the God-fighter, who by phantasms was lifted up into the heights of the air, Peter is called blessed.

Grant remission of sins by thy prayers, and enlightenment of heart and gladness of spirit, unto those who sing thy memory, O Apostle.

All that is thine is filled with glory, sanctification, and wonders: thy words, relics, dust, and the divine chains, which are venerated with faith and love, O Peter.

*Theotokion:*
The Fruit of thy womb made us choristers to sing a hymn unto thee, who gavest birth to Joy, loudly proclaiming: Rejoice!

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*
Thou hast surpassed the ordinances of nature, having conceived the Creator and Lord, and hast become the door of salvation to the world; therefore, O Mother of God, unceasingly we magnify thee.

*Troparia:*
We venerate thy chains, which thou didst bear for Christ as an evildoer; and we kiss the wounds, O Paul, which thou bearest upon thy glorious and victorious body.

Now thou hast departed, O Apostle, unto Him Who was unceasingly desired by thee; dwelling with Him as a minister, by unceasing entreaties draw thy servants unto thyself.

Now no longer in dark riddles nor in a mirror is Christ seen by Thee, but rather face to face is He beheld,[^925] revealing the knowledge of the Godhead perfectly unto Thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thou, O all-holy Mother of God, wast the boast of Christ's Apostles, the glory of the martyrs, and the foundation of the prophets; therefore all we the faithful worthily magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion. Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
Let us all hymn the summit of the Apostles, Peter and Paul the divine, luminaries of the universe, preachers of the faith, God-proclaiming trumpets, expounders of doctrines, pillars of the Church, and destroyers of deceit. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Great and all-glorious is the mystery of thy childbearing, O Maiden full of the grace of God, and Virgin Mother of God! The prophets proclaimed it, the Apostles taught it, the martyrs confessed it, the Angels sing it, and men venerate it.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4. Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Having received grace from heaven, * when the Savior addressed the question to the disciples, * to the twelvefold number of Apostles: * "Whom do men say that I am?" * Then indeed the chief of the Apostles, Peter,[^926] * theologizing answered, clearly crying aloud: * "Thou art the Christ, the Son of the living God." * Therefore is he worthily called blessed, * as having received revelation from on high, * and justly receiving [power] * to bind and to loose.

When Christ, the Word of God Who appeared from the Father, asked: * "Whom do ye suppose Me to be?" * "Thou art the Son of the living Father!" * as the God-proclaiming mouth of all, * thou straightway didst cry aloud; * therefore also: "Blessed art thou, Simon Bar-Jonah!" * didst thou receive as reward. * For God glorifies * those who faithfully glorify Him, * and richly calls them to reception, * and He hath set thee as an unashamed rock, * the establishment and foundation of the Church.

Being called from on high and not of men, * when earthly darkness obscured the bodily eyes, * exposing the worldliness of impiety, * then the heavenly light shone upon * the spiritual eyes, * revealing the beauty of piety. * Therefore thou didst come to know Him Who brings forth light * out of darkness, Christ our God.

Whom entreat * to save and enlighten our souls!

Being a preacher of Christ, * bearing the boast of the Cross: * thou didst truly discern above all the divine love that seeks after many, * which binds those that see unto the Desired One. * Therefore wast thou also called the prisoner of Christ, * having chosen the discomfort of temptations * as the sweetest of all food, * and wast deemed worthy of the dissolution of an honorable end, * living unto thy Master; * Whom entreat to save and enlighten our souls!

*Glory... Tone 6. By Cosmas the Monk:*[^927]
The all-venerable feast of the Apostles hath arrived for the Church of Christ, mediating salvation for all. Mystically clapping our hands, let us say unto them: Rejoice, ye luminaries unto those in darkness, who flash with the rays of the spiritual Sun! Rejoice, Peter and Paul, unshakable foundations of divine doctrines, friends of Christ, honorable vessels! Come invisibly into our midst, vouchsafing immaterial gifts unto those who praise your feast in hymns!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the true Vine...

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes: From the Canon of the Apostle Peter, Ode 3, on 4; and of Paul, Ode 6, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*
*Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*
*Apostle to the Corinthians, composite reading 193.*[^928] *Alleluia, Tone 1:*
*The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.*
*Gospel of Matthew, composite reading 67.*[^929]
*Communion Hymn: Their sound hath gone forth...*
*The ordinary Epistle and Gospel we read beforehand.*
*At the trapeza, great consolation for the brethren. If this feast of the holy Apostles falls on a Wednesday or a Friday, we eat fish and drink wine.*
*It should be known that if the feast of the holy Apostles falls on a Sunday, all the resurrectional hymns take precedence.*

*JUNE 30:*

*The Synaxis of the Holy, Glorious, and All-Praised Twelve Apostles.*[^930]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6: of Peter and Paul, 3, Tone 2, Automelon: "With what crowns of praise" (Кі́ими похва́льными):*

With what crowns of praise shall we crown Peter and Paul, divided in bodies yet united in the Spirit, the foremost leaders of the God-proclaiming heralds: the one indeed as the prime leader of the Apostles, the other as having labored more than all the rest?[^931] For Christ our God, Who hath great mercy, worthily crowns them with crowns of immortal glory.

With what beauties of hymnody shall we sing of Peter and Paul: the wings of the knowledge of God, which flew over the ends of the earth and ascended unto heaven; the hands of the Gospel of grace; the feet of true preaching; the rivers of wisdom; the horns of the Cross,[^932] whereby Christ cast down the arrogance of demons, He that hath great mercy!

With what spiritual hymns shall we praise Peter and Paul: who cut down the unclosable mouths of godlessness, the dread swords of the Spirit; the radiant adornments of Rome; the nourishers of the whole universe; the spiritual God-inscribed tablets of the New Testament, which Christ proclaimed in Sion, He that hath great mercy!

*Other Stichera of the Twelve Apostles, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ћкѡ до́бля):*

As eyewitnesses * and witnesses of the incarnation of the Word, * O exceedingly rich disciples, ye are called blessed; * for shining like lightning, * ye appeared unto the world; * and like spiritual mountains ye drop sweetness; * like ever-flowing * rivers of Paradise being divided, * ye water the Churches of the nations * with divine waters.

Flashing like rays * with spiritual dawn, * ye were sent into all the world, * unstintingly granting the operation of wonders; * having become ministers of the mysteries of Christ, * and God-inscribed tablets * written with the grace of God, * of the God-taught Law, * O exceedingly rich initiates of sacred mysteries.

The fisherman's reed * overthrew the arrogance of philosophers * and the streams of orators, * the God-wise teachings, * the dogmas of representations; * and of the good mysteries and having clearly set forth the Gospel, * and the participation in everlasting food, * and angelic enjoyment, * and abiding glory.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
The all-venerable feast of the Apostles hath arrived for the Church of Christ, mediating salvation for all. Mystically clapping our hands, let us say unto them: Rejoice, ye luminaries unto those in darkness, who flash with the rays of the spiritual Sun! Rejoice, Peter and Paul, unshakable foundations of divine doctrines, friends of Christ, honorable vessels! Come invisibly into our midst, vouchsafing immaterial gifts unto those who praise your feast in hymns!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Who will not call thee blessed...

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Thou hast given as a boast * unto Thy Church, O Lord, * Thine honorable Apostles, * wherein those spiritual luminaries shine, * Peter and Paul; * like noetic stars, * enlightening the universe, * through whom Thou hast illuminated * the darkness of the West,[^933] * O Jesus Almighty, * Savior of our souls.

*Verse: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*

Thou hast given as establishment * unto Thy Church, O Lord, * the firmness of Peter, * and the understanding and brilliant wisdom of Paul, * and the true God-spoken voice of them both, * which drives away the Hellenic deceit.[^934] * Therefore, initiated by both, * we sing unto Thee: * O Jesus Almighty, * Savior of our souls.

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*

Thou hast given as an image of conversion * unto those who sin, * both of Thine Apostles: * the one indeed who denied Thee at the time of the Passion * and repented, * and the other who opposed Thy preaching * and believed;[^935] * and both leading the assembly of Thy friends: * O Jesus Almighty, * Savior of our souls.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
A joyful feast hath shone forth unto the ends of the earth today: the all-venerable memory of the all-wise and supreme Apostles, Peter and Paul. Therefore Rome also rejoices, dancing in hymns and chants. Let us also feast, brethren, having accomplished this all-venerable day: Rejoice, O Apostle Peter, intimate friend of thy Teacher, Christ our God; rejoice, O Paul most beloved, preacher of the faith, and teacher of the universe; as having boldness, O hundredfold-chosen pair, pray unto Christ our God that our souls may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the true Vine...

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^936]
O foremost of the Apostles and teachers of the universe, entreat the Master of all to grant peace unto the universe, and great mercy unto our souls.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*
The spiritual choir of the Apostles was sent mystically unto the world from God the Most High; and unto those who suffered in bondage they appeared as physicians: invoking the Tri-hypostatic Unity, they wisely recorded the divine incarnation of Emmanuel the Lord.[^937] *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The Bush which Moses saw unconsumed; the Mountain of God; the holy Cloud; the Tabernacle undefiled; the God-received Table; the Palace of the exalted King; the all-radiant and impassable Gate: O Virgin, we hymn thee!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*
O divine trumpets of the Comforter, sounding forth the breath of words of salvation, sound aloud unto the world; and having arisen, lead those who sleep in the gloom of deceit unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostles of divine light, praying unto Christ God that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
He was not separated from the divine nature, becoming flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained [God], Who after childbearing preserved thee a Virgin Mother, all-immaculate as before childbearing, the only Lord: Him fervently entreat, to grant us great mercy.

*Canon of Peter and Paul, with the Heirmos on 8. Whose Acrostic is: "I honor Peter, and praise Paul with understanding."*[^938]
*Tone 4, Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I will be seen keeping radiant festival, and rejoicing I will sing her wonders.

*Troparia:*
Grant unto me, O Lord, drops of gifts of Thy Holy Spirit, an appointed word, that I may faithfully rejoice in praises, [singing] the achievements of Thine imperial first Apostles.

Thou didst readily leave behind temporal things, O all-praised Peter, and didst follow in Christ's footsteps; and having left behind ignorance, O Paul, thou didst follow Him Who called thee.

Having received the divine Grace of the All-Holy Spirit that descended upon you, O Apostles, Peter and Paul, ye passed through all the earth, teaching the ends [thereof].

*Theotokion:*
Rhetoricians of many words, and musical eloquent tongues, cannot praise or hymn thee, O pure one; for more than human are the divine mysteries accomplished in thee alone.

*Another Canon of the Twelve Apostles, to 6.[^939] Whose acrostic is: "I honor the wise Apostles of Christ."[^940] By Theophanes. The same Tone.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the Red Sea's deep on foot with unwetted steps of old, Israel, by Moses' hands stretched out crosswise, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparia:*
As I desire to hymn the apostolic choir, O Christ, through their prayers do Thou, as God, grant unto me the radiance of the All-Holy Spirit, and the light of Thy wisdom.

Having been strengthened by Thy power and Grace, O Christ, Thine honored Apostles, having become eyewitnesses of God, destroyed the power of the enemy adversaries through their unfailing inclination toward Thee.

Performing healings in Thy Name, O Master, the glorious Apostles captured the gatherings of the nations through the knowledge of Thee, and enlightened them with Thy light.

Having learned heavenly wisdom, the all-glorious Apostles of Christ, manifestly wise, made foolish the loquacity of the ungodly through the success of their preaching.

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure Virgin Mother, by the good pleasure of Him Who begot Him, and by the operation of the All-Holy Spirit, thou didst give birth to One of the transcendent Trinity, Who through thee appeared in the flesh like unto us.

*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Birthgiver of God, living and unstinting fountainhead: spiritually establish those united in choir who sing thy praises; and in thy divine glory, vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*Troparia:*
Andrew, a kinsman unto thee, O Peter, proclaimed Christ the Messiah, unto Whom thou didst draw near through faith; and thou, O marvelous Paul, through the beneficent baptism by Ananias, wast enlightened.[^941]

Peter, having led the race of Israel that deceitfully fell away through the letter of the Law unto the enlightenment of Grace; Paul, likewise, delivered the flocks of the Gentiles from delusion.

Thou didst send forth Thy disciples naked and simple into the nations, bearing Thy holy Name instead of weapons, and through faith they destroyed the deceitful warfare, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Through your divine preaching, O all-praised Peter and Paul, all the earth was filled with the knowledge of the Lord Who shone forth from the Virgin and renewed the world.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and foundation.

*Troparia:*
Proclaiming new and honorable teachings on earth, and confirming them with tongues of fire, ye preachers of Christ have handed them down.

Thou hast shown Thy disciples to be spiritual heavens, O Master, recounting Thy glory unto all ends.

Written in the heavens, and revealed as inhabitants of Christ, O all-wise ones, preserve now those who honor you with love.

*Theotokion:*
He Who dwells in the highest, O all-pure one, took up His abode among us; for taking flesh from thee without seed, He appeared.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
