Having taken up your abode in the unapproachable light, as being dwellings of the light, ye ever enlighten your holy temple with divine preachings; therefore with faith we cry aloud: Deliver us from darkness, and from all tribulations, and from the cruel incursion of the heathen, O Apostles, entreating the Creator!

*Glory... Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^942]
Let us praise Peter, the great and radiant luminary, and the all-wise Paul, who appeared as the chief leaders of the disciples; for having shone with the fire of the divine Spirit, they burned up all the gloom of delusion. Therefore they also worthily appeared as citizens of the Kingdom on high, and partakers of the throne of Grace. Therefore we cry aloud unto them: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly celebrate your holy memory!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
As a Virgin and alone among women, who without seed hast borne God in the flesh, all we generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead took up its abode in thee, and as an Infant with milk thou feedest the Creator and Lord. Therefore, we of angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with faith bow down before thy most holy childbearing!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of the Incarnation from the Virgin of Thee, the Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Strengthened though weak, and simple before rhetoricians, having engaged them piously, Peter and Paul, the Apostles of Christ, carried off the victory.

The divine Peter denounced the fierce natural sorcerer Simon Magus as a lover of money; and now the wise Paul has blinded Elymas, who was perverting the ways of Christ.[^943]

Though the fisherman and the orator possessed unlearned speech, yet seasoned with divine salt, they sweetened the souls of the philosophers, and brought them through faith unto Christ.[^944]

*Theotokion:*
With God-inspired eyes of old, the divine Habakkuk called thee a pure and shaded mountain, clearly foretelling Him Who comes from the south and was incarnate through thee, O Lady.[^945]

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
The deep utterance of the divine Apostles went forth like fire through all the universe, burning up the material of deceit, and enlightening the assemblies of the pious through Grace.

The God-radiant luminaries enlightened with rays of Grace the world that had grown dark with the gloom of godlessness, the disciples of the Lord having appeared through the brightness of preaching.

Enlightened by the sanctified rays of the spiritual Sun, ye shine like the sun in the world, O all-praised ones, driving away the gloom of delusion by divine light.

Having the Cross, Thy rod of strength, Thy glorious eyewitnesses, O Word, cut through the sea of life, like horses troubling the waters of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*
The animate heaven, adorned with diverse radiance, the most pure Maiden, is now glorified as Birthgiver of God unto Thee, O Christ, the King of kings.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who praise thee.

As being firm, Christ named thee a rock, O Peter, and upon thee [He founded] the Church, against which the gates of Hades shall not prevail; and thee, O Paul, He called a chosen vessel, to carry His Name before the Gentiles.

Peter denied the Master thrice, and by the threefold calling to love was restored; while Paul, who formerly was a blasphemer, grievously tormenting the faithful, afterwards preached Him Whom he formerly persecuted.[^946]

Peter, with Paul, found a renowned calling and ever-existing glory from Christ: the one, indeed, received the keys of heaven, while the other was caught up into Paradise, having learned unspeakable words surpassing man.[^947]

*Theotokion:*
Isaiah, filled with the divine Spirit, foretold the childbearing without a father of Emmanuel born of thee, O Virgin, Who is "God with us," Whom thou, O pure one, didst bear. Therefore we all hymn thee.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Thou hast come into the world, O my Lord, a Light, a holy Light, turning back from dark ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparia:*
Thou hast shown forth Thy divine and all-wise ministers as a light in the world, proclaiming Thee, the Unwaning Light, unto all.

The Apostles, having manifestly attained unto every virtue, have destroyed the diverse entanglements of demonic malice.

The Apostles have shown forth unto us the Trinity's radiance in the unity of the Godhead, uttering with tongues of fire.

*Theotokion:*
We set thee forth as an invincible weapon against enemies; thee have we acquired as the foundation and hope of our salvation, O Bride of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, O ye wise in God, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her!

*Troparia:*
Radiant are Peter's wonders, and honorable are Paul's signs; for their garments and shadows, when applied, healed the diseases of the sick.[^948]

Peter, truly glorious, and Paul, in truth marvelous, the divine pair and most honorable dwellings of the life-originating Trinity, are remembered by all.

As an eminent divine physician, Peter healed the souls and bodies of the sick; Paul illuminated the Gentiles, and sealed them with the light of the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
We are bound to hymn thee, but in truth we cannot do so worthily; therefore we hymn thee in silence, honoring the unutterable mystery accomplished in thee, O Virgin.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparia:*
Having strengthened Thy disciples, O Savior, with wisdom and wonders, Thou hast made them stronger than Hellenic vain speech; and they destroyed their deceitful decrees.

The divine rivers of wisdom filled all the valleys of the Church with waters of salvation, having enriched them with streams from the saving fountains.

Ye appeared as living stars, O all-praised ones; ye destroyed all dark delusion with light-bearing rays, and made the light of the knowledge of God to shine forth.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee, this perfect and all-good dove, a radiant lily and flower of the valleys, O Mother of God, the spiritual Bridegroom took up His abode in thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^949]
Christ the Rock glorifies radiantly the rock of faith, the pre-eminent of the disciples, and with Paul the whole choir of the Twelve today; whose memory celebrating faithfully, we glorify Him Who glorified them.

*Ikos:*
Celebrating now your all-honorable and all-blessed memory, in songs we fervently cry aloud unto you: O all-radiant and God-beaming luminaries of the world, beloved Twelve disciples of Christ, as having boldness before Him to pray for us—for unto you was given the grace of healings and forgiveness of sins—fervently entreat that we may be delivered from sins and tribulations, that we may glorify the only Lover of mankind Who glorified you!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The God-wise ones did not worship creation rather than the Creator; but having manfully trampled underfoot the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers, praised above all!

*Troparia:*
Peter was called blessed by Christ, since he declared Him to be the Son of the living God; and Paul was honored as a vessel fit for good use; and they cried: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers!

Peter, indeed, instructed those serving the Law, even as it was commanded him; and Paul, the Gentiles; and both brought them through Grace unto Christ, and cried: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^950]

As an eminent shepherd of Christ, Peter received the flock;

and Paul became a divine teacher of the Church, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Mary, eminent in thy standing, who didst bear God in the flesh, O Virgin pure and unwedded: entreat Him to deliver us from every assault and sin.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, Abraham's Children, burning with love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Having made Thy disciples sons, who beforehand was God by nature, through love Thou hast shown them to be heirs of the Father's inheritance, O All-Good One; and Thou wast well pleased to walk with them as with God and Master.

Having bestowed an outpouring of wisdom, breadth of heart, and an eloquent tongue, O Word, upon Thy divine disciples, Thou didst send them forth to preach the Gospel of the Kingdom unto all ends.

The Apostles appeared as clouds of God's light, raining down life-creating water upon all, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast appeared adorned with divine glory, O all-pure one, who alone throughout the ages didst conceive the Word of God, O Virgin Mother: blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious Children in the furnace, then prefigured, but now in act, it raises up all the universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by divine power, Peter raised the dead Tabitha; and Paul raised the dead Eutychus who had fallen from a height; both crying: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^951]

By a fearful and marvelous vision, Peter enlightened Cornelius; and Paul baptized Gaius, Crispus, Pannestius, and the household of Stephanas; both crying: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^952]

When Peter was held in prison, Christ freed him at night by an Angel; and Paul and Silas, when they straightway prayed, He led forth when their feet were firmly bound in the stocks, and shook the prison. Therefore let us hymn Christ unto all ages![^953]

Deliver [us] from all-destructive passions, and tear asunder now the cords of transgressions, wherewith each is burdened. For we all have sinned, and ask for cleansing, being guilty; which do thou grant, O Birthgiver of God, by thy supplications, unto those who exalt Christ above all unto the ages!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Stretching forth his hands, Daniel shut the lions' mouths in the pit; and having girded themselves with virtue, the Children, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
By the fire of the Spirit, the divine and all-wise choir of Christ's Apostles burned up the manifold demonic shrines like tares, and enlightened the hearts of the faithful, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

With one accord let us honor the divine disciples and Apostles of Christ, who thundered forth unto us the divine commandments, the havens of the faithful, the common benefactors of mankind, the servants of the Savior.

Let us honor the all-honorable and receptive vessels of virtues, the firstfruits of mankind, the trumpets of preaching, the streams of incorruptible life, the God-bearing lightnings, the fountains of healings, the beautiful feet of the Gospel.[^954]

*Theotokion:*
The Son Who is full empties Himself for our sake, that we may partake of His fullness; for having entered into thy most pure womb, the Uncircumscribed One, not departing from the Father's heavens, was incarnate. Therefore we all bless thee, O Mary, Bride of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit, being enlightened; and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and crying aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Birthgiver of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparia:*
Together ye passed through the whole world as winged ones, and having preached the Gospel of Christ unto all tribes, tongues, and lands, ye drew all unto the faith, O all-blessed Peter and marvelous Apostle Paul!

Having reached deep old age, and fulfilled the debt of nature, O Peter, after the likeness of Christ thy Teacher, thou didst endure the Cross. And thou, O Paul, wast beheaded in thy head. O fearful wonder! Instead of blood, thou didst pour forth streams of milk, and didst convert the unbelieving![^955][^956]

O divine pair, who wisely entrusted yourselves unto the souls of the whole world, and turned them from impiety unto piety, and after death still save the souls of the faithful: remember us now also, and present [us] unto Christ, O chief Apostles!

*Theotokion:*
O Good Savior, Who by nature art Lover of mankind and plenteous in mercy: by the divine supplications of Thine all-hymned Virgin Mother, and by the prayers of Peter and Paul Thine Apostles, send down upon us Thy mercy and help from heaven!

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from the unhewn mountain, from thee, O Virgin, without hands of man, even Christ, Who joined together the parted natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God!

*Troparia:*
Having received authority from the Master to loose the cords of transgressions, O eyewitnesses of God: graciously cleanse the sins of those who hymn you, and vouchsafe them salvation!

All ye glorious Apostles received the all-spiritual radiance that sacredly appeared unto you; having been taught lofty preaching and teaching in the upper room, ye are now worthily called blessed!

Christ Himself now gives unfading crowns unto His friends who have fallen asleep, and fills them with divine vision: Him do ye now entreat to save all the Churches!

*Theotokion:*
Desiring to come in the flesh, He Who adorned all things by His Word took up His abode in thee; He found thee alone holier than all, and showed thee forth as the true Birthgiver of God, O Virgin Mother.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "While the disciples looked on" (Оу҆чени́кѡмъ):*
Let us run together gladly to praise the all-honorable Twelve of the disciples. Rejoice, ye who traversed all under the sun, and having caught the malicious flocks of the nations, were instructed in divine things!

*Glory:*
Today let all we faithful praise the choir of the Twelve, as having been confirmed by them, and unto Christ let us cry aloud: By their prayers, O Savior, pacify Thy world!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
In thee we boast, O Birthgiver of God, and thee have we as intercession before God: stretch forth thy holy hand and crush our enemies; send down upon thy servants help from the Sanctuary!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
O chief foundation of the Apostles, having left all things, thou didst follow the Teacher, crying aloud unto Him: With Thee will I die, that I may also live the blessed life. And thou wast the first bishop of Rome, the glory and boast of the greatest of cities, and the confirmation of the Church, O Peter; and the gates of Hades shall in truth not prevail against her, even as Christ foretold.[^957] Entreat Him to save and enlighten our souls!

Separated from thy mother's womb, thou didst escape every weight of worldly boastfulness; having winged thyself with the longing of divine love in truth unto divine height, O Paul, where having ascended into the all-radiant darkness of the divine Light, like one incorporeal, thou wast enriched with the teachings of unutterable words; and thou wast sent unto those in darkness, preaching Christ our God as Light.[^958] Entreat Him to save and enlighten our souls!

Thou Who art Light before all ages, when Thou wast well pleased to come unto me, man, for the sake of ineffable love of mankind, and to be made flesh out of goodness: then as secondary lights of Thy brightness, and flashing lightning, Thou didst show forth Thine Apostles and disciples, O Savior; who being sent forth, illuminated all creation with Thy divine light, entreating Thee to save and enlighten our souls!

Peter and Paul, workers of the Word; Andrew, James, and all-wise John; Bartholomew and Philip; Thomas and Matthew; Simon, Jude, and divine James; all-honorable choir of the Twelve disciples of the universe, who preached in the world the All-Holy Trinity, God by nature ever-existing; the Church's truly indestructible walls and unshakable pillars: entreat the Master of all that we may be saved!

*Glory... Tone 6. By Cosmas the Monk:*[^959]
Ye passed through creation, having enlightened it, O disciples of the Savior; having burned up the idolatrous deceit like brushwood by your teachings, ye caught the nations from the depth of unreason unto divine understanding, and saved them. And now entreat Christ, that He may be merciful unto us on the Day of Judgment.

*Both now... Theotokion: O Birthgiver of God, thou art [the true Vine]...*

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes from the Canon of the Twelve Apostles of Christ, Ode 3, and Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the universe [Ps. 18:4]. Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God [Ps. 18:1]. Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 131 [1 Cor. 4:9–16].[^960] Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord [Ps. 88:5]. Gospel of Mark, pericope 21 [Mark 3:13–19].[^961] Communion Hymn: Their sound is gone out into all the earth... [Ps. 18:4].*

*(Vacat — Fol. 119v: Unnumbered blank verso)*

---

# CONCLUDING APPENDICES AND COLOPHON

*Resurrectional Theotokia for the Eight Tones:*

*Tone 1. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^963][^962]
Let us hymn the Virgin Mary, the glory of all the world, who budded forth from men and gave birth unto the Master, the gate of heaven, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful! For she was shown forth as heaven and the temple of the Godhead; she, having broken down the barrier of enmity, ushered in peace and opened the Kingdom. Having her therefore as the confirmation of faith, we have the Lord Who was born of her as our Defender. Take courage therefore, take courage, O people of God, for He will conquer the enemies, as He is all-powerful!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^964]
Behold, the prophecy of Isaiah is fulfilled; for as a Virgin thou didst give birth, and after childbearing thou didst remain as thou wast before giving birth; for God was the One born, therefore He also renewed the course of nature. But O Mother of God, despise not the supplications of thy servants offered unto thee in thy temple: but as carrying the Compassionate One in thine arms, have mercy upon thy servants, and pray that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 2. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^965]
The shadow of the Law passed away when Grace arrived: for as the bush was not consumed when wrapped in flame, so as a Virgin thou didst give birth, and a Virgin thou didst remain. Instead of a pillar of fire, the righteous Sun shone forth; instead of Moses, Christ, the salvation of our souls!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^966]
O new wonder of all ancient wonders! For who has known a Mother who gave birth without a husband, and carried in her arms Him Who upholds all creation? The One born is the counsel of God! Whom as an infant having carried in thine arms, O all-pure one, and possessing maternal boldness toward Him, cease not to pray for those who honor thee, that He may have compassion and save our souls!

*Tone 3. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^967]
How shall we not marvel at thy God-man childbearing, O most honored one? For having received no temptation of a man, O all-immaculate one, thou didst give birth in the flesh without a father unto the Son, Who was born of the Father without a mother before the ages; Who suffered no change, nor mingling, nor division, but preserved entire the property of both natures. Therefore, O Mother, Virgin, Sovereign Lady, pray Him to save the souls of those who in orthodox manner confess thee as the Birthgiver of God!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^968]
Without seed from the Holy Spirit, but by the Father's will, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who exists before the ages from the Father without a mother; and Who for our sake became of thee without a father; thou didst give birth unto Him in the flesh, and didst nurse Him as an infant with milk. Therefore cease not to pray that our souls may be delivered from dangers!

*Tone 4. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^969]
David the Prophet, the forefather of God, melodiously proclaimed beforehand concerning thee unto Him Who has done great things for thee:

"The Queen stood at Thy right hand."[^970] For God, Who was pleased to become man of thee without a father, revealed thee as the Mother and mediatress of life, that He might renew His own image corrupted by passions; and having found the sheep gone astray upon the mountains, taking it upon His shoulder, He might bring it unto the Father, and according to His own will, unite it with the heavenly powers, and save the world, O Birthgiver of God, Christ Who has great and rich mercy!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^971]
Look down upon the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, quenching the fierce uprisings against us, releasing us from every affliction! For thee alone do we have as a firm and sure confirmation, and we have acquired thy patronage, that we who call upon thee may not be put to shame, O Sovereign Lady! Hasten unto the entreaty of those who faithfully cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, help of all, joy and protection, and salvation of our souls!

*Tone 5. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^972]
In the Red Sea an image of the unwedded Bride was inscribed of old: there Moses, the divider of the water; and here Gabriel, the minister of the wonder! Then the deep was traversed dryshod by Israel; and now Christ is born seedlessly of the Virgin! The sea remained impassable after the passage of Israel; the Immaculate one remained undefiled after the birth of Emmanuel! O Thou Who art, and art before all, and appeared as man: O God, have mercy upon us!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^973]
Thou art the temple and the gate, the palace and throne of the King, O all-honored Virgin, through whom my Redeemer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto those sleeping in darkness, being the Sun of Righteousness, wishing to enlighten those whom He created according to His own image with His own hand. Therefore, O all-hymned one, as having acquired maternal boldness toward Him, cease not to pray that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 6. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^974]
Who will not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who will not hymn thy most pure childbearing? For the Only-begotten Son, Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the same came forth from thee, pure one, ineffably incarnate: being God by nature, and having become man by nature for our sake; not divided into two persons, but known in two natures without confusion. Him do thou entreat, O pure and all-blessed one, that our souls may be shown mercy!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^975]
My Creator and Redeemer, O all-pure one, Christ the Lord, coming forth from thy womb and clothing Himself in me, delivered Adam from the first curse. Therefore, O all-pure one, as the Mother of God and Virgin, in truth we cry aloud unto thee unceasingly with the angel: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, intercession and protection, and salvation of our souls!

*Tone 7. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^976]
Thou wast known as a Mother beyond nature, O Birthgiver of God, and thou didst remain a Virgin beyond word and understanding; and the tongue cannot tell the wonder of thy childbearing: for the conception was most glorious, O pure one, and the manner of childbearing is incomprehensible: for where God so wills, the order of nature is overcome! Therefore, knowing thee to be the Mother of God, we all earnestly pray thee: Entreat Him that our souls may be saved!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^977]
Fleeing beneath thy protection, O Sovereign Lady, all we earthborn cry aloud unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, our hope, deliver us from immeasurable transgressions, and save our souls!

*Tone 8. Theotokion [Dogmatikon]:*[^978]
The King of Heaven out of love for mankind appeared upon the earth, and dwelt with men: for having taken flesh from the pure Virgin, and having come forth from her with the assumption [thereof], He is one Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis. Therefore, truly proclaiming Him perfect God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God: Whom do thou entreat, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may be shown mercy!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^979]
O unwedded Virgin, who ineffably conceived God in the flesh, Mother of the most high God: accept the entreaties of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all cleansing of transgressions; receiving now our supplications, pray that we all may be saved!

*Theotokia of the Eight Tones, sung when there is "Glory..." to the saint in the Menaion, and "Both now..." according to the Tone of the saint:*[^980]

*Tone 1. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^981]
Joy of the heavenly ranks, strong intercession of men upon earth, O most pure Virgin: save us who have recourse unto thee, for after God we have placed our hope in thee, O Birthgiver of God!

*On Monday at Matins, Theotokion:*[^982]
Brighter than all the holy hosts, more honorable than all creation, O Birthgiver of God, Sovereign Lady of the world: save us, O thou who didst give birth unto the Savior, from myriads of transgressions and perils, as thou art good, by thy prayers!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^983]
O Virgin celebrated in hymn, the mystery within thee Moses beheld with prophetic eyes: the bush burning and unconsumed; for the fire of the Godhead did not consume thy womb, O pure one! Therefore we pray thee, as the Mother of our God: ask peace for the world, and great mercy!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^984]
I have surpassed the harlot and the prodigal, and the thief in sins, and the publican and the Ninevites! Woe is me, what will become of me? How shall I, wretched one, escape torment? O pure one, I fall down before thee: have compassion upon me by thy mercy, even as thy Son saved them!

is.[^984]

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^985]
Rejoice, joy of the righteous, gladness of the Apostles and martyrs, and protection of us thy servants, O Virgin!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^986]
Rejoice, O Birthgiver of God and Virgin; rejoice, boast of all the universe; rejoice, all-pure and blessed Mother of God!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^987]
Truly beyond understanding are thy marvelous wonders, O Bride of God, all-glorious things which all the prophets proclaimed concerning thy childbearing: the conception and childbearing, O all-hymned one, are incomprehensible and ineffable, whereby the world is saved, as He is compassionate!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^988]
Rejoice from us, O holy Birthgiver of God and Virgin, pure vessel of all the universe, unquenchable lamp, receptacle of the Uncontainable, indestructible temple; rejoice, from whom was born the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the whole world!

*Tone 2. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^989]
Rejoice, Mary, Birthgiver of God, temple indestructible, and rather holy, even as the Prophet cries aloud: "Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness!"

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^990]
We have placed our hope in thee, O Birthgiver of God: let us not fall away from our expectation; save us from perils, O helper of those in perplexity, and destroy the counsels of the adversaries; for thou art our salvation, O blessed one!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^991]
Impassable gate, mystically sealed, O blessed Virgin Birthgiver of God: accept our supplications, and bring them before thy Son and God, that through thee He may save our souls!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^992]
Rejoice, Mary, Birthgiver of God, temple indestructible, and rather holy, even as the Prophet cries aloud: "Holy is Thy temple, wonderful in righteousness!"

*On Wednesday morning, Theotokion:*[^993]
As a fruitful olive tree, the Virgin brought Thee forth as the Fruit of life, to bear the fruit of great and rich mercy for the world!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^994]
All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God: preserve me beneath thy protection!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^995]
Save thy servants from perils, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; for after God we all have recourse unto thee, as unto an unbreakable rampart and intercession!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^996]
Come, calling out with unceasing hymns, let us all glorify the Mother of the Light, for she gave birth unto our salvation; and let us offer her: Rejoice! as the only one who gave birth unto God, the most exalted of all, Who exists before the ages! Rejoice, thou who didst raise up fallen Eve again! Rejoice, O all-pure, unwedded Virgin!

*Tone 3. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^997]
O Birthgiver of God, intercessor of all who pray unto thee: in thee do we take courage, and in thee do we boast, and in thee is all our hope; entreat Him Who was born of thee on behalf of thine unworthy servants!

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^998]
O fiery boast of the heavenly ranks, song of the Apostles, and fulfillment of the prophets: O Sovereign Lady, accept our prayers!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^999]
O holy unwedded Mother, Birthgiver of God among women: entreat Him Whom thou didst bear, the King and God, that He may save us, as He loves mankind!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^1000]
Without seed thou didst conceive by the Holy Spirit, and glorifying, we hymn thee: Rejoice, O all-holy Virgin!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^1001]
As I sin exceedingly, O Maiden, snatch me by thy great mercy from the cruel flame; and set me upright, O pure one, by thy supplications, guiding me unto the paths of salvation by thy maternal prayers!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^1002]
*Without seed thou didst conceive...*

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*
*O holy unwedded Mother, Birthgiver of God among women...*

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*
*Without seed thou didst conceive...*

*Tone 4. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^1003]
Rejoice, cloud of light; rejoice, radiant lampstand; rejoice, urn in which is the manna; rejoice, rod of Aaron; rejoice, unburnt bush; rejoice, bridal chamber; rejoice, throne; rejoice, holy mountain; rejoice, haven; rejoice, divine table; rejoice, mystical gate; rejoice, joy of all!

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^1004]
O Birthgiver of God, Queen of all, boast of the Orthodox: destroy the uprisings of heretics, and put to shame the faces of those who neither venerate nor honor, O all-pure one, thine honored icon!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^1005]
Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God, who didst give birth unto the Creator of all, that we all may cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, sole intercession of our souls!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^1006]
Preserve thy servants from all perils, O blessed Birthgiver of God, that we may glorify thee, the hope of our souls!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^1007]
Having thee, O Birthgiver of God, as our hope and intercession, we will not fear the plots of the enemies, for thou dost save our souls!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^1008]
We have acquired thee as a wall, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, and a calm harbor and confirmation; therefore I pray, I pray who am tempest-tossed in this life: guide me, and save me!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^1009]
Unquenchable lamp, righteous throne, O all-pure Sovereign Lady: pray that our souls may be saved!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^1010]
O only pure and all-immaculate Virgin, who didst give birth unto God seedlessly: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 5. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^1011]
Dread and all-glorious and great is the mystery: the Uncontainable was contained in the womb, and the Mother after childbearing remained a Virgin again; for she gave birth unto God incarnate of her. Unto Him let us cry aloud, unto Him let us say a song, hymning with the angels: Holy art Thou, O Christ God, Who for our sake became man, glory to Thee!

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^1012]
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^1013]
Allay the sicknesses of my heavily sighing soul, O thou who didst wipe away every tear from the face of the earth; for thou dost drive away the sicknesses of men, and dost loose the afflictions of sinners; for we all have acquired thee as hope and confirmation, O all-holy Virgin Mother!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*
*O full of grace, intercede...*

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^1014]
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, and we the faithful glorify thee as is meet: unshakable city, indestructible wall, firm intercessor, and refuge of our souls!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^1015]
We call thee blessed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, for from thee shone forth Christ, the Sun of Righteousness, Who has great mercy!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^1016]
Unto thee do we pray as unto the Mother of God: O blessed one, pray for the salvation of our souls!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^1017]
O wretched soul of mine, what answer wilt thou have to give the Judge at that hour, when the thrones are set at the judgment, and the Judge comes from the heavens, descended with ten thousands of angels? When He sits upon the judgment seat to make trial with unprofitable servants like unto me, what wilt thou answer? What wilt thou bring forward then? Truly nothing, having defiled thy mind and body! Therefore fall down before the Virgin, and cry out unceasingly, that rich forgiveness of sins may be granted thee!

*Tone 6. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^1018]
With the Archangel let us the faithful hymn the heavenly bridal chamber, and the truly sealed gate: Rejoice, for whose sake Christ, the Savior of all, the Giver of life and God, sprang up for us! Cast down the tormentors, our godless enemies, by thy hand, O all-pure Sovereign Lady!

hope of Christians!

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^1019]
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast shown forth as the cherubic throne, and in thine arms didst carry the hope of our souls, O Birthgiver of God!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^1020]
No one who has recourse unto thee departs from thee put to shame, O all-pure Virgin Birthgiver of God, but asks for Grace and receives a gift according to his profitable petition!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^1021]
Of great gifts wast thou vouchsafed, O pure Virgin Mother of God: for thou didst bear in the flesh One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^1022]
Unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, do I direct the eye of my heart: despise not my faint sighing at the hour when thy Son judges the world; be unto me protection and helper!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^1023]
Thou art the transformation of the sorrowful and the relief of the sick, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God: save the city and the people, O pacification of those who do battle, calmness of the tempest-tossed, sole intercessor of the faithful!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^1024]
Through the prayers of her who gave Thee birth, O Christ, and of Thy martyrs, and Apostles, and prophets, and hierarchs, venerable ones, and the righteous, and all the saints: give rest unto Thy departed servants!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^1025]
We have known God Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: entreat Him for the salvation of our souls!

*Tone 7. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^1026]
With the angel we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice! O Bride of God, having called thee bridal chamber and gate, and fiery throne, and unhewn mountain, and unburnt bush!

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^1027]
Pacify our life through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, as we cry aloud unto Thee: O merciful Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^1028]
Rejoice, cloud of the spiritual and ineffable Sun, O Sovereign Lady; rejoice, all-radiant lamp; rejoice, all-golden lampstand: through thee, O all-pure one, Eve was delivered from the curse! But as having boldness toward thy well-pleasing Son and God, cease not to pray with thy maternal prayer, O all-pure one!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^1029]
O Christ, Thou didst sprout forth as Light from the Virgin, and didst enlighten the human race: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^1030]
Let us hymn the only all-pure Virgin after childbearing, as the Mother of God the Word, saying: Glory to Thee!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^1031]
Thou wast born ineffably of the Virgin, O Christ, and didst enlighten those in darkness who cry aloud: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^1032]
O thou who alone didst receive the Uncontainable, and didst give birth unto God the Word incarnate, pray that our souls may be saved!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^1033]
Pray, O Virgin, with the Apostles and martyrs, that we may find great mercy at the Judgment, O all-holy one!

*Tone 8. On Sunday evening, Theotokion:*[^1034]
Taking up the voice of the Archangel Gabriel, let us say: Rejoice, O Mother of God, who didst give birth unto Christ the Giver of life for the world!

*On Monday morning, Theotokion:*[^1035]
The heavenly powers hymn thee, O unwedded Mother full of grace, and we glorify thine unsearchable childbearing, O Birthgiver of God: pray that our souls may be saved!

*On Monday evening, Theotokion:*[^1036]
Rejoice, boast of the universe; rejoice, temple of the Lord; rejoice, overshadowing mountain; rejoice, haven of all; rejoice, golden lampstand; rejoice, honored boast of the Orthodox; rejoice, Mary, Mother of Christ God; rejoice, paradise; rejoice, divine table; rejoice, tabernacle; rejoice, all-golden urn; rejoice, hope of all!

*On Tuesday morning, Theotokion:*[^1037]
Thy protection, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, is spiritual medicine; for having recourse unto it, we are delivered from spiritual infirmities!

*On Wednesday evening, Theotokion:*[^1038]
I have recourse unto thy protection, O holy Virgin Birthgiver of God; I know that through thee I will find salvation, for thou, pure one, art able to help me!

*On Thursday morning, Theotokion:*[^1039]
Snatch me, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand of the manslaying serpent who desires to swallow me down to the end through craftiness; crush his jaws, I pray thee, and destroy his snares, that I may escape from his talons, magnifying thy patronage!

*On Friday evening, Theotokion:*[^1040]
O pure Virgin, gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved!

*On Saturday morning, Theotokion:*[^1041]
O Sovereign Lady, accept the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from all necessity and sorrow!

*Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokia of the Eight Tones:*[^1042]

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*[^1043]
When Gabriel announced unto thee: Rejoice! O Virgin, with the voice the Master of all was incarnate in thee, the holy Ark, even as righteous David said; thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having carried thy Creator! Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us through thy childbearing!

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*[^1044]
All beyond understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Birthgiver of God! In purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God: Him do thou entreat, that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*[^1045]
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: for having assumed the Passion upon the Cross in the flesh taken from thee, Thy Son and our God delivered us from corruption, as He loves mankind!

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*[^1046]
The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown to angels was shown forth through thee, O Birthgiver of God, unto those upon earth: God incarnate in unconfused union, Who voluntarily accepted the Cross for our sake, whereby having raised up the first-fashioned man, He saved our souls from death!

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*[^1047]
Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord; rejoice, wall and protection of those who have recourse unto thee; rejoice, haven safe from tempests and unwedded one, who didst bear thy Creator and God in the flesh: cease not to pray for those who hymn and worship thy childbearing!

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*[^1048]
Thou Who didst call Thy Mother blessed, didst come unto the Passion by voluntary choice, shining forth upon the Cross, desiring to seek out Adam, saying unto the angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost drachma is found! O our God, Who didst wisely order all things: Glory to Thee!

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*[^1049]
As the treasury of our resurrection, O all-hymned one, lead up from the pit and depth of transgressions those who place their trust in thee! For thou didst save those guilty of sin, having given birth unto our salvation: thou who wast a Virgin before childbearing, and a Virgin in childbearing, and after childbearing still remainest a Virgin!

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*[^1050]
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst overthrow death, and as God didst reveal the Resurrection: despise not those whom Thou didst create with Thy hand; manifest Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Birthgiver of God who bore Thee, who prays for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people!

*Dismissal Theotokia following the Troparia of the Saints, sung throughout the whole year at Vespers, at "God is the Lord" at Matins, and again at the end of Matins:*[^1051]

*Tone 1. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1052]
Wonder of wonders, O full of grace: creation rejoicing beholds [it] in thee! For thou didst conceive seedlessly, and didst give birth ineffably unto Him Whom the ranks of angels cannot behold: entreat Him for our souls!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1053]
O all-pure Birthgiver of God, blessed in the heavens and glorified upon earth: Rejoice, unwedded Bride!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1054]
O thou who without burning didst conceive the divine Fire, and seedlessly didst give birth unto the Fountain of life, the Lord: O Birthgiver of God full of grace, save those who magnify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1055]
Him Who is without mother in heaven beyond understanding and hearing, thou didst bear upon earth without a father: Him, O Birthgiver of God, do thou entreat for our souls!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1056]
Having thy patronage, O all-pure one, and delivered from cruel things through thy prayers, everywhere preserved by the Cross of thy Son, as is meet, we all piously magnify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O all-pure Birthgiver of God, [blessed] in the heavens...*

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1057]
*O thou who without burning didst conceive the divine Fire...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Him Who is without mother in heaven...*

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*Having thy patronage, O all-pure one...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*O all-pure Birthgiver of God, [blessed] in the heavens...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*
When Gabriel announced unto thee: Rejoice! O Virgin, with the voice the Master of all was incarnate in thee, the holy Ark, even as righteous David said; thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having carried thy Creator! Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us through thy childbearing!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Him Who is without mother in heaven...*

*Tone 2. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1058]
Being the fountainhead of compassion, vouchsafe mercy unto us, O Birthgiver of God; look down upon a sinning people, manifest thy power as always: for placing our hope in thee, we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice! even as Gabriel of old, the supreme commander of the bodiless hosts!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1059]
O holy Mother of the ineffable Light, honoring thee with angelic hymns, we piously magnify thee!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1060]
Partakers of the divine nature were we made through thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, ever-virgin: for thou didst bear for us God incarnate; therefore, as is meet, we piously magnify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1061]
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, cloud of the unsetting Light, who didst carry in thy bosom the Lord of glory!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1062]
Most glorified art thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, we hymn thee: for by the Cross of thy Son Hades was overthrown, and death was put to death, we the slain were raised up, and were vouchsafed life; we received paradise, the ancient delight! Therefore giving thanks, we glorify Christ our God as mighty, and the only many-merciful One!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Thee do we magnify* Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, O rod from which God sprouted forth seedlessly, destroying death upon the Tree![^1063]

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*
*Partakers of the divine nature were we made...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud...*

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*Most glorified art thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Rejoice, O rod...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1064]
All beyond understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Birthgiver of God! In purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God: Him do thou entreat, that our souls may be saved!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O holy Mother of the ineffable Light...*

*If Alleluia should occur in any Tone whatsoever, these are said:*[^1065]
Apostles, martyrs, and prophets, hierarchs, venerable ones, and the righteous, who have accomplished the good fight and kept the faith, having boldness toward the Savior: entreat Him as good on our behalf, we pray, that our souls may be saved!

Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, the souls of Thy servants, and forgive whatever sins they committed in this life; for no one is without sin, except Thee Who art able to grant rest also unto the departed.

*O holy Mother of the ineffable Light...*

*Tone 3. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1066]
At the beauty of thy virginity and the most radiant splendor of thy purity Gabriel was amazed, and cried aloud unto thee, O Birthgiver of God: What worthy praise shall I offer thee? What shall I call thee? I am in perplexity and I am astonished! Therefore, even as I was commanded, I cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1067]
Whithersoever anyone finds salvation, thither rightfully does he flee; and what other refuge is such as thou, O Birthgiver of God, sheltering our souls?

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1068]
Our refuge and strength, O Birthgiver of God, mighty help of the world: by thy prayers shelter thy servants from every necessity, O only blessed one!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Whithersoever anyone finds salvation...*

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1069]
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of power, O Birthgiver of God, thereby do we cast down the insolence of enemies, unceasingly magnifying thee with love!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Our refuge and strength...*

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1070]
The prophets preached, the Apostles taught, the martyrs confessed, and we believed thee to be truly the Birthgiver of God: therefore also do we magnify thine ineffable childbearing!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Whithersoever anyone finds salvation On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of power...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Our refuge and strength, O Birthgiver of God...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1071]
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: for having assumed the Passion upon the Cross in the flesh taken from thee, Thy Son and our God delivered us from corruption, as He loves mankind!

*At the end of Matins:*
*The prophets preached...*

*Tone 4. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1072]
Unto her who was reared in the temple in the Holy of Holies, clothed with faith and wisdom and measureless virginity, Gabriel the Supreme Commander brought greeting from heaven, and the "Rejoice!": Rejoice, O blessed one; rejoice, O most glorified one, the Lord is with thee!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1073]
As thou art higher than all creations, we are at a loss to hymn thee worthily, O Birthgiver of God; therefore we pray thee: Have mercy upon us!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1074]
Let us sinners and humble ones now earnestly flee unto the Birthgiver of God, and fall down in repentance, crying aloud from the depth of soul: O Sovereign Lady, help, having shown compassion upon us! Hasten, we perish from the multitude of transgressions; turn not thy servants away empty, for thee alone do we have as hope!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1075]
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Thou art the bush wherein without burning Moses beheld the flame of the fire of the Godhead!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1076]
O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced through thy most holy soul when thou didst behold Thy Son and God voluntarily crucified: Whom do thou, O blessed one, not cease to entreat, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1077]
Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Thou art the mountain from which the stone was ineffably cut off and crushed the gates of Hades!

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1078]
The Father's Word, Christ our God, Who was incarnate of thee, have we known, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, only pure, only blessed one: therefore unceasingly hymning thee do we magnify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
*As thou art higher than all creations...*

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Thou art the mountain...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1079]
The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown to angels was shown forth through thee, O Birthgiver of God, unto those upon earth: God incarnate in unconfused union, Who voluntarily accepted the Cross for our sake, whereby having raised up the first-fashioned man, He saved our souls from death!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Thee do we magnify, O Birthgiver of God, crying aloud: Thou art the bush...*

*Tone 5. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1080]
With the angels the heavenly things, with men the earthly things, with the voice of gladness, O Birthgiver of God, do we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O gate more spacious than the heavens! Rejoice, sole salvation of the earthborn! Rejoice, pure one full of grace, who didst bear God incarnate!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1081]
O Christ God, Who didst shine forth unto the world from the Virgin, showing forth sons of light through her: Have mercy upon us!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1082]
Show forth thy speedy protection and help and mercy upon thy servants, and calm the waves of vain thoughts, O pure one, and raise up my fallen soul, O Birthgiver of God: for I know, I know, O Virgin, that thou canst do whatsoever thou desirest!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1083]
O most holy Mother of God, rampart of Christians: deliver thy people as is thy wont, who earnestly cry unto thee; oppose shameful and proud thoughts, that we may cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, Ever-virgin!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1084]
By the Cross of thy Son, O woman graced of God, all idolatrous delusion was abolished, and the demonic might was trampled down; therefore we the faithful, as is meet, ever hymn and bless thee, and confessing thee to be truly the Birthgiver of God, we magnify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O Christ God, Who didst shine forth...*

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1085]
A strange mystery of the Virgin was shown saving unto the world: for from her Thou wast born without seed, and in the flesh Thou wast revealed without corruption, O Joy of all: Lord, glory to Thee!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O most holy Mother of God...*

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*By the Cross of thy Son...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*O Christ God, Who didst shine forth...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1086]
Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord; rejoice, wall and protection of those who have recourse unto thee; rejoice, haven safe from tempests and unwedded one, who didst bear thy Creator and God in the flesh: cease not to pray for those who hymn and worship thy childbearing!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O Christ God, Who didst shine forth...*

*Tone 6. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1087]
The beginning of salvation was Gabriel's utterance unto the Virgin: for she heard the "Rejoice!", and did not flee from the greeting; she doubted not, as Sarah in the tent, but thus she spoke: Behold the handmaid of the Lord, be it unto me according to thy word!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1088]
Thou didst receive the archangelic word, and wast shown forth as the cherubic throne, and in thine arms didst carry the hope of our souls, O Birthgiver of God!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1089]
Good Virgin Birthgiver of God, hope of the world: we ask thy sole and sovereign protection; have compassion upon a people without defense; entreat the merciful God that our souls may be delivered from every threatening, O only blessed one!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1090]
No one who has recourse unto thee departs from thee put to shame, O all-pure Virgin Birthgiver of God: but asks for grace and receives a gift according to his profitable petition!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1091]
O Virgin Birthgiver of God, entreat Thy Son, Who was voluntarily nailed upon the Cross and delivered the world from delusion, Christ our God, to have mercy upon our souls!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1092]
Him Who before the ages was born of the Father without a mother, the Son and Word of God, in the last years thou didst bear without a husband, incarnate from thy pure blood, O Birthgiver of God: Whom do thou entreat, that forgiveness of sins may be granted unto us before the end!

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1093]
O holy Sovereign Lady, pure Mother of our God, who didst ineffably give birth unto the Creator of all: together with the sacred Apostles ever entreat His goodness to deliver us from passions and grant us forgiveness of sins!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1094]
Of great gifts wast thou vouchsafed, O pure Virgin Mother of God: for thou didst bear in the flesh One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, unto the salvation of our souls!

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*O Virgin Birthgiver of God, entreat Thy Son...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Him Who before the ages was born of the Father without a mother...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1095]
Gideon foretells the conception, and David tells of thy childbearing, O Birthgiver of God: for as rain upon the fleece the Word descended into thy womb, and without seed thou didst sprout forth the salvation of the world, Christ our God, O holy earth full of grace!

*At the end of Matins:*
*Him Who before the ages was born of the Father without a mother...*

*Tone 7. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1096]
O immaculate Virgin Birthgiver of God: entreat Thy Son with the celestial powers that forgiveness of transgressions may be granted before the end unto us who faithfully glorify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1097]
Thou hast surpassed the heavenly powers, for thou wast shown forth as a divine temple, O blessed Birthgiver of God, as having given birth unto Christ, the Savior of our souls!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1098]
"Rejoice!" do we offer unto thee, O Birthgiver of God: for thou wast shown forth higher than the Angels, having given birth unto God!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1099]
Pacify our life through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God, as we cry aloud unto Thee: O merciful Lord, glory to Thee!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1100]
O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unceasingly entreat Christ God, Who was crucified for us and destroyed the dominion of death, that He may save our souls!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1101]
Deliver us, O Birthgiver of God, from the sins holding us fast: for we the faithful have no other hope save thee, and God Who was born of thee!

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1102]
The Fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one, is the fulfillment of the prophets and the Law: therefore glorifying thee with knowledge as the Birthgiver of God, we piously magnify thee!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1103]
The Fruit of thy womb, O Bride of God, was shown unto men as mediating salvation: therefore glorifying thee with mind and tongue as the Birthgiver of God, we the faithful magnify thee!

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unceasingly entreat Christ God, Who was crucified for us...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*Deliver us, O Birthgiver of God...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1104]
As the treasury of our resurrection, O all-hymned one, lead up from the pit and depth of transgressions those who place their trust in thee! For thou didst save those guilty of sin, having given birth unto our salvation: thou who wast a Virgin before childbearing, and a Virgin in childbearing, and after childbearing still remainest a Virgin!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1105]
Rejoice, thou who in thy womb didst contain Him Who is uncontainable in the heavens; rejoice, O Virgin, the preaching of the prophets, through whom Emmanuel shone forth; rejoice, Mother of Christ God!

*Tone 8. On Sunday evening, and on Monday at "God is the Lord":*[^1106]
Rejoice, thou who through the Angel didst receive the joy of the world; rejoice, thou who didst give birth unto thy Creator and Lord; rejoice, thou who wast accounted worthy to be the Mother of God!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1107]
Rejoice, gate of the King of Glory, through which the Most High alone passed, and preserved it alone sealed, unto the salvation of our souls!

*On Monday evening, and on Tuesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1108]
The unshakable confirmation of faith and honored gift of our souls, the Birthgiver of God, do we the faithful magnify with hymns: Rejoice, thou who in thy womb didst contain the Rock of life; rejoice, hope of the ends of the earth, protection of the sorrowful; rejoice, unwedded Bride!

*At the end of Matins:*[^1109]
O all-pure Virgin, save us by thy prayers, moving the maternal heart toward Thy Son and our God!

*On Tuesday evening, and on Wednesday at "God is the Lord":*[^1110]
Beholding the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Savior of the world upon the Cross, she that bare Thee weeping said: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption, but my maternal heart burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for all, O my Son and my God!

*At the end of Matins:*
*The Fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one...*

*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at "God is the Lord":*[^1111]
O all-pure Birthgiver of God, spiritual gate of life: deliver from perils those who faithfully flee unto thee, that we may glorify thy most holy childbearing unto the salvation of our souls!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O all-pure Virgin, save us...*

*On Thursday evening, and on Friday at "God is the Lord":*
*Beholding the Lamb, and Shepherd...*

*At the end of Matins:*
*The Fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one...*

*On Friday evening, and on Saturday at "God is the Lord":*[^1112]
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst overthrow death, and as God didst reveal the Resurrection: despise not those whom Thou didst create with Thy hand; manifest Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Birthgiver of God who bore Thee, who prays for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people!

*At the end of Matins:*
*O all-pure Virgin, save us by thy prayers, moving the maternal heart toward Thy Son and our God!*

*End of the Theotokia.*

*[Woodcut Tailpiece: Baroque ecclesiastical heraldic cartouche featuring papal tiara and crossed keys of Saint Peter] (Binding — Lower cover exterior: Blind-tooled dark brown leather over wooden boards) (Binding — Text block edge: Red tinted trimmed book edges) (Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet: Blank rear endpaper) (Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet: Blank rear flyleaf) (Binding — Upper cover exterior: Blind-tooled dark brown leather over wooden boards with central ornamental cross) (Binding — Upper cover interior: Dark blue-grey archival paper pastedown) (Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet: Blank front flyleaf)* — Archival inscription)*

Stefan *(Flyleaf — Canonical visitation inscription of Metropolitan Andrey Sheptytsky)*
Presented during the canonical visitation.[^1115]
Lviv, the 31st day of May 1934.
+ Andrey,
M[etropolitan] *(Binding — Fore-edge: Red tinted trimmed book edges) (Binding — Spine: Leather spine with raised bands and archival library label)*

# FOOTNOTES AND SCHOLARLY COMMENTARY



[^1]: Augustus III of Poland (Frederick Augustus II of Saxony), King of Poland and Grand Duke of Lithuania (1733–1763).

[^2]: Sylvester Lubieniecki-Rudnytsky (Sylwester Rudnycki-Lubieniecki), OSBM, Bishop of Lutsk and Ostroh (1756–1777), and Exarch of the Metropolitan See of Kyiv, Halych, and all Rus'.

[^3]: Hypatius (Ipatij) Biliansky (Bilynsky), OSBM, Protoarchimandrite of the Order of Saint Basil the Great from 1759 to 1771.

[^4]: The year of creation 7269 in the Byzantine era corresponds to 1761 A.D. (September year calculation: 7269 - 5508 = 1761).

[^5]: Justin the Philosopher (Justin Martyr), martyred in Rome under Emperor Marcus Aurelius around A.D. 165, along with Chariton, Charita, Euelpistus, Hierax, Peon, and Valerian. The Pochaiv Menaion also commemorates "another Martyr Justin", reflecting the distinction sometimes preserved in the Byzantine Synaxaria between Justin Martyr and Justin the Companion.

[^6]: Saint Charita (whose name in Greek, *Charito*, means "grace" or "grace-filled"), martyred together with Saint Justin.

[^7]: Saint Nikephoros I (ca. 758–828), Patriarch of Constantinople (806–815), theologian, historian, and staunch defender of the veneration of holy icons against the iconoclast Emperor Leo V the Armenian. He was deposed and exiled, reposing on June 2, 828.

[^8]: Cf. Heb. 8:2 ("of the true tabernacle, which the Lord pitched, and not man").

[^9]: The common Troparion for a Hierarch (*Rule of faith...*), Tone 4.

[^10]: The acrostic in the Greek Menaion reads: *Νικηφόρον ὡς τρόπαιον ᾄσμασιν μέλπω. Θεοφάνους.* Saint Theophanes the Branded (Graptos, ca. 778–845), renowned hymnographer and fellow confessor who defended icon veneration under Leo V and Theophilus.

[^11]: An explicit punning allusion to Emperor Leo V the Armenian (reigned 813–820; Greek *λέων*, Slavonic *левъ*, "lion"), who revived the iconoclast persecution, deposing Patriarch Nikephoros in 815. Leo was assassinated in 820 ("swiftly shattered near Hades").

[^12]: An allusion to the iconoclast Emperor Leo V the Armenian (reigned 813–820; Greek *λέων*, Slavonic *левъ*, "lion"), whose ferocious persecution of the Orthodox defenders of holy icons is termed "the lion's savagery" (*львово сыровство*).

[^13]: Kontakion of St. Nikephoros, Tone 4, to the Podoben "Thou hast appeared today" (*Явился еси*), the classical kontakion model for Theophany by Romanos the Melodist.

[^14]: Cf. Dan. 2:34, 45 (the mystical stone cut from the mountain without hands, prefiguring the virginal birth of Christ).

[^15]: Exapostilarion model of the Ascension, "While the disciples looked on" (*Учеником зрящим*).

[^16]: Saint Lucillian was an elderly pagan priest at Nicomedia who converted to Christ in extreme old age. Arrested under the governor Silvanus, he was cast into a fiery furnace together with four Christian youths—Claudius, Hypatius, Paul, and Dionysius. Miraculously preserved, they were transferred to Byzantium, where the four youths were beheaded and Lucillian was crucified. Saint Paula, a holy virgin who ministered to the martyrs in prison, was subsequently seized, scourged, and beheaded.

[^17]: Textual witness: Church Slavonic *господобы́инственныѧ* (a printing variant of *господоубі́йственнїи*, "Lord-slaying" / "Deicide"), alluding to the unbelieving Jews who denounced the newly converted elder to the Roman authorities.

[^18]: The acrostic of the canon in the Greek Menaion reads: *Λουκιλλιανοῦ μάρτυρος μέλπω κλέος* ("I sing the glory of the Martyr Lucillian").

[^19]: Sessional hymn model, Tone 4, "Thy tomb, O Savior" (*Гроб твой, Спасе*).

[^20]: Cf. Ps. 124:1 (LXX, "They that trust in the Lord shall be as Mount Zion; he that dwells in Jerusalem shall never be shaken").

[^21]: Cf. Rom. 8:35 ("Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? Shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?").

[^22]: Cf. Ps. 1:3 (LXX, "And he shall be like the tree planted by the outgoings of the waters, that shall bring forth its fruit in its season").

[^23]: Saint Metrophanes (reposed ca. 326), Bishop of Byzantium and first Patriarch of Constantinople under Saint Constantine the Great. Prevented by advanced age from attending the First Ecumenical Council of Nicaea (325), he sent his vicar Alexander, who succeeded him upon the patriarchal throne.

[^24]: Model sticheron Tone 6, Podoben: "Having set aside all" (*Все отложше*).

[^25]: Cf. Heb. 9:12, 24.

[^26]: Cf. Isa. 6:6 (the burning coal or ember taken with tongs by the Seraph from the celestial altar, a classical patristic and hymnographic type of the divine Incarnation borne in the hands and womb of the Mother of God).

[^27]: The acrostic of the canon in the Greek Menaion reads: *Μητροφάνους μέλπω σεπτόν, ἄναξ, κλέος* ("I sing, O King, the revered glory of Metrophanes").

[^28]: Kontakion model Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (*Вышних ища*).

[^29]: Sessional hymn model Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (*Премудрости*).

[^30]: Ps. 44:2 (LXX 44:1): "my tongue is the pen of a quickly writing scribe."

[^31]: The canonical incipit for this Tone 2 Automelon is "Thou hast appeared today" (Slavonic: *Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь*).

[^32]: Heb. 7:26: "For such a High Priest became us, who is holy, harmless, undefiled, separate from sinners, and made higher than the heavens."

[^33]: The canonical incipit for this Exapostilarion Automelon is "Give ear, ye women" (Greek: Γυναῖκες ἀκουτίσθητε; Slavonic: *Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите*).

[^34]: Tone 8 Automelon *Мꙋ́ченицы твоѝ гдⷭ҇и* corresponds to Greek Οἱ Μάρτυρές σου Κύριε ("Thy martyrs, O Lord").

[^35]: Tone 3 Automelon *Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры* corresponds to Greek Θείας πίστεως ("Of the divine faith").

[^36]: Cf. Heb. 12:23: "To the general assembly and church of the firstborn, which are written in heaven."

[^37]: Alluding to Ps. 96:5 (LXX): "The mountains melted like wax at the presence of the Lord."

[^38]: Cf. Ps. 109:3 (LXX): "Out of the womb before the morning star have I begotten thee."

[^39]: Ps. 103:2 (LXX): "Who coverest Thyself with light as with a garment."

[^40]: Dorotheus of Tyre (c. 255–362) was traditionally credited with compiling early hagiographical catalogues and the acts of the Seventy Apostles.

[^41]: Ps. 18:5 (LXX 18:4): "Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world."

[^42]: Cf. Ps. 22:2 (LXX): "He hath brought me up on the water of rest."

[^43]: Saint Dorotheus attained the venerable age of 107 years before his martyrdom under Julian the Apostate in 362.

[^44]: A reference to Saint Dorotheus's scholarly role as a liturgical compiler, annalist, and ecclesiastical writer.

[^45]: Ps. 140:2 (LXX): "Let my prayer be set forth before Thee as incense, the lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice."

[^46]: Tone 8 Automelon *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* corresponds to Greek Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος ("O most marvelous wonder").

[^47]: St. Bessarion of Egypt (4th–5th c.) lived as an ascetic open-air wanderer, never dwelling under a roof or sleeping in a bed.

[^48]: The Life of Saint Bessarion records his crossing of the Nile dryshod by the sign of the Cross and turning bitter sea water sweet for his thirsty disciple.

[^49]: Saint Bessarion brought down rain from heaven during a great drought through his prayers, like the Prophet Elijah (3 Kings 17–18 / LXX).

[^50]: Tone 4 Automelon *Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе* corresponds to Greek Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν ("Thou hast given a sign").

[^51]: Ps. 51:10 (LXX 51:8): "I am like a fruitful olive tree in the house of God."

[^52]: Slavonic *кѡ́нъ* translates the Greek τέλος / πέρας, signifying the completion or full course of ascetic struggle (cf. 2 Tim. 4:7).

[^53]: Eph. 4:13: "unto a perfect man, unto the measure of the stature of the fulness of Christ."

[^54]: Slavonic *бг҃омꙋ́жнѡ* translates Greek θεανδρικῶς (theandrically / God-manfully), expressing the divine-human mystery of the Incarnation.

[^55]: 1 Kings (1 Sam.) 2:1–2: "My heart is established in the Lord... because I have rejoiced in Thy salvation. There is none holy as the Lord, and there is none righteous as our God."

[^56]: Alluding to Moses entering the thick darkness where God was (Exod. 20:21).

[^57]: St. Bessarion of Egypt (4th–5th c.) lived in complete apostolic poverty, possessing neither dwelling nor clothing beyond a single garment, wandering like the birds of the air (cf. Matt. 6:26).

[^58]: Luke 2:35: "Yea, a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also."

[^59]: Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX): "O Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid: I considered Thy works, and was amazed."

[^60]: Cf. Heb. 11:38: "They wandered in deserts, and in mountains, and in dens and caves of the earth."

[^61]: Ps. 1:3: "And he shall be like a tree planted by the streams of waters."

[^62]: The Life of Saint Bessarion records that, like Joshua the son of Nun (Josh. 10:12–13), he caused the sun to stand still until he had finished his journey to a dying monk.

[^63]: Phil. 4:3; Rev. 20:12.

[^64]: Col. 2:14: "Blotting out the handwriting of ordinances that was against us."

[^65]: Jonah 2:2–9.

[^66]: The Life of Saint Bessarion records that he stood for forty days and forty nights without sitting or sleeping, with hands outstretched to heaven.

[^67]: Col. 3:5: "Mortify therefore your members which are upon the earth."

[^68]: 1 Kings (1 Sam.) 17:49–51.

[^69]: Referring to Saint Hilarion the Great of Palestine (c. 291–371), the ascetic forerunner of Saint Hilarion the New of Dalmatos.

[^70]: Saint Hilarion the New suffered torture, imprisonment, and exile for defending holy icons against the iconoclast Emperor Leo V the Armenian (reigned 813–820).

[^71]: Dan. 3:18–28.

[^72]: St. Bessarion miraculously crossed the Nile and Chrysorrhoas rivers dryshod, walking upon the surface of the waters.

[^73]: Matt. 22:11–12.

[^74]: Ps. 103:27; Ps. 144:15: "The eyes of all look unto Thee, and Thou givest them their food in due season."

[^75]: Ps. 96:11: "Light is dawned for the righteous, and gladness for the upright of heart."

[^76]: Ps. 17:40 (LXX 17:39): "And Thou hast girded me with strength for battle; Thou hast subdued under me them that rose up against me."

[^77]: Hosea 14:6 (LXX 14:5): "he shall blossom as the lily, and cast forth his roots as Lebanon."

[^78]: Ps. 91:13 (LXX 91:12): "The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be increased like a cedar in Lebanon."

[^79]: Saint Theodotus, Bishop of Ancyra in Galatia, suffered martyrdom under Governor Theotecnus during the Diocletianic persecution (c. 303).

[^80]: Tone 1 Automelon *Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ* corresponds to Greek Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων ("Joy of the celestial ranks").

[^81]: Rom. 8:35–39: "Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?"

[^82]: 2 Tim. 2:15: "rightly dividing the word of truth."

[^83]: Heb. 12:4: "Ye have not yet resisted unto blood, striving against sin."

[^84]: An alphabetical Greek acrostic playing on the name *Theodotus* (Greek Θεόδοτος, "God-given"), here rendered into Church Slavonic as *Бж҃їе даѧ́нїе бы́лъ є҆сѝ и҆́стиннѡ блаже́нне*.

[^85]: Ps. 17:10 (LXX 17:9): "He bowed the heavens also, and came down: and darkness was under His feet."

[^86]: 1 Cor. 1:24: "Christ the power of God, and the wisdom of God."

[^87]: Allusion to the torture instruments (iron claws / scraper, Slavonic *стро́гъ*) used upon Saint Theodotus to tear his flesh.

[^88]: Isa. 26:9 (LXX): "With my soul have I desired Thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I seek Thee early: for when Thy judgments are in the earth, the inhabitants of the world will learn righteousness."

[^89]: Dogmatic confession of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681) affirming two perfect natures (divine and human) united in the single Hypostasis of the incarnate Word.

[^90]: Jonah 2:2–9.

[^91]: Referring to the seven holy virgin martyrs of Ancyra: Tecusa, Alexandra, Claudia, Phaina, Euphrasia, Matrona, and Julitta, whom St. Theodotus supported during their trial and whose bodies he rescued from the lake where they had been drowned.

[^92]: Dan. 3:49–50 (LXX/Theodotion): "The angel of the Lord came down into the oven together with Azarias and his fellows, and smote the flame of the fire out of the oven, and made the midst of the furnace as it had been a moist whistling wind."

[^93]: The Life of St. Theodotus recounts that Fronto (Fronton), a pious Christian priest of the village of Malus, was given the martyr's body by miraculous arrangement and brought it to Malus, where a church was later erected over his holy relics.

[^94]: An explicit formulation of Chalcedonian and Sixth Ecumenical Council Christology: Christ possessing two natural wills and two operations, corresponding to His divine and human natures.

[^95]: 3 Kings (1 Kings) 18:38: "Then the fire of the Lord fell, and consumed the burnt sacrifice, and the wood, and the stones, and the dust, and licked up the water that was in the trench."

[^96]: Joel 3:18 (LXX 4:18): "the mountains shall drop down sweetness, and the hills shall flow with milk."

[^97]: St. Theodore Stratelates ("the General"), great-martyr of Heraclea under Licinius (c. 319). While his primary passion is commemorated on February 8, the feast on June 8 celebrates the translation of his honorable relics from Heraclea to his ancestral estate in Euchaita.

[^101]: Ps. 109:3 (LXX); 2 Pet. 1:19: "until the day dawn, and the day star arise in your hearts."

[^102]: Tone 2 Prosomoion *Ра́дꙋйсѧ* corresponds to Greek Χαίροις ἀσκητικῶν.

[^103]: The name *Theodore* (Greek Θεόδωρος) literally means "Gift of God", playing upon his name as distributor of divine blessings.

[^570]: Saint Julian of Tarsus (also known as Julian of Cilicia or of Antioch), martyred under Diocletian (c. 305–311), who was sewn into a sack filled with sand and venomous serpents and cast into the sea.

[^573]: The recovery of the martyr's body by a pious Christian woman on the shores of the Mediterranean near Alexandria.
