[^621]: Physical leaf p159 (Fol. 154r).

[^622]: Physical leaf p159 (Fol. 154r). Kontakion modeled on *Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь)*.

[^623]: Physical leaf p159 (Fol. 154r). Ode 7 Heirmos referencing Daniel 3.

[^624]: Physical leaf p159 (Fol. 154r). Reference to Psalm 109:3 (LXX) ("before the morning star have I begotten Thee").

[^625]: Physical leaf p159 (Fol. 154r). Ode 8 Heirmos referencing Daniel 3:57.

[^626]: Physical leaf p159 (Fol. 154r). Reference to Eusebius's martyrdom at the hands of the Arians.

[^627]: Physical leaf p160 (Fol. 154v). Ode 9 Heirmos.

[^628]: Physical leaf p160 (Fol. 154v). June 23 commemoration of the Holy Martyr Agrippina of Rome (martyred c. 257 under Valerian).

[^629]: Physical leaf p160 (Fol. 154v). Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried", modeled on *Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе)*.

[^630]: Physical leaf p160 (Fol. 154v). St. Agrippina was martyred in Rome, but her relics were translated secretly to Mineo in Sicily by the pious virgins Bassa, Paula, and Agathonica.

[^631]: Physical leaf p160 (Fol. 154v). Reference to Sts. Bassa and Paula who transported the relics of St. Agrippina across the sea to Sicily.

[^632]: On the commemoration of the Holy Martyr Agrippina (June 23), when the service is celebrated with "Alleluia" in place of "God is the Lord" during penitential or lesser weekday orders, these three penitential stichera to the Birthgiver of God are appointed to be chanted prior to the primary troparia.

[^633]: Common dismissal troparion for a holy female martyr (*Агница Твоя, Иисусе*), in Tone 4.

[^634]: The Slavonic acrostic (*краегране́сїе*) reads: *Хрїсто́ву невѣ́сту Агриппи́ну хвалю́* ("I praise Christ's bride Agrippina").

[^635]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the Canon to the Birthgiver of God (Tone 4): *Отверзу уста моя*, commonly utilized as the model heirmos for canons composed in Tone 4.

[^636]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the Canon (Tone 4): *Лукъ сильныхъ изнеможе*, citing the Canticle of Hannah (1 Kingdoms / 1 Samuel 2:4).

[^637]: Sessional Hymn to St. Agrippina following Ode 3, referencing her martyrdom under Emperor Valerian (c. 253–260) and the transfer of her relics to Sicily.

[^638]: Heirmos of Ode 4 (Tone 4): *Сѣдяй въ славѣ*, referencing the vision of the Prophet Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3).

[^639]: Heirmos of Ode 5 (Tone 4): *Ты, Господи мой, свѣтъ*, evoking Isaiah 26:9 and John 8:12.

[^640]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the Canon (Tone 4): *Пожру ти со гласомъ хваленїя, Господи*, quoting Jonah 2:10.

[^641]: Refers to Saints Bassa, Paula, and Agathonica, who transported the relics of St. Agrippina from Rome to Sicily following her martyrdom.

[^642]: Kontakion of St. Agrippina, Tone 4 (*Явился еси днесь*).

[^643]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the Canon (Tone 4): *Спасый во огни*, evoking the Three Holy Youths in the furnace of Babylon (Daniel 3).

[^644]: Historical reference to the miraculous deliverance of the city of Mineo in Sicily from Saracen invaders through the intercession of St. Agrippina.

[^645]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the Canon (Tone 4): *Отроки благочестивыя въ пещи*.

[^646]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Canon (Tone 4): *Камень нерукосѣчный*, quoting the prophetic vision of Daniel 2:34, 45.

[^647]: Recalls St. Agathonica along with St. Bassa and St. Paula, companions in honor of St. Agrippina.

[^648]: Feast of the Nativity of the Holy Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (June 24). This is ranked among the Great Feasts of the liturgical year with All-Night Vigil.

[^649]: Opening sticheron at Little Vespers, Tone 4 (*Званный свыше*), celebrating the dissolution of Elizabeth's barrenness and the herald of Christ.

[^650]: Refers to the muteness of the Priest Zechariah upon doubting the Archangel Gabriel's Annunciation in the temple (Luke 1:18–20, 64).

[^651]: Doxasticon at Little Vespers (Tone 1, *Подобаетъ Иоанну благовонїе*), celebrating the prophetic leap of St. John in the womb of Elizabeth (Luke 1:41).

[^652]: First Aposticha sticheron at Little Vespers, Tone 2, Podoben "O House of Ephratha" (*Доме Евфраѳовъ*).

[^653]: Luke 1:68 (The Benedictus of Zechariah).

[^654]: Alludes to Christ's testimony concerning John the Baptist: "Among them that are born of women there hath not risen a greater than John the Baptist" (Matthew 11:11; Luke 7:28).

[^655]: Luke 1:76.

[^656]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Little Vespers (Tone 8, *Виждь Елисаветь*), setting forth the dialogue between Elizabeth and the Mother of God during the Visitation (Luke 1:39–45).

[^657]: Dismissal Troparion of the Nativity of St. John the Baptist, Tone 4 (*Пророче и предтече пришествїя Христова*).

[^658]: Opening Idiomelon of John the Monk on "Lord, I have cried" at Great Vespers, Tone 4.

[^659]: Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" by Anatolius of Constantinople (9th century), celebrating the divine economy in the miraculous births of the Forerunner and the Messiah.

[^660]: Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried" by St. Andrew of Crete (c. 660–740).

[^661]: Quoting the Canticle of Zechariah (Luke 1:68) upon the naming of John and the recovery of speech.

[^662]: Doxasticon at Great Vespers (Tone 6, *Елисаветъ зачатъ предтечу благодати*), recalling the meeting of the Virgin Mary and Elizabeth (Luke 1:39–45).

[^663]: Composite liturgical Paremia for the Nativity of St. John the Baptist, weaving Genesis 17:15–19; 18:11–14; and 21:1–8, setting forth the miraculous birth of Isaac to the aged Abraham and barren Sarah as the Old Testament archetype of the birth of the Forerunner.

[^664]: Septuagint LXX numbering is followed for all Genesis references.

[^665]: Second Paremia at Great Vespers, taken from Judges 13:2–8, 13–14, 17–18, recounting the birth and Nazirite consecration of Samson from his mother's womb as a figure of John the Baptist.

[^666]: Judges 13:18: *вскую ты вопрошаеши имене моего? и то есть чудно* ("Why askest thou after my name, seeing it is secret / wonderful?").

[^667]: Third Paremia at Great Vespers: a centonized reading from the Book of the Prophet Isaiah combining Isaiah 40:1–3, 9; 41:17–18; 45:8; 48:20–21; and 54:1. The marginal rubrics in the printed codex explicitly indicate the transition across these chapters (*м҃, м҃а, м҃є, м҃и, н҃д*).

[^668]: Isaiah 54:1 is applied by the Church Fathers and hymnographers to the barren Elizabeth who brought forth the greatest among those born of women.

[^669]: Opening sticheron at the Litiya (Tone 1, *Искапайте горы сладость*), adapting the imagery of Amos 9:13 and Joel 3:18 to the birth of the Forerunner.

[^670]: John 1:29: *Се агнецъ Божїй вземляй грѣхи мiра* ("Behold the Lamb of God, which takes away the sin of the world").

[^671]: Quoting the Benedictus, the Canticle of Zechariah (Luke 1:76): *И ты, отроча, пророкъ вышняго наречешися: предыдеши бо пред лицемъ Господнимъ уготовати пути его*.

[^672]: Theotokion dogmatikon (*Храмъ и дверь еси*), referring to the Mother of God as the mystical temple and the closed Eastern gate of Ezekiel 44:1–2.

[^673]: Canticle of Zechariah (Luke 1:68; LXX Ps. 40:14 / 71:18).

[^674]: At the bottom of physical leaf p171, the catchword is *путь*, anticipating *путь спасенiя* ("the path of salvation"); however, on leaf p172, column 1, the top line reads *намъ спасенiя*, omitting *путь* due to a typesetter omission between folios 85r and 85v.

[^675]: Luke 1:76.

[^676]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha by the 9th-century hymnographer St. Kassia (Cassia) the Nun (*Исаиинъ нынѣ пророка гласъ*).

[^677]: Conflating Malachi 3:1 and Isaiah 40:3 as cited in Matthew 11:10 and Mark 1:2.

[^678]: Luke 1:43 (*И откуду мнѣ сiе, да прiиде Мати Господа моего ко мнѣ?*).

[^679]: The festal troparion of the Nativity of St. John the Baptist (*Пророче и предтече пришествiя Христова*), Tone 4, given in full at Little Vespers (see fol. 84r, cohort 17).

[^680]: Luke 1:76.

[^681]: Luke 1:68.

[^682]: Matins Gospel reading for the Feast of the Nativity of the Forerunner: Luke 1:24–25, 57–68, 76, 80 (Slavonic pericope 3: *Зачало 3*).

[^683]: Tone 4, Ode 1 Heirmos (*Тристаты крѣпкiя*), evoking Exodus 15:4 (*тристаты потопи въ Чермнѣмъ мори*), spiritualized as the drowning of the tripartite powers of the soul (the rational, irascible, and concupiscent faculties) in the deep of dispassion.

[^684]: Tone 4, Ode 1 Heirmos of the Second Canon by St. Andrew of Crete (*Наставльшему древле Израиля*), recalling the Exodus journey and the feeding with manna in the wilderness.

[^685]: The standard Katavasia for feasts of the Theotokos and major saints: Tone 4, Ode 1 (*Отверзу уста моя*).

[^686]: Tone 4, Ode 3 Heirmos (*Не мудростiю, и силою, и богатствомъ хвалимся*), based on the Canticle of Hannah (1 Kings / 1 Samuel 2:2–3) and Jeremiah 9:23–24.

[^687]: Tone 4, Ode 3 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Утверждаяй громъ, и созидаяй духъ*), echoing Amos 4:13 (*Яко се азъ утверждаяй громъ, и созидаяй духъ, и возвѣщаяй въ человѣцѣхъ христа своего*).

[^688]: Magnificat (Luke 1:48: *Се бо от нынѣ ублажатъ мя вси роди*).

[^689]: Tone 4, Ode 4 Heirmos (*Сѣдяй въ славѣ на престолѣ Божества*), drawing upon the vision of Isaiah 19:1 (*Се Господь сѣдитъ на облацѣ легцѣ*) and Habakkuk 3:2.

[^690]: Conflating Isaiah 40:3 and Malachi 3:1.

[^691]: Tone 4, Ode 4 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Услышахъ славное смотреніе твое*), reflecting the prophetic awe of Habakkuk 3:2 (*Господи, услышахъ слухъ твой и убояхся*).

[^692]: Typological Marian prophecies: the unburnt bush of Exodus 3:2, and the mountain not cut with hands of Daniel 2:34–35, 45.

[^693]: Tone 4, Ode 5 Heirmos (*Нынѣ востану, пророчески рече Богъ*), derived from Psalm 11:6 (LXX: *Нынѣ воскресну, глаголетъ Господь, положуся во спасенiе, не обинюся о немъ*).

[^694]: Alluding to Psalm 84:12 (LXX: *Истина от земли возсiя, и правда съ небесе приниче*).

[^695]: Alluding to Psalm 131:17–18 (LXX: *Уготовахъ свѣтильникъ помазанному моему: враги его облеку въ студъ*).

[^696]: Tone 4, Ode 5 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Возсiяй ми Господи свѣтъ повелѣнiй твоихъ*), drawn from Isaiah 26:9 (*От нощи утренюетъ духъ мой къ тебѣ, Боже, зане свѣтъ повелѣнiя твоя на земли*).

[^697]: Luke 1:13–17 (*И самъ предыдетъ предъ нимъ духомъ и силою Илiиною*).

[^698]: Ezekiel 44:1–2; Exodus 3:2; Daniel 2:34–35.

[^699]: Tone 4, Ode 6 Heirmos (*Прiидохъ во глубины морскiя*), based on the Canticle of Jonah 2:3–7.

[^700]: Tone 4, Ode 6 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Буря мя помышленiй постигши*), also drawing upon Jonah 2:3–6.

[^701]: Matthew 3:10 and Luke 3:9 (*Уже бо и сѣкира при корени древа лежитъ*).

[^702]: Tone 4, Ode 7 Heirmos (*Юношы три въ Вавѷлонѣ*), based on the Prayer of Azariah and the Song of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:26ff).

[^703]: Tone 4, Ode 7 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Въ пещи, авраамстiи отроцы*), Daniel 3:52 (*Благословенъ еси въ храмѣ святыя славы твоея*).

[^704]: Matthew 3:12 and Luke 3:17 (*Емуже лопата въ руцѣ его, и отребитъ гумно свое, и соберетъ пшеницу свою въ житницу*).

[^705]: Tone 4, Ode 8 Heirmos (*Избавителю всѣхъ всесилне*), Daniel 3:57 (*Благословенъ еси въ храмѣ святыя славы твоея* / *Благословите вся дѣла Господня Господа*).

[^706]: Tone 4, Ode 8 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Случескаго владыко премудростiю твоею составилъ еси*), alluding to Job 38:4–6 and Psalm 103:2, 5.

[^707]: Luke 1:63 (*И испрошь дщицу, написа, глаголя: Iоаннъ будетъ имя ему: и чудишася вси*).

[^708]: Daniel 3:57 (*Благословите вся дѣла Господня Господа*).

[^709]: Tone 4, Ode 9 Heirmos of the First Canon (*Сокровенное Божiе неизреченное въ тебѣ совершается*).

[^710]: Matthew 3:3; Mark 1:3; Luke 3:4; Isaiah 40:3.

[^711]: John 1:29 (*Се Агнецъ Божiй, вземляй грѣхи мiра*).

[^712]: The Ruthenian Pochaiv recension retains the supplication for the ruling King (*подкориваго подложи подъ нозѣ королю*), reflecting the historical civic petition of the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth.

[^713]: Tone 4, Ode 9 Heirmos of the Second Canon by St. Andrew of Crete, citing the Magnificat (Luke 1:49–50).

[^714]: Luke 1:17 (*Уготовати Господеви люди совершенны*).

[^715]: John 1:18 (*Единородный Сынъ, сый въ нѣдрѣ Отчи*).

[^716]: Theotokion concluding the Exapostilaria, linking the witness of the Prophets, Apostles, and Martyrs with the Mother of God.

[^717]: Tone 8 Prosomoia on the Praises (Lauds) celebrating the Nativity of the Forerunner.

[^718]: Luke 1:17; Malachi 3:1; Isaiah 40:3.

[^719]: Luke 1:41 (*И взыграся младенецъ въ чревѣ ея*).

[^720]: Luke 1:78 (*Посѣтилъ есть насъ востокъ свыше*).

[^721]: The standard festal Theotokion dismissal chant at Matins (*Богородице, ты еси лоза истинная*).

[^722]: Theotokion Kontakion (*Предстательство христiанъ непостыдное*), Tone 6.

[^723]: Psalm 63:11 (LXX).

[^724]: Psalm 63:2 (LXX).

[^725]: Liturgical Epistle for the Nativity of the Forerunner: Romans 13:11–14:4 (Slavonic pericope 112: *Зачало 112*).

[^726]: Luke 1:68; LXX Ps. 40:14.

[^727]: Luke 1:76.

[^728]: Liturgical Gospel for the Nativity of the Forerunner: Luke 1:1–25, 57–68, 76, 80 (Slavonic pericope 1: *Зачало 1*).

[^729]: Psalm 111:6b (LXX).

[^730]: St. Febronia of Nisibis (d. c. 304), virgin and venerable-martyr under Diocletian.

[^731]: Idiomelon by Anatolius on June 25, celebrating the afterfeast of the Nativity of the Forerunner.

[^732]: Selenus (Silvanus/Selenus), the Roman governor and persecutor who tortured and condemned St. Febronia to death in Nisibis.

[^733]: Luke 1:43 (*И откуду мнѣ сiе, да прiиде Мати Господа моего ко мнѣ?*).

[^734]: Luke 1:41–44.

[^735]: The standard Troparion of the Nativity of the Forerunner, Tone 4 (*Пророче и предтече*).

[^736]: The General Troparion for a Woman-Martyr (*Агница твоя, Иисусе*), Tone 4, applied to St. Febronia; cf. Romans 6:3–4; 2 Timothy 2:11–12.

[^737]: Canon of the afterfeast of the Nativity of the Forerunner by St. John of Damascus (Tone 8).

[^738]: Tone 8, Ode 1 Heirmos (*Воду прошедъ яко сушу*), recalling Exodus 14.

[^739]: Luke 1:11–13.

[^740]: Alluding to Psalm 115:6 (LXX: *Честна предъ Господемъ смерть преподобныхъ его*) and Exodus 13:2 / Luke 2:23.

[^741]: Canon of the Venerable-Martyr Febronia by St. Theophanes the Branded (Tone 8), bearing the Slavonic acrostic: *Пѣсньми тя пою мудренно Февронiе*.

[^742]: Tone 8, Ode 3 Heirmos (*Небеснаго круга Верхотворче, Господи*).

[^743]: Isaiah 11:1 (*И изыдетъ жезлъ изъ корене Иессеова, и цвѣтъ отъ корене его взыдетъ*).

[^744]: Psalm 50:19 (LXX: *Жертва Богу духъ сокрушенъ: сердце сокрушенно и смиренно Богъ не уничижитъ*).

[^745]: Numbers 17:8; Hebrews 9:4.

[^746]: Tone 8, Ode 3 Heirmos of the Second Canon (*Ты еси утвержденiе притекающихъ къ тебѣ, Господи*).

[^747]: The Kontakion of St. Febronia, drawing upon the Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25:1–13) and the cup of the Lord's suffering (Matthew 20:22).

[^748]: Sessional Hymn of the Nativity of the Forerunner, Tone 8.

[^749]: Sessional Hymn of St. Febronia concluded.

[^750]: Stavrotheotokion at Matins, mourning at the Cross.

[^751]: Tone 8, Ode 4 Heirmos (*Ты моя крѣпость, Господи*), based on Habakkuk 3:2.

[^752]: Matthew 5:8 (*Блажени чистiи сердцемъ, яко тiи Бога узрятъ*).

[^753]: Matthew 3:10 and Luke 3:9 (*Уже бо и сѣкира при корени древа лежитъ: всяко убо древо, еже не творитъ плода добра, посѣкается и во огнь вметается*).

[^754]: Philippians 3:20; Hebrews 11:10; 13:14.

[^755]: Galatians 4:26 (*А вышнiй Iерусалимъ свободь есть, иже есть мати всѣмъ намъ*).

[^756]: Song of Songs 4:7 (*Вся добра еси, ближняя моя, и порока нѣсть въ тебѣ*).

[^757]: The Slavonic idiom *отъ молодыхъ ногтей* ("from early youth", literally "from tender fingernails") translates the Greek idiom ἐξ ὀνύχων, denoting earliest childhood.

[^758]: Referring to St. Febronia's ministry in Nisibis reading and expounding the Holy Scriptures to women and catechumens.

[^759]: Tone 8, Ode 5 Heirmos (*Вскую мя отринулъ еси*), drawing upon Psalm 42:2 and Isaiah 26:9.

[^760]: Ode 5 Troparion 3 of the First Canon concluded.

[^761]: Psalm 45:5 (LXX: *Рѣчная устремленiя веселятъ градъ Божiй: освятилъ есть селенiе свое Вышнiй*).

[^762]: Commemorating the severe bodily mutilation suffered by St. Febronia, who had her limbs, breasts, and teeth violently severed by order of Selenus.

[^763]: Alluding to the extraction of St. Febronia's teeth during her interrogation.

[^764]: In the 1761 Pochaiv print, this stanza is labeled with the red rubric *Богородиченъ:*, though its content specifically commemorates the severed feet of St. Febronia while addressing Christ born of the Virgin and Febronia as bride of God.

[^765]: Tone 8, Ode 6 Heirmos (*Молитву пролiю ко Господу*), based on Jonah 2:3–8 and Psalm 141:2.

[^766]: Emphasizing the angelic life of St. John the Baptist in the wilderness (*земенъ ангелъ и небесный человѣкъ*).

[^767]: Genesis 28:12 (Jacob's ladder); Ezekiel 44:1–2 (the closed gate); Hebrews 9:4 (the golden jar of manna).

[^768]: Lysimachus, the nephew of the persecutor Selenus, witnessed St. Febronia's martyrdom, was converted to Christ along with his kinsman Primus, and was subsequently baptized.

[^769]: Bryene (Vryena), the abbess of the convent in Nisibis who raised and trained St. Febronia from childhood.

[^770]: Song of Songs 2:2 (*Яко кринъ въ тернiи, тако искренняя моя посредѣ дщерей*).

[^771]: Kontakion of the Nativity of the Forerunner, Tone 3 (*Дѣва днесь* model by St. Romanos the Melodist).

[^772]: Ikos of the Nativity of the Forerunner; cf. Matthew 11:9–11; Luke 1:41, 76; 7:26–28.

[^773]: Tone 8, Ode 7 Heirmos (*Отъ Iудеи дошедше отроцы*), Daniel 3:26ff.

[^774]: Luke 1:15 (*И вина и сикера не имать пити, и Духа Святаго исполнится еще изъ чрева матере своея*).

[^775]: Matthew 3:11; Luke 3:16.

[^776]: Concluding line of the seventh troparion of Ode 7 of the Canon to St. John the Baptist, concluding the sentence begun on physical leaf p190 (fol. 94v).

[^777]: The golden urn or jar (Heb. 9:4; Ex. 16:33; LXX: στάμνος, Slavonic: рꙋ́чка) containing the manna preserved in the Ark of the Covenant is a classical Byzantine typological figure of the Theotokos bearing Christ the living Manna.

[^778]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the Canon to the Holy Virgin-Martyr Febronia of Nisibis (Tone 8, incipit: *Ѻ҆́троцы є҆вре́йстїи въ пещѝ* / *Παῖδες Ἑβραίων ἐν καμίνῳ*).

[^779]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the Canon to St. John the Baptist (Tone 4, incipit: *Седмери́цею пе́щь* / *Ἑπταπλασίως κάμινον*), based on Daniel 3:19–23.

[^780]: Typological contrast between the water drawn from the rock of Meribah (Ex. 17:6; Num. 20:11; LXX Ps. 77:15–16) for the murmuring children of Israel, and the spiritual waters flowing through the birth of the Forerunner from barren loins.

[^781]: Direct liturgical quotation of Proverbs 10:7: "The memory of the righteous is with praises" (LXX: μνήμη δικαίων μετ' ἐγκωμίων).

[^782]: Concluding phrase of the Theotokion of Ode 8 of the Canon to St. John the Baptist, begun on physical leaf p191 (fol. 95r).

[^783]: Evoking the imagery of the Song of Songs 1:2–3 ("Thy breasts are better than wine..."), applied to the physical mutilation of St. Febronia under the judge Selenus during the Diocletianic persecution.

[^784]: Slavonic: *ѿ плѣни́цъ дꙋшетлѣ́нныхъ* (from the soul-destroying bonds/cords; cf. Prov. 5:22).

[^785]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Canon to St. John the Baptist (Tone 4, incipit: *Оу҆жасе́сѧ ѡ҆ се́мъ не́бо* / *Ἐξέστη ἐπὶ τούτῳ ὁ οὐρανός*).

[^786]: Slavonic: *є҆́же вонмѝ* (give ear unto it), rendered strictly in active indicative according to the Anti-Slop Standard, avoiding pseudo-archaic "hearken".

[^787]: St. John the Baptist was a kinsman according to the flesh of the Virgin Mary through her cousin Elizabeth (Luke 1:36).

[^788]: Evoking the self-identification of St. John the Baptist as the "friend of the Bridegroom" (John 3:29).

[^789]: In the 1761 Pochaiv print, the phrase *ве́щь дꙋшы́мѧ* (the matter of my soul) was mistakenly set twice through eye-skip: once following *пала́ющаго ꙋ҆́глѧ*, marked with deletion expunction dots above the letters in the physical ink, and properly in its syntactical position in the petition *ве́щь дꙋшѝ моеѧ̀ ѻ҆гне́мъ невеще́ственнымъ попалѝ*. The translation omits the expunged dittography in accordance with epigraphic fidelity.

[^790]: Typological reference to the coal touched to the lips of Isaiah by the seraph (Isa. 6:6–7), prefiguring the Christ-bearing womb of the Mother of God.

[^791]: Scriptural allusion to Psalm 44:14 LXX (Ps. 45:13 Masoretic): "All the glory of the king's daughter is from within" (πᾶσα ἡ δόξα τῆς θυγατρὸς τοῦ βασιλέως ἔσωθεν).

[^792]: Exapostilarion of the Nativity of the Forerunner, sung to the automelon *Tone 2, Podoben: "Hear, ye women"* (Slavonic: *Жєны̀ ꙋ҆слы́шите* / Greek: *Γυναῖκες ἀκουτίσθητε*).

[^793]: Theotokion of the Exapostilarion, addressing Christ as the hypostatic Wisdom (1 Cor. 1:24) and transcendent Word born of the Virgin.

[^794]: Aposticha Doxastikon (Glory) in Tone 2, summarizing the titles and dignity of the Forerunner.

[^795]: Tone 2 Stavrotheotokion to the automelon *When from the Tree* (Slavonic: *Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва* / Greek: *Ὅτε ἐκ τοῦ ξύλου*).

[^796]: Prokeimenon of the Liturgy in Tone 4, from Psalm 67:36 LXX (Ps. 68:35 Masoretic).

[^797]: Stichoi from Psalm 67:27 LXX (Ps. 68:26 Masoretic).

[^798]: Liturgical lection for the Holy Martyrs and Monastics (1 Cor. 12:7–11, Slavic Pericope 181).

[^799]: Alleluia verse in Tone 4, from Psalm 39:2 LXX (Ps. 40:1 Masoretic).

[^800]: Gospel lection from Luke 7:36–50 (Slavic Pericope 33), the woman who anointed the Lord's feet in the house of Simon the Pharisee.

[^801]: Communion hymn (Communion Hymn) from Psalm 111:6b LXX (Ps. 112:6b Masoretic).

[^802]: June 26 commemoration of St. David of Thessalonica (c. 450–535), tree-dwelling dendrite hermit and wonderworker.

[^803]: Tone 4 Sticheron to St. David. The 1761 Pochaiv rubricator printed *Подо́бенъ: Пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* (normally a Tone 8 incipit); the poetic meter conforms to the automelon *As one valiant among martyrs* (Tone 4, *Я́ко до́бля* / *Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν Μάρτυσιν*).

[^804]: Allusion to St. David's ascetic discipline of constructing a wooden hut or platform in an almond tree outside the walls of Thessalonica, where he dwelt exposed to the elements for three years before his reclusion.

[^805]: Commemorating the historical miracle when St. David appeared before the Byzantine Emperor Justinian I in Constantinople, holding live glowing charcoal in his bare hands without burning, to plead successfully for Thessalonica.

[^806]: Concluding lines of the Doxastikon (Glory) of the Stichera at Lord, I have cried, begun on physical leaf p194 (fol. 96v).

[^807]: Stavrotheotokion in Tone 6, model *Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day"* (Slavonic: *Трїдне́венъ* / Greek: *Τριήμερος ἀνέστης*).

[^808]: Aposticha Doxastikon (Glory) in Tone 8, the common monastic veneration incipit *Мона́хѡвъ мно́жества* (*Μοναχῶν τὰ πλήθη*).

[^809]: Tone 8 Stavrotheotokion to the automelon *O marvelous wonder* (Slavonic: *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* / Greek: *Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος*).

[^810]: Common Dismissal Troparion of a Venerable Monastic Father in Tone 8 (*Въ тебѣ̀ ѻ҆́ч҃е и҆звѣ́стнѡ спасе́сѧ* / *Ἐν σοὶ Πάτερ ἀκριβῶς διεσώθη*).

[^811]: Canon composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886).

[^812]: The Greek acrostic reads: ᾄδω μάκαρ σου τὸν βίον θείοις λόγοις (I sing thy life, O blessed one, with divine words), transliterated into Church Slavonic as *Пою̀ бл҃же́нне твоѐ житїѐ бж҃е́ственными словесы̀*.

[^813]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Конѧ̀ и҆ вса́дника въ мо́ре чермно́е* / *Ἵππον καὶ ἀναβάτην*), based on Exodus 15:1–19.

[^814]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Водрꙋзи́вый на ничесо́мже* / *Ὁ πήξας ἐπ' οὐδενός*), celebrating the cosmology of Job 26:7.

[^815]: St. David of Thessalonica was the namesake of the Holy Prophet and King David.

[^816]: Sessional Hymn (Kathisma) in Tone 8 to the automelon *Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom"* (Slavonic: *Премꙋ́дрости* / Greek: *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον*).

[^817]: Theotokion of the Sessional Hymn, imploring the unceasing mediation of the Mother of God amid manifold temptations.

[^818]: Sessional Stavrotheotokion in Tone 8, evoking the sorrow of the Mother of God standing beside the Cross.

[^819]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Бж҃е́ственное твоѐ разꙋмѣ́въ и҆стоща́нїе* / *Τὴν θείαν ἐννοήσας σου κένωσιν*), based on Habakkuk 3:2–13.

[^820]: Slavonic: *Гла́въ живото́чныхъ* represents Greek *πηγῶν ζωηρρύτων* (life-flowing fountains/springs), rendered epigraphically from the Church Slavonic text.

[^821]: Allusion to the hesychastic stillness (*безмо́лвїе* / *ἡσυχία*) practiced by St. David during his reclusion.

[^822]: Referring to St. David's endurance beyond normal human bodily limitations during his three years in the almond tree.

[^823]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Ѡ҆дѣѧ́йсѧ свѣ́томъ ꙗ҆́кѡ ри́зою* / *Ὁ ἀναβαλλόμενος φῶς ὡς ἱμάτιον*), based on Psalm 103:2 LXX.

[^824]: Continued commemoration of St. David's dendrite ascetical struggles in the open air, enduring winter frost and summer heat in imitation of Christ's patience on the Cross.

[^825]: Second commemoration of the miracle of the coals in the presence of Justinian I.

[^826]: Direct allusion to 1 Corinthians 13:12 ("For now we see through a glass, darkly; but then face to face").

[^827]: Christ the true Vine (John 15:1) producing the wine of compunction through His ascetics to cure the spiritual drunkenness of the passions.

[^828]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Неи́стовствꙋющеесѧ бꙋ́рею* / *Μαινομένην κλύδωνι*).

[^829]: Referring to the cell built for St. David beneath the almond tree by Metropolitan Dorotheus of Thessalonica, wherein he lived enclosed for over forty years.

[^830]: Bowing under the yoke of Christ's commandments (Matt. 11:29–30).

[^831]: The miracles and exorcisms performed by St. David throughout Macedonia and Thrace.

[^832]: Kontakion of St. David in Tone 2 to the automelon *Tone 2, Podoben: "The angelic choir"* (Slavonic: *Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй* / Greek: *Χορὸς ἀγγελικός*).

[^833]: Ikos of St. David, continuing the themes of the Kontakion and celebrating his boldness before Emperor Justinian.

[^834]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Превозноси́мый ѻ҆тце́въ гдⷭ҇ь* / *Ὁ ὑπερυψούμενος τῶν Πατέρων Κύριος*), based on Daniel 3:26–28.

[^835]: Description of the saint's stance upon the boughs of the almond tree.

[^836]: The ascetic heart transformed into a vessel of the Holy Spirit.

[^837]: Emperor Justinian I and the imperial court in Constantinople bowing in veneration before St. David after witnessing the unburning coals.

[^838]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *Тебѣ̀ вседѣ́телю въ пещѝ ѻ҆́троцы* / *Σοὶ τῷ παντουργῷ ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ παῖδες*), based on Daniel 3:57.

[^839]: Figurative identification of the saint as the morning star or dawn of spiritual discernment.

[^840]: Scriptural allusion to Psalm 36:11 LXX and Matthew 5:5 ("Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth").

[^841]: Commemorating St. David's prophetic foresight, including the prediction of his own death on board the ship returning from Constantinople.

[^842]: Typological quotation of Psalm 46:5 LXX (Ps. 47:4 Masoretic): "The beauty of Jacob which He loved."

[^843]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Canon to St. David (Tone 5, incipit: *И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й* / *Ἡσαΐα χόρευε*), based on Isaiah 7:14 and Zechariah 6:12 LXX.

[^844]: Allusion to Psalm 1:3 LXX: "He shall be like a tree planted by the streams of waters."

[^845]: St. David foretold that as soon as the ship returning from Constantinople rounded the cape of Thessalonica, he would surrender his soul to God, which took place c. 535.

[^846]: Prophetic typology of Ezekiel 44:2 regarding the shut eastern gate of the sanctuary through which the Lord alone entered.

[^847]: Exapostilarion of St. David to the automelon *Tone 2, Podoben: "Hear, ye women"* (Slavonic: *Жєны̀ ꙋ҆слы́шите* / Greek: *Γυναῖκες ἀκουτίσθητε*).

[^848]: Theotokion of the Exapostilarion, confessing the true Theotokos who bore the Conqueror of death.

[^849]: Aposticha Doxastikon (Glory) in Tone 6, citing Psalm 131:4 LXX ("I will not give sleep to mine eyes, nor slumber to mine eyelids") and John 14:23 (the Father and Son making an abode in him).

[^850]: Tone 5 Stavrotheotokion modeled on the automelon *Rejoice, O container of ascetics* (Slavonic: *Ра́дꙋйся по́стникѡвъ* / Greek: *Χαίροις ἀσκητικῶν*).

[^851]: June 27 commemoration of our venerable father Sampson the Hospitable (Xenodochos, d. c. 530), physician of Rome and founder of the great hospital in Constantinople.

[^852]: Tone 1 Sticheron to St. Sampson to the automelon *Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs"* (Slavonic: *Прехва́льніи мꙋ́ченицы* / Greek: *Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες*).

[^853]: Second Sticheron to St. Sampson, celebrating his entry into the heavenly paradise and the tree of life.

[^854]: Physical leaf p201 (Fol. 100r [р҃]). The sticheron concluding the Vespers aposticha of St. Sampson the Hospitable (Xenodochos of Constantinople, d. c. 530) continues from physical leaf p200.

[^855]: The Heirmos of Ode 1 draws on Exodus 15:1ff.

[^856]: On physical leaf p201, the lower margin bears a paper repair slip beneath the opening words of the Heirmos of Ode 3, over which the completion of the phrase is supplied in an early hand; the full text is printed at the head of physical leaf p202.

[^857]: Physical leaf p202 (verso of fol. 101, printed page unmarked). Sessional Hymn to St. Sampson following Ode 3 of the Canon.

[^858]: The Stavrotheotokion invokes the prophecy of Simeon from Luke 2:35 ("a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also").

[^859]: The Heirmos of Ode 4 draws upon the Canticle of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:2 LXX).

[^860]: Physical leaf p203 (recto, printed page 102 [рв]). The Heirmos of Ode 5 echoes Isaiah 26:9.

[^861]: The Heirmos of Ode 6 echoes the prayer of Jonah from the belly of the whale (Jonah 2:3, 7 LXX).

[^862]: The Kontakion of St. Sampson follows the classical Byzantine model tone and meter of Romanos the Melodist's kontakion for the Holy First-Fruits.

[^863]: Physical leaf p204 (verso of fol. 102). The Heirmos of Ode 7 draws on the narrative of the Three Holy Children in Daniel 3.

[^864]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 draws upon the Song of the Three Holy Children (Dan. 3:57–88 LXX).

[^865]: Alluding to Psalm 111:3, 9 (LXX).

[^866]: Physical leaf p205 (recto, printed page 103 [рг], mispaginated in the original Pochaiv foliation). Ode 9 of the Canon to St. Sampson concludes at the top of the leaf.

[^867]: The Exapostilarion follows the prosomoion model tone and meter "The sky with stars" (*Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми*).

[^868]: Psalm 115:6 (LXX).

[^869]: The liturgical Epistle reading appointed for a venerable monk-healer (Gal. 5:22–6:2, Pericope 213).

[^870]: The Gospel reading appointed for the venerable (Luke 12:32–40, Pericope 67).

[^871]: Psalm 111:6b–7a (LXX).

[^872]: Physical leaf p206 (verso of fol. 103). The feast of the Translation of the Relics of the Holy Unmercenaries Cyrus and John from Canopus to Menouthis in Egypt under St. Cyril of Alexandria (c. 412).

[^873]: The Doxasticon contrasts the distinct vocations of St. Cyrus (the physician and solitary monk) and St. John (the Christian soldier).

[^874]: The Doxasticon at the Aposticha continues across the leaf boundary onto physical leaf p207.

[^875]: Physical leaf p207 (recto, printed page 104 [рд]). The Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Great Vespers concludes at the top of the leaf.

[^876]: The Stavrotheotokion is set to the model melody "When from the Tree" (*Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва*).

[^877]: The Heirmos of Ode 1 invokes the Song of Moses in Exodus 15:1ff.

[^878]: The Heirmos of Ode 3 invokes the prayer of Hannah in 1 Kingdoms (1 Sam.) 2:2 (LXX).

[^879]: Invoking the theological triad of 1 Corinthians 13:13 (faith, hope, love).

[^880]: Alluding to 1 Corinthians 10:4 ("they drank of that spiritual Rock that followed them: and that Rock was Christ").

[^881]: Physical leaf p208 (verso of fol. 104). The Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Canon concludes with an explicit citation of Colossians 2:9 ("for in Him dwells all the fulness of the Godhead bodily").

[^882]: The Sessional Hymn is chanted twice to the melody of the Kontakion of the Exaltation of the Holy Cross (*Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ во́лею*).

[^883]: The Heirmos of Ode 4 draws upon Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX).

[^884]: The Heirmos of Ode 5 draws upon Isaiah 26:9, 13 (LXX).

[^885]: Referring to the virgin martyr Athanasia and her three young daughters (Theoctiste, Theodota, and Eudoxia), whom Cyrus and John accompanied and strengthened to martyrdom in Canopus.

[^886]: Physical leaf p209 (recto, printed page 104 [рд], mispaginated for 105). Ode 5 of the Canon concludes at the top of the leaf.

[^887]: The Heirmos of Ode 6 draws upon Psalm 103:2 (LXX).

[^888]: The Kontakion is set to the celebrated model tone and meter of Romanos the Melodist's Nativity kontakion (*Дѣ́ва дне́сь*). The term *рꙋкодѣ́йствїемъ* literally denotes surgical intervention or manual medical practice, highlighting Cyrus's professional craft.

[^889]: The Ikos echoes 1 Timothy 1:15 ("whereof I am chief / first").

[^890]: The Heirmos of Ode 7 draws upon Daniel 3:49–50 (LXX).

[^891]: The third troparion of Ode 7 continues across the leaf boundary onto physical leaf p210.

[^892]: Physical leaf p210 (verso of fol. 105). Ode 7 of the Canon concludes at the top of the leaf.

[^893]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 draws upon the Canticle of the Three Holy Children (Dan. 3:57ff. LXX).

[^894]: Alluding to Romans 12:1 ("present your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable unto God, which is your reasonable service").

[^895]: Addressed directly to the virgin martyrs St. Athanasia and her daughters, who were martyred together with Cyrus and John.

[^896]: The Heirmos of Ode 9 draws upon the Magnificat (Luke 1:46–55).

[^897]: The Exapostilarion is chanted to the model melody "The sky with stars" (*Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми*).

[^898]: The Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Matins concludes on physical leaf p211 with the opening of the Great Feast of the Holy Apostles Peter and Paul (June 29).

[^899]: Saints Cyrus and John the Unmercenaries (commemorated June 28) suffered martyrdom under Diocletian together with the holy woman Athanasia and her three virgin daughters: Theodota, Theoctiste, and Eudoxia.

[^900]: Liturgical pericope composite 153 to the Corinthians corresponds to 1 Cor. 12:27–13:8; Gospel pericope composite 34 from the midpoint corresponds to Matt. 10:1, 5–8.

[^901]: In the 1761 Pochaiv imprint, the bottom lines of folio 105r, column 2, are lightly inked: *и́хъ богосло́віе и́стинноє є́ллин-* (*their true theology dispelling Hellenic delusion*), completing the second sticheron at Small Vespers.

[^902]: Slavonic *е́йже* (dative feminine) refers to the Church (*це́рковь*): "pray now for her, that she may be delivered from tribulations..."

[^903]: Slavonic *Андре́а пи́ррскагѡ* renders the Byzantine hymnographer Andrew of Pyrgos (Pyrrhus).

[^904]: The three readings at Great Vespers for the Feast of the Preeminent Apostles Peter and Paul are taken from the First Catholic Epistle of Peter: 1 Peter 1:3–9; 1 Peter 1:13–19; and 1 Peter 2:11–24.

[^905]: Slavonic *Андре́а і҆еросолими́та* designates Saint Andrew of Jerusalem (Saint Andrew of Crete, who lived in Jerusalem before moving to Constantinople and Crete).

[^906]: Attributed to Arsenius the Hymnographer (9th c.).

[^907]: Saint Germanus I, Patriarch of Constantinople (ca. 659–740).

[^908]: Slavonic *Вѷзанті́ево* indicates a hymn composed by the Byzantine hymnographer (Leo VI the Wise or Byzantios).

[^909]: Slavonic *Є҆фре́ма ка́ріа* renders the Byzantine hymnographer Ephrem of Caria.

[^910]: Slavonic *Гла́вную добле́нія ѡ҆ста́вль* translates the Greek incipit Τὴν ἄκран ἐκλιπὼν ἀνδρείαν (referring to the abandonment of reliance on earthly vigor to receive divine revelation).

[^911]: Saint John of Damascus composed the first canon to Saint Peter; the second canon to Saint Paul is attributed to Saint John of Damascus or Saint Cosmas.

[^912]: The Katavasia incipit *Ѿве́рзꙋ ꙋ҆ста̀ моѧ̑* corresponds to the Heirmoi of the Canon of the Mother of God (Tone 4).

[^913]: The liturgical text contrasts Rome, the site of the martyrdom of both Holy Apostles Peter and Paul, with Tarsus in Cilicia, the native city of the Apostle Paul (cf. Acts 22:3), which piously preserves the memory of his infancy and youth.

[^914]: Cf. Matthew 16:19: "And I will give unto thee the keys of the kingdom of heaven: and whatsoever thou shalt bind on earth shall be bound in heaven: and whatsoever thou shalt loose on earth shall be loosed in heaven."

[^915]: Cf. Acts 3:6: "Silver and gold have I none; but such as I have give I thee: In the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth rise up and walk."

[^916]: An allusion to the judgment upon Ananias and Sapphira in Acts 5:1–11, who lied to the Holy Spirit and fell dead at the Apostle Peter's rebuke.

[^917]: The celebrated festal Kontakion of the Holy Pre-eminent Apostles Peter and Paul in Tone 2 (*Твердыя и боговещанныя проповедатели*), composed by St. Romanos the Melodist.

[^918]: An allusion to Christ's threefold questioning of Peter on the shore of the Sea of Tiberias following the Resurrection: "Simon, son of Jonas, lovest thou Me?" (John 21:15–17), restoring him after his threefold denial during the Passion.

[^919]: Cf. Romans 15:19: "Through mighty signs and wonders, by the power of the Spirit of God; so that from Jerusalem, and round about unto Illyricum, I have fully preached the gospel of Christ."

[^920]: Cf. 2 Corinthians 12:2–4: "I knew a man in Christ above fourteen years ago... how that he was caught up into paradise, and heard unspeakable words, which it is not lawful for a man to utter."

[^921]: Cf. John 21:18–19: "Verily, verily, I say unto thee... when thou shalt be old, thou shalt stretch forth thy hands, and another shall gird thee, and carry thee whither thou wouldest not. This spake He, signifying by what death he should glorify God."

[^922]: An allusion to the miracles of the Apostle Peter in Lydda and Joppa: healing the paralyzed Aeneas (Acts 9:33–34) and raising the disciple Tabitha (Dorcas) from the dead (Acts 9:36–41).

[^923]: Cf. Acts 5:15: "Insomuch that they brought forth the sick into the streets, and laid them on beds and couches, that at the least the shadow of Peter passing by might overshadow some of them."

[^924]: The patristic tradition of the confrontation between the Apostle Peter and Simon Magus in Rome, recounted in the early apocryphal *Acts of Peter* and affirmed by Eusebius, Cyril of Jerusalem, and Arnobius, wherein Simon attempted to demonstrate supernatural power by flying in the air, but plummeted to earth at Peter's prayer.

[^925]: Cf. 1 Corinthians 13:12: "For now we see through a glass, darkly; but then face to face: now I know in part; but then shall I know even as also I am known."

[^926]: An extensive hymnographic paraphrase of the Confession of Peter at Caesarea Philippi in Matthew 16:13–19.

[^927]: St. Cosmas the Melodist (Bishop of Maiuma, c. 675–c. 752), author of many festal canons and stichera in the Byzantine liturgical corpus.

[^928]: Epistolary reading: 2 Corinthians 11:21–12:9 (composite pericope 193 of the Slavonic Apostolos).

[^929]: Gospel reading: Matthew 16:13–19 (composite pericope 67 of the Slavonic Evangelion).

[^930]: The Synaxis of the Holy, Glorious, and All-Praised Twelve Apostles (*Собор святых славных и всехвальных апостол 12*), celebrated on June 30 as the joint commemoration of all twelve Apostles following the feast of Saints Peter and Paul on June 29.

[^931]: Cf. 1 Corinthians 15:10: "But by the grace of God I am what I am: and His grace which was bestowed upon me was not in vain; but I laboured more abundantly than they all: yet not I, but the grace of God which was with me."

[^932]: The "horns of the Cross" (*кр(ес)тныя роги*) employs biblical and patristic sacrificial altar imagery, comparing the arms of the Cross to the horns of the temple altar (cf. Exod. 27:2; Ps. 117:27 [LXX]), upon which Christ offered Himself and shattered the power of demons.

[^933]: Alluding to the joint apostolate and ultimate martyrdom of Saints Peter and Paul in Rome, the imperial center of the Western world.

[^934]: "Hellenic deceit" (*еллинскую прелесть*) is the standard Byzantine liturgical designation for pagan classical philosophy, polytheism, and idolatry, which were overcome by the divine preaching of the Apostles.

[^935]: A theological juxtaposition of the two supreme Apostles as models of repentance: Peter, who denied Christ during the Passion and wept bitterly (Luke 22:54–62), and Paul, who fiercely persecuted the nascent Church before his conversion on the road to Damascus (Acts 9:1–22).

[^936]: The common Troparion of the Holy Pre-eminent Apostles Peter and Paul in Tone 4 (*Апостолов первопрестольницы*).

[^937]: Cf. Isaiah 7:14; Matthew 1:23: "Behold, a virgin shall be with child, and shall bring forth a son, and they shall call His name Emmanuel, which being interpreted is, God with us."

[^938]: The acrostic of the canon for the Synaxis of the Twelve Apostles on June 30 (*Петра почитаю, Павла пою разумно*), translated from the original Byzantine Greek alphabetical acrostic.

[^939]: The second canon for the Synaxis of the Twelve Apostles on June 30, attributed to St. Theophanes the Branded (c. 778–845), Metropolitan of Nicaea, celebrated hymnographer and confessor for the holy icons.

[^940]: The acrostic of the canon: "I honor the wise Apostles of Christ" (*Христовы почитаю мудрыя апостолы*), corresponding to the Greek acrostic Χριστοῦ γεραίρω τοὺς σοφοὺς Ἀποστόλους.

[^941]: An allusion to the calling of Simon Peter through his brother Andrew (John 1:40–42) and the baptism of Saul (Paul) by Ananias in Damascus (Acts 9:17–18).

[^942]: A festal Sessional Hymn in Tone 8 honoring the pre-eminent Apostles Peter and Paul, highlighting their role as luminaries who dispelled the darkness of idolatry.

[^943]: An allusion to the Apostle Peter rebuking Simon the sorcerer for attempting to purchase the gift of the Holy Spirit with money (Acts 8:18–24), and the Apostle Paul striking Elymas the sorcerer with temporary blindness at Paphos (Acts 13:6–12).

[^944]: The hymnographer contrasts the unlettered speech of the Galilean fisherman Peter and the eloquence of secular Greek poets and philosophers such as Homer (*Омиръ* / Ὅμηρος), noting that apostolic preaching, seasoned with divine salt (cf. Col. 4:6), captivated and converted philosophers to faith in Christ.

[^945]: Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX): "God shall come from Teman [the south], and the Holy One from mount Paran: the shaded mountain." Patristic exegesis interprets this prophecy as prefiguring the Incarnation of the Son of God from the ever-virgin Mother of God.

[^946]: The contrast between Peter's threefold denial (Matt. 26:69–75) followed by his threefold restoration through love (John 21:15–17), and Paul's former persecution of the Church (Acts 9:1–2; Gal. 1:13) followed by his apostolic mission.

[^947]: The distinct charisms of the pre-eminent Apostles: Peter receiving the keys of the Kingdom of Heaven (Matt. 16:19), and Paul being caught up to the third heaven and Paradise to hear ineffable words (2 Cor. 12:2–4).

[^948]: Cf. Acts 5:15 (the shadow of Peter falling upon the sick to heal them) and Acts 19:11–12 (handkerchiefs and aprons brought from Paul's body healing diseases and casting out evil spirits).

[^949]: The festal Kontakion for the Synaxis of the Twelve Apostles in Tone 2 (*Камень Христос, каменя веры*), celebrating the Apostles as foundational pillars of the Church.

[^950]: Cf. Galatians 2:7–9: the partition of the apostolic ministry, with Peter entrusted with the Gospel to the circumcised (the Jews) and Paul with the Gospel to the uncircumcised (the Gentiles).

[^951]: The parallel resurrection miracles performed by the chief Apostles: Peter raising Tabitha (Dorcas) at Joppa (Acts 9:36–41), and Paul raising the youth Eutychus at Troas after he fell from the third loft (Acts 20:9–12).

[^952]: Peter receiving the heavenly vision of the vessel descending and baptizing the Roman centurion Cornelius (Acts 10), and Paul baptizing early converts: Crispus, Gaius, and the household of Stephanas (cf. 1 Cor. 1:14–16; Acts 18:8).

[^953]: Divine liberations from bondage: the Angel of the Lord leading Peter out of Herod's prison (Acts 12:6–11), and the midnight earthquake releasing Paul and Silas from their chains and stocks in the prison at Philippi (Acts 16:23–30).

[^954]: Cf. Romans 10:15; Isaiah 52:7: "How beautiful upon the mountains are the feet of him that brings good tidings, that publishes peace; that brings good tidings of good, that publishes salvation."

[^955]: The patristic tradition of the martyrdom of the holy Apostle Peter in Rome during the reign of Nero (c. 64–67 AD), where according to Origen and Eusebius he asked to be crucified upside down, deeming himself unworthy to die in the same manner as the Lord Jesus Christ.

[^956]: The ancient liturgical and patristic tradition regarding the martyrdom of the Apostle Paul outside the walls of Rome on the Via Ostiense: when he was beheaded with the sword, milk rather than blood flowed forth from his neck, leading several onlookers and soldiers to convert to faith in Christ.

[^957]: An explicit affirmation of the Roman episcopate and primacy of the Apostle Peter and the perpetuity of the Church founded upon the rock of the apostolic confession against the gates of Hades (cf. Matt. 16:18).

[^958]: Cf. Galatians 1:15–16 ("When it pleased God, Who separated me from my mother's womb, and called me by His grace, to reveal His Son in me...") and 2 Corinthians 12:2–4 (Paul's ascent to the third heaven).

[^959]: St. Cosmas of Maiuma (the Hymnographer), foster brother of St. John of Damascus, author of many festal idiomelon compositions in the Byzantine Menaia.

[^960]: Epistolary reading for the Synaxis of the Twelve Apostles: 1 Corinthians 4:9–16 (pericope 131), detailing apostolic sufferings and humiliation for the sake of Christ.

[^961]: Gospel reading for the Synaxis of the Twelve Apostles: Mark 3:13–19 (pericope 21), recounting the ascent into the mountain and the calling and appointment of the Twelve Apostles.

[^962]: Resurrectional Theotokia (Воскресны Богородичны) for the Eight Tones. This appendix provides the Dogmatika (Theotokia sung at "Lord, I Have Cried" after "Glory... both now...") and the Aposticha Theotokia for Saturday Vespers across all Eight Tones of the Octoechos, commonly appended to Menaia for daily or festal reference when a saint's service prescribes singing the Resurrectional Theotokion of the current Tone.

[^963]: Tone 1 Dogmatikon by Saint John of Damascus: *Всемірную славу* (Greek: Τὴν παγκόσμιον δόξαν).

[^964]: Tone 1 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Се исполнися Исаїино прореченіе* (Isa. 7:14).

[^965]: Tone 2 Dogmatikon: *Прейде сѣнь законная* (Greek: Παρῆλθεν ἡ σκιὰ τοῦ νόμου), drawing on the typology of the burning bush (Exod. 3:2).

[^966]: Tone 2 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Ѡ чудесе новагѡ, всѣхъ древнихъ чудесъ*.

[^967]: Tone 3 Dogmatikon: *Како не дивимся, богомужному рождеству твоему* (Greek: Πῶς μὴ θαυμάσωμεν), expressing classical Chalcedonian Christology.

[^968]: Tone 3 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Без сѣмене ѿ божественнагѡ духа*.

[^969]: Tone 4 Dogmatikon: *Иже тебе ради богоотецъ пророкъ Давидъ* (Greek: Ὁ διὰ σὲ θεοπάτωρ προφήτης Δαυῒδ).

[^970]: Ps. 44:10 (LXX: Παρέστη ἡ βασίλισσα ἐκ δεξιῶν σου).

[^971]: Tone 4 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Призри на моленія твоихъ рабъ*.

[^972]: Tone 5 Dogmatikon: *Въ чермнѣмъ мори* (Greek: Ἐν τῇ Ἐρυθρᾷ θαλάσσῃ), typologically comparing Moses dividing the Red Sea with Gabriel and the Virgin.

[^973]: Tone 5 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Храмъ и дверь еси, палата и престолъ царевъ*.

[^974]: Tone 6 Dogmatikon: *Кто тебе не ублажитъ пресвятая дѣво* (Greek: Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε, παναγία Παρθένε), an explicit exposition of the two natures of Christ united unconfusedly.

[^975]: Tone 6 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Творецъ и избавитель мой пречистая*.

[^976]: Tone 7 Dogmatikon: *Мати убѡ позналася еси, паче естества богородице* (Greek: Μήτηρ μὲν ἐγνώσθης, ὑπὲρ φύσιν Θεοτόκε).

[^977]: Tone 7 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Подъ кровъ твой владычице вси земнородніи прибѣгающе*.

[^978]: Tone 8 Dogmatikon: *Царь небесный за человѣколюбіе на земли явися* (Greek: Ὁ βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν διὰ φιλανθρωπίαν).

[^979]: Tone 8 Resurrectional Aposticha Theotokion: *Безневѣстная дѣво, яже бога неизреченнѡ заченши плотію*.

[^980]: Rubric heading opening the second appendix: Daily Theotokia of the Eight Tones (Богородичны осми гласовъ, поемыя егда есть слава святому въ минеи, и нынѣ по гласу свята). These weekday Theotokia from the Oktoechos are sung at Vespers and Matins when a saint's service prescribes "Glory... saint, Both now... Theotokion according to the Tone of the saint."

[^981]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Небесныхъ чиновъ радованіе*.

[^982]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Свѣтлѣйшая святыхъ всѣхъ силъ*.

[^983]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Дѣво воспѣтая [всепѣтая], юже въ тебѣ тайнѡ*. The text in the 1761 Pochaiv print reads *Дѣ[в]ко воспѣтая* due to typographical ligature, corresponding to the standard Oktoechos incipit *Дѣво всепѣтая, въ тебѣ тайну*.

[^984]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): *Блудницу и блуднаго азъ, и разбойника побѣдихъ*.

[^985]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Радуйся радосте праведныхъ*.

[^986]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Радуйся Богородице Дѣво*.

[^987]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Воистинну паче ума чудная твоя величія*.

[^988]: Tone 1 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Радуйся, ѿ насъ святая Богородице Дѣво*, citing John 1:29.

[^989]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Радуйся Маріе Богородице, храме неразрушимый*, citing Ps. 64:5 (LXX).

[^990]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *На тя упованіе Богородице возложихомъ*.

[^991]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Непроходимая врата, таиннѡ запечатствованная*, drawing on the prophetic imagery of Ezek. 44:2.

[^992]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): identical to Sunday evening (*Радуйся Маріе Богородице...*).

[^993]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Wednesday morning (at Matins): *Яко плодовита маслина Дѣва*, drawing on Ps. 51:10 (LXX).

[^994]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Все упованіе мое на тя возлагаю*.

[^995]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Спаси ѿ бѣдъ рабы твоя Богородице Дѣво*.

[^996]: Tone 2 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Пріидите Матерь свѣта, пѣсньми немолчными зовуще*.

[^997]: Tone 3 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Богородице предстательнице всѣхъ молящихся тебѣ*.

[^998]: Tone 3 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Ѡгнезрачная похвало сущи небесныхъ чиновъ*.

[^999]: Tone 3 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Въ женахъ святая Богородице Мати безневѣстная*, citing Luke 1:28, 42.

[^1000]: Tone 3 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): *Без сѣмене зачала еси ѿ Духа святагѡ*.

[^1001]: Tone 3 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Зѣлми согрѣшающа мя Ѻтроковице*.

[^1002]: Tone 3 Theotokia for Thursday morning, Friday evening, and Saturday morning: abbreviated incipits referencing the full texts printed on Tuesday morning and Monday evening.

[^1003]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Радуйся свѣта облаче*, a litany of Old Testament Marian typologies (cloud of light, lampstand, pot of manna, rod of Aaron, unburnt bush, bridal chamber, throne, holy mountain, haven, table, mystical gate).

[^1004]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Богородице всѣхъ Царице*. This hymn notably references the veneration of the holy icons: "who neither venerate nor honor, O all-pure one, thine honored icon" (не кланяющихся, нижѐ чтущихъ Пречистая честный твой образъ), reflecting post-Iconoclastic hymnography.

[^1005]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Избави насъ ѿ нуждъ нашихъ, Мати Христа Бога*.

[^1006]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): *Ѡт всѣхъ бѣдъ рабы твоя сохрани*.

[^1007]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Имуще тя Богородице упованіе и предстательство*.

[^1008]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Тя стѣну стяжахомъ, Богородице пречистая*.

[^1009]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Свѣще негасимая, престоле праведный*.

[^1010]: Tone 4 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Едина чистая и пречистая Дѣво*.

[^1011]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Страшно и преславно, и веліе таинство*.

[^1012]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Ѡбрадованная, ходатайствуй твоими молитвами*.

[^1013]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Утоли болѣзни многовоздыхающія души моея*.

[^1014]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Блажимъ тя Богородице Дѣво, и славимъ тя вѣрніи по долгу*.

[^1015]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Блажимъ тя Богородице Дѣво, яко изъ тебе возсія солнце правды*.

[^1016]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Тебѣ молимся яко Божіей Матери*.

[^1017]: Tone 5 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Ѡле окаянная душе, кій ѿвѣтъ имаши рещи Судіи*. A penitential compunctionate hymn anticipating the Last Judgment.

[^1018]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Архаггелски воспоимъ вѣрніи*.

[^1019]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Архаггелское слово пріяла еси*.

[^1020]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Никтоже притекаяй къ тебѣ*.

[^1021]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): *Великихъ дарованій, чистая Дѣво Богомати*.

[^1022]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Ѡко сердца моего воспущаю къ тебѣ Владычице*.

[^1023]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Преложеніе скорбящихъ, премѣненіе болящихъ еси*.

[^1024]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Молитвами рождшія тя Христе, и Мученикъ твоихъ*. A prayer for the departed.

[^1025]: Tone 6 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Бога изъ тебе воплотившагося разумѣхомъ*.

[^1026]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Еже радуйся, тебѣ зовемъ со Анггеломъ*.

[^1027]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Умири молитвами Богородицы жизнь нашу*.

[^1028]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Радуйся солнца облаче мысленнагѡ*.

[^1029]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): *Свѣтъ Христе прозяблъ еси ѿ Дѣвы*.

[^1030]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Едину по рождествѣ пречистую Дѣву воспоимъ*.

[^1031]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Родился еси ѿ Дѣвы несказаннѡ Христе*.

[^1032]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Яже едина невмѣстимаго пріимши*.

[^1033]: Tone 7 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Моли Дѣво, со Апостолы и Мученики*.

[^1034]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Sunday evening (at Vespers): *Архаггела Гавріила гласъ воспріимше*.

[^1035]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Monday morning (at Matins): *Небесная поютъ тя, ѡбрадованная Мати безневѣстная*.

[^1036]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Monday evening (at Vespers): *Радуйся вселенныя похвало*, another comprehensive litany of typological titles.

[^1037]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Tuesday morning (at Matins): *Кровъ твой Богородице Дѣво, врачевство есть духовное*.

[^1038]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Wednesday evening (at Vespers): *Азъ Дѣво святая Богородице, къ покрову твоему прибѣгаю*.

[^1039]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Thursday morning (at Matins): *Исхити мя Владычице руки змія человѣкоубійцы*.

[^1040]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Friday evening (at Vespers): *Чистая Дѣво, Слова врата*.

[^1041]: Tone 8 Theotokion for Saturday morning (at Matins): *Владычице пріими молитвы рабовъ твоихъ*.

[^1042]: Rubric heading opening the third appendix: Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokia across the Eight Tones (Ѡтпустителныя воскресны, осми гласовъ богородичны). These are the apolytikia/theotokia sung at the end of Saturday Vespers and Sunday Matins, following the Resurrectional Troparion of the Tone.

[^1043]: Tone 1 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Гавріилу вѣщавшу тебѣ Дѣво: Радуйся* (Greek: Τοῦ Γαβριὴλ φθεγξαμένου σοι Παρθένε τὸ Χαῖρε), citing Ps. 131:8 (LXX).

[^1044]: Tone 2 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Вся паче смысла, вся преславная твоя Богородице таинства* (Greek: Πάντα ὑπὲρ ἔννοιαν, πάντα ὑπερένδοξα).

[^1045]: Tone 3 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Тя ходатайствовавшую спасеніе рода нашего* (Greek: Σὲ τὴν μεσιτεύσασαν τὴν σωτηρίαν τοῦ γένους ἡμῶν).

[^1046]: Tone 4 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Еже ѿ вѣка утаеное, и Анггеломъ несвѣдомое таинство* (Greek: Τὸ ἀπ' αἰῶνος ἀπόκρυφον, καὶ Ἀγγέλοις ἄγνωστον μυστήριον).

[^1047]: Tone 5 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Радуйся двере Господня непроходимая* (Greek: Χαῖρε πύλη Κυρίου ἡ ἀδιόδευτος).

[^1048]: Tone 6 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Благословенную нарекій Твою Матерь* (Greek: Ὁ τὴν εὐλογημένην καλέσας σου Μητέρα), citing Luke 15:8–9 (the parable of the lost coin / drachma).

[^1049]: Tone 7 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Яко нашего воскресенія сокровище* (Greek: Ὡς τῆς ἡμῶν ἀναστάσεως θησαύρισμα).

[^1050]: Tone 8 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion: *Иже насъ ради рождейся ѿ Дѣвы* (Greek: Ὁ δι' ἡμᾶς γεννηθεὶς ἐκ Παρθένου).

[^1051]: Rubric heading opening the fourth appendix: Dismissal Theotokia following the Troparia of the Saints, sung throughout the whole year at Vespers, at "God is the Lord" at Matins, and again at the end of Matins (Богородичны ѿпустителныя, по тропарѣхъ святыхъ, поемыя во все лѣто, въ вечерню же, и на Богъ Господь, во утренни, и паки въ конецъ утренни).

[^1052]: Tone 1 Dismissal Theotokion for Sunday evening at Vespers and Monday at "God is the Lord": *Чудо чудесъ Благодатная*.

[^1053]: Tone 1 Dismissal Theotokion for the end of Matins: *Пречистая Богородице на небесѣхъ благословенная*.

[^1054]: Tone 1 Dismissal Theotokion for Monday evening at Vespers and Tuesday at "God is the Lord": *Заченшая неопальнѡ огнь божественный*.

[^1055]: Tone 1 Dismissal Theotokion for the end of Matins: *Безматернаго на небеси паче мысли*.

[^1056]: Tone 1 Dismissal Theotokion for Tuesday evening at Vespers and Wednesday at "God is the Lord": *Твое предстателство имуще пречистая*.

[^1057]: Tone 1 Dismissal Theotokia for Wednesday evening / Thursday, Thursday evening / Friday, and Friday evening / Saturday: abbreviated cross-references to the texts printed above.

[^1058]: Tone 2 Dismissal Theotokion for Sunday evening at Vespers and Monday at "God is the Lord": *Благоутробія сущи источникъ*.

[^1059]: Tone 2 Dismissal Theotokion for the end of Matins: *Мати святая неизреченнагѡ свѣта*.

[^1060]: Tone 2 Dismissal Theotokion for Monday evening at Vespers and Tuesday at "God is the Lord": *Божественнагѡ быхомъ ѡбщницы естества*.

[^1061]: Tone 2 Dismissal Theotokion for the end of Matins: *Тя величаемъ Богородице вопіюще*.

[^1062]: Tone 2 Dismissal Theotokion for Tuesday evening at Vespers and Wednesday at "God is the Lord": *Препрославлена еси Богородице Дѣво*.

[^1063]: Slavonic: *Тѧ̀ велича́емъ Бц҃е вопїю́ще: ра́дуйсѧ же́зле...* Dismissal Theotokion of Tone 2, concluding the verse prescribed at the end of Matins on Tuesday evening / Wednesday morning.

[^1064]: Slavonic: *Всѧ̀ па́че смы́сла, всѧ̑ пресла̑внаѧ твоѧ̑ Бц҃е та́инства...* Tone 2 Dismissal Theotokion for Friday evening / Saturday at *God is the Lord*, identical to the Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion of Tone 2.

[^1065]: Rubric for Lenten or weekday services with *Alleluia*: Troparion of All Saints (*А҆по́столи, мꙋ́ченицы, и҆ проро́цы...*) followed by the Troparion for the Departed (*Помѧнѝ, Го́споди, ꙗ҆́кѡ бла́гъ...*).

[^1066]: Tone 3 Dismissal Theotokion (*Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства твоегѡ̀...*), common to Sunday evening / Monday morning.

[^1067]: Tone 3 Dismissal Theotokion at the conclusion of Matins (*Кі́йждо и҆дѣ́же спаса́етсѧ...*).

[^1068]: Tone 3 Dismissal Theotokion for Monday evening / Tuesday at *God is the Lord* (*Прибѣ́жище и҆ си́ла на́ша...*).

[^1069]: Tone 3 Stavrotheotokion (*Же́злъ си́лы стѧжа́вшїи Кре́стъ Сы́на твоегѡ̀...*) for Tuesday evening / Wednesday at *God is the Lord*.

[^1070]: Tone 3 Dismissal Theotokion for Wednesday evening / Thursday at *God is the Lord* (*Проро́цы проповѣ́даша...*).

[^1071]: Tone 3 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion (*Тѧ̀ хода́тайствовавшую спасе́нїе ро́да на́шегѡ...*), repeated here for Friday evening / Saturday at *God is the Lord*.

[^1072]: Tone 4 Dismissal Theotokion for Sunday evening / Monday at *God is the Lord* (*Воспита́вшейсѧ во хра́мѣ во свѧта́ѧ свѧты́хъ...*).

[^1073]: Tone 4 Dismissal Theotokion at Matins end (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ всѣ́хъ є҆сѝ творе́ній вы́сшаѧ...*).

[^1074]: Tone 4 Dismissal Theotokion for Monday evening / Tuesday at *God is the Lord* (*Ко Богоро́дицѣ прилѣ́жнѡ ны́нѣ притеце́мъ...*).

[^1075]: Tone 4 Dismissal Theotokion at Matins end (*Тѧ̀ велича́емъ Бц҃е вопїю́ще: ты̀ є҆сѝ купина̀...*).

[^1076]: Tone 4 Stavrotheotokion (*Дѣ́во пренепоро́чнаѧ Ма́ти Хрїста̀ Бо́га, ѻ҆рꙋ́жїе про́йде...*) for Tuesday evening / Wednesday at *God is the Lord*.

[^1077]: Tone 4 Dismissal Theotokion at Matins end (*Тѧ̀ велича́емъ Бц҃е, вопїю́ще: ты̀ є҆сѝ гора̀...*).

[^1078]: Tone 4 Dismissal Theotokion for Wednesday evening / Thursday at *God is the Lord* (*Сло́во Ѻ҆́чее, Хрїста̀ Бо́га на́шего...*).

[^1079]: Tone 4 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion (*Е҆́же ѿ вѣ́ка оу҆тае́ное...*), appointed for Friday evening / Saturday at *God is the Lord*.

[^1080]: Tone 5 Dismissal Theotokion for Sunday evening / Monday at *God is the Lord* (*Со А҆́ггелы небе́снаѧ, съ человѣ́ки земна́ѧ...*).

[^1081]: Tone 5 Dismissal Theotokion at Matins end (*Ѿ Дѣ́вы возсїѧ́вый мі́ру Хрїстѐ Бо́же...*).

[^1082]: Tone 5 Dismissal Theotokion for Monday evening / Tuesday at *God is the Lord* (*Ско́рый тво́й покро́въ, и҆ по́мощь...*).

[^1083]: Tone 5 Dismissal Theotokion at Matins end (*Ма́ти Бо́жїа пресвѧта́ѧ, стѣно̀ хрїстїа́нъ...*).

[^1084]: Tone 5 Stavrotheotokion (*Кресто́мъ Сы́на твоегѡ̀ богоблагода́тнаѧ...*) for Tuesday evening / Wednesday at *God is the Lord*.

[^1085]: Tone 5 Dismissal Theotokion for Wednesday evening / Thursday at *God is the Lord* (*Стра́нное Дѣ́вы та́инство мі́ру показа́сѧ...*).

[^1086]: Tone 5 Resurrectional Dismissal Theotokion (*Ра́дуйсѧ две́ре Госпо́днѧ непроходи́маѧ...*), appointed for Friday evening / Saturday at *God is the Lord*.

[^1087]: Tone 6 Dismissal Theotokion for Sunday evening / Monday at *God is the Lord* (*Нача́ло спасе́нїѧ, Гаврїи́лово провѣща́нїе...*).

[^1113]: Manuscript shelfmark and title inscription in cursive ink in the upper left corner of the front flyleaf: *Минїея* (Menaion).

[^1114]: Inscription in blue ink recording the 1927 book restoration and rebinding: *Зробивъ Р. 1927 / Стефанъ* ("Made [repaired/bound] in the Year 1927 / Stefan"), accompanied by lower corner paper reinforcement.

[^1115]: Ukrainian Greek Catholic canonical visitation inscription written and signed by the Venerable Metropolitan Andrey Sheptytsky (*+ Андрей Митрополит*), Metropolitan of Lviv and Primate of the Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church: *Предложено в часі канонічної візитації. Львів, дня 31 травня 1934. + Андрей М[итрополит]* ("Presented during the canonical visitation. Lviv, the 31st day of May 1934. + Andrey, Metropolitan").

[^1116]: Archival library paper label affixed to the head of the leather spine: *Мінея / Почаїв 1761 / травень 2 / Сгд 157/2 (інв 261)* (Menaion, Pochaiv, 1761, inventory number 261).
